# OneSpace — Project Book

**Two of Us. One Space.**

This is the **one** document for the whole OneSpace project: how we work, what exists, every decision (with what replaced what), the history, and — at the end — every earlier document kept word for word. It replaces the separate `.md` files that used to live in `docs/`, `validation/` and `release/` of the three repositories (merged here on 26 Nov 2026).

## 0. Rules for this book (read first)

1. **One book.** Do not create new project documents. Add to this book. The only other `.md` files allowed are a repository's `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, the release marker files in `release/`, and short READMEs that sit next to code that needs them (e.g. `supabase/email-templates/README.md`, `supabase/founders/README.md`).
2. **Every iteration updates the book** before it is called done: add the dated decisions to §4, the outcome to §6 (history) and the current state to §5. If something was only discussed, record it too ("Discussed, not decided").
3. **Never delete a decision.** When a new decision changes an old one, add the new one and mark the old one **⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by Dnn (date)** — in §4.2 and, if the old one is in the archive, next to it. The newest decision wins.
4. Dates are written as *26 Nov 2026*. Decision numbers continue the original log (D1–D80 are in the archive, §8).
5. This book lives in the **private** repository `BoyofKay/OneSpace` at `docs/PROJECT_BOOK.md`. The website and Command Center repositories point here; an assistant working there must also open this repository.

---

## 1. What exists (the system)

| Piece | Where | Visibility | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **App source code** (Android + iOS, Flutter) | GitHub `BoyofKay/OneSpace` | **Private** | One source code for all four apps. Also holds the database files (`supabase/`), the Founder waitlist backend (`supabase/founders/`) and this book. |
| **Website** onespace.live | GitHub `BoyofKay/onespace-app` | **Public** (required: GitHub Pages hosts it for free) | Generated by `build.py`, 5 languages (EN, AR, ES, FR, DE). Only public-safe content. |
| **Command Center** (owner's private admin console) | GitHub `BoyofKay/OneSpace-Command` | **Private** | Cloudflare Worker behind Cloudflare Access (admin login). Deploys itself on every merge to `main` (Cloudflare Workers Builds). Read-only access to both databases. |

### The four apps and two databases

| | **QA** (testing) | **Production** (real users) |
|---|---|---|
| Android app | `onespace.qa` — "OneSpace QA", amber icon | `live.onespace.app` — "OneSpace" |
| iOS app | `com.onespace.onespace.qa` | `com.onespace.onespace` |
| Database (Supabase) | **OneSpace** `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`, Seoul — test users + demo couple only | **OneSpace Production** `letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz`, Frankfurt — real users, Founder waitlist |
| Android signing | QA test key `tools/dev-signing/` (never for Play) | Play upload key (GitHub secrets); Google re-signs |
| Android build | `android-dev.yml` → APK in GitHub Releases `qa-0.1.N` | `android-play.yml` → AAB in the run's Artifacts → owner uploads to Play Console |
| iOS build | `ios-testflight.yml` (environment `qa`) | `ios-testflight.yml` (environment `production`, only for a commit already tested on QA, with typed approval) |
| Distribution | APK from GitHub Releases | Play Console closed testing (Founders = UAT) → production. App Store via TestFlight once Apple approves the developer account. |

QA and Production never share data or signing keys. The app picks its database from `--dart-define=ONESPACE_ENV=qa|production`; anything that is not `production` is QA, so a build can never reach real users by accident.

### Build workflows (GitHub Actions) — what starts them (as of 26 Nov 2026)

| Workflow | Starts when | Runner | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `android-dev.yml` | `release/READY_TO_BUILD.md` changes on `main`, or manual | Linux | **QA APK**: analyzer + all tests + signed APK + GitHub Release |
| `android-play.yml` | `release/PRODUCTION_READY_TO_BUILD.md` changes on `main`, or manual | Linux | **Production AAB** for Play (never uploads to Play itself) |
| `ios-testflight.yml` | Manual only | **macOS** (≈10× cost) | Signed iOS build, optional TestFlight upload |
| `ios-build.yml` | Manual only | **macOS** | Unsigned compile check of iOS QA + Production |
| `ios-preflight.yml` | Pull request touching iOS files, or manual | Linux | Cheap iOS source checks |
| `prod-db-upgrade.yml` | Manual only, typed confirmation | Linux | Production database change: dry run, or backup + apply |
| `db-backup.yml` | Sundays 02:41 Riyadh, or manual | Linux | Encrypted QA database backup |
| `qa-android-startup-*.yml`, `qa-invitation-handoff-regression.yml` | Pull request touching those files | Linux | Focused regression checks |
| `qa-archive-ciphertext-integrity.yml` | Push to `main` touching archive crypto files | Linux | Archive crypto tests (archive feature itself is cancelled, see ADR-2026-11-20) |
| `resolve-flutter-dependencies.yml` | `pubspec.yaml` changes on `main`, or manual | Linux | Writes `pubspec.lock` |
| `qa-recovery-verify.yml`, `verify-iteration.yml`, `screenshots.yml`, `ui-audit.yml` | Manual only | Linux | Occasional checks / robots |
| Website `site-check.yml` etc. | Website pushes / PRs | Linux | Free (public repository) |
| Command `ci.yml` | Push to `main`, pull requests | Linux | Build + tests of the Command Center |

## 2. How we work (SDLC) — agreed 26 Nov 2026 (D85–D88)

**Roles.** Ahmed (owner) = Product Owner: ideas, priorities, acceptance, UAT, approvals, GitHub visibility switches, store uploads. The AI assistant = engineer: analysis, code, database files, tests, builds, reports, and keeping this book up to date.

### One iteration

| # | Step | Who | What exactly |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Idea / feedback | Owner | Describe the requirement, idea or tester feedback. |
| 2 | Analysis | Assistant | Confirm understanding, list the changes, flag conflicts with earlier decisions (§4). Owner agrees the scope. |
| 3 | Build on QA | Assistant | Work on **one** working branch `iteration/YYYY-MM-DD-topic` made from `main`. Code + numbered database file in `supabase/schema/` + tests run locally. Database change applied to **QA only**, from the committed file. Update this book. |
| 4 | "Done" | Assistant → Owner | Merge the branch into `main` once. Tell the owner: *"Iteration done. Please make `BoyofKay/OneSpace` public"* — only if the build needs paid minutes (see note). Security check of what becomes visible is done first. |
| 5 | QA release | Assistant | After the owner confirms it is public: update `release/READY_TO_BUILD.md` once → one QA APK (`qa-0.1.N`) on GitHub Releases. Confirm it exists, then tell the owner: *"Make it private again."* |
| 6 | UAT | Owner | Test on real phones (both accounts). Feedback goes back to step 1. |
| 7 | Approval | Owner → Assistant | Owner approves. Assistant tags that exact commit `approved-YYYY-MM-DD` and records it in §6. **No production build.** The production database files for this iteration are already in `supabase/schema/` and listed as *pending for Production* in §5. |

### Go-live (after several approved iterations)

| # | Step | Who |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Owner says **"go Production"** and makes `BoyofKay/OneSpace` public. | Owner |
| 2 | Production database: dry run of all pending files → backup → apply (via `prod-db-upgrade.yml` or tracked migrations). Never test a migration on Production. | Assistant |
| 3 | Build **once** from the latest `approved-…` tag: Production AAB (`android-play.yml`) — owner uploads it to Google Play Console. iOS Production (`ios-testflight.yml`) when the Apple developer account is approved. | Assistant, then owner uploads |
| 4 | Confirm the artifacts exist, update §5/§6, tell the owner: *"Make it private again."* | Assistant → Owner |

Each new build already contains every earlier approved change, so one go-live build carries all lined-up iterations.

**Note on paid minutes.** Private repositories get a free monthly allowance of Linux minutes; macOS minutes count about 10×. In Nov 2026 the private app repository ran **1,471 workflow runs**, and from ~25 Nov every run in the private repositories fails at start-up (`startup_failure`), which is what GitHub does when the allowance or spending limit is used up. The trigger clean-up of 26 Nov removes most automatic runs. The owner checks GitHub → Settings → Billing; if minutes remain, Android QA builds can run while private.

### Branch rule (D86)

- `main` is the only lasting branch. Every assistant starts from the latest `main`.
- One working branch per iteration, created from `main`, merged back into `main` when the iteration is done. Never branch from another working branch. Never leave finished work unmerged.
- Old branches are **not deleted** (owner's choice, 26 Nov 2026); they are listed and labelled in §7. Do not continue work on them.

### Rules every assistant follows (summary; full text in each repository's `AGENTS.md`)

- Read this book first, then the repository's `AGENTS.md`.
- Every database change is a numbered file in `supabase/schema/` **before** it is applied to QA. No ad-hoc SQL on QA or Production (0319 and 0320 below exist because this rule was broken).
- QA first, Production only at go-live with the owner's approval.
- Never change repository visibility, credentials or production without the owner.
- Use `[skip ci]` for documentation-only commits.
- Report: commit, workflow run, artifact/version, tests, what is unverified, and whether the owner must act.

## 4. Decisions

The original decision log (**D1–D80**, 15–17 Nov 2026), the 20 Nov ownership/deletion decision record (**ADR-2026-11-20**) and all iteration notes are in the archive (§8) word for word, with override marks added. New decisions continue below from **D81**.

### 4.1 Decisions after D80

| # | Date | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-2026-11-20 | 20 Nov 2026 | **Personal storage belongs to each account; shared storage belongs to the current couple.** No automated archiving, export, restore or "My Archives" on leaving, removal, shared-space deletion or account deletion. When a relationship ends, the pair's shared content is permanently erased after an explicit warning; each person keeps their private records and is unpaired. Account deletion has a 7-day cancelable grace period. Full text: archive, `docs/ADR_2026-11-20_PERSONAL_SHARED_OWNERSHIP_AND_DELETION.md`. | **Active** |
| D81 | 22 Nov 2026 | **Founding 500 = the first 500 people** (not couples) who confirm their email, on their own or as a couple; website and app share one list matched by email. **No Founder number is ever shown or emailed**; the app shows a "Founder Member" badge. (QA 0318, Production 22 Nov.) | **Active** |
| D82 | 21–22 Nov 2026 | **Sign-in is email and password.** Google/Apple sign-in buttons only appear if a provider is switched on in Supabase Auth, so they must stay switched off (not re-verified on 26 Nov 2026). | **Active** |
| D83 | 20 Nov 2026 | **Tagline is exactly "Two of Us. One Space."** An earlier slogan was abandoned; tests block it in the app, emails and documents. | **Active** |
| D84 | 22 Nov 2026 | **Founder eligibility is decided by the server only** (0319): a sign-up cannot make itself a Founder by sending `founder=true`; offers are reserved in server-only metadata and counted so the last places cannot be over-claimed. | **Active** |
| D85 | 26 Nov 2026 | **AI engineering and build-cost policy** (`AGENTS.md` in all three repositories) adopted: owner = Product Owner; assistants own engineering; approvals needed for production, destructive changes, visibility, credentials and spending; temporary-public procedure for paid builds. | **Active** |
| D86 | 26 Nov 2026 | **Branch rule** (§2): `main` only; one working branch per iteration, merged back at the end; no branch-from-branch; old branches kept and labelled, not deleted. | **Active** |
| D87 | 26 Nov 2026 | **SDLC** (§2): idea → analysis → build on QA → "done" (owner makes repo public if needed) → one QA APK (owner makes it private) → UAT → approval (tag `approved-…`, no production build) → … → go-live: owner approval + public → Production database → one Production AAB (+ iOS when Apple approves) → private. | **Active** |
| D88 | 26 Nov 2026 | **Fewer automatic builds**: iOS (macOS) builds manual only; old branch triggers removed; checks that duplicated the QA release gate are manual or pull-request only; QA backup weekly. | **Active** |

The waitlist **data** was never public (Production database, service role only); only the server code was visible. Its history remains in the public repository's git history. | **Active** |

| D91 | 26 Nov 2026 | **Every database change is a file first.** Reaffirms the 17 Nov lesson after two violations were found: 0319 (applied to QA 22 Nov, file only on an unmerged branch) and an undocumented QA edit of `demo_reset` (recorded as 0320). | **Active** |

Landscape layout work is not needed while this holds; Today shortcuts and Quick add were still made width-safe. | **Active** |

Country holiday calendars stay, because each person chooses their own countries. | **Active** |

Storage unchanged (`linked_counter_id`); only counters in the same space can be connected. | **Active** |

Chosen on Infrastructure from a dropdown (Free / Pro / Team); cards and hourly alerts use it; a Cloudflare limit still wins. Quotas are Supabase's published included amounts, to be confirmed on the billing page. | **Active** |
| D99 | 26 Nov 2026 | **Support inbox:** Open (new, in progress) and Closed (answered, closed) tabs; 10 per page for the inbox and the access log. Command Center version 1.5.0. | **Active** |
| D100 | 26 Nov 2026 | **Named counter timelines:** every timeline has a name (rename/delete); one counter can be on several timelines; private timelines hold private counters, shared ones shared counters. Database 0321 (tables counter_timelines, counter_timeline_members); old one-to-one links were moved onto timelines; `linked_counter_id` is no longer written. The Command Center's only database change, through one key-protected function (0322; Supabase stores only the key's SHA-256). | **Active** |

The Arabic test now also checks texts split across lines and both sides of `condition ? A : B`. | **Active** |

The **internal note** and the admin who changed it stay private: people can no longer read those columns (0323). Website tickets have no app view and are answered by email. | **Active** |

Arabic for "Planning intelligence" is «التخطيط الذكي». | **Active** |

### Command Center
- 26 Nov: support-ticket screenshots can be opened from the Support page (audited, server-side). Needs two new Cloudflare secrets before it works: `QA_SUPPORT_STORAGE_READ_KEY`, `PRODUCTION_SUPPORT_STORAGE_READ_KEY` (owner decision: these are server keys with storage read access; until set, the feature says "not configured").

### Google Play reports (Command Center, built 26 Nov 2026 — needs 3 owner steps)
1. Play Console → **Download reports → Statistics** → copy the Cloud Storage URI (`gs://pubsite_prod_<number>/…`). In Cloudflare (Workers & Pages → onespace-command → Settings → Variables and Secrets) add secret **`PLAY_REPORTS_BUCKET`** = `pubsite_prod_<number>`.
2. Google Cloud project `onespace-b70a2` → IAM → **Service accounts** → create `onespace-command-play-reports` with **no roles** → Keys → Add key → JSON. Add secret **`PLAY_REPORTS_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`** = the whole file.
3. Play Console → **Users and permissions → Invite new users** → the service-account email → account permission **"View app information and download bulk reports"** only. Google can take up to 24 hours to grant access; reports appear about 2 days after the first installs.

The Mobile App tab then shows new installs and uninstalls (30 days) and devices with the app; Systems & Connections shows the row as Live. Store-listing conversion is not in these reports (Play Console shows it).

### Support ticket outcomes — owner setup (once per environment)
1. Make a long random key (at least 32 characters, e.g. from a password manager). Never paste it in chat.
2. Cloudflare → Workers & Pages → onespace-command → Settings → Variables and Secrets → add secret **`SUPPORT_OPS_KEY_QA`** with that key.
3. Supabase → project **OneSpace** (QA) → SQL editor → run `select private.set_support_ops_key('<the same key>');`
4. Repeat with a different key for Production (`SUPPORT_OPS_KEY_PRODUCTION`, project OneSpace Production). Easiest: `select encode(extensions.gen_random_bytes(24), 'hex');` makes a 48-character key. QA key set 26 Nov 2026 and tested by the owner.

### Open items for the owner
1. **GitHub account locked for a billing issue** (confirmed 26 Nov 2026: GitHub's message on the website's jobs is "The job was not started because your account is locked due to a billing issue"). Every GitHub Actions job in all three repositories is blocked, **including the public website** — so making a repository public does not help until the lock is lifted. Owner: GitHub → Settings → Billing and plans (payment method / unpaid invoice). GitHub Pages deploys and Cloudflare builds still work.
2. Decide the Command Center screenshot keys (above).
3. Apple Developer account (iOS).
4. Website: the server code of the Founder backend remains in the public repository's *history* (D89). Rewriting public history is possible but destructive; not done.

### 26 Nov 2026 — clean-up iteration (this book)
- Owner adopted the AI engineering and build-cost policy (D85), the SDLC (D87) and the branch rule (D86).
- Merged into `main`: closed-tester feedback (app, Command Center, website — all three by the owner with ChatGPT the same morning), Founder server authority 0319 (#145), iOS readiness (#134).
- Fixed: website FAQ count checks for the 6 new answers; Command Center screenshot check that did not compile; FAQ wording "valid for 48 hours".
- Found and recorded: 0319 applied to QA without a merged file; QA-only `demo_reset` edit (0320).
- Workflow triggers reduced (D88). Founder backend moved to the private repository (D89). All documents merged into this book (D90).
- Local results: app `flutter test` 427/427, iOS tool checks 11/11, Command Center build + 218/218 tests, website 6/6 checks.
- **Command Center feedback (26 Nov, later):** "See the inbox" button for Zoho Mail; service cards and Systems rows coloured by status; sidebar badges for Website, Founder Outreach and Systems load in the background; the Firebase "Reported dates in window" tile (always 7, because Firebase always answers with a 7-day window) became "Days with counts", saying how many days Firebase hid for privacy; Website page visits now come from the Command Center's own GoatCounter reading (the Production report showed 0 while other figures were filled); one Reload refreshes every panel on Communications and Mobile App (per-panel buttons removed, "Updated hh:mm" shown); Support inbox (up to 200) and access log paginated, 8 per page; Systems rows updated (iOS, closed testing, repositories, Founder backend location, email-only sign-in) plus new rows "GitHub Actions builds" and "Google Play reports"; Google Play installs reader built (needs the 3 steps above). Local: build OK, 238/238 tests.
- **App feedback iteration (26 Nov):** portrait lock (D93), all world currencies (D95), regional themes removed (D94), connected counters timeline (D96), 166 missing Arabic texts added plus a coverage test (D97), 46 dialogs and 14 bottom sheets made scrollable, Quick add scrolls, Today shortcuts keep a phone-like size on wide screens. Local: flutter analyze no errors/warnings, flutter test 439/439, iOS checks 11/11. Not built: QA APK waits for the GitHub billing lock and the owner's go.
- **Command Center v1.5.0 (26 Nov):** Supabase plan dropdown drives all quotas (D98); Support inbox Open/Closed tabs, 10 per page (D99). Local: build OK, 243/243 tests. onespace.live "What's new" is updated when these app changes reach testers.
- **Timelines, tickets, Zoho (26 Nov, evening):** 0321 and 0322 applied to QA and checked (a stranger sees no timelines; the ticket function refuses calls without the key and signed-in users cannot call it). App: named timelines (D100), Arabic added, flutter test 437/437. Command Center v1.6.0 deployed (Cloudflare build success 19:45 UTC): ticket outcomes (D101) and the **Zoho fix** — Zoho allows ~10 new access tokens per 10 minutes and every Overview/Systems/background read asked for one; the token (1 h) and counts (5 min) are now reused and each failure names its step. 249/249 tests.
- Website and Command Center merged to `main` with the owner's go-ahead (no GitHub cost): onespace.live redeployed by GitHub Pages; Command Center deployed by Cloudflare Workers Builds (build 0521bfa1, success). The website's own GitHub checks and IndexNow ping could not start (billing lock).
- **QA APK `qa-0.1.482` built (26 Nov, 20:45 UTC):** owner made BoyofKay/OneSpace public and cleared the GitHub billing lock; run 45891190056 (attempt 1 hit the billing lock → startup failure; attempt 2 success: build + publish). Release `qa-0.1.482` has `OneSpace-QA-0.1.482.apk`. Owner returns the repository to private after downloading. Next: owner UAT, then approval tag.
- **UAT fixes (26 Nov, night; branch `iteration/2026-11-26-uat-fixes`):** owner UAT of qa-0.1.482 (16 pass, 3 fail, 2 not tested). Fixed: **currency** — the saved currency was cached in memory and never cleared, so a new account signed in after an SAR account showed SAR; now per account and reset at sign-out (new accounts start on USD). **Arabic** (D104) — ~200 missing texts added, required-field labels, shared editor fields, contact card fields, experience fields, Activity priority/status words, server "Needs attention" reasons, account notices, invitation declined, occasion planning messages, "updates you have already seen"; the terms screen followed the account's saved English. **Tickets** (D105) — reply for the person, status and outcome in Your messages; 0323 on QA; Command Center v1.6.1 (Reply to the person + internal note). **Examples** (D106). Notifications re-written on language change (0324 on QA). Local: flutter analyze no errors/warnings; flutter test 440/440 (3 new Arabic checks); Command Center build OK, 250/250 tests. HawkScan not run (no API key in this environment).
- **Round-2 release (26 Nov, 22:18 UTC):** owner made BoyofKay/OneSpace public; release marker commit `f9105c6`; run 45902755025 success (build + publish); release `qa-0.1.483` has `OneSpace-QA-0.1.483.apk`. Command Center v1.6.1 merged (`eae6d67`) and deployed by Cloudflare Workers Builds (success); its GitHub "Command CI" job did not start (Actions budget on the private repo, no charge; tests 250/250 run locally). 0323 applied to Production (D105). ## 8. Archive — every earlier document, word for word
Moved here on 26 Nov 2026 from the three repositories (D90). Ordered by the date each document was first written. Each entry shows its source, dates and status. Headings inside the documents are pushed down one level so this book keeps one outline; nothing else was changed, except the ⚠️ override marks added to the decision log and one quoted use of the abandoned slogan (website brand table), written as *the abandoned slogan* because tests block it everywhere. Links inside them point to their old locations.

### A1. OneSpace · `docs/AUTH_CALLBACK_SETUP.md`

*First written 12 Nov 2026 · last changed 12 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Preview — Email Confirmation and Password Recovery

##### Why this exists

Supabase Auth sends email links for confirming a new account or resetting a password.
The app now contains an Android auth callback and password recovery screens, but Supabase
must approve the destination. Until the approved callback is configured, a reset or
confirmation link may lead to the default Site URL rather than the app, or requests may fail.

##### One required Supabase dashboard setting

Project: **OneSpace** (`ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`).

1. Open [Supabase → Authentication → URL Configuration](https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo/auth/url-configuration).
2. Under **Redirect URLs / Additional Redirect URLs**, add this exact URL:
   `onespace-preview://auth-callback/`
3. Save changes. Keep existing valid redirect URLs.
4. Ensure the email provider and **Confirm email** configuration are as intended.
5. For testers beyond Supabase's default email quotas, configure a supported
   external SMTP sender before sending frequent account emails.

Do not paste or commit SMTP credentials, service-role keys or secret keys.

##### Android preview behavior

- Sign-up supplies `emailRedirectTo` pointing at the callback above.
- Resend confirmation supplies the same `emailRedirectTo`.
- Forgot password calls `resetPasswordForEmail(email, redirectTo: callback)`.
- When a reset email returns through the Android deep link, the Supabase Flutter
  library processes it; the auth gate displays a **new password** screen.
- After changing the password, the app signs out and requires normal sign-in.
- The callback is registered in the debug Android manifest only; do not use the
  preview URI for future production packages.
- The preview uses a custom URI scheme. For production, plan verified HTTPS app
  links and registered domains to reduce link-interception risk.

##### Test gate (must be done on device to mark complete)

- [ ] New user receives confirmation email, taps link on Android, sees signed-in app.
- [ ] Resending verification works and older links expire as expected.
- [ ] Existing user requests recovery from sign-in; link returns to app.
- [ ] New password screen validates strength and matching confirmation.
- [ ] Reset password updates successfully and returns user to sign-in.
- [ ] Old password cannot sign in; new password works.
- [ ] Link replay and expired tokens do not grant access.
- [ ] Confirm the process on two physical test accounts/devices.
- [ ] Verify the provider's SMTP/rate limits are adequate.

The code and unit validation checks alone are **not** proof that the email-to-app
flow works. The backend redirect configuration and real email delivery need validation.

### A2. OneSpace · `docs/CALENDAR_MEMORIES_PERFORMANCE_QA.md`

*First written 12 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace – Calendar, Memories and Speed Acceptance

##### Release scope (developed, not yet user-accepted)

###### Performance and instrumentation
- One `get_home_snapshot()` request replaces multiple space/member/summary calls.
- A two-minute user-scoped memory cache avoids duplicate membership queries for tabs.
- Home reuses authenticated partner display names in Plans, avoiding two sequential
  Seoul queries in the common Home → Plans path; direct Plans entry still fetches safely.
- Tasks stream starts before optional member-name lookups finish.
- Home does not re-request data on every quick tab switch. Pull down to refresh.
- App performance (Profile icon → App performance) displays timing numbers only.
- Database remains in **Seoul (`ap-northeast-2`)**. A round trip from Saudi Arabia
  can be slow; do not relocate the live project during this beta without a
  dedicated migration and rollback plan.

###### Calendar
- Date-only events and annual occasions; includes task target dates.
- Future countdowns; annual dates recur, including Feb 29 only on leap years.
- Create, edit, archive/restore; private/shared RLS.
- Live Calendar refreshes for event/task edits and private grant changes while
  visible; on tab return, it requeries RLS before treating cached data as current.
  Subscriptions are suspended when Calendar is not on screen.
- Private owners can grant and revoke read-only access.
- Not delivered: timed meetings, time zone selection, scheduled push notifications.

###### Memories
- Dated text journal; create/edit, pin, archive/restore.
- Shared/private, revocable read-only grant.
- Conflict detection for stale edits.
- Not delivered: secure photo/video attachments, rich text, geotags.

##### Acceptance steps: Samsung and older phone

- [ ] Install the published APK over the existing `.preview` version; do not clear data.
- [ ] Cold-launch OneSpace. Use the OS Back gesture to leave a phone-auth prompt.
- [ ] Open Home, Tasks, Calendar, Lists and Notes; none should show a permanent spinner.
- [ ] Open top-right Profile → App performance. Copy the measured timings.
- [ ] Switch Home → Tasks → Home 5 times. No new Home HTTP request should
      be launched on every switch.
- [ ] Test over Wi-Fi and cellular and on the older Android device. Record
      cold-launch time, Home time, and Tasks time separately.
- [ ] Verify partner event edits and due-date changes update the open Calendar
      without pull-to-refresh; check Calendar tab switching stops unnecessary
      background subscriptions.
- [ ] Account A creates a shared calendar event, an annual occasion, and a
      private occasion. Account B sees only the shared entries.
- [ ] Account A shares the private occasion view-only. B can read but not edit.
      A revokes; B refreshes Calendar and can no longer fetch it.
- [ ] Test Feb 29 yearly anniversary and that the same day remains correct
      when both phones have different time zones.
- [ ] A creates a shared memory with an old date and a private memory.
      B reads the shared one, but not the private one.
- [ ] A grants the private memory view-only. B cannot modify the record;
      after revocation it disappears on a fresh query.
- [ ] Verify that existing tasks, lists, notes and pairing remain intact.
- [ ] Note any Arabic RTL clipping or untranslated strings.

##### Diagnostic data handling

The in-app timing report stays only in device process memory and is not
sent automatically. It does not collect email addresses, access tokens,
user-generated journal entries or identifiable content.

##### Server-side regression

- `supabase/tests/0007_calendar_home_rls.sql`: home response + private
  calendar sharing and revocation for paired accounts.
- `supabase/tests/0008_memories_rls.sql`: private Memory isolation,
  owner-controlled sharing and authorized Home totals.
- `test/calendar_rules_test.dart`: yearly recurrence, leap dates and
  timezone-neutral day countdowns.

Release-mode build success means **compiled**, not a verified performance
improvement. Verify actual timing before claiming the app is faster.

### A3. OneSpace · `docs/SECOND_ACCOUNT_TESTING.md`

*First written 12 Nov 2026 · last changed 12 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Two-account pairing acceptance test (OneSpace Preview)

This is a **development-only** test. Keep actual personal/sensitive data out of the shared space until the feature passes end-to-end acceptance testing.

##### Create a temporary partner account through Supabase Auth (not SQL)

Project: `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`

1. Open [Supabase Authentication → Users](https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo/auth/users).
2. Select **Add user → Create new user**.
3. Use the non-deliverable test address `onespace.qa.partner.20261112@example.invalid`.
4. Set a **unique, strong password**, stored privately by the tester.
5. Check **Auto Confirm User** and select **Create user**.

This creates a real Supabase Auth identity using the official admin interface,
while **preserving email verification for all ordinary registrations**.
Never insert fake users directly into `public.profiles`: a profile alone cannot authenticate.
Do not turn off confirmation globally for this test.

Because the address has no inbox, **forgot-password and confirmation emails cannot work for this account**. Use a real email alias for those tests.

##### Test on one Android phone

1. Sign in using the first account that already owns the shared space.
2. Open **Home → Create invitation code**. The code appears on screen and is copied to the clipboard.
3. Open **Sign out**.
4. Sign in as the temporary partner account using its private password.
5. Under **Home → Join your partner**, tap **Paste invitation code**, then **Join space**. Do not create a second space.
6. Confirm that the home screen shows **2 of 2 members** and both display names.
7. Open **Plans**, create a shared task, then sign out and sign back in as the first user.
8. Confirm the task is present under Plans. Complete it, switch back to the test user, and confirm the new status is visible.
9. Try reusing the invitation from a third test account later. It must be rejected.
10. In normal testing, generating a replacement code must make the old code unusable; revoking a pending invitation must also invalidate it.

##### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Both test identities can sign in independently, restoring the correct account session.
- [ ] Each profile is separate, but both accounts share the same space.
- [ ] The second account can accept exactly one valid invitation.
- [ ] Both accounts see each other's **display names**, but not private credentials.
- [ ] Shared tasks survive sign-out, reopening, and account switching.
- [ ] An outsider cannot view or change space data.
- [ ] Revoked, expired and reused invitations are rejected.

The database SQL regressions are already passing. This test verifies **actual app behavior**, not merely SQL permissions.

##### Cleanup and caution

The dummy email cannot receive recovery emails. Its password must not be shared in a repository, issue, or chat. An accepted test user has genuine access to that shared space, so do not pair it with sensitive real-life data.

Before deleting a paired test user, review linked shared records and deletion rules. The current beta does **not** yet implement a safe self-service unlink or account deletion process; deleting a user without considering linked task/invitation data may be blocked by foreign-key constraints. Cleanup is a separate acceptance task.

##### Privacy, assignment and editing acceptance

- [ ] With account A, create a Shared task and assign it to account B.
- [ ] Sign in as B. Confirm it appears and displays B as the assignee.
- [ ] As B, edit its title, priority or target date, then complete it.
- [ ] Sign back into A and verify the edits and completion survived account switching.
- [ ] As A, create a Private task. Sign into B and verify it does **not** appear under All visible, Shared or Private. Home's private-task count must not include A's task.
- [ ] Switch back to A and verify that only A can see and edit the Private task.
- [ ] The task editor must not offer a visibility switch for an existing task.
- [ ] Check Home's open shared, assigned-to-me, my private, and completed shared counts.

The role-based SQL privacy regression in `supabase/tests/0003_task_privacy_and_assignment.sql` passed on the live database within a rolled-back transaction. Physical app acceptance remains separate.

### A4. OneSpace · `docs/SECURITY_AUTH_AND_SHARING.md`

*First written 12 Nov 2026 · last changed 12 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace authentication, privacy and shared-content security review

Status: November 2026, development preview. This is a design and implementation
review, not a certification or penetration-test report.

##### Threat model

- Two separately authenticated people share one space; either person may keep
  private data that the other is not entitled to see.
- Partners may own different physical phones, or may be testing two accounts on
  one phone.
- Shared content must not grant the recipient any editing access when the owner
  grants **view-only** access to a previously private item.
- A stolen, unlocked or unattended device must not expose existing content via
  an automatically restored app screen.
- A leaked access or refresh token can be used without going through an app
  lock screen: local app locking is NOT a substitute for backend session policy.

##### Implemented safeguards

1. **Identity**: Supabase Auth controls password verification and token refresh.
   OneSpace stores no passwords, fingerprint images, biometric templates or
   custom numeric PIN. The app's recent-account list contains only emails.
2. **Device unlock**: local_auth 3.0.2 defaults to
   `authenticate(biometricOnly:false)`, permitting OS biometric recognition
   or fallback to device PIN, pattern or password. The phone decides which
   methods are offered. A damaged fingerprint reader does not require
   OneSpace to implement its own PIN system.
3. **App privacy timeout**: local lock after 5 minutes without UI interaction.
   Content is covered as soon as the app becomes inactive; returning after
   30 seconds away or after the idle threshold requires device-unlock or
   account-password verification. These are **local presentation controls**.
4. **Saved auth session**: Android Keystore-backed encrypted secure storage
   replaces plaintext shared_preferences for session persistence. A one-time
   migration only removes the old copy after verifying the encrypted copy.
   If secure storage is unavailable, the user may need to sign in again.
   Android application data backup is disabled.
5. **Independent devices**: switching or signing out uses local Supabase
   session scope, so it should not revoke the user's other phone's session.
6. **RLS**: a couple's shared content can be read by current members of that
   space. Private content is owner-only unless the owner explicitly grants
   view-only access to the current partner. Every object permission is
   enforced by Postgres, not merely by hiding Flutter buttons.
7. **Private sharing**: object_shares only exposes grants to their owner or
   recipient. A SECURITY DEFINER RPC verifies ownership, the object being
   private, current membership and the recipient before inserting/revoking
   a grant. Normal mobile clients cannot write to grants directly.
8. **Revocation**: a revoked grant immediately prevents fresh database reads.
   A partner may still possess information they already viewed or copied,
   and old on-screen views may require refresh. Revocation cannot erase
   screenshots or knowledge; do not promise otherwise.

##### Different kinds of timeout

| Control | In this preview | Guarantee |
| --- | --- | --- |
| App idle privacy lock | 5 minutes | UI-only; no remote token revocation |
| Background privacy shield | Immediately on inactive/hidden | Hides Flutter content while backgrounded |
| Unlock after background | 30 seconds | UI-only |
| JWT expiration | Supabase configuration (verify in dashboard; typical 1 hour) | Signed JWT expires on server; refresh may continue |
| Absolute server session limit | Not configured or verified | Requires plan with session timeout support |
| Server inactivity termination | Not configured or verified | Requires plan with session timeout support |
| Forced sign-out everywhere | Not enabled by routine account switching | Use explicitly for account compromise/security action |

**Recommended for a public release**: plan-eligible server-side absolute
session lifetime (for example 30 days), backend inactivity timeout (for
example 7 days), short JWT life (normally default 1 hour), plus a
device-local 5-minute privacy lock. These are **proposed defaults**, not
settings already enabled. Consider the consequences for remote long-distance
partners and offline mobile use before enforcing remote session limits.

##### Remaining security gates before public use

- [ ] Confirm both physical Android devices can use a system PIN when
      fingerprint is missing or broken (OS version/hardware dependent).
- [ ] Device-test 5-min idle, 30-sec background, notification switch,
      hardware Back, offline and cold-start session restoration.
- [ ] Test the existing session's one-time encrypted-storage migration and
      logout/relogin; verify old plaintext session key is removed.
- [ ] Verify recoverability when Android Keystore data is damaged or restored
      to a new phone; fall back to password sign-in without leaking tokens.
- [ ] Validate RLS with two users and a third outsider for all shared and
      private parent/child objects.
- [ ] Validate grant/revoke changes on two phones, including reopening a
      previously displayed view-only list/note and losing membership.
- [ ] Check Supabase Auth rate limiting, email confirmation, password policy,
      SMTP, leaked-password protection (plan-dependent), and sign-in audit
      logs in the project dashboard.
- [ ] Implement account unlinking, deletion, data retention/export and breach
      response. Never claim revocation removes copies already seen.
- [ ] Build a proper production signing setup, protect CI releases and
      enforce verified HTTPS deep links instead of preview custom schemes.
- [ ] Consider passkeys/MFA for high-sensitivity users after evaluating
      beta support and Android Digital Asset Links setup.
- [ ] Independent code review and privacy/security testing before listing
      this app publicly.

Supabase documentation: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/sessions
Android local_auth: https://pub.dev/packages/local_auth
Encrypted-storage package: https://pub.dev/packages/flutter_secure_storage

### A5. OneSpace · `docs/SHARED_WORKSPACE_ACCEPTANCE.md`

*First written 12 Nov 2026 · last changed 12 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — Shared Workspace Slice (Preview QA)

##### Delivered in this iteration

| Capability | Database | App | Tests |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Shared/private lists | `lists` with member/owner RLS | Lists tab; type and privacy at creation | SQL visibility tests |
| Shopping and checklists | `list_items`, inherited parent privacy | Add, edit, quantities, units, check off, remove, restore, bulk clear | SQL isolation + Dart validation |
| Archived lists | `archived_at` | Archive, show archived, restore | DB constraints |
| Shared/private notes | `notes`, member/owner RLS | Notes tab, create/edit, pin, archive/restore | SQL privacy + Dart validation |
| Concurrent note editing | Monotonic `updated_at` trigger | Conditional updates block stale saves | SQL concurrency regression |
| Home dashboard | RLS-filtered reads | Task summary and counts for lists and notes; quick links | RLS baseline + existing task summary tests |
| English and Arabic | Existing profile locale | New screens translated, RTL inherited | Translation-key parity test |

The entire iteration is designed as **remote persistent data**. Creating an item on one account should make it visible to the other account if and only if it was created as **Shared**.

##### Two-account acceptance checklist

Use your first account and the test partner account. Do not put important private information in test content.

- [ ] Account A opens Lists, creates **Shopping** named "Weekly groceries", visibility **Shared**.
- [ ] A adds "Milk", quantity 2, unit "litres", plus a second item without a unit.
- [ ] Account B opens Lists and sees the same list and items.
- [ ] B checks Milk; A sees it checked after opening the list again (and eventually via live updates when both devices are open).
- [ ] A chooses **Clear completed items**. B does not see them in the active list, but they are visible through **Show removed items**.
- [ ] B restores Milk. Both can see it again.
- [ ] A creates a **Private** checklist. B cannot see the list or its child items.
- [ ] A archives and restores a list; the items remain intact.
- [ ] A creates a **Shared** note and B edits it; A sees B's changes.
- [ ] If A and B both open the same note, B saves first and A tries to save a stale copy: A sees a conflict warning rather than silently overwriting B.
- [ ] A creates a **Private** note. B cannot see it, including in Home totals.
- [ ] A pins and unpins a note, then archives and restores it.
- [ ] Check Home counts for shopping lists, checklists and notes; counts should reflect only content visible to the current user.
- [ ] Change one account to Arabic in More. Lists/Notes and Home should be right-to-left; the other account's language remains independent.
- [ ] Account A and B retain their previous Tasks and pairing after updating the preview APK.

##### Privacy and architectural decisions

- Data is shared by **space membership** with Row Level Security, not by client-side hiding.
- List and note visibility is immutable after creation to avoid unintended private/shared transitions.
- All child items inherit the parent list's access control.
- Removal/archiving is recoverable; hard DELETE is not granted to normal client users.
- Server-side timestamps are authoritative. For notes, conditional updates reject a save if another partner has already changed the record.
- Concurrent **list item** edits currently use last-write-wins; note conflicts are protected.
- No notifications, offline edit queue, file/photo attachments, rich-text formatting, or third-party sharing exist in this slice.
- Account deletion, unpairing, retention decisions, security hardening and production signing still require work before public distribution.

##### Regression tests

- `supabase/tests/0004_lists_notes_rls.sql`: shared membership vs private content.
- `supabase/tests/0005_notes_concurrent_edit.sql`: rejecting stale note edits.
- `test/lists_notes_validation_test.dart`: bilingual input and numeric/text limits.
- `test/onespace_localization_test.dart`: English/Arabic key parity.

##### Backlog after this iteration

The next high-value verticals are a shared calendar with timezone-aware occasions and reminders, milestones/countdowns, memories/attachments, share inbox and cross-app sharing, then privacy and consent workflows including unlinking/account deletion. Include full Arabic accessibility and offline behavior in each vertical.

### A6. OneSpace · `docs/CONSOLIDATED_PREVIEW_R12_R16_QA.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — consolidated private Android preview (R12–R16)

##### Status
**Source committed, database migrations deployed, NOT yet a compiled, signed or phone-accepted APK.**
No GitHub Actions were dispatched for R16; the release marker at `release/READY_TO_BUILD.md` must remain untouched until a single full validation build is authorized. The existing downloadable preview 0.1.395 predates R12–R16.

##### Why these changes belong together
This bundle closes gaps in the “Two of Us. One Space” vision:
- R12: stand-alone saved contacts, collapsible contact cards, link-to-origin only where applicable, editing/history, readable Daily Check-Ins, Letters fixes, expanded editable gesture pools.
- R13: shared movies, TV, music, books and games; suggested/planned/enjoyed statuses; partner-specific reviews and conversations; notifications, To-day agenda.
- R14: Trips & Journeys; itinerary, flights/hotels/bookings, partner collaboration, archive, audit, currency-aware costs and travel countdowns.
- R15: private travel-document PDFs/photographs with authorized signed previews, archiving, per-trip file policies, native OS document picker.
- R16: Android Share to OneSpace and manual Save Link; file into a new/existing Coordinate Activity or Shared Experience; optional comparison option (price, specs, currency). Occasions & Surprises with preparation and saving lead times, private gift planning and steps. Daily selfie and photo memories with grid, featured, Polaroid and overlap layouts.

##### Contract that must not be broken
- Keep both authentication accounts, their existing pairing, the single shared space and every existing record; do not reset test data.
- Allow only non-destructive schema migrations where existing rows are carried forward.
- Privacy is per object: Only me must remain invisible to partner, search, Today, media buckets and notifications; shared items are collaborative.
- Keep one account per phone, with Android biometric/device credential sign-in and session controls; do not reintroduce account switching.
- No changes to package ID or stable preview certificate. Confirm upgrade installation without uninstalling.
- No silent data submission: Android shared text stays local until the user chooses a destination and saves.
- Never publish a public production release from this development branch.

##### Essential on-phone acceptance (both devices)
1. Install new signed APK over 0.1.395 **without uninstalling**. Confirm existing logins, PIN/biometrics, pair, and existing Activities survive.
2. Launch both devices and verify Today loads within reasonable time, without a black/red error widget, and navigates correctly across all five tabs.
3. Open saved contact cards, edit, collapse, restore; for Activity-origin cards, only the From link should navigate to Activity.
4. Record personal feeling and request/respond to a partner check-in; verify privacy, log readability, and in-app notifications.
5. Add/edit a gesture message; verify manual pool, thoughtful ritual pool, not just the visible first three presets.
6. Read sent and received letters, reply, and verify read receipts.
7. Suggest/plan/enjoy Shared Experiences; add separate ratings from the two accounts, comments, and verify partner notification routing.
8. Build a shared trip and a private trip. Verify private trip invisible on partner phone and in search; shared itinerary syncs without time shift.
9. Attach PDF and image to shared trip; signed preview works on both phones. For private trip, partner may not list/view even with known file path.
10. Archive/restore travel files and itinerary items; actual bytes and older data remain.
11. Android: from Chrome/Amazon/Target-like browser Share menu choose OneSpace. Confirm it proposes saving a link, does not auto-publish, and supports a comparison option with price/specs.
12. Attach an incoming link to an existing Activity; inspect components, activity conversation, reactions and comparison.
13. Create a private Activity from an incoming link; verify the partner cannot find it. Explicitly share a different Activity and verify the partner sees it.
14. Verify manual Save a link or idea option works even without Android Share; iOS native Share extension remains a separate task.
15. Create private holiday/birthday plan, lead time 30/60 days, budget and preparation steps. Partner must not see the plan, steps, notifications, or upcoming schedule.
16. Create shared occasion; partner receives in-app notice. Due occasion, saving start, prep start and individual steps appear in Coming Up Together within next seven days.
17. Add daily selfie photo, then several images to collage memory; switch photo layout, preview, caption, archive/restore on both devices.
18. Share a private memory by existing explicit view-only grant; recipient sees only authorized photos, cannot alter the content. Revocation closes access.
19. Check Global Search for Activity, trip, travel document, experience, and occasion; verify correct deep links and RLS visibility.
20. Exercise offline/slow network/retry, layout on small Android screen, screen reader, landscape, Arabic/English settings, and notification tap.
21. Inspect privacy policies with two independent accounts, including direct URL attempts on private signed media.
22. Verify no changes to existing account rows, membership rows or pre-existing content counts after installation.

##### Build gate — ONE consolidated APK
A. Before touching the release marker, run Flutter analyze, all Flutter tests, Android native compile and app integration tests.
B. Resolve analyzer/test errors **in source only**; no repeated release builds. One validation job when quota is available and specifically authorized.
C. Then update `release/READY_TO_BUILD.md` exactly once for R12–R16 and create one optimized signed Android preview build. Verify stable package ID and signing certificate.
D. The APK is retained in GitHub Actions Artifacts. GitHub Releases publishing is attempted once; an HTTP 403 must not trigger three retries or discard the signed binary.
E. Verify the actual APK download, install it over the older preview on both Android devices, and record acceptance defects before publicly advertising this version.

##### What remains outside this bundle
- iOS native Share extension and full Xcode/device build testing (Android manual share intake works; iOS can manually paste a link).
- Actual OS push notification delivery and guaranteed background alarms; in-app notifications/agenda are distinct.
- Phone-calendar bidirectional syncing, Netflix/Spotify/Amazon account syncing, automatic flight status/PNR parsing.
- OS lock-screen emergency integration or emergency service dispatch, which require special platform/privacy review.
- Automatic currency conversion (estimates deliberately stay in original currencies).
- Permanent deletion policy for shared memories is intentionally unresolved, so all removals are reversible.
- User-entered content is not translated; UI internationalization is independently configurable.

##### Security / process
- DB migrations through 0069 (R16 photos) applied; no destructive cleanup.
- Storage buckets for travel documents and memory photos are private and restrict read with RLS.
- Source-level delimiter and import/path checks are **not the same as compilation**.
- There may be code/runtime integration defects until the one actual build and two-phone tests are executed.

### A7. OneSpace · `docs/COORDINATE_CHAT_VOICE_UX_R2.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Coordinate — Chat identities and voice-note UX

**Two of Us. One Space**

- Show **You** for the current user's messages. The avatar displays the initials of the user's own saved profile name. The other partner's messages show their saved full display name and initials. Unknown identities use safe fallbacks, not raw user IDs.
- Chat microphone tap is the user's explicit recording gesture, so recording starts as the voice panel opens. No extra "Record" tap. This works for both conversation voice comments and Activity/Option voice components.
- Compact controls: elapsed recording time, Stop, optional Preview, Send, and clear Cancel/Discard. Permission denial leads to a retry action; stopping when the app leaves the foreground remains mandatory. No recording is automatically sent.
- The recorder indicates reply or component context, preserving it in the posted contribution.
- Audio notes use a single-line player: Play/Pause, seek slider, remaining duration. No redundant component-type header or large empty card. Player fetches a private short-lived signed link on demand.
- Voice component title and caption can be edited without replacing, overwriting or republishing the existing audio file. Editing goes through the existing component revision history.
- Voice bubbles no longer interpret a playback tap as "Reply"; long-press exposes the Reply action. Text bubbles retain tap-to-reply. Copy-message applies only to text messages.
- All earlier Activity components, messages, edit histories, sharing and private storage continue to work.

Acceptance on physical phones: permission grant/deny, recording auto-start only after microphone tap, foreground interruption, stop/preview/send/cancel, storage cleanup, voice reply context, pause/seek, option-linked audio, two-partner playback, avatar initials, long names, RTL and narrow screens.

---

### A8. OneSpace · `docs/COORDINATE_COMPACT_ACTIVITY_R2.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Coordinate R2 — Compact Activity Interaction

OneSpace · Two of Us. One Space

##### Layout (implemented)
- App bar displays the Activity title with an edit pencil; no duplicate 36px heading.
- One metadata action strip: Priority, Status, Due date, Add component, History; all icon buttons with tooltips and accessible labels. A single subdued metadata summary remains visible.
- Activity details appear as readable body text without a large container. Tap body/edit pencil to revise; previous revisions are preserved.
- Components show the information first. Type is indicated through a 19px icon, not a text prefix. Notes and specifications are shown in full, not truncated into one subtitle line.
- The only permanent action on an item is a compact overflow menu. Tap opens a URL, opens Maps, toggles a Step, or opens contextual actions depending on item type. Long-press exposes discuss, like/dislike, edit, attach subcomponent, and history.
- Reactions display only when present, in text-size notation rather than full-height Like/Dislike controls.
- Compare appears when at least two Option components are present. Price, currency, specifications, source, and notes can be captured on each Option.
- Conversation shows message bubbles, author initials and original date/time. Tap a bubble to reply, long-press to copy/reply. Composer stays at bottom.
- Comments may attach to a component; reply threading is preserved.

##### Component behavior
- URL links: validate HTTP(S) URL and open default external browser.
- Locations: store a place/address (not live GPS), open Google Maps search externally. No location permission necessary.
- Options: named candidates with link, observed price/currency, specifications, notes and compare sheet.
- Prices: numeric observed amount + ISO currency; price is not automatically refreshed.
- Steps: completion toggle; existing history and author ID remain protected by database permissions.
- Dates/reminders: a date picker, with an explicit warning that push notifications are not yet active.
- Notes/specifications: free-form readable text with type-aware editor.
- Tags: short labels attached to Activity or Option.
- Mentions: existing data type remains compatible; actual notification dispatch is not available.

##### Voice notes — interaction and security specification (next implementation slice)
Voice is a first-class contribution that can be attached either to an Activity/Option as a component or sent in the Conversation as a comment. It must have the *same author, original timestamp, contextual reply association and RLS access control* as text contributions.
1. The composer mic opens a record interface: **Record → Stop → Preview → Send or Discard**. A mic in the Add-item sheet creates a standalone attached voice component.
2. Keep voice UI compact: play/pause button, duration, sender initials and timestamp. No autoplay and no large waveform-only card. Long press allows replying and viewing details.
3. Record only when the user taps Record and grants device microphone permission. No background capture.
4. Store the recording in a **private Supabase Storage bucket**, validate content type/size, and use authenticated, short-lived signed playback URLs; do not put public permanent audio links in database rows.
5. Preserve the file on a failed upload for user-controlled retry, without silently posting a comment or losing the draft. Explicitly clean up discarded local files and incomplete uploads.
6. No automatic transcription or third-party voice processing without clear user opt-in.
**Not shipped in R2**: do not display an inert microphone control. Voice needs a secured media implementation and Android device recording/playback tests before release.

##### Deferred
- Images/screenshots need the same private storage rules and view-only grants.
- Native Android share-in and searchable comparison data are follow-on milestones.
- A more advanced comparison should compare structured fields row-by-row and preserve per-partner choices.
- The next R2 refinement groups Options with their child prices/specs, Steps, and Notes & References separately so a component's context is visible at a glance.

##### Acceptance
- One-line toolbar doesn't wrap on 360dp phones.
- Activity title appears in app bar and not duplicated in body.
- Notes display multiline detail without truncation.
- Step tapping toggles status; links and map locations open their respective apps.
- Each reaction is independently recorded per partner; menu labels make the action discoverable.
- Replies preserve chronological order, timestamps and references.
- All existing Activity rows and old JSON component values still render.
- Two-account physical-device verification required before production release.

### A9. OneSpace · `docs/COORDINATE_CONTACT_INFORMATION.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Coordinate — Contact information component

**Two of Us. One Space**

The reusable **Contact Information** component belongs to either the whole Activity or one specific Option (for instance, gymnastics provider B). It can be added through the Activity add menu or an Option context menu.

##### Structured fields
- Card title (e.g., Gym B admissions)
- Person to contact (optional)
- Telephone number (optional), including international dialling codes
- Email address (optional)
- Physical address (optional)
- Working hours and time zone if known (free text; do not infer open-now status)
- Notes (optional)

The user must fill at least one contact detail. Phone and email inputs are validated; long fields are bounded to fit within the existing component payload limit. Unknown payload fields survive edits.

##### Actions
- **Phone** opens the device dialer; does not place a call.
- **Email** opens the local email composer; does not send mail.
- **Address** opens the existing external Google Maps search flow. It does not request GPS permission.
- Missing fields do not show fake actions.
- On devices without a compatible dialer/email client, the field can be copied.

##### Integration
- Contact cards render in readable form without redundant type headings, while retaining original creator and time.
- They can be discussed, edited, reacted to, and audited using the existing Activity component workflow.
- Option-attached contacts contribute common rows (person, phone, email, address, working hours, notes) to the side-by-side comparison.
- Existing RLS and append-only revision history continue to apply; no contact directory or account-level sharing has been created.
- Current email and phone controls are manual, not synced with Google Contacts or a CRM.

##### Acceptance tests
- An Activity can hold a standalone contact.
- Gym A and Gym B can hold different named contacts and working hours.
- Comparison aligns those fields and marks unknown values clearly.
- Phone/email/map interactions open their system handlers, without sending or calling automatically.
- Invalid contacts are rejected without destroying an existing saved component.
- Two-partner sync, narrow-screen layout and external-action availability remain device-level acceptance checks.

### A10. OneSpace · `docs/COORDINATE_DECISIONS_OUTCOMES.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Coordinate — Decision & Outcome log

##### Why this exists
Partners need a permanent record of why they selected a heater, registered for gymnastics, postponed a trip, or reopened an Activity. A status badge is not sufficient.

##### Event model
- `activity_decisions`: append-only, server-attributed event log. No direct insert, update or delete privileges for signed-in clients.
- `record_activity_decision(activity_id, action, component_id, reason)`: authenticated, validated, atomic command to add an outcome and update the Activity stage together.
- Actions: chosen, purchased, delivered, registered, postponed, reopened, cancelled.
- Chosen requires a matching nonarchived Option in the same Activity.
- Purchase, delivery and registration reuse the last recorded chosen candidate when none is supplied.
- Reads inherit parent Activity visibility via RLS.
- Option name is resolved in the UI from the same Activity's visible components. Comments, notes and original research do not disappear on completion.
- Status revision trigger is kept, so the original stage and subsequent changes remain in the audit history.

##### Current UI
- 'Choose this option' appears on the Option context menu; the Activity's Decision panel also offers a candidate picker.
- The latest outcome is visible with a reason, creator and local display of its server timestamp. Prior entries expand below.
- Everyday exploring/comparing statuses remain selectable from the compact toolbar. Decided, purchased, registered and completed actions use the new outcome log.

##### Security invariants
- One authenticated shared-space partner can record an outcome on a shared Activity.
- Only a private Activity's creator can record its outcome.
- An arbitrary option from another Activity is rejected.
- Anonymous users cannot call the command or read the table.
- Do not modify/delete historical outcome events, or infer a joint vote from a single partner's like/dislike.
- No changes to legacy task/list/note records or partner accounts.

##### Device acceptance cases
1. Partner A creates three options. A chooses option B with a reason.
2. Partner B sees chosen option, name and reason in the Decision panel.
3. B records a purchase, A records delivery; history shows all three, Activity ends completed.
4. Reopen and choose another option without losing prior decisions or discussion.
5. Attempt to select a component from a different Activity; database rejects it.
6. Private Activity is never writable by its nonowner partner.
7. Both users see the same outcome after reopening the Activity on their Android phones.

### A11. OneSpace · `docs/COORDINATE_PRIVATE_PHOTO_ATTACHMENTS.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Coordinate — Private photo / screenshot attachments

This feature is additive. Existing Activities, conversations and pairings remain intact.

- Choose **Add → Photo / screenshot** in any Activity, or attach a photo beneath a specific Option.
- Android invokes the system gallery picker. The user selects a specific image; there is no broad gallery scanning or device-location access.
- Validate the actual image signature for JPEG, PNG, WebP and enforce a maximum size of 6 MiB. Gallery selection may compress images down to 1600px / 82 quality where supported.
- Upload to a private Supabase bucket named `onespace-activity-media`. The path is `activity-uuid/user-uuid/file-uuid.ext` and is never a public URL.
- Upload and signed URL creation require an authenticated, RLS-visible Activity. Only the contributing user can upload to their own folder.
- Existing photo records use the `image` reusable Coordinate component. Selecting a photo creates an attributed component within the Activity; screenshots are not free-floating notes.
- The Activity viewer obtains a fresh short-lived signed URL (120 seconds). Tapping the thumbnail opens a larger view with zoom.
- Upload failure before component creation attempts cleanup. Storage DELETE policy only permits cleanup by the uploader if no component already references the asset; existing published photos cannot be silently erased.
- Component revisions preserve edits to photo captions. Media itself is not overwritten in place.
- No public bucket, no service role in the app, no background capture.

**Not yet verified on physical Android devices:** actual photo selection, HEIC conversion, file picker cancellation, mid-upload network loss, multi-account photo viewing, and offline behavior. Those tests must run before treating this as production-ready.

**Next phase:** Secure audio voice messages will use the same parent-specific permissions and private-media design, with microphone consent, record/preview/discard, and authenticated playback.

### A12. OneSpace · `docs/COORDINATE_VOICE_NOTES.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Coordinate — Private voice notes, R1

**Two of Us. One Space**

##### User experience
- Inside an Activity use Add → **Voice note** to attach audio, including beneath an Option.
- In the Activity discussion, use the microphone beside the message composer to create a **voice comment** (including a reply to an earlier comment or a discussion tied to a component).
- Recording never starts automatically. Tap **Record**, grant explicit microphone permission when requested, then **Stop**.
- The draft can be **previewed**, replayed, **sent**, or explicitly **discarded**.
- Voice is capped at 120 seconds (2 minutes) and 5 MiB, mono AAC in an M4A file.
- On upload failure the sheet keeps the local recording for Retry or Discard. No silent posting; do not record in background.
- On success, the component/comment retains the original author, timestamp, parent reference and activity association.
- Compact **play/pause** button and duration appear within the relevant component or message. Playback uses a short-lived (120-second) private signed URL.

##### Storage/security
- New bucket `onespace-activity-voice` is private. Allowed content type is audio/mp4 (plus audio/x-m4a compatibility).
- File path: `activityUUID/userUUID/randomUUID.m4a`; only authenticated contributors can upload to their own folder when authorized for the Activity.
- Audio playback reads require the same Activity visibility rules enforced by RLS; no public bucket or permanent public URL.
- Unreferenced failed uploads may be cleaned up by their own uploader. Published audio referenced by a component or comment cannot be deleted by that cleanup policy.
- `activity_comments.voice_path` and `voice_seconds` are nullable, additive fields. Existing text comments are unchanged. Voice comments retain a non-empty descriptive `body` for compatibility.
- Recording files are temporary and cleared on explicit Discard or successful Send; no automatic transcription.

##### Acceptance tests before production
- Android permission grant/deny and permanent-deny handling; recording start/stop and background interruption.
- Preview, retry on network interruption, and successful Send in both component and comment contexts.
- Cross-account playback from the authorized partner and denial for unrelated accounts.
- Correct reply/option association, replay after signed URL expiry, audio focus with calls, restart/rotation handling.
- Flutter analyzer/tests, Android APK build and preview signing checks must pass.

### A13. OneSpace · `docs/COORDINATE_WORKSPACE_OVERVIEW.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Coordinate — Workspace overview and discovery

##### Sections
- **Active:** everything currently being explored, compared, purchased, etc. Does not include cancelled, completed, or archived Activities.
- **Attention:** active, unarchived Activities that are marked high/urgent priority or have a due date within seven calendar days, including overdue.
- **Finished:** completed or cancelled Activities. Completed research remains accessible, without erasing options or conversation.
- **All:** includes archived records for historic access.
- A case-insensitive search matches the Activity title or body without making a new database query on every keystroke.
- The main list remains live via Supabase Realtime and respects existing Activity RLS.

##### Creation
- Minimal title/body with optional private toggle. Priority, due date, and other components can be added later.
- A connected couple sees no legacy project-management requirement to assign a Task or choose a deadline.

##### Outcomes
- Registration is a terminal successful outcome, so it now sets the Activity stage to `completed`. The Decision event remains `registered` for display and historical clarity.
- Purchase remains in progress until delivery; delivery completes the Activity.
- Reopening creates a new Decision event without erasing earlier history.

##### Acceptance
- Create three Activities, complete one, archive one, mark one as urgent; counts and filters must agree.
- An overdue Activity appears in Attention. An Activity due more than seven days later does not, unless high/urgent.
- All lists show only Activities returned by RLS.
- A registered gymnastics enrollment goes to Finished and stays fully searchable.
- Toggling between views and searching must not create extra round-trips or duplicate realtime subscriptions.

### A14. OneSpace · `docs/PHONE_TIME_CITY_WEATHER.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — Phone-driven Time, Approximate City & Weather

**Two of Us. One Space**

##### What changed
- Time zones are read from each Android/iOS device using the OS-native IANA identifier; there is no manual time-zone selector or location choice.
- Local time comes from the device; partner time is derived from the time zone their phone last shared. Daylight saving follows IANA rules. The clocks refresh each minute, and settings synchronize on opening/resuming OneSpace.
- Each partner may **individually opt in** under Profile → **Share my city and weather**. The app displays a clear explanation, then uses the OS foreground location permission dialog.
- Only coarse location is requested (ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION on Android); no precise (ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION), background location or tracking service permissions.
- On user consent, the app uses a **single one-shot location fix**, resolves it to city/region/country on the device, and requests nearby weather from Open-Meteo using rounded latitude/longitude (one decimal degree, approximately 10 km). Exact GPS coordinates or addresses are never stored in Supabase.
- Each profile stores only opt-in state, approximate city, temperature, condition code, last-updated time and native time-zone identifier. The paired partner sees these values inside Today; weather is marked potentially stale after two hours.
- Weather refresh is limited to once per 45 minutes while OneSpace is open. Failed requests never block Today.
- When location permission is revoked and the app next checks, the app clears previously shared city/weather. Turning sharing off in Profile clears it immediately upon successful server response.
- Missing location permission, unavailable system geocoder, or network failure never fabricate a city or weather. Opting out does not affect the clock.

##### Privacy and provider
- **No continuous tracking; no live GPS pin or exact address.** Each partner explicitly enables sharing; the other partner cannot enable it.
- The database profiles RLS restricts updates to the account owner and reads to the paired partner.
- The Today RPC returns city/weather values **only if opt-in is enabled**.
- Open-Meteo receives rounded coordinate values for weather requests. Attribution: **Weather data by Open-Meteo.com**.
- Open-Meteo's free service is for noncommercial usage with attribution. Before OneSpace is offered commercially, evaluate licensing or a paid weather provider.
- The phone may itself use third-party geocoding services at the operating-system layer.

##### Technical
- Flutter packages: flutter_timezone, geolocator, geocoding, http.
- No extra network call on the critical first Today snapshot. Optional phone/profile/weather refresh is asynchronous, throttled, and only requested after successful auth.
- Profile no longer allows manual zone selection; auto-detection is the default.
- Shared city data is not suitable for emergency location tracking.
- Physical-device tests remain necessary: permissions granted/denied/revoked, coarse city result, multiple devices, system clock zone changes, phone resume/background, cross-account RLS, daylight-saving behavior, denied network/offline, and small-screen display.
