### A15. OneSpace · `docs/POST_R17_QUALITY_HARDENING.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — Post-R17 source-only quality hardening

**Not in the R12–R17 APK. No release/build trigger. No migration or data reset.**

##### Separation of versions
The R12–R17 APK build was initiated from commit `b946024327d59331671980f3abe644df91dc1e5f` (run 43451873921). All changes documented here were committed afterward. Do not assume they are in the APK the two testers are installing.

##### Improvements now committed
- **Global Search reliability and performance.** Eleven RLS-protected search sections are independently guarded, so one source error no longer hides all other results. Restrict query length to 100, search at most 12 rows per section, debounce typing for 500 ms, and stop stale network responses from overwriting a newer query. Escape literal ILIKE wildcard characters. Exclude archived objects in applicable sections. Keep results scoped to the current authenticated account and avoid navigating without an ID.
- **Stable notifications.** Reuse one realtime stream per active account in the bell and notification inbox rather than recreating subscriptions on every rebuild. Distinguish travel, occasion and experience notifications with contextual icons. On compact layouts, the unread filter and Mark all read action wrap without crushing text.
- **Two-phone occasions.** Live updates for occasion plans and preparation steps, debounced refresh, revision history, stale-response rejection and proper subscription cleanup. Preserve private-plan RLS and existing audit history.
- **Trip navigation and account safety.** Reject outdated list/detail responses when account or space changes; dispose of old Realtime channels; open direct trip notifications even when their target is not among the first 200 list rows. The backend remains authoritative for authorization.
- **Resilient detail pages.** A temporarily unavailable conversation or revision-history lookup does not blank out an otherwise accessible trip or occasion. Keep previously loaded sections when one refresh fails; show a retry warning. Trip history fetches only fields that the collapsed history UI actually uses, not large before/after JSON snapshots.
- **Date accuracy.** Occasion saving/preparation lead times use calendar-day field arithmetic, rather than subtracting a fixed number of 24-hour durations (DST protection). Cross-year and leap-day rules are retained.
- **Shared-space cache integrity.** An in-flight older membership request cannot overwrite a newer seeded account/space snapshot or the result of a refreshed membership lookup.
- **Responsive form design.** Two-field groups in occasion setup, budget/step editing and itinerary expenses stack on narrow screens or when text scaling requires more width. Trip and occasion action headers wrap. Global Search gains a sensible maximum reading width, clear-search affordance, keyboard dismissal, and empty-state guidance.

##### Focused QA to perform with the NEXT APK, not the current R17 build
1. Type rapidly into Search, replace the search term and clear it. Results must never show the previous term. Search literal % and _ characters without returning unbounded matches.
2. Disconnect/reconnect internet while searching: show a useful warning, not a blank/error screen. Verify results never include a partner's private objects.
3. Open notifications repeatedly and switch between unread/all. Confirm no duplicate notifications, excessive spinners or subscription storm.
4. Create a shared occasion on Phone A and edit/complete a preparation step on Phone B. Verify both devices converge without losing previously loaded history.
5. Create a private surprise plan on A: B must not see it or its steps in list, Search, Calendar, Today or notification routes.
6. Edit and archive a shared trip on B while A displays its itinerary. Confirm refreshing, deep links, and archived/recoverable entries still work.
7. With slow or intermittent network, verify a failure in the optional history section does not prevent viewing the trip or occasion details already loaded.
8. Check birthday/anniversary preparation dates crossing an expected DST boundary in Pennsylvania, as well as dates spanning a year boundary. Dates must not shift by a calendar day.
9. Rotate narrow Android devices and increase font scaling to 150–200%. Budget/currency, prep/saving, notifications and Search must remain readable without horizontal overflow.
10. Sign out and back into one authorized account (without uninstalling/clearing data). Old account/space content should not flash during refresh.
11. Verify both old accounts, memberships, photos, letters, trips, activities and other records are still present before and after testing. No destructive cleanup is permitted.

##### Verification and release policy
- All modified Dart files passed lexical delimiter and relative-import existence checks in this source-only iteration.
- Regression-test source was added for literal search queries, DST-safe lead times and responsive form layout; **these tests have NOT been executed** for the post-R17 changes.
- Flutter analyze, Flutter test, Android compilation, signing and two-phone acceptance remain future quality gates for this next candidate.
- **Do not** update `release/READY_TO_BUILD.md`, invoke CI or publish another APK until test feedback from the existing R12–R17 APK is reviewed and the user requests the next release.
- No database migrations, auth resets, saved-data cleanup or deletion were performed for this hardening batch.

### A16. OneSpace · `docs/R10_DAILY_LIFE_UX_CHECKINS_DAYS_OFF_QA.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace R10 — partner-reviewed UX and shared-life business logic

##### Scope implemented
- AppBar: compact OneSpace heading, notifications bell, second-from-right
  overflow (Settings, Search, Language), and separate Profile last.
- Relationship Settings: Together / Living apart segmented choice; independent
  Last Met and Next Meeting date pickers with explicit clear; changes saved
  for both partners. Dashboard counts no longer depend on clock/weather.
- Today: Living together shows a warm placeholder. Meeting date counters
  remain independently visible. Weather/clocks stay hidden when together.
  All three Today accordions share spacing and show collapsed hints.
- Recent Discussions: latest message per Activity, with a count of recent
  comments rather than repeated Activity cards.
- Coming Up Together: a new invoker-only RLS-filtered 7-day source aggregator
  includes Calendar events, open To-dos, active Activity due dates, dated
  Activity components, and yearly Calendar occurrences.
  It does not duplicate stored events. A separate optional read avoids
  blocking the first dashboard paint.
- Saved people and contacts moved from World into Remember.
- Daily check-ins: record your own mood 1–5, thoughts, gratitude and
  private/shared choice; private journals do not notify the partner.
  Separate partner check-in request and response, with rating, thoughts,
  something to discuss, sent/responded timestamps and two-person-only RLS.
  New shared journals/requests/replies create in-app notifications and open
  the Check-ins screen when tapped.
- Love gestures: new themed preset categories—thank you, lucky to have you,
  miss us together, miss home, miss kids, miss furry ones. Settings opens a
  management-only gesture screen rather than sending screen, with creator-
  owned editing and removal; thoughtful ritual controls appear in the same
  area. Removed duplicated automatic-consent settings link.
- Days off: each partner can add and maintain personal day-off dates,
  optional bedtime for the night before and later wake time for the day off.
  Shared Calendar shows them in Month, Week and Day views with owner labels.
  Consented automatic gesture delivery respects the adjusted sleep window
  and skips after-work rituals on a configured day off.
- Language is accessible from the global overflow, not weather settings.

##### Needs Attention proposal — awaiting product decision
Classify *action required*, not just high priority:
1. Overdue unfinished work, reminders and dated Activity steps.
2. Actionable items due today/tomorrow.
3. Direct mentions, partner check-in requests, assignee-specific items.
4. Explicit urgent items with no date.
5. High-priority tasks only near a configured preparation threshold.
Collapse multiple references to one Activity, separate informational
Calendar occasions, and respect private visibility, user snooze, completion,
cancellation, archiving and quiet hours. Never automatically infer urgency
from a personal emotional check-in.

##### Boundaries
- Check-in alerts are in-app records; OS push/remote delivery in background
  is not yet implemented.
- One personal daily-checkin entry per date remains the existing rule.
  Partner check-in requests are separately stored, each answerable once.
- Check-in request/response history is not exposed outside its participants.
- Days-off exceptions adjust *consented scheduled gestures*. They are not
  yet a universal OS-level quiet-hours controller for all future push.
- New source aggregation and UI require real two-device validation before
  declaring complete. Do not hard-delete shared history.

##### Two-phone acceptance tests
1. Verify header layout and overflow menu on narrow Android screens.
2. Toggle Together/Apart and confirm both counters survive on Today.
3. Clear either relationship date, save and reload.
4. Expand/collapse all Today sections: uniform alignment and hints.
5. Post 3 comments to one Activity: one Recent Discussions entry, count 3.
6. Create Calendar event, appointment, To-do deadline, Activity due date and
   dated Activity component within 7 days; confirm correct source labels.
7. Enter a private feeling: recipient sees no entry or notification.
8. Share a feeling: partner receives in-app notification and can read it.
9. Send check-in request: recipient opens it, rates 1–5, adds thoughts and
   discussion topic, replies; sender receives response notification.
10. Change one user’s day off; verify both calendars, as well as per-account
    overrides and skip of after-work ritual on that date.
11. Edit/remove personal gesture template, verify the sender cannot modify
    the other partner's templates, and send each new built-in gesture.
12. Repeat voice message, Calendar/To-do/Notes navigation, Android system
    back and two-back exit, privacy and performance tests.

### A17. OneSpace · `docs/R11_ACTIONABLE_ATTENTION_QA.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R11 — Personal Needs Attention & one release per iteration

##### Implemented

- Changed .github/workflows/android-dev.yml to dispatch manually or only
  on the explicit release marker update, no auto-build on regular pushes.
  Removed CI's self-generated pubspec.lock commit/push. A failing release
  won't be cancelled merely by a newer commit.
- New invoker-only RPC onespace_my_attention(date) combining:
  * Active Activity deadlines, including overdue, today, tomorrow;
  * Open Activity-component reminders with the same near-term threshold;
  * Open To-dos assigned to the viewer or unassigned/shared;
  * Pending partner check-in requests addressed to the viewer;
  * Unread Activity mentions and replies addressed to the viewer;
  * Explicit undated urgent items and near-term high-priority dated work.
- Discard informational Calendar events; they stay in Coming Up Together.
  Avoid surfacing all high-priority work indefinitely.
- One row per Activity regardless of the number of related reminders/mentions,
  choosing the highest-ranked actionable reason.
- Exclude completed/cancelled/archived items, completed reminders, snoozed
  objects, and partner-assigned To-dos. Existing Activity reminder snoozes
  remain respected. Source-table RLS remains the authority.
- New user_attention_snoozes supports per-user snoozes for Activities, To-dos
  and pending check-in requests. Snoozing does not modify or hide shared data
  on the other partner's phone.
- Action rows navigate to their original Activity, To-dos, or Check-ins.
  Opening an Activity through Needs Attention marks that viewer's unread
  mentions/replies for that Activity as read.
- "Show all actions" actually expands the rest of the list rather than
  opening only the seventh item. Optional attention RPC doesn't block
  Today first paint; reports retry if it fails.
- Notification check-in requests now deep-link to the particular pending
  request's reply form; already-answered requests remain in history.

##### Priority design

0 = overdue unfinished obligation
1 = deadline today
2 = direct partner request / unread Activity mention or reply
3 = tomorrow's deadline
4 = urgent work with no date
5 = high-priority work dated within the next three calendar days

A nearby event on the Calendar alone is not an action. The server returns at
most 20 top-ranked unique objects. A user's local calendar date is sent to
RPC as YYYY-MM-DD to avoid UTC date shifts. Do not infer urgency from mood
entries or a partner's personal journal. Any user-specific snooze is local
to that person's account.

##### R11 two-Android-phone acceptance

1. Create an overdue Activity, a due-today To-do, tomorrow's reminder, an
   urgent undated Activity, and a high-priority item two days out. Verify
   order and reasons.
2. Create a Calendar-only appointment in five days; ensure it appears in
   Coming Up Together but not Needs Attention.
3. Create two reminder components and multiple mentions in one Activity.
   Verify only one Needs Attention card appears for that Activity.
4. Assign a To-do to partner A; ensure B doesn't get a personal action for
   that assignment. Ensure each partner's private records remain private.
5. Snooze an Activity on A for three days. Confirm it disappears only from
   A's Needs Attention, not B's list or Coordinate.
6. Mark a task completed or Activity cancelled. It must stop appearing.
7. Open an Activity via Needs Attention. Confirm the viewer's unread
   Activity mentions/replies are acknowledged and the list refreshes.
8. Partner A sends B a check-in request. B sees it in Needs Attention and
   can open the exact response form from the notification. Once answered,
   B's pending action disappears.
9. Expand the first six action rows with Show all; verify no actions become
   inaccessible.
10. Repeat performance, Android keyboard, app-back navigation, sleep/day-off
    timing and both-account RLS tests.

##### Not included yet

- System-level Android background push notifications (existing notices are
  in-app, under their current permissions).
- Cross-device remote notification delivery while OneSpace is terminated.
- User-configurable thresholds and customized priority coefficients.
- A partner's private personal emotional journal as an alert source.

### A18. OneSpace · `docs/R12_CONTACTS_LOVE_GESTURES_QA.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace R12 — Contacts, gesture pools, love, letters and check-ins

##### Built
- Contacts in Remember may be standalone or derived from Activity components;
  standalone contacts do not create an Activity in the background.
- Same full-page contact editor for both sources: name, person, phone, email,
  address, working hours, Notes; direct contacts have Only Me switch.
- Contact cards collapse/expand by tapping the card heading. Edit pencil
  opens the contact editor. Origin activity opens only from clickable From.
  Standalone cards show non-clickable Created by.
- Top header History rather than duplicate Add Person shortcut.
- Soft removal, Recently Deleted filter and Restore for both sources.
  Standalone contact archive records 30-day purge-eligibility timestamp
  and immutable edit/remove/restore revisions. **No automatic irreversible
  purge is enabled** until deletion consent is decided. Existing Activity
  component audit remains in effect.
- Check-in journal and request/response logs have expandable cards,
  distinct date, mood, privacy, thoughts, gratitude, topic and response data.
- Gestures: sending screen contains only gestures to send. Custom creation
  and editing live in Settings. Settings groups 13 gesture types into
  expandable message pools, counts active messages, permits edit/hide of
  built-in messages and edit/delete of personal variants.
  Sender randomizes over active built-in variants plus custom messages of
  the same type; avoids immediately repeating the last sent text.
  Morning, support/after-work and goodnight each include their original ten
  backend ritual phrases alongside three UI presets (13 built-in choices).
  Edited/hidden presets affect both manual sending and scheduled delivery;
  explicitly scheduled custom messages of the same type join the ritual pool.
- You Got This and Miss Us Together use recognizable 💪 and 👩‍❤️‍💋‍👨
  artwork in matching colored circles. Gesture notifications show the
  same type-specific icon; received gestures retain their dialog.
- Thoughtful Little Rituals is separate in Settings (morning, after-work,
  bedtime), not mixed into gesture message administration.
- One A Little Love entry appears in Today.
- Days Off editor no longer asks for individual bedtime/wake overrides.
  Existing overall sleep schedule and workday-off behavior remain.
- Letters: sent/received detail fetches a fresh authenticated record by
  ID; read receipt and reply lookup failures cannot block reading the main
  letter, and detail has explicit retry feedback.

##### Two-phone acceptance
1. Create a standalone shared Contact. Confirm that no new Activity exists;
   edit from the pencil and inspect Created by.
2. Create an Activity Contact component; edit from Contacts and navigate back
   to the original Activity only via From.
3. Add a private contact and verify partner cannot see it.
4. Expand/collapse cards, search Notes, soft-remove and restore a direct and
   Activity-derived card; inspect revision audit.
5. Create a private and a shared check-in, send partner request, respond.
   Inspect expanded logs and privacy.
6. In gesture Settings, expand every type and verify the active count,
   including 13 initial presets for morning, support/after-work and goodnight.
   edit/disable one default and add one custom in the same type. Send many
   times; confirm randomization includes both sources, never disabled text.
7. Confirm custom editing never appears in A Little Love sending view;
   open the Today shortcut.
8. Confirm thoughtful ritual settings appear separately and workday-off
   editor has no custom sleep time controls.
9. Send and receive Letters on two phones; open each from Sent and Received;
   confirm read receipt does not prevent opening and that replies can open.
10. Confirm gesture icons in in-app notification list and popup.
11. Android back navigation, keyboard fit, loading performance, narrow screen
    text sizes, and no new private-data leakage.
12. Verify signing and package ID of final APK. No public launch until
    both phones pass acceptance.

##### Caveats
- No phone push transport is added here; notifications remain in-app.
- Automatic gesture delivery has its established server-side ritual source;
  per-user edits to 13 preset messages are honored by automatic rituals
  as well as manual sending. Only explicitly scheduled custom messages can
  be automatically delivered; unscheduled custom messages remain manual-only.
- 30-day eligible-for-purge metadata does **not** imply the system silently
  hard-deletes shared contacts. The irreversible deletion policy is pending.

### A19. OneSpace · `docs/R14_TRIPS_JOURNEYS_QA.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R14 — World: Trips & Journeys

##### Built, committed and deployed (no APK or GitHub Actions used)
- World now has a dedicated Trips & Journeys destination instead of only a placeholder.
- Create shared or Only Me journeys, with name, origin, destination, departure and return dates, notes, progress status, archive and restore.
- Trip privacy is chosen at creation and deliberately cannot change afterward, preventing accidental exposure of booking details. No existing account or previous content was migrated or removed.
- Add editable itinerary entries: flights, accommodation, transport, activities, reservations and notes.
- Store departure/arrival dates and local clock times exactly as written on travel bookings. **The app does not convert flight times automatically**, because itinerary timestamps can refer to different localities.
- Optional provider, confirmation reference (copyable), external booking link, notes, estimated cost and three-letter currency.
- Mark individual stops complete and soft-remove/restore; travel history remains intact.
- Aggregate estimated costs **separately by currency**, with no fictitious FX conversion.
- Shared trip conversations with personal message retraction and edit-history audit for trips and itinerary rows.
- A new trip and a new trip message notify the partner **only when the trip is shared**; those notifications open the trip.
- Today → Coming Up Together includes trip departures and dated, incomplete itinerary entries in its seven-day RLS-filtered agenda. Tapping either opens the original trip.
- Global search includes trip names, itinerary entries and Shared Experiences.
- RLS protects shared and private trips and all dependent tables. Real-time subscription plus debounced reload refreshes trip summaries, detailed itinerary and conversations between devices.

##### Data preservation contract
- **Never** delete or recreate the two test accounts, the shared space, or any existing activities, notes, contacts, calendar items, letters, experiences or check-ins.
- Migrations 0063 and 0064 are additive except extending notification check constraints to accept new types. No destructive data modification was used.
- Future schema changes requiring normalization must retain existing records and make a targeted transformation with a verification query.
- No irreversible purge, account reset, or automatic cleanup.

##### Two-phone acceptance
1. Sign into one account on each phone; check that pairing and older content remain available.
2. Create a shared journey on phone A; confirm it appears on B after synchronization.
3. Create a private journey on A; confirm it is **not visible on B**, including in Today and global search.
4. Edit a shared trip title, dates and status from phone B; confirm the result on A and inspect the audit.
5. Add a flight with local departure and arrival dates/times, route, provider, booking ref and source link. Confirm local time does not shift across the two phones or daylight-saving changes.
6. Add hotel, transport, activity, reservation and note entries; edit, mark completed, remove, and restore. Verify old data remains in revision history.
7. Add USD and SAR costs to different items; confirm totals remain separately labelled rather than summed across currencies.
8. Add a trip conversation message on A; verify notification on B deep-links to the original trip; retract own message and check partner cannot retract it.
9. Create a trip and an unfinished dated itinerary entry within seven days; verify Coming Up Together shows each and opens its trip. Entries outside seven days should not appear.
10. Use global search for trip name, itinerary title and Shared Experiences; verify only authorized results appear.
11. Test narrow Android layouts, back navigation, slow network, unexpected offline/retry behavior, RTL and per-partner language preferences.
12. Test edit/archive/restore on both phones and ensure no previous unrelated data is altered.

##### Known limitations before public launch
- Flight itineraries and booking references are structured text and links; there is **no file storage or boarding-pass upload** in R14.
- No calendar write-back, external flight status API, Google Travel import, email sync or OS push notification is claimed.
- This source has **not been Flutter-analyzed, APK-built or physically phone-tested** since the GitHub Actions quota was exhausted. The static source review does not replace those gates.
- Do not publish or install a new release until Flutter analyzer and tests, signing/package checks, and real two-phone acceptance succeed. No R14 release marker was changed.

### A20. OneSpace · `docs/R15_TRAVEL_DOCUMENTS_QA.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R15 — Private Travel Documents & Attachments

##### Product scope (committed to source, migrations deployed)
- Attach PDF boarding passes, tickets, itineraries, receipts and scanned document images to any trip; optionally associate each file with a specific itinerary item.
- Android system document picker (`ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT`) and iOS system document picker (`UIDocumentPickerViewController`) use an existing Flutter platform channel. No new Flutter package or lockfile change.
- 10 MB maximum per file; only PDF, JPEG, PNG and WebP; validate file signatures on the Flutter side, not filename extension alone.
- Private Supabase Storage bucket `onespace-trip-documents` and metadata `trip_attachments`, with RLS matching the parent trip visibility.
- Paths are trip UUID / uploader UUID / random document UUID + validated extension. No public bucket, predictable filename paths or permanent public URLs.
- Files are opened through user-authenticated, 90-second signed URLs. Photos preview in a zoomable dialog; PDFs open through the device's external document viewer/browser.
- Add, relabel, categorize, associate to an itinerary item, archive and restore. **No irreversible file deletion** on normal user actions; failed metadata inserts may roll back only the requesting uploader's unreferenced file.
- Shared-trip new document notices route directly to the corresponding trip; no notification for private trips. Realtime metadata stream keeps document lists synchronized across devices.
- Global search includes document titles and routes the result to its authorized trip.

##### Data-preservation contract
- Existing auth accounts, household pairing, activities, check-ins, documents, memories and previous trips are untouched.
- Database migrations 0065/0066 only add a bucket, table, helper function, policies, notification category and realtime publication.
- Existing R14 trip data remains valid; there is no forced backfill.
- Soft archival preserves metadata and the original binary for restoration.

##### Security acceptance tests
1. Account A uploads a boarding-pass PDF under a shared trip; account B can see metadata and open the document using a short-lived signed link.
2. Account A attaches an image scan under a private trip; account B must not see the trip, document title, image, or storage bytes, including through Global Search.
3. Account B tries to use a known `object_path` for account A's private trip; signed URL generation must be denied.
4. Owner attempts an out-of-trip or forged `object_path` INSERT: database/storage policies must reject it.
5. Partner cannot edit `file_name`, `object_path`, `trip_id`, `created_by` or `mime_type` via API.
6. Archive and restore on either phone changes visibility; bytes are retained and files become viewable again after restore.
7. The owner cannot delete a registered document directly in Storage; only an orphaned own upload is removable for rollback.
8. A PDF larger than 10 MB, unsupported MIME/signature, empty file or interrupted upload produces an actionable error, with no partial document metadata.
9. A document linked to an itinerary item appears with the correct label; a removed item can still be recovered using the trip history.
10. Both devices receive a shared-document notification when appropriate; private documents never notify the other user.
11. Test Android device PIN/biometric flows and both device profiles to ensure no signed file URL is stored in permanent preferences.
12. Test iOS UIScene document picker and PDF viewing independently: native platform code is committed, but a macOS/Xcode compile and actual iOS device test are **still required**.

##### Engineering status and limitations
- SQL migrations have been applied successfully and storage bucket verified private; RLS enabled.
- Static delimiter/source integrity checks performed for touched Dart, Swift and Kotlin files.
- Dart unit tests for file signatures, size limits and path restrictions have been added but **not run**.
- Flutter analyzer, Kotlin compile, Swift compile, Android APK build and two-phone acceptance **not performed** in this iteration.
- GitHub Actions quota is exhausted. No workflow dispatch, no CI marker change, no Actions retry and **no APK build triggered** by R15.
- Before release: run Flutter analyze/test, Android signed preview, verify iOS via Xcode and manual file/document/privacy testing.
- This feature does not automatically parse flight PNRs or extract itinerary fields from uploaded PDFs; it stores and previews files.

### A21. OneSpace · `docs/R17_MODERN_DESIGN_AND_HOLIDAY_CALENDAR_QA.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R17 — Modern design, seasonal themes and calendar occasions

##### Implemented in source (not yet a compiled APK)
- Four independently selectable Material 3 color collections: Coastal Blue, Rose & Plum, Sage & Sand, Midnight Indigo.
- Light, dark or Follow Phone brightness, using the OS appearance setting. Preferences are local to each device, do not overwrite a partner's data, and do not require network or login.
- Consistent shape/touch-target language for AppBars, cards, chips, inputs, primary actions and navigation. Dark theme uses semantic foreground/background contrast.
- Wide screen (900+ logical pixels): NavigationRail and centered content with a maximum content width. Narrow phones: original five-tab bottom navigation.
- Responsive two/four-column theme selector and small-screen safe brightness controls.
- Subtle, optional seasonal accents: Valentine's (Feb 10–15), spring (Mar–May), Halloween (Oct 25–31), winter holidays (Dec 18–Jan 2). Manual preview and Off are available. No distracting automatic fullscreen animations.
- Seasonal banner on Today and Shared Calendar; animated changes honor phone reduced-motion settings.

##### Calendar integration
- Shared, opt-in holiday sources for US, Egypt, Saudi Arabia, stored in RLS-enabled `space_holiday_sources`. No automatic subscriptions at migration time.
- US and Egyptian **national** public holidays are pulled from Nager.Holidays community API v4 and cached in memory for seven days. Some state/subdivision holidays are intentionally omitted to avoid misleading national availability claims.
- Saudi Arabia uses **only** known fixed civic dates (Founding Day Feb 22; National Day Sep 23). These are date labels, not a claim about adjusted employer/agency days off. Lunar/Eid dates are not guessed; user can add the confirmed dates using a personal special occasion or calendar event.
- Holiday overlay does **not** create `space_events` or `occasion_plans` rows and cannot delete or change personal occasions.
- Personal occasion plans are RLS filtered from existing `occasion_plans`, displayed in month/week/day calendar with separate markers, label, annual recurrence and navigation to existing detail.
- Quick Birthday, Anniversary and Special day buttons prefill the selected date/type; the editor supports a named honoree, privacy (Only me / Both of us), optional budget, saving/preparation lead times, and preparation checklist. Existing plans remain valid (new honoree field defaults empty).
- Imported holidays do not appear as editable personal events. Provider/network failures leave original calendar usable and show a descriptive partial-data warning.

##### Design and usability acceptance
1. On both Android phones, test each theme in Light, Dark and Follow Phone. Toggle Android system dark mode with Phone selected and confirm colors follow it.
2. Theme on phone A must not change phone B. Relaunch app and confirm preference persists without touching either account.
3. Use 320, 360, 393, 600, 900 and 1200 logical-pixel widths where possible. No clipped buttons, text overflow, off-screen controls or horizontal-scrolling main content. At >=900 confirm side rail; on phones confirm five bottom tabs.
4. Use text scaling (up to 200%), Arabic RTL and Android screen reader. Check themed cards, labels, selected states, error surfaces, touch targets and screen reader order.
5. Turn on reduced motion. Animated theme transitions should be minimized or disabled by the platform if supported; avoid flashing/pulsing.
6. For each seasonal setting Off/Auto/manual, verify banner appears only as configured, does not obscure notifications or agenda, and remains legible in dark mode.
7. Opt into US + EG holidays from phone A, confirm phone B sees the same selected countries. Check a national holiday in month, week and day. Unselect one and confirm only its overlay disappears.
8. Add a Saudi civic source; verify February 22 and September 23 are displayed as civic dates without claiming Eid or observed employer holiday dates.
9. Force network failure: US/EG holiday load must not blank, block, or destroy calendar events. Retry later.
10. Create a shared birthday and anniversary with named honorees; verify on both phones including selected-day view and existing Today Coming Up logic.
11. Create a private special occasion/gift plan; it must remain absent from partner's calendar, notifications, search, and Today. No visible seasonal change may leak a private occasion to partner.
12. Create a Feb 29 annual birthday, check its behavior in a non-leap year; adjust product policy if partners prefer March 1 rather than Feb 28.
13. Check holiday source toggles with both accounts concurrently, duplicate inserts and offline retry without losing previously saved events.
14. Confirm there are still two test accounts, two memberships, one space, preexisting Activities, and that no existing date or theme setting was deleted.
15. Verify only one later release marker update/build is used for the entire R12–R17 bundle.

##### Explicit limitations
- This is not bidirectional Google Calendar, Apple Calendar or Outlook synchronization. Existing calendar UI integrates external regional holiday overlays **visually**.
- Regional-holiday data availability depends on the public provider; provider terms and production suitability require review before public launch. Source: https://nagerholidays.com/api
- US holidays are national only (not Pennsylvania-specific) and Saudi lunar holidays require official dates; no guesses, no guaranteed closure or days off.
- Automatic holiday color themes are decorative and locally timed, not linked to religious identity, nationality, or partner preference.
- No new physical-device screenshots or performance metrics have been collected.
- No Flutter analyzer, Flutter tests, native compile, real screenshots, keyboard-screenreader device QA, or APK builds were run during this source iteration.

##### Build gate
Combine R12–R17 in **one** Android release only after a compilation + test suite pass. Keep `release/READY_TO_BUILD.md` unchanged until build authorization. A GitHub Release publishing HTTP 403 must not consume retry attempts or discard a signed artifact.

### A22. OneSpace · `docs/R18_IMMERSIVE_DESIGN_OCCASION_PRODUCT_STRATEGY.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace R18 — Immersive Design & Connected Occasion Intelligence

**Status (Nov 13, 2026): R18 source and additive migration 0072 committed/applied. Verify-only workflow 43460884916 passed locked dependencies, Flutter analysis, and the full automated test suite. No new APK triggered. Physical-device contrast, usability and two-phone acceptance are still pending.**

##### 1. Product truth
OneSpace is **one relationship workspace**, not a collection of forms. Core journey:

`Discover / Receive inspiration → Compare → Discuss → Decide → Plan → Reserve budget → Purchase → Celebrate → Remember`

Each state should preserve continuity: what prompted the idea, what the two partners thought, the final decision, budget impact, receipts, lead-time preparation, and eventually the memory. Links are the system's connective tissue.

The product must feel intimate without assuming gender roles, imposing a type of relationship, automatically revealing private surprises, or inferring emotional states from sparse data.

##### 2. Original user-approved theme concepts (R18 source restored)
| Concept | Intended mood | Primary treatment | Surface |
|---|---|---|---|
| Midnight Blue | cinematic, premium, immersive | near-black navy, luminous azure and dimensional gradients | deep-navy panels |
| Monochrome | sophisticated, minimal, bold | high-contrast charcoal / ivory, disciplined accent use | graphite panels |
| Clean Light | modern, professional, effortless | cool whites, soft blue and accessible outlines | airy layered white |
| Soft Rose | warm, personal, gentle | rose, plum, soft blush, elegant typography | warm blush panels |

There are **four named design concepts** plus separate Light / Dark / Follow Phone controls; this avoids choosing six arbitrary themes just to maximize count. Existing local preferences are migrated non-destructively from prior palette names.

The theme selector shows a miniature OneSpace composition instead of single-color circles. Every selected concept must be recognizable at five levels: atmosphere, typography, navigation, surfaces, and contextual action affordances.

##### 3. Seasonal means a whole experience
App-wide tokens now style the Scaffold atmosphere, background pattern, AppBar, navigation bar/rail, cards, chip states, modal bottom sheets, dialogs, menus, snack bars, inputs, buttons and status accents. They use semantic color contrast, not merely a banner on Today.

Seasonal choices in source: Valentine's, spring, Halloween, winter, Eid, birthday, anniversary, Thanksgiving, Christmas, New Year, Automatic and Off.

- *Automatic fixed-date* overlays currently include Valentine's, spring, Halloween, U.S. Thanksgiving, Christmas and New Year. These are decorative windows, not legal holiday declarations.
- Eid appearances are manual until official lunar dates are available; no date is guessed. Birthday and anniversary appearances can be selected manually **or activated automatically** from the signed-in user's RLS-authorized Occasion records after app unlock. Context is cleared on sign-out and never synchronized to the other device.
- Seasonal vector motifs are subtle: petals, stars, moon shapes, snowflakes and hearts. Never overwhelm user text, especially for dark-mode, accessibility large text, or reduced-motion settings.
- Persist a theme locally per device; each partner can have a different experience.
- Official country holidays, personal occasions, workplace time off and decorative seasons are separate concepts and must not silently change each other.

###### Visual design debt to pay down next
1. Full phone screenshots across all five tabs and both auth states for each concept and holiday.
2. Responsive text-scale testing (1x–2x) in calendar, chat, planning, budgets, memories, documents and dialogs.
3. Contrast measurements of all semantic text/background combinations; accessible color must not depend on decorative gradients.
4. Rich but purposeful imagery: user-owned memory cover art, context-specific illustrations, reliable remote image preview, not random stock shots.
5. Consistent motion grammar: route transition, expand/collapse, action confirmation and sheet entrance, respecting reduced motion.
6. Story-driven hero cards in Memories, Connect, and upcoming milestones using actual available content only.

References:
- Flutter Material 3 component theming: https://docs.flutter.dev/release/breaking-changes/material-3-default
- Apple HIG semantic color: https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/color
- Material 3 expressive hierarchy: https://developer.android.com/design/ui/wear/guides/get-started/levels-expression
- Apple materials / hierarchy: https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/materials

##### 4. Actual Occasion budget lifecycle (R18 source implemented)
The R16 budget was a number and its steps were tasks. That was **not** budgeting.

R18 adds independent `occasion_budget_items`, with:
- wishlist idea, shortlisted option, reserved item, purchased item, skipped item;
- title, quantity, estimated unit price, actual unit price, currency, notes and source URL;
- linked Coordinate Activity and option IDs when imported;
- soft-removable / reversible status and transaction history;
- live aggregate summary (budget, reservations, actual spending, wishlist).

###### Money logic
`Committed = Reserved estimate × quantity + Purchased actual (or estimate if actual missing) × quantity`

`Available = Total occasion budget − Committed`

Wishlist and shortlisted ideas do not deduct money. Moving an item to Reserved deducts its estimate. Marking Purchased replaces the reservation with actual spending. **Overspending remains negative and visible.** Unknown prices generate an explicit warning. Other currencies never get invisibly converted or deducted into the budget.

The reference model is the difference between *planned, available, and overspent* emphasized by YNAB. References:
https://support.ynab.com/en_us/focused-views-a-guide-BksnNYqLh
https://support.ynab.com/en_us/overspending-in-ynab-a-guide-ryWoxEyi?mobile-help=true

###### Interactivity
- **Add wish** starts in Wishlist; it has no budget impact.
- **Reserve** moves a wish or shortlisted option to Planned, immediately affecting available budget.
- **Mark purchased** opens actual-price capture and changes status to Purchased.
- **Undo purchase** moves back to Planned without deleting history.
- **From Activity** imports an existing Coordinate comparison option.
- An **Activity option card** now has **Send to occasion plan**: select the private/shared destination, link source IDs, copy current data, reserve cost and write an automatic planning log entry.
- A private Activity cannot be linked into a shared occasion. RLS and a database trigger both enforce authorization. A linked source must be from the same space.
- Existing preparation steps remain a separate checklist, not silently transformed into purchases.

###### Contextual intelligence implemented
Simple local, explainable rule engine surfaces actions when:
- the occasion is close and nothing is reserved or purchased;
- the budget is overcommitted;
- some committed items have no price;
- costs span unconverted currencies;
- wishlist choices have not been reserved;
- the plan has no items or no budget.

Each insight leads to an action (open shopping, import from Activity, or edit plan). **No third-party AI inference or private-data model calls.**

##### 5. Product intelligence we SHOULD build next (R&D, not delivered)
**A. Surprise Studio / Gift Path**
Relationship-safe private planning with a wish inbox, recipient preference signals explicitly shared by that person, vendor links, gift arrival timeline, confirmation and “reveal” moment. Privacy is per item and per workspace.

**B. Decision → Purchase automation**
When an Activity is marked “chosen,” show a contextual optional action: “Reserve for birthday / anniversary / trip,” carrying selected option, partner opinions, price, linked source and known delivery deadline. No silent purchase or AI extraction.

**C. Shopping intelligence**
Compare captured options side by side (price, merchant, specs, vote, warranty, delivery ETA), suggest what *information is missing*, and highlight duplicates. Do **not** invent prices/availability, scrape external vendors without permission, or assume currencies are interchangeable.

**D. True savings pot**
A separate actual-funds ledger for money set aside, deposits and withdrawals. Distinguish *budget target*, *funds actually saved*, *planned reservation*, and *actual paid*. This is NOT implemented by R18: don't claim a running savings balance until recorded deposits exist.

**E. Occasion timeline and dependency graph**
“Choose gift” → “Place order” → “Expected delivery” → “Wrap” → “Celebrate”; lead-time risk reminders, timezone-aware notifications, and optional handoff to partner. Connect Calendar, Coordinate, Tasks, Travel and Memories without duplicating objects.

**F. Celebration into Memories**
After the event, offer a private/shared “Capture the moment” route that copies no private planning surprises unless the owner explicitly chooses to reveal them.

**G. Smart, consensual assistance**
Optional recommendations from user-authorized data only, transparent reasons, quick edit/undo, strong privacy safeguards and zero external model calls unless the person explicitly opts in.

##### 6. Business intelligence and product KPI framework
These are **proposed metrics**, not a claim that analytics instrumentation already exists.

North Star: **shared-life outcomes completed together**, not screen taps or message counts.

Leading signals:
- inspiration-to-Coordinate conversion;
- comparison-to-decision rate;
- chosen-Activity-option-to-Occasion-plan flow completion;
- plans with a trustworthy budget and linked items;
- celebrations planned with appropriate lead time;
- partner collaboration and explicit consent rate;
- notification → useful action (not notification quantity);
- budget overruns acknowledged and resolved;
- incomplete purchases with missing actual amount;
- reported privacy violations and permission-denial correctness.

Guardrails:
- never send raw private notes, gift descriptions, intimate messages, locations, photos or partner identifiers into product analytics;
- aggregate event counts on device first; explicit opt-in for telemetry;
- allow exporting/deleting analytics;
- keep private surprises out of shared badges, search and global counters.

##### 7. Architecture rules
- One object can be referenced from another; avoid duplicating data as a new independent copy.
- When snapshots are necessary for audit or purchase history, keep immutable origin IDs and the timestamp of copied values.
- Row-level security protects each family/partner boundary; UI hiding alone is insufficient.
- Mutations are idempotent where retries are possible. Preserve existing account sessions, pairing, activities and memories.
- Use bounded queries and stale-response guards on high-frequency lists.
- Never run a new release build because a source-only feature was committed.

##### 8. R18 acceptance and caveats
Source has been committed and migration 0072 applied; no new release. Verify-only run https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/actions/runs/43460884916 passed Flutter analysis and tests; it is not a new APK or phone acceptance.
Before a future APK:
1. Run Flutter analyze and all widget/unit tests.
2. Validate theme appearance over all screens and modal surfaces in Light, Dark and Follow Phone, including RTL/large text.
3. Test all seasonal choices and graceful suppression when Off.
4. Create occasion budget 500 USD, add Wishlist 200, Reserve 100, Purchase actual 90: available must be 410 USD.
5. Confirm foreign-currency rows are not silently deducted.
6. On phone A make a private gift plan and link a private Activity option; phone B must see nothing about either object, even through known IDs or logs.
7. Attempt linking private Activity to shared occasion: database must reject.
8. Confirm shared Activity option can link to shared occasion, with provenance and history and without mutating original Activity.
9. Confirm both existing test accounts/memberships and pre-existing content are preserved.
10. Only after acceptance, produce a single consolidated test APK. The R12–R17 APK remains a frozen testing baseline.

**Do not confuse static source checks with compiled, tested or polished UI.**

### A23. OneSpace · `docs/R4_SCHEDULED_RELATIONSHIP_GESTURES.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace R4 — Scheduled relationship gestures

Three opt-in schedules: morning (15 minutes after partner wake time), after work (15 minutes after the partner's workday ends, on their selected workdays), and goodnight (20 minutes before the partner's bedtime).

##### Privacy and consent
- The sending partner explicitly enables each ritual; each schedule defaults off.
- The receiving partner independently enables "Allow scheduled gestures" in Profile; this defaults off and may be disabled at any time.
- No automated delivery occurs until the recipient has an IANA time zone plus both bedtime and wake-up times. Morning requires wake-up, after-work requires a work-end time and working days.
- The scheduler uses the recipient's *phone time zone*, not their selected weather city. Postgres IANA timezone conversion handles seasonal DST.
- Delivery skips the recipient's sleep interval (including crossing midnight) and only occurs in the 10-minute window following the target. Missed messages are not backfilled.
- One automatic message per sender/moment/recipient-local-day, enforced by a unique index. Each ritual rotates among 10 copy variants; existing manual gesture functionality remains unchanged.
- Automated messages are clearly labeled in gesture history and notifications. In-app alerts only; remote push notifications are not configured.

##### Scheduling and verification
- Supabase Cron job: `onespace-consented-gestures`, 5-minute cadence.
- Private non-exposed function: `private.send_due_gestures(timestamptz)`. No execute grant to application users.
- Relevant schema files: 0042–0044. These were saved from the applied remote Supabase migration history.
- Quick sanity query (does not add a schedule): `select private.send_due_gestures(now());` invoked by the privileged DB operator.
- Device QA: both accounts, consent on/off transitions, profile times saved, morning/night and after-work local time, automatic labeling, no duplicate delivery. Test real DST transitions with simulated time only in a controlled test environment; never send surprise messages to production users.

##### Not yet supported
Push delivery outside the app, one-off custom schedules, offline sending, holidays or exceptions, and recipient-managed review of individual rituals. These belong to later iterations.

##### Delivered in source
- Gesture history moved to the top-right history action, with Received and Sent filters.

- Seven colored default gesture cards and creator-owned custom gestures.
- Custom gestures allow an optional ritual trigger; automatic delivery still requires an enabled ritual schedule and recipient consent.
- Recipient-facing auto-generated gestures remain transparently labeled.
- Profile supports multiple preferred partner labels; Today chooses one for the current session.
- Activity tag editor looks up a shared saved-tag inventory, allows creating tags, and global search can open matching Activities.
- Today feature grid uses real modules and a state-specific relationship icon.

##### QA priority
Verify the APK before replacing an existing installed release. On two physical phones, test the gesture popup and history, RLS visibility, custom manual and scheduled gestures, keyboard positioning and voice attachments, @ matching including email addresses, tag creation/search, and the previously reported Calendar/To-dos/Notes red screens. Runtime issues cannot be declared fixed until reproduced and tested with phone logs.

##### Current boundaries
The red-screen root cause is still unknown without a device exception. Flutter ErrorWidget diagnostic is a recovery/reporting path, not a root-cause fix. Push notification delivery, complete calendar syncing, multi-step voice press-and-hold interaction, and thorough dark/RTL polish are subsequent work.

### A25. OneSpace · `docs/R7_REAL_LIFE_CONTINUITY_QA.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace R7 — Real-life continuity

Source: the user's OneSpace project brief, including its examples about
remembering a partner's acquaintances, repeated morning car checklists,
shopping research, reunions, travel, and preparation for celebrations.

##### Built in this iteration
- Reusable Checklist: mark any editable checklist reusable; view templates
  through the Reusable filter; choose "Use today" to create a fresh copy.
  Each active item is copied unchecked, original completion remains untouched.
  Copies inherit the source's visibility; a private source remains private.
  A copy references its template and displays the phone's local creation date.
  The server performs the copy in one transaction and checks membership/privacy.
- Countdown Activity component: date-only targets with optional yearly repeat,
  useful for trips, reunions, birthdays and celebrations. Render past dates as
  elapsed time rather than negative numbers; annual Feb 29 falls on Feb 28 in
  non-leap years. Date-only interpretation avoids timezone shifts.
- Today: a secondary, non-blocking countdown fetch shows the nearest
  authorized Activity milestones; tapping one opens its original Activity.
  Countdown data is NOT copied into the Calendar table.
- World People & Contacts: a dedicated quick-create flow for remembering names,
  relationship context, and optional contact information. It creates one
  normal Activity with a Contact component. Shared/private choice follows the
  same existing RLS rules; World indexes those components rather than creating
  a second address book. Original Activity discussion and edits remain available.
- Lists entry now has a standalone Scaffold, matching the prior fixes for
  Calendar, To-dos, and Notes.
- Added pure date/annual countdown regression tests and performance timing
  for the optional Today countdown query.

##### Security decisions
- Never duplicate a private checklist into a shared one without consent.
- The clone RPC checks that the caller is a space member and can see the
  template, and is granted only to authenticated users.
- RLS remains the authority for World contacts, Activity components, and
  displayed Today milestones.
- Existing original items, histories, and user data are not deleted.
- User-input dates are validated and the template clone is limited to 150
  active items to prevent accidental runaway duplication.

##### Two-device acceptance
1. From phone A, create a shared morning checklist, add three items, mark
   one complete, make the list reusable; confirm B sees the template.
2. From phone B, choose Use today; verify the copied items are all unchecked,
   source template unchanged, and both partners see the new shared copy.
3. Create a private checklist on A and verify B cannot read or copy it.
4. Add an Activity Countdown for a reunion and verify both phones show the
   same local calendar date with appropriate time-remaining labels.
5. Create an annual birthday countdown and test annual rollover.
6. Add a person in World with a relationship note and verify they appear in
   searchable Contacts; private people must remain private.
7. Open Calendar, To-dos, Notes, Lists and World; back gesture should pop
   the child route correctly, and twice-back from Today exits.
8. Test both phones' first frame, startup and page loading times; capture
   exceptions via Copy error details when anything fails.

##### R8 priorities / not yet delivered
P0: real-phone validation and performance tracing; correct any remaining
crashes on Calendar/To-dos/Notes and keyboard/navigation regressions.
P1: actionable time-zone-aware alarms and push delivery with quiet-hours
policies and recipient permissions (do not claim current in-app reminders
are OS alarms). Allow early gift/holiday and budget-preparation reminders.
P1: Android native share-in for product links/photos/flight documents, with
a destination chooser that attaches content to the correct Activity or list.
P1: more helpful travel/reunion plans and Calendar linking without duplicate
source-of-truth records.
P2: entertainment lists, watched status, shared reactions, and memories layouts.
P2: per-user themes, Arabic/English UI parity, and iOS test build parity.
Keep emergency-contact OS integration research and shared-data deletion
consent rules as explicit design decisions, not implemented claims.

No public launch until both phones pass the private trial and security review.

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace R9 — Calm Today, central settings, accountable lifecycle

##### What is delivered
- Today shows two synchronized partner columns: name and clock above the
  selected city, region, country, current temperature, conditions, high/low
  and probability of rain. No redundant clock date/city/offset/difference.
  Living together hides the entire clock/weather card.
- Removed the "Our space" heading and living-mode popup from Today.
  Relationship mode and visit dates live under Settings.
- Needs attention, Coming up together and Recent discussions are equal-rank
  collapsible accordions with matching typography, icons and count headings.
  Recent discussions remains accessible even with zero messages.
- Remember now focuses only on Calendar, To-dos and Notes. The upcoming
  date/due and recent note lists are no longer duplicated from Today.
- Settings is a dedicated header-gear destination separate from Profile.
  Relationship, preferred partner names, work/sleep schedule,
  scheduled-gesture consent, weather city, language and personal gesture
  management are reachable there. Preferences open context-specific editors.
- Gesture Sent/Received history includes localized date and local time.
- Activity actions: Mark completed, Cancel with optional reason, Reopen,
  Archive, Restore and Audit history. Archived Activities remain searchable
  in Coordinate > All; they are never hard-deleted.
- Component actions: Remove from Activity and Restore, with a "View removed
  items" control. Removal is soft and audit-backed.
- Database audit triggers now distinguish ordinary edits, Activity archive/
  restore, and component remove/restore with server timestamps and user.
  Existing prior revisions default to "edited" without deleting history.

##### Device QA
1. On both phones, Today partner columns show the correct distinct city
   selections and each weather metric in the matching column.
2. Recheck living-together mode: no clocks or weather, and no empty heading.
   Return to living apart through Settings > Relationship.
3. Confirm three Today accordions share exactly one visual level and counters.
4. Open Remember; no duplicated dates, upcoming dues or recent notes.
5. In Settings, modify preferred labels, automatic consent, a city and
   relationship mode; confirm each persists after reopening.
6. Send gestures from phone A to B; view history on both and compare localized
   send time to phone clocks.
7. Complete an Activity, reopen it, cancel with a reason, archive and restore.
   Confirm all stage and archive events appear in audit history.
8. Remove a child component; verify it disappears from the active view,
   appears under View removed items, and can be restored. Verify old version
   and author remain in the audit trail.
9. Verify Android system-back gesture and double-back exit protection.
10. Repeat Calendar / To-dos / Notes opening and check performance on both
    Android phones. These are not considered device-verified yet.

##### Continued iteration
- True device performance profiling, notification push delivery, work/sleep
  quiet hours and time-zone-aware alarms.
- Native Android share-in for links, images and flight details, with an
  Activity/list destination chooser.
- Press-and-hold / slide-to-cancel messenger-style voice UX polish.
- Shared Calendar source-of-truth integration, monthly/weekly/daily refinement,
  proactive gift-planning reminders and recurrence.
- Richer relationship memories, journeys and shared media experiences.
- Explicit mutual-deletion consent policies before allowing irreversible
  deletion of any shared object.

**No permanent deletion is implemented by the Archive/Remove actions.**

### A27. OneSpace · `docs/RELEASE_POLICY_ONE_APK_PER_ITERATION.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 13 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ Partly OVERRIDDEN by D86–D88 (26 Nov 2026). The release-marker rule still applies; the package name in it is outdated.*

#### OneSpace release discipline — one preview per completed iteration

##### Source commits are not releases

The Android workflow **does not run on normal pushes to main**. It has two
explicit triggers:

1. A final-iteration commit that adds/updates release/READY_TO_BUILD.md.
2. A human-initiated workflow_dispatch from GitHub Actions.

Do not edit the release marker while developing. Complete all source changes,
SQL migrations, documentation, tests and code review first. Then update the
marker once. Build and publish the single resulting preview APK.

##### Release acceptance gates

The single release workflow performs, in order:

- Checkout the exact release-triggering commit;
- Flutter 3.50 dependency resolution from the committed lockfile (must not
  modify or push that file during the release workflow);
- Flutter analyzer and automated tests;
- Optimized signed APK build using existing preview-only signing identity;
- App signature and com.onespace.onespace.preview package verification;
- Signed APK artifact retention;
- GitHub Release publication, and verification of the released asset.

Do not call a release published until the matching GitHub Release lists its
signed APK. Do not call it phone-tested until both accounts and real Android
devices have passed the acceptance checklist.

Build number 0.1.N is GitHub's run number, not the iteration identifier.
A failed release is reported explicitly; fix the cause and trigger one
replacement candidate instead of starting background builds per commit.
If only publication failed, prefer re-running the publish job over rebuilding.

This avoids development-triggered cancelled runs and protects GitHub Actions
minutes, release history and the user's phone-testing experience.

### A28. OneSpace · `docs/SELECTED_CITY_WEATHER_AND_LETTERS.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — City-based weather and Couple Letters

**Two of Us. One Space**

##### Weather
- The phone clock and time zone remain automatic.
- Profile → Weather city → **Choose a city** searches Open-Meteo's geocoding catalogue, with name, region and country shown to disambiguate results.
- Each person explicitly selects the city *they want to monitor*. **The app does not access saved locations in other weather apps.** No GPS, location services, address book or coarse/precise location permissions are needed.
- The selected city is stored as its public city-centroid coordinates, Open-Meteo city ID and a human-readable label; these represent the city, **not the person's live position**.
- Today displays each partner's selected city, temperature, weather conditions, today's high/low and rain probability. Auto refresh is throttled to approximately 45 minutes while the app is foregrounded; no background tracking.
- Changing the city fetches today's conditions before committing the selection. Failed fetch keeps the prior setting; old permission-derived city snapshots are no longer shown as selected cities.
- **Stop monitoring** clears the saved city name, public city ID, centroid and forecast from the profile, so it is not available even through direct profile queries; re-selecting resumes sharing.
- API source: Open-Meteo (geocoding based on GeoNames). Attribution is visible in Today. Commercial/public use must obtain suitable data licensing.

##### Connect → Letters
- Actual inbox, sent letters, private drafts and an editor. Letters have a title/body and immutable send timestamp.
- Drafts are visible only to their author. They may be edited or deleted until sent; publishing requires nonempty message.
- Sent letters are visible only within the paired space, **immutable after sending**, and may receive replies. Reply references are restricted by composite space ID foreign key.
- The recipient opening a sent letter writes an individual immutable read receipt. The sender sees whether their partner has marked it read. No fake push alerts or delivery assertions.
- Table and read-receipt policies are protected by RLS and explicit grants. All writes are scoped to authenticated space membership. Nothing is publicly published.
- No existing user data is reset.

##### Testing
- Repeated city names in different states, no-results, network outage, choosing/change/stop, two authorized accounts, weather stale warning, no location permissions.
- Draft visibility and deletion, send, immutable sent letter, reply under same space, recipient-only read receipt, denying unrelated spaces, multi-device viewing.
- Analyze/tests, signed Android APK, correct package ID and actual GitHub Release asset.

##### Permission regression fix
The profiles table uses column-level UPDATE grants. Earlier added time-zone/weather columns did not inherit profile write grants. Migration 0036 explicitly grants UPDATE only for the new fields, while owner-only profile RLS continues to block changes to a partner's settings. Tested with an authenticated rollback-only city/profile write.

### A29. OneSpace · `docs/SHARED_LIFE_ACTIVITY_MODEL.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — the Shared-Life Activity Model
**Architecture baseline, 13 November 2026.** This document is a product and implementation plan, not functionality already shipped.

##### Original promise, restored
**Two of Us. One Space** Three pillars: **Remember · Coordinate · Connect**. Original experience areas: Our Day, Commitments, World, Entertainment, Memories, Journeys. The original **commitment loop** was: *request → accept → research/add links → review → select/purchase/postpone*. The original M01–M22 catalog remains a capability backlog, not 22 navigation destinations.

The app must not become a generic task/calendar/notes bundle. Partners need a persistent space where they can jointly make decisions, capture the reasoning, exchange experiences, and remember what happened across different time zones.

##### Canonical model
**Activity** = one enduring purpose (e.g., *Find a heater*, *Find gymnastics classes*). It begins with a **title** and optionally a **body**, with immutable author and original creation time. It can grow through connected child records, each attributed and time-stamped:
- **Comments:** chronological conversation, reply threads, target links to an Activity, Option or Step; no silent overwrites.
- **Edits/revisions:** partners may revise the current Activity title/body; preserve original text, all subsequent versions, actor, UTC date/time, and a readable before/after diff.
- **Options/findings:** comparable candidates with source URL, screenshot, store/provider, observed price/currency/date, specs, reviews with source, and free-text notes. Do not imply manually recorded prices are live.
- **Opinions:** independent like/dislike/neutral reactions on each option; reactions never constitute a joint decision.
- **Steps:** optional nested actions created at any time, with assignee, status, result, deadline if wanted, supporting evidence. An Activity does not require steps.
- **Decisions/outcomes:** chosen/rejected candidate, why, by whom, when; purchased/returned, registered/withdrawn or postponed/cancelled, all reversible with history.
- **Evidence:** screenshots/photos/files stored privately and attached to the relevant Activity, Option, Comment, or Step. Partner authorization is checked for all reads, signed URLs and uploads.
- **Activity feed:** interleaved comments and meaningful change events with optional type filters. Preserve original timestamps; render local time to each viewer.

*Activity* is the container. *Option* is a research candidate. *Step* is a unit of work. *Comment* is a conversation item. *Memory*, *Check-in*, and *People/World reference* can also exist independently and be linked to an Activity when helpful. Avoid creating a universal Task object and renaming it.

##### Acceptance: finding a heater
1. A creates **Find a heater** with body and immutable authorship.
2. A attaches Amazon and Walmart options: links, screenshots, observed prices, reviews/specs and notes.
3. A comments *Don't forget these*; B replies *I'll look after work*.
4. B dislikes both and updates body to require **timer and automatic shutoff**. Original body and edit history remain accessible.
5. B adds a third option and comments specifically about voltage. A replies and likes the candidate.
6. B chooses candidate 3. They record the reason. Later B marks **Purchased**, with optional price paid/receipt and eventual **Delivered**.
7. Every original link, screenshot, opinion, comment, old body and decision stays visible under the Activity as authorized.

##### Acceptance: finding gymnastics classes
1. A creates **Find gymnastics classes**.
2. Partners add 3 providers and compare **age range, dates/times, fees, parent attendance, swimming, trial classes, websites and travel context**.
3. B creates step **Email three places** and marks provider-specific emails sent. Replies are attached as evidence under the relevant provider.
4. The couple discusses the responses, adds an in-person visit with a date/time-zone and completes that step.
5. They choose provider B, record why, then complete the **Register** step. Activity outcome is **Registered**.
6. All information remains searchable; none disappears simply because the Activity is done.

##### Experience architecture
**Bottom tabs: Today | Remember | Coordinate | Connect | World**
- **Today:** Our Day/My Day, clocks, moments that matter, reply requests, recent partner updates, next decisions, upcoming commitments.
- **Coordinate:** the collaboration engine; Waiting for me / Updated by partner / Comparing / Decided / Completed. Start with minimal title/body; optional templates thereafter.
- **Remember:** shared calendar, to-do lists, notes, upcoming dues and upcoming events.
- **Connect:** check-ins, memories, connection, shared rituals, entertainment and conversation prompts.
- **Memories (within Connect):** original stories, photo albums, shared occasions and recollections; protect each author's contribution.
- **World:** people/family context, travel/reunions, safe knowledge library and references.
Calendar access is contextual (Today, Activity, global search), not the app's identity. Activity inbox, search and profile appear in the header. Existing Tasks/Lists/Notes/Goals/Calendar remain available during migration.

An Activity detail page must show: **original byline; current body; options/comparison; optional next steps; conversation & changes; explicit decision/outcome; edit history**. No part of the detail should be gated by an unnecessary assigned-to/due-date field.

##### Suggested database entities
`activities`, `activity_revisions`, `activity_comments`, `activity_options`, `activity_option_reactions`, `activity_steps`, `activity_attachments`, `activity_decisions`, `activity_events`, `activity_reads`. Prefer structured, type-aware attributes for options; use server timestamps, backend-protected audit, optimistic concurrency and parent-inherited RLS. A revision and its content edit must be **atomic**.

##### Specializations, not separate apps
- **Shopping/Compare:** merchant, price, currency, specs, reviews, shipping, purchase/delivery outcome.
- **Find/Enroll:** provider, age, classes, schedule, requirements, emails, visit, registration.
- **Travel/Journeys:** flights, hotel, itinerary, people, bookings, local times, documents.
- **Choose:** alternatives, criteria, personal preferences, jointly recorded outcome.
- **Remember/World:** contextual people, contacts, documents, life history, saved knowledge.
- **Experience/Together:** celebration, relationship moment, activities and reflections.

Templates configure optional fields, evidence types and typical stages, but users can choose a blank Activity. Stages are flexible; *Purchased* is not a valid universal end state for all Activities.

##### Security and preservation
Equal partners. Shared Activities collaborative. Private Activities creator-controlled. Revocable view-only access must never imply commenting/editing rights. Child records inherit the parent's RLS permissions. Author IDs and time come from the server; no personally identifying data in public previews/notifications. Attachments require a private bucket with size/type checks and short-lived access. Preserve both partners' histories. Shared Memories require **both partners' approval** for permanent deletion. All old data remains available through the transition.

Original constraints: English/Arabic UI independently per account, preserve author-entered text without machine translation; Android then iOS parity; hybrid external/native integrations; native Android share-in for links and images; security and acceptable phone performance.

##### Delivery gates
0. Fix existing CI lint/build failures; keep the preview signing identity stable.
1. Add an Activity core with author/time, title/body, atomic edits/revisions, secure RLS and an Activity detail screen.
2. Add contextual comments/replies, options, source links, notes, price/specs, reactions, comparison, decision history.
3. Add secure screenshots/photos, Android share-to-OneSpace, steps, contacts/emails, date/time-zone and outcomes.
4. Move the navigation, Today dashboard and unified activity inbox to the new interaction model without data loss.
5. Expand World, Memories, Entertainment, Journeys, AI-assisted recall and privacy-safe reminders.

**No full release until both concrete heater and gymnastics flows pass two-account Android tests.** Existing object screens cannot simply be removed or relabeled; this is a new shared-life interaction model.

### A30. OneSpace · `docs/TODAY_ATTENTION_AND_REMINDERS.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — Today attention and in-app reminders
**Two of Us. One Space**

Today is an action-oriented dashboard, not just a count screen. The new focus feed is included in the same **one-request Home snapshot** (get_home_snapshot_v2(p_today date)), using the signed-in person's local calendar date.

- Overdue shared or privately visible Activities, due-today Activities, high/urgent Activities, and active **reminder components** surface as compact rows.
- Press an item to open its original Activity with the discussion, options, and editing history intact.
- **Snooze for me** (tomorrow/three days/one week) stores a personal preference keyed by the viewer and Activity or reminder component. It never changes the partner's feed or the shared Activity status.
- The Today feed shows upcoming calendar occasions for the next seven days, including date-safe yearly recurrence, and links into the shared calendar.
- Recent Activity discussions deep-link to the specific Activity rather than the general Coordinate landing page.
- The long dashboard totals remain accessible behind an expandable "More shared totals" section.
- Reminders are now addable to any Activity/Option via **Remind us**. The date picker creates a component with creator, timestamp, revision history and 'open' status. A partner may mark a shared reminder **handled**, or reopen it.
- The Remember due list includes reminder components and navigates to the original Activity instead of attempting to open a component UUID as an Activity.
- No push notifications, alarm permissions, background scheduling, emails, or automatic calls are introduced. This release provides **in-app attention/reminders only**.

##### Security
The backend is a **SECURITY INVOKER** function. All Activity, component and calendar queries use the signed-in user's existing RLS; source data is not republished to other spaces. Snooze rows have RLS policies requiring the viewer's own user ID and visibility of the underlying Activity. Only the current user can create/update their snoozes.

##### Acceptance
1. Create an Activity due tomorrow, open Today on either partner's phone and confirm access.
2. Create a reminder under an Option (e.g., "Ask Gym B about swimming" due Friday); verify it appears in Today and Remember and opens the original Activity.
3. Snooze the reminder for yourself; it disappears for you but stays in your partner's feed.
4. Mark it handled; it disappears from both upcoming feeds while remaining visible with history in the Activity.
5. Add a recurring anniversary; verify the next local calendar occurrence appears only within the next seven days.
6. Verify a private Activity/reminder doesn't appear on an unauthorized partner account.
7. Verify refresh and deep links don't incur additional unrelated network requests.

Device testing and multi-account behavior still need confirmation before production.
