### A31. OneSpace · `docs/TODAY_PARTNER_CLOCKS.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — Partner clocks on Today

**Two of Us. One Space**

##### Experience
- Each partner voluntarily chooses an IANA time zone under **Profile → Your time zone**. The list is searchable; no GPS/location permission is requested.
- **Today** shows the two partner clocks, time zone names, local calendar dates, and current UTC offsets. The time difference accounts for DST automatically.
- **Living together** shows one local clock rather than unnecessary long-distance messaging.
- **Living apart, same time zone** shows two clocks and flags a configuration mismatch when their saved time zones currently differ.
- **Long-distance** shows both clocks and the current hour/minute time difference.
- An unset partner zone is clearly marked **not set**, never inferred from an abbreviation, name or location.
- The viewer's clock may use the device's local time as an explicit fallback until the user configures their zone.
- Times refresh locally once per minute and require **no new network request**. The existing get_home_snapshot_v2 RPC includes the two zones in the member records; therefore startup avoids an extra round-trip.
- Changing Profile settings and returning to Today requests a refreshed snapshot.

##### Security and correctness
- A time zone is personal profile data and only visible under the preexisting profile RLS policy (the owner and their paired partner).
- Only the account owner may update their stored zone. No location tracking or address storage.
- Server limits time_zone length and characters; Flutter validates against its bundled IANA catalogue. Missing or invalid zones display as unknown.
- The bundled IANA database supports DST shifts, half-hour offsets, and different calendar dates across the same UTC instant.
- No timezone is assumed based on the user's IP, GPS, or the relationship-mode choice.

##### Acceptance
1. Set one profile to Asia/Riyadh and another to America/New_York; confirm the times and difference.
2. Verify the difference is 8 hours in January and 7 hours in July (US DST).
3. A partner with no zone configured shows a clear setup message.
4. Switch relationship mode across living together / apart same zone / LDR.
5. Changing profile zone and returning to Today refreshes its clock.
6. Validate that unauthenticated users cannot request the dashboard, and other spaces cannot see profile settings.
7. Check Arabic RTL and small Android screens before production.

### A32. OneSpace · `docs/WORLD_CONTACTS_INDEX.md`

*First written 13 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace World — Contacts from Coordinate

**Two of Us. One Space**

This first World directory is intentionally a **read-only index**, not a second independently editable address book.

##### Behavior
- World now contains a **Saved contacts** entry.
- Only contacts from the signed-in partner's RLS-visible Activities appear.
- Contacts are retrieved on demand with a bounded query (up to 200 recent entries), ordered by most recently created.
- Search matches the contact card title, original Activity title, named person, phone number, email address, physical address and working hours.
- Cards present the same tap-to-call, email and map actions as Coordinate. Calls/emails are never sent automatically.
- Each card links back to its **original Activity** for editing, discussion, evidence and audit history.
- Data is not cloned or migrated into a duplicate table. Existing per-Activity privacy and editing-history rules continue to govern access.

##### Deferred
- A full World person/contact master record with multiple associated Activities, deduplication and explicit relationship roles (family, school, doctor, etc).
- Contact import/export and native address-book integration (requires explicit permission and privacy considerations).
- Pagination when the contact index grows beyond the first 200 RLS-visible entries.
- Bilingual World/Contact labels and advanced date handling.

##### Acceptance
- Create a shared Activity with a Contact component; it appears in the World index on both authorized accounts.
- Create a private Activity contact; unauthorized partner cannot see it.
- Search by phone, person's name, business title, or hours.
- Tap "From" to return to the original Activity with full collaboration context.
- RLS and app test validation are required before production.

#### Planned private preview — R12 through R16 (NOT AN APK BUILD TRIGGER)

##### Release intent
Consolidate all committed source since the last verified APK preview-0.1.395 into exactly one signed, upgrade-installable Android preview.

Current readiness: source and database additions staged; static checks performed; **Flutter analyzer, test suite, Android compilation, APK signing and device acceptance pending**.

##### Included
- R12 contact cards, Daily Check-In refinements, Letters/gesture pools.
- R13 Shared Experiences, ratings, discussions, Today agenda.
- R14 Trips & Journeys, itinerary, currency-aware estimates and realtime.
- R15 private travel documents (PDF/images), authorized signed previews, native picker.
- R16 Android share-sheet intake for external links/text with Coordinate comparison options; Occasions & Surprises planner with budgets/lead time; daily selfie gallery and scrapbook presets.
- Release publish job avoids repeat attempts after permissions failure; signed APK retained as Actions artifact.

##### Before the ONE build
1. Run a single `flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile` and `flutter analyze --no-fatal-infos` plus `flutter test` when build capability is available.
2. Resolve failures in source first; keep `pubspec.lock` and signing key stable.
3. Confirm DB migrations 0058–0069 are applied and existing two accounts/memberships/space/content unchanged.

9. Do not advertise a public launch; iOS testing and several external/background integrations remain separate.

##### Guardrails
- Never reset/delete auth accounts, space memberships, or saved records.
- Don't run Actions verification repeatedly for individual commits.
- Don't modify `release/READY_TO_BUILD.md` until explicitly ready for the build.
- If GitHub Releases returns HTTP 403, retrieve verified APK from the completed build's Artifacts instead of wasting retries.

Require-Firebase-Config: true

##### One signed QA APK only
- Source includes merged PR #74 (invitation signup/signin), PR #75 (approved
  exact tagline `Two of Us. One Space`), and PR #76 (stable QA signing
  certificate and aligned source for email templates), and PR #77 (safe

- Android package: `onespace.qa`.

  `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`. Production Supabase untouched.

  `90375bb8d7dbd6527cca17f626da149534eaffe10076c38a5c69ac86a3aac043`.
  Any mismatch must fail the build; never silently rotate QA keys.

  APK publication must all pass before considering this release available.

##### Real device acceptance before closing
1. Install QA APK **over** existing QA app (do not uninstall and lose a
   saved incoming invitation).
2. New invited user: latest invitation, signup, dedicated confirm-email page,
   click confirmation email, sign in, automatically join existing space.
3. Existing invited user: latest invitation, sign in, automatically join.
4. Wrong account cannot redeem; current invitation must not be treated as a
   replaced/expired link.
5. QA Supabase hosted Confirm signup + Reset password templates still need
   matching HTML applied in dashboard; source-only commit does not update
   hosted Auth settings. Send real emails and verify logo + link.
6. No QA record deletion, space/account separation or Production database
   changes during this release.

##### Future roadmap (NOT an excuse to block safe invitation testing)
- Founder expressly authorizes a QA-only historical record reset if needed
  for structural redesign of ownership tables.
- Structured private and shared content must remain eligible for archive and
  restore. Uploaded media, photos and documents are **excluded** with a
  clear user-facing disclosure. Do not remove all archive/restore features.
- Do not enable destructive lifecycle actions until ownership and structured
  restore are proven safe.

One Space** tagline in themes, strings and historical documentation; broader brand regression) and merged PR #103 (newest QA invitation source, original-space after-login handoff, already-joined idempotence and single contextual join card).

- Real hosted Supabase signup/forgot-password templates and delivered emails still require external/manual verification. No space or account deletion, leave, partner removal, or automatic cleanup while full structured archive+restore milestone is incomplete. Media/documents excluded from structured archive by design.

##### P0 black-screen recovery candidate — R468 regression
- **Reported impact:** installed QA app remains entirely black on launch beginning with R468, potentially affecting both R468 and subsequent releases. This is a **P0** blocker. Do not claim launch-ready from static Flutter tests.
- **Source:** merged PR #113, commit `4625aa84f435facd8f6bd828d40b6bec1dece8eb`; R464→R468 introduced a native `onespace/environment` package identity call before Supabase initialization without a timeout. Root cause on the affected phones remains unproven until device logs/runtime acceptance.
- **Mitigation:** Android app/package identity verification now times out safely after six seconds instead of hanging forever; verifies QA `onespace.qa` against QA backend only and fails closed. Supabase secure-session initialization is bounded with an explanatory error. The first Flutter frame has a reliable light theme and the sole approved tagline **Two of Us. One Space**, independent of theme/translation loading; Android native launch surface is not silently black in night mode.
- **Tests:** targeted startup/identity/widget regression and broad QA Flutter CI passed on PR #113. Installed QA APK emulators are separate additional checks; their running/unknown state must not be represented as successful evidence. Actual device behavior cannot be inferred from emulator/build success.
- **Release:** one signed QA APK for `onespace.qa` and QA Supabase only, original trusted QA certificate + Firebase package check mandatory. Do NOT clear app storage, uninstall an already installed QA app or delete any database content to fix startup. Install newer QA APK **over** existing one to preserve saved invitation, then verify first app frame, sign in, device unlock, invitation handoff and push on both Android devices.
- **Failing cases:** if still black, capture Android logcat around launch and whether a logo/startup error ever appears; only then consider graphics/engine-specific workarounds. Do not rotate signing keys, switch backends, relax RLS or enable destructive lifecycle actions.

- Worker password is stored in Supabase Vault (`onespace_push_worker_secret`).
- Supabase migration `0089` fixes the PostgREST service-role JWT claim compatibility; regression test `0013` passed.
- Live authorized server invocation returned HTTP 200, with 1 notification accepted by Firebase and 0 retries.
- Supabase migration `0090` enabled `onespace-fcm-push-delivery` every minute. **Do not run the setup schedule again.**
- Physical Android lock-screen delivery remains an acceptance test; server acceptance alone is not proof of display on a handset.



**Current product:** Supabase remains the authority for messages, reminders,
privacy and accounts. Firebase delivers a generic lock-screen prompt only.
Android preview application ID: **com.onespace.onespace.preview**.

##### Part A — Firebase Console, your Google account
1. In https://console.firebase.google.com create a **separate** Firebase project
   named OneSpace. Google Analytics is optional.
2. Project Settings → General → Your apps → Add Android App.
   Package ID: `com.onespace.onespace.preview`.
   Nickname: `OneSpace Android Preview`.
   The SHA certificate is not required for basic FCM.
3. Download **google-services.json** for this exact Android registration.
   Do not rename it to include (1) or (2).
4. Project Settings → Cloud Messaging: check that the Firebase Cloud Messaging API
   is enabled for the Google Cloud project.

##### Part B — GitHub private Android build configuration
The app's build workflow will continue to build without Firebase until the
real JSON is provided. Supply it using a private GitHub Actions secret.

1. On a Windows PC, open PowerShell in the folder containing your downloaded
   `google-services.json`. Encode the file **locally**:
   ```powershell
   [Convert]::ToBase64String([IO.File]::ReadAllBytes((Resolve-Path '.\google-services.json')))
   ```
2. In the private `BoyofKay/OneSpace` GitHub repository go to
   Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → New repository secret.
3. Name: `ONESPACE_GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON_BASE64`. Value: paste the base64
   output. Do not paste a Firebase **service account** JSON in GitHub.
4. Save. The next verified preview build will read this secret, validate that
   the Android package matches, install the config only on the CI build runner,
   and enable the Google Services Gradle plugin. It is not committed to Git.

##### Part C — Server sending key: Supabase Edge Functions Secrets
1. Firebase console → Project settings → Service accounts →
   Firebase Admin SDK → Generate new private key. Download JSON securely
   and store it in your password manager. The file contains a PRIVATE KEY.
2. In the Supabase project `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo` open
   Edge Functions → Secrets. Add `ONESPACE_FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`
   with the complete JSON file, represented as a single-line JSON string.
   Do NOT upload the key to GitHub, a chat, or your Android phone.
3. The project's `onespace_push_worker_secret` has already been generated
   directly inside Supabase Vault using 256 bits of cryptographic randomness.
   Do **not** create another random string or call `vault.create_secret` again.
4. In the Supabase **SQL Editor**, run:
   ```sql
   select decrypted_secret
   from vault.decrypted_secrets
   where name = 'onespace_push_worker_secret';
   ```
   Copy the returned secret **only inside your browser**; do not paste it into
   ChatGPT, a GitHub commit, or an Android config file.
5. Go to Supabase → Edge Functions → Secrets. Add a new secret:
   Name: `ONESPACE_PUSH_CRON_SECRET`; Value: the exact clipboard contents.
   Save it and clear the clipboard.
6. The Firebase Admin SDK private-key JSON must remain separately stored in
   `ONESPACE_FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`. Never include it in a query,
   cron command, or Android build.

##### Part D — Activate the scheduled worker, only after C is complete
The connected Supabase administrator can activate the following cron job after
the Edge secret is confirmed. The cron command references Vault at execution
time, **never** embeds the decrypted worker secret in GitHub or SQL source.

```sql
select cron.schedule('onespace-fcm-push-delivery', '* * * * *', $cron$
  select net.http_post(
    url := 'https://ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo.supabase.co/functions/v1/onespace-push-delivery',
    headers := jsonb_build_object(
      'Content-Type', 'application/json',
      'x-onespace-push-secret',
      (select decrypted_secret from vault.decrypted_secrets
       where name='onespace_push_worker_secret' limit 1)
    ),
    body := '{}'::jsonb,
    timeout_milliseconds := 15000
  );
$cron$);
```

**Do not activate the job until the Edge Function secret is set.** Once enabled,
the worker polls about once per minute. Recurring reminder generation currently
runs every five minutes, so delivery is best-effort, not exact-to-the-second.

##### Part E — Two-phone acceptance
1. Install the newest signed preview over the previous one. DO NOT uninstall
   and DO NOT clear app data.
2. On each phone, sign into that partner's OneSpace account, unlock, and allow
   the Android notification permission. Confirm notifications are not disabled
   in device Settings.
3. Create a shared Reminder due a few minutes into the future.
4. Background (do not force-stop) each app and verify each phone displays a
   **generic** OneSpace notification; tap to open the secure OneSpace
   Notifications center. The recipient must still unlock if the session locked.
5. Test snooze and a second reminder. Confirm there are no duplicate push
   deliveries and partner private reminders don't cross accounts.
6. Sign out explicitly and confirm that device stops receiving private alerts.

##### Important limits
- Android only for the first configured preview. iPhone will require an
  independent iOS Firebase registration, APNs key and Xcode integration.
- No order tracking integration, offline exact-time guarantee or Firebase
  authentication is introduced.
- Notifications sent in the foreground appear in the in-app feed; the OS may
  suppress system alert banners for an actively open app.
- Network, battery savings, OS force-stop and denied permissions affect FCM.
- Pushing begins only after the Firebase config, service account and scheduled
  worker secret are ALL installed.

### A39. OneSpace · `docs/R19_PRODUCT_FEEDBACK_ACCEPTANCE.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R19 — Feedback-led acceptance checklist, implementation status, and open design work

**Source-only iteration. No R19 APK or release marker update. User test accounts and their records are never reset.**

**Verification:** [R19 verify-only workflow 43520422462](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/actions/runs/43520422462) passed committed dependencies, Flutter analysis, and automated tests. Native Android compilation and physical phone acceptance remain pending.
The shipped R12–R17 APK remains an unchanged baseline. R18 source and the subsequent R19 changes have NOT been built into an installable APK.

##### P0 stability and privacy

| User feedback | R19 source response | Status / next gate |
|---|---|---|
| Tagline disappeared | Restored on Today and saved-session sign-in (normal sign-in already uses localized tagline). | Implemented, device QA pending |
| Welcome only first name | FirstName utility for Today; matched cached/fetched profile first name on sign-in and unlock | Implemented, device QA pending |
| Fingerprint stopped | OS authentication now persists across the temporary inactive/background transition; existing FlutterFragmentActivity and USE_BIOMETRIC retained. Device credential/PIN remains supported, Cancel label retained. | Suspected fix, OS device QA needed |
| Getting unexpectedly kicked out | Inactivity gate 5 min → 30 min; background lock 30 seconds → 5 minutes. Does not alter auth token/refresh policy or log out users. | Local lock eased; unexpected server sign-out requires exact logs |
| Experience rating Permission denied | Insert new reviews; update only editable rating/review columns for existing reviews, instead of full-row upsert. | Least-privilege code fix, test with real user credentials |
| Activity likes/dislikes not working | Split insert/update likewise, respecting column grants. | Least-privilege code fix, dual-account test required |
| @ partner in modules | Reusable mention chip, full display-name insertion + notification triggers for shared Calendar events, To-dos, Occasions, Shared Experiences. Notes' existing triggers kept. | Implemented, verify private-content exclusion on both phones |
| Contacts history shows Activity edits | Filter contact audit history by actual visible contact component IDs or standalone contact IDs. | Implemented, QA pending |

##### Today / personalization

| User feedback | Source response | Status |
|---|---|---|
| A little love should be a square | Removed wide special card; inserted matching square entry into Today Explore grid. | Implemented |
| Shared Experience square | Added matching square with media icon. | Implemented |
| Regional holiday selection in Settings > Preferences | Removed selection panel from Calendar; added Settings entry. | Implemented |
| Weather temperature unit US/Metric | Local per-device Fahrenheit/Celsius setting, same backend Celsius data, live refresh. | Implemented |
| Birthdays/anniversaries/special dates settings | Settings entry to existing Occasion manager. | Navigation implemented; dedicated date editor still a future UX enhancement |
| Calendar archive through top History | Moved archived toggle to top AppBar history icon. | Implemented |
| Notes archive through top History | Same Notes AppBar pattern. | Implemented |

##### To-do and Checklists: actual information model

| User feedback | Source response | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Rename page and + button | To-do and Checklists / Add to-do list | Implemented |
| List with independently checkable items | Existing task row becomes list container, new RLS-protected task_checklist_items and per-cycle task_checklist_checks; list no longer has main checkbox. | Implemented, device QA pending |
| Recurring routine lists | Daily / weekly / monthly / yearly choice, nonrecurring sentinel. Each recurrence cycle has separate completion records and historical checks. | Implemented, midnight/two-phone QA pending |
| Filters to top icon | Visibility, status, due-today in top filter sheet. | Implemented |
| Cancelled red strikethrough | Cancelled parent list uses semantic error color and strikethrough. | Implemented |
| Preserve prior records | No rewriting/deleting existing tasks; recurrence defaults to none. | Verified non-destructive schema migration |

##### Notes

- Existing markdown-like note formatting toolbar enhanced with visible, labeled actions for heading/bold/checklist; preview retained. **Not full WYSIWYG**, which requires a richer content model/editor.
- Added RLS-protected note photos table and a private image storage bucket; upload from Android photo picker, signed image previews, soft removal. Save a new note first, then attach photos.
- Notes mention insertion and existing scoped mention notifications retained.
- History entry in top AppBar; existing note versioning/edit concurrency remains.

##### A Little Love

- Primary screen regains **Create my own gesture** at the top.
- Removed counts/hints from individual sending tiles.
- Replaced platform-specific emoji for encouragement/missing-together with consistent Material symbols.
- Added **I miss you** gesture category, three baseline messages, and all database validation updates.
- Existing custom templates and sent history preserved.
- Logical grouping/order should be user-tested and further refined.

##### Daily Connection

- Removed inappropriate "How is your heart today?" card in favor of two equal action squares: Record my feeling / Check on my partner.
- Underlying requests, replies, feelings journal and permissions preserved.
- **Still open:** redesign request states (pending/answered/expired), records timeline, reaction/response affordances, clearer unread badges and meaningful contextual notifications. Do not pretend this is finished.

##### Shared Experiences

- Six category cards: Movie Watchlist, TV Shows, Music Playlist, Books and articles, Games, Others.
- Per-item focus on title and platform; search by title/platform.
- Watch / Play / Open opens the saved URL in its platform, without inventing a streaming link.
- Movies and shows can be marked watched on their card; the transition prompts for a rating. Rating/review available directly from card.
- Removed Planned for and Enjoyed on controls from the editor, preserving existing legacy dates in DB.
- Filters moved into top filter icon, edit history moved to Shared Experiences level, scoped to their own revision rows.
- Archive / Restore via item action menu with an archive confirmation, rather than making archive the primary control.
- **Still open:** richer platform metadata, posters/cover art, playlist/library collection semantics, separate status labels for books/music/games (avoid the term Watched), collection-level analytics and media-provider deep links.

##### Data preservation and architecture

Applied additive migrations:
- 0073 recurring nested to-do checklists and completion cycles
- 0074 "I miss you" gesture enum checks
- 0075 protected note images + storage
- 0076 shared content mention triggers + notification object type

RLS enabled on task_checklist_items, task_checklist_checks, note_images, occasion_budget_items. Only shared content triggers cross-module mention notices; private surprise plans do not.

No destructive migration, auth reset, uninstall, data purge, or account switch.

##### R19 acceptance steps for the NEXT release — not yet performed on phones

1. Upgrade in place on both existing Android phones; confirm both account sessions, pairing and records.
2. Test saved-session fingerprint and device PIN with phone lock, cancellation, temporary app backgrounding, long idle interval, and failed biometric flow.
3. Create a shared To-do list with five subitems; mark different items from each phone; confirm simultaneous sync and daily/weekly cycle resets. Test cancelled list styling and top filters.
4. Test US vs metric temperature preference per device with original Celsius backend value.
5. Test archived Notes/Calendar behind History and Regional Holidays through Settings; create birthday and anniversary.
6. Test new Note photo upload, signed display, access permissions, and removal on both private/shared notes.
7. Test mention insertion in each shared module, one notification to partner, correct detail navigation; verify no mention notification on a private note/occasion.
8. Rate the same Shared Experience twice from each account; check only own rating updates, no permission error. Like/dislike Activity options, including change of vote.
9. Test six Experience collections, quick Watch/Play/Open, inline watched/rate, archive/restore, and history strictly scoped to Experiences.
10. Verify Contact history contains only Contact revisions, not unrelated Activity edits; inspect review history and UI on both phones.
11. Check all controls under Android 150–200% text scaling in light, dark and seasonal themes.
12. Capture app logs if the app actually signs out (different from a local privacy lock) for more precise auth refresh diagnosis.

##### Product-design work not yet delivered

A proper rich-text Notes editor with image blocks interleaved within text, streamlined check-in record lifecycle, an event-driven mention system across individual Comments as well as core documents, more powerful media collections and recommendations, and the full R18 visual polish remain follow-up epics.

Do not conflate a source-only Flutter verification with completed physical Android acceptance testing.

### A40. OneSpace · `docs/R26_SCRAPBOOK_STUDIO_ACCEPTANCE.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R26 — Two-device Scrapbook Studio Acceptance

Scope: Android preview built from R26 marker, upgrading over 0.1.403.
Do not uninstall or clear app data. Both partners keep their existing accounts.

##### Creation & template gallery
1. Remember your current version and photograph the Memories home screen.
2. Open **Connect → Memories → Scrapbook Studio**.
3. Preview each of the four real designed layouts: Love Letters, Our Adventures,
   Little Moments and Milestone Story. All thumbnails should fit without
   clipped text or image placeholders escaping their artwork.
4. Create a **private** scrapbook. Confirm it appears only on its creator's
   phone and does not appear in the partner's Memories list.
5. Create a **shared** scrapbook. Confirm its name is visible on the second
   phone, but editing remains disabled until an invitation is accepted.

##### Partner collaboration + native push
6. On the first phone, tap **Invite**. Background the second phone.
   Expect the generic OneSpace push. Open it through Notifications and
   accept the invitation. If push does not appear, check the in-app feed;
   record whether the issue is transport vs UI.
7. On the second phone, add another scrapbook page, write a new quote,
   and add a photo. Verify the first phone sees the updated design.
8. Check that a contributor cannot edit a **private** scrapbook or a
   **shared** scrapbook before accepting the invitation.
9. Open the same page on both phones, save a change on one, then attempt
   saving an older version on the other: it should show a conflict rather
   than silently overwriting the first edit.

##### Photo slots, metadata & PNG
10. Add photo 1 via Android camera and photo 2 via Gallery. Try using an
    existing scrapbook photo for a third slot (where available).
11. Expand **Photo library & searchable tags**. Add people, location,
    other tags and captions. Search Memories by a unique tag and confirm
    the scrapbook is found.
12. Change template on a populated page; existing photos and writing
    must remain associated with the same page.
13. Tap **Export this page as PNG**. Verify a high-resolution PNG appears
    in Android **Pictures/OneSpace**, with correctly rendered photos and
    text. Confirm the native Android share sheet opens.
14. Confirm none of these actions affected existing To-do Checklists,
    Reminders, Notes, or Firebase message delivery.

##### Backend acceptance
- Rollback SQL regression: `supabase/tests/0014_r26_collaborative_scrapbooks.sql`
- Private memory cannot be viewed by partner.
- Partner cannot edit pages before invitation acceptance.
- Accepted partner can create and edit pages.
- Photo slot references cannot point to another scrapbook's photo.
- Inviting the partner produces one in-app notification and FCM outbox item.
- R26 release must keep the exact `com.onespace.onespace.preview` package
  and existing signing identity; install **over** 0.1.403.

##### Open issues to document rather than guess
- A push received on only one of the phones is not yet a verified two-device
  delivery result. Record which phone/recipient, notification state,
  whether the app was force-stopped and Android notification permission.
- PNG export is Android-only and one page at a time, not a bound PDF book.
- Signed photo previews require a network connection. Offline scrapbook
  caching is not claimed in this release.

#### R27 — Trips: Flight Research & Travel Preparation Acceptance

This is a source-verified development milestone; release APK requires its own
signed R27 build. It does not change the already-building R26 APK.

##### Flight research
1. Open World → Trips and create or select a trip.
2. Under **Flight research & comparison**, choose **Search Google Flights
   externally**. It opens a browser. OneSpace does not scrape prices.
3. Save two realistic candidate flights with different dates, currencies,
   stop counts, baggage and personal preference scores.
4. Select both comparison checkboxes. Confirm the comparison displays
   separate alternatives and per-currency ranges, **not a combined fare**.
5. Mark one option Booked. Confirm no ticket is purchased automatically.
6. Use **Add to itinerary** and confirm the copied itinerary includes airline,
   source URL, booking date, currency and baggage notes.
7. Archive one candidate and restore it; private option data remains private.

##### Unified travel preparation
8. Choose **Travel essentials** and **Leaving home**. Verify both appear
   in Remember → To-do and Checklists rather than a new shopping-list module.
9. Generate the same template twice. Confirm it returns the existing
   checklist without duplicate items.
10. Open the generated checklist via the Trip link and verify it is
    expanded. Edit the items to customize to the journey.
11. For a shared trip, verify each partner's completion checks remain
    independent. For a private trip, the other account must not see it.
12. Optional: generate **Traveling with baby** and **Traveling with pets**.
    Only use relevant lists and remove/customize items as needed.

##### Test evidence
- RLS regression 0015: candidate flight privacy and shared collaboration.
- RLS regression 0016: linked travel checklist creation and idempotence.
- No automated booking or Google Flights price API is claimed.
- Offline encrypted document cache and hands-free read-aloud remain
  outstanding and require separate secure implementations.

### A42. OneSpace · `docs/R29_SHARED_EXPERIENCES_REALTIME_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R29 — Shared Experiences live collaboration and scheduling acceptance

**Scope:** Source-only changes after R28 and R27; no new table, secret, migration,
account reset, automatic purchase or additional phone permissions.

##### Built
- Subscribes to realtime changes of shared experiences in the current paired space,
  with 650 ms debounced refresh, stale-response guards, and channel cleanup.
- On the experience detail screen, subscribes to the selected experience, comments
  and partner reviews; refreshes after 550 ms and cleans up on navigation.
- Preserves locally drafted reviews and comments when partner activity arrives.
  A new review is saved only when the author explicitly taps Save.
- Prevents stale edits of experience metadata, one-tap completion, archive, and
  date planning using the `updated_at` version already maintained by the database.
  Conflicts refresh the current record and give actionable feedback.
- "Plan together" now selects a calendar date and immediately saves status
  `planned` and `planned_on`, instead of merely opening an uninitialized editor.
  This is an existing record update (no duplicate event creation).
- Existing manual ExperienceEditor supports status, planned date and completion date,
  and saves dates as local calendar dates rather than converting time zones.
- Experience cards show current progress and relevant dates.
- Quick completion and review support all six categories, with *watched*, *listened*,
  *read*, *played*, and *enjoyed* wording as appropriate.
- Preserves existing R13 server-side new-suggestion/comment notifications, and
  R25 native FCM delivery; no additional background push type was added.

##### Quality gates
- Unit tests for filtered shortlist, per-category labels, date-only formatting,
  and deterministic cross-time-zone suggestions.
- Run committed dependency resolution, `flutter analyze`, and `flutter test`.
- Android signed preview release only after green verification.
- Keep preview package `com.onespace.onespace.preview`, certificate, accounts,
  paired space, app state, and existing Firebase configuration unchanged.
- Do not clear data, uninstall either phone, or expose private objects.

##### Two-phone QA after a verified APK
1. Keep the existing logins and pairing. Leave both phones on Shared Experiences.
2. Create an experience on A. B should see it without pulling to refresh.
3. On B, schedule a date through **Plan**. A should see Planned and the same
   calendar date, even when the devices have different time zones.
4. In Today, confirm the planned experience appears under Coming Up Together
   if its date is in the next seven days, and opens the original experience.
5. Open the same experience on both devices. Type an *unsent* review on A.
   On B, add a comment; A should see the comment without losing its draft.
6. Save a review on A; B should see it. Each partner edits only their own review.
7. Both partners open the editor for the same experience. A saves first;
   B attempts a stale save. B must receive a conflict warning, not overwrite A.
8. Complete a movie/show, book, music title, game, and miscellaneous item.
   Confirm correct wording, completion date, and the rating prompt.
9. Archive on A and restore on B; the change should refresh on both devices.
10. Test deep link from a suggestion/comment notification with a valid older item.
11. Confirm R25 push delivery, To-do/Checklists, Notes, Trips, and Scrapbooks
    still function; verify no new permissions or account changes.

##### Not claimed
- Realtime transport can be interrupted by network or OS restrictions. Pull to
  refresh remains available; permanent offline event queue is not implemented.
- Existing push notices for *new suggestions and comments* are retained.
  Planning changes do not yet generate a distinct scheduled-notification type.
- These steps are not marked phone-tested until both devices are actually checked.

### A43. OneSpace · `docs/R30_PARTNER_PICKS_ACCEPTANCE.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R30 — Partner Picks, mutual experiences and realtime QA

##### Delivered in source
- Each partner can independently set **Interested**, **Maybe**, **Not now**
  or **Clear pick** on a shared experience. Clear pick does not erase partner's choice.
- A **Mutual pick** requires two distinct users each selecting Interested.
- The experience collection shows mutual-pick counts, labels, and an opt-in
  **Show our matches** filter. Archived/completed entries do not count as
  outstanding mutual picks.
- The suggestion card prioritizes jointly selected experiences. Otherwise it
  uses the existing stable daily rotation from the unfinished catalog.
- Realtime subscriptions update partner choices on the collection and detail
  views without overwriting unsent comments and review drafts.
- **No new background push notifications:** changing a preference is a quiet
  relationship decision. Existing FCM gesture, suggestion and comment pushes
  remain untouched.
- The same existing Shared Experiences and Today navigation remains; there is
  no new duplicate module, purchase flow, hidden external content, tracking,
  account switch or migration of old records.

##### Backend changes and evidence
- `supabase/schema/0095_r30_shared_experience_partner_interests.sql`
  deployed additively to the existing OneSpace Supabase project.
- New `shared_experience_interests` relation has primary key
  (experience_id,user_id), four constrained choices, RLS and explicit
  column-level grants. The server fills user_id from the Supabase Auth user.
- Partner sees both choices but can update only own choice; cannot
  forge other member's identity or reassign user_id.
- `supabase/tests/0016_r30_partner_interests_rls.sql`: rollback-only
  regression passed on the existing paired test space.
- Security introspection confirms RLS enabled, three policies,
  no authenticated UPDATE grant on user_id and realtime publication.
- Existing Supabase advisor notices for preexisting privileged RPCs
  and Auth leaked-password protection require separate security review;
  they were not introduced by R30.

##### Two-device acceptance after a signed preview exists
1. Upgrade installed APK in place; do not uninstall or clear storage.
2. Sign in with the existing two different accounts, preserving the pairing.
3. On phone A open one shared experience, select Interested.
   On B verify "Partner: Interested" without manual refresh.
4. On phone B select Maybe, then Interested. Both phones should show
   Mutual pick automatically, not before the second Interested selection.
5. On A choose Not now. Mutual pick must disappear without changing
   B's preference. Clear pick returns only A to Undecided.
6. Create two mutual picks. Filter the collection by Show our matches
   and test category filter, search, archive and restore interactions.
7. Verify the suggested experience prefers mutual picks; when no mutual
   picks remain, it falls back to unfinished suggestions.
8. Open detail on both phones; make a review draft on A.
   Change the preference on B; A's draft must remain untouched.
9. Complete an experience: it must leave the outstanding mutual count.
   Reopen via the history filter; saved preferences should remain.
10. Verify previously working love-gesture Firebase notification on
    both phones, Reminders, To-do/Checklists, Trips, Notes, and Scrapbooks.
11. Verify no preference operation generates an unexpected push notification.
12. Test Android rotation, large font scaling, offline retry and RTL.

##### Release gates
- Full Flutter dependency lock check, analyzer, and unit tests, including
  `experience_interest_rules_test.dart`.
- Signed APK preview with unchanged
  `com.onespace.onespace.preview` application ID, certificate and FCM config.
- Verify GitHub Release asset before distributing; do not mark a source
  change as shipped simply because its test passed.
- Real two-phone acceptance remains necessary even after CI succeeds.

### A44. OneSpace · `docs/R31_DAILY_CONNECTION_CHECKIN_LIFECYCLE_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R31 — Daily Connection: calm request lifecycle and two-phone QA

##### Built
- New partner check-in requests remain open for seven days by default, then
  show **Expired** rather than remaining an indefinite pending obligation.
- Existing old requests keep `expires_at = NULL`, preserving their original
  answerability. No historical record is purged or rewritten.
- The server-owned expiration default cannot be forged from the Android
  client; the existing recipient-only RLS and column grants remain unchanged.
- A server trigger rejects replies to expired requests and still requires
  a rating and only one reply.
- The request timeline distinguishes **To answer**, **Sent**, and **History**,
  with counts, status icons, remaining time, collapsible details and contextual
  reply actions. Answered/expired history remains viewable.
- On-screen expiry labels refresh while the page stays open without polling
  the server or scheduling additional push delivery.
- Expired deep links do not automatically open a reply editor.
- Existing personal Daily Check-in journal, private-sharing controls,
  reply notifications, and Firebase push configuration are unchanged.
- Today → Needs Attention excludes expired, unanswered requests at the
  SQL aggregation layer, even though those same records remain in
  Daily Connection history and original notification history.
- No auto nagging, default resending, or emotionally urgent inference.

##### Database and regression
- Additive migration `supabase/schema/0096_r31_gentle_checkin_expiry.sql`
  deployed to the existing OneSpace Supabase project.
- `supabase/schema/0097_r31_exclude_expired_checkin_attention.sql`
  updates the existing Today attention aggregator without changing its
  privacy model or unrelated activity/task rules.
- Rollback-only test `supabase/tests/0017_r31_checkin_expiry.sql` passed:
  new request seven-day deadline; server rejects a late reply;
  valid reply is accepted with a server timestamp; legacy no-deadline
  request remains answerable; expired request is absent from Today →
  Needs Attention; no test records persist.
- Pure Dart rules and Flutter unit tests for status boundaries,
  personalized filters, legacy compatibility, timeline ordering, and hints.

##### Verify on two Android phones
1. Upgrade over the current preview using the same signed APK identity;
   keep both original accounts and pairing.
2. Phone A sends a gentle request; phone B receives its existing FCM
   notification and sees it in **To answer** automatically.
3. Phone A sees the request in **Sent**, with the deadline label.
4. B responds; A receives the existing response notification. Both
   see **Answered** and the same conversation details in **History**.
5. Try opening an already answered request from Notifications: it must
   not open a second reply editor.
6. Ensure old requests without a deadline are still answerable.
7. An expired test request must show **Expired**, disable Reply, remain
   visible in History, and reject a forged API response.
8. Confirm neither partner receives automatic resend/escalation spam.
9. Daily feeling journal: create private and shared entries; private
   entries remain protected, shared entries notify the partner normally.
10. Check biometric/PIN unlock, long idle/backgrounding, two phone
    time zones, large fonts, and Arabic layout.
11. Confirm Reminders, To-do/Checklists, Shared Experiences, Trips,
    Scrapbooks, and two-way Firebase love gestures remain intact.

##### Gates
- Verify current committed Flutter dependencies, analyze and full test suite.
- Then trigger exactly one signed preview build after verification succeeds.
- Do not call it tested on phones before real acceptance.

### A45. OneSpace · `docs/R32_CHECKIN_ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R32 — Optional Check-in Acknowledgements

##### Product intent
After receiving a response to a gentle check-in, the original sender may
acknowledge it with one quiet supportive choice: Sending love, Thank you for
sharing, or Sending a hug. This does not imply a new request, automatic
follow-up, partner obligation, or push notification. Both participants see
the current choice, but only the original sender can create/change it.

##### Implementation
- Additive table `public.partner_checkin_acknowledgements`, with one row
  per answered request. No rows from R31 are rewritten or deleted.
- SQL migration: `supabase/schema/0098_r32_checkin_acknowledgements.sql`.
- RLS restricts SELECT to both current participants of an *answered* request,
  INSERT/UPDATE to the original request sender, with no UPDATE permission
  for user_id or request_id.
- The database serves choices through one realtime stream on the Daily
  Connection page, instead of a subscription for every history card.
- Senders can select/update their own acknowledgment without touching the
  response or other partner's data. Recipients see the saved acknowledgment
  automatically.
- No notification type, Firebase worker configuration, existing gesture
  scheduler, account settings or Android permissions are modified.

##### Verification
- Rollback-only RLS regression:
  `supabase/tests/0018_r32_checkin_ack_rls.sql` PASS. Assert:
  no acknowledgment before reply; sender-only insert and change; respondent
  can view but not edit; all QA data rolled back.
- Security introspection confirms RLS true, three policies, no authenticated
  user_id UPDATE permission, and Supabase realtime publication enabled.
- Flutter automated tests for exact request matching, labels and sender-only
  eligibility. Analyzer and full Flutter tests required before any APK.

##### Two-phone acceptance (future signed preview)
1. A sends a check-in; B replies.
2. A sees the reply and optional acknowledgment choices.
3. A chooses Sending love; B sees the same acknowledgment without
   refreshing manually; existing push stays unchanged.
4. B cannot change A's acknowledgment. A switches to Thank you for sharing,
   and B sees the updated choice without duplicate data.
5. Neither person can react before a request is answered.
6. Acknowledge a previously answered legacy request without a deadline.
7. Verify no accidental new check-in, duplicate response or surprise push.
8. Confirm R31 pending/answered/expired categories remain consistent on both
   phones and Today omits expired requests.
9. Verify private feelings, shared records, pairing, biometrics, Notes,
   To-do/Checklists, Trips, and Shared Experiences remain unchanged.

##### Release policy
This is **source-only** until analyzer/tests and the next controlled signed
APK succeed. It is NOT included in the separately triggered Release 408.

### A46. OneSpace · `docs/R33_CHECKLIST_CRASH_BLOCKER_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R33 — To-do and Checklists crash blocker

##### Incident
User reports the module still crashes when opened on Android, including
after previously published Release 407. User offered permission to remove
old checklist data *if the old data is the cause*.

##### Live database investigation (2026-11-14)
- One paired space, two members.
- 8 task records: 4 historical standalone records already migrated and
  hidden, 1 **Old checklist** container, 3 newer active containers.
- 21 checklist child rows (4 linked to migrated legacy tasks) and 3 per-user
  completion records.
- No null recurrence/rule/title/creator, no non-object recurrence rules,
  unexpected priority/status values, or empty active checklists.
- Both the four migrated records and the new lists have normal column types.
- No evidence presently implicates old data. **No records deleted, archived,
  reassigned, reset or otherwise mutated in this iteration.**

##### Source hardening
- Task parsing tolerates old optional/string field formats instead of
  using unchecked casts, with conservative visibility fallback.
- List boundary ignores migrated rows and isolates invalid records.
- An invalid task or checklist child will show a recoverable warning
  without taking down the entire list.
- Sign-in/session gaps do not dereference a null currentUser.
- Checklist check records require a matching item/user and boolean
  completion value before being rendered.
- All server-side RLS, existing row IDs, check history, assignment,
  recurrence, per-user completion and archived history remain unchanged.
- Added pure regression tests for migrated legacy records, malformed rows,
  null fields, and valid current checklists.

##### What is not claimed
An Android exception stack trace from the affected phone is not yet
available, so the underlying native/Flutter crash is not proven.
The hardening reduces plausible rendering failure points but must be
validated with a signed installable APK and the *same account/data*
on both Android phones. Do not equate unit test passing with a confirmed
physical-device crash fix.

##### Acceptance after next signed APK
1. Upgrade **in place** over existing APK; do not uninstall or clear data.
2. From Today, tap To-do and Checklists. It must open promptly.
3. Open **Old checklist**, then each of the three newer checklists.
4. Add and remove a subtask; mark complete and incomplete on both phones.
5. Verify each user's per-cycle checkmarks stay independent.
6. Test a recurring checklist and day change without reset of history.
7. Test archive/restore, task status actions, priority/assignment,
   and private versus shared visibility.
8. Verify FCM gestures remain delivered on both phones.
9. If the app still crashes, collect Android Logcat around the precise
   tap for a full stack trace; do not blindly delete all task data.

##### Destructive-data policy
Only if future evidence isolates a specific problematic legacy record:
first export a restorable copy of its task, child and completion records;
then explicitly verify references and remove only the implicated old
record(s) in a transaction. Do not delete newer checklists or unrelated
tasks without cause.

### A47. OneSpace · `docs/R34_CHECKLIST_EXPANSION_CRASH_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R34 — Expanding a checklist still fails in Release 409

##### Report and scope
The user tested a published signed preview and reports **every** checklist
fails on tap to expand, including newly created checklists. R33 guarded
model conversion, but this demonstrates R33 did not solve the UI blocker.

##### Evidence
- Existing paired live space: one Old checklist plus three newer lists.
- Four migrated historical standalone tasks, 21 subitems, three checkmarks.
- Only **one** of the four migrated items contains legacy_target_date; the
  target-date field does **not** participate in TaskChecklistPanel expansion.
- Current visible checklist rows use `recurrence = 'none'` and normal
  recurrence JSON. Supabase RLS read tests for both members passed R33.
- Both checklist data tables are in the Supabase realtime publication.
- Widget tests reproduced the crash: Flutter's ScrollPosition.restoreScrollOffset
  reads an ExpansionTile/PageStorage bool as double? when the nested
  EditableText in the subtask input is created. This is a shared UI-state
  key conflict, independent of database contents.
- A physical-device stack trace is still unavailable; phone acceptance
  remains required.
- Avoid deleting legacy records or clearing application data without proof.

##### Changes under test
- Checklist expansion stream construction is surrounded by exception
  handling, so failure to initialize a realtime subscription cannot
  propagate out of the expansion callback.
- Replaced the checklist ExpansionTile's PageStorageKey with ValueKey
  (its expanded state is already retained in _expandedLists) and set the
  nested TextField scroll controller to keepScrollOffset:false so it never
  tries to read the ExpansionTile's stored bool as a scroll double.
- The widget regression intentionally retains an old PageStorageKey parent
  and verifies nested input rendering does not throw.
- Subscription to checkmarks now uses one parent-task realtime filter,
  and the current recurrence cycle is selected from the stream in memory.
  This reduces cross-filter complexity in widget initialization. Existing
  RLS, per-person filtering and latest 500-row cap remain.
- Add explicit widget tests with the actual collapsed-to-expanded tap
  transition on both new items and old migrated items with a target date.
- Assert a checkmark from another user or another cycle is not displayed.
- Assert network transport errors leave a readable, recoverable view.

##### Important limitations
- Automated tests and RLS checks do not prove physical-device behavior.
  The existing app has `OneSpaceErrorFallback`, which presents a
  **Copy error details** control for Flutter build exceptions. If
  expansion still fails, request its exact copied error text, or capture
  `adb logcat` around the tap if the app force-closes.
- The goal is to diagnose the shared expansion path. Do not delete or
  modify actual checklist rows, and do not call the feature fixed before
  both phones can expand and toggle checkmarks.

##### Two-phone acceptance
1. Upgrade signed preview in place; don't uninstall/clear data.
2. On both phones, open To-do and Checklists.
3. Expand each of the four lists; never see the error fallback or a
   forced closure. Collapse/reopen and navigate away/back.
4. Check Old checklist, including migrated subitem with date metadata.
5. Add subtask, save, and verify remote sync. Check and uncheck;
   other partner must maintain independent cycle state.
6. Verify Check-in, Firebase love gestures, plans, partner picks and
   biometrics remain functional.
7. If any expansion fails, capture precise diagnostic text before any
   further destructive action.

### A48. OneSpace · `docs/R35_OCCASION_PREPARATION_REMINDERS_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R35 — Occasion preparation reminders and cross-module navigation

##### Goal and product contract
Connect an occasion's planning milestones to the existing first-class
Reminders feature. Avoid copying the occasion, keeping parallel countdowns,
sending surprise spoilers, or generating notifications before consent.

##### Feature
- Occasion details show four milestones based on that occasion's date,
  `saving_lead_days`, and `prep_lead_days`:
  Save money, research options, review ordering deadlines, final prep.
- Uses the *next occurrence* of an annual event (with Feb-29 fallback),
  not the historical original date. Date math remains calendar-based.
- Every suggested milestone can create an ordinary OneSpace Reminder with
  an editable local date/time, initially 9:00 AM local when future.
- Past suggested dates are never inserted as overdue notifications; the
  picker visibly suggests the next 9:00 AM and lets the user choose.
- The new reminder starts **Only me**. If and only if the occasion is
  shared, the creator may explicitly choose **Remind both of us**.
  Nothing is sent until the user taps Create reminder.
- Existing reminder engine owns recipients, outcome/follow-up/snooze,
  due alerts, outbox delivery, push pipeline and source authorization.
- One active phase per creator per occasion occurrence; prevents
  double-taps and duplicates while allowing annual re-planning.
- An existing phase appears as **Reminder already saved** and opens
  its existing record. It can be edited/archived from Reminders.
- Reminders' Open related item navigates directly to the original
  Occasion detail, Calendar item, or Checklist instead of a generic tab.

##### Data integrity and privacy
- Additive migration
  `supabase/schema/0099_r35_occasion_preparation_reminders.sql`
  adds optional `planning_phase` and `occasion_instance_on` columns
  to the existing reminders relation. Old reminders remain valid.
- Partial unique index scoped to creator, original occasion, annual
  occurrence and phase, excluding archived records.
- Existing RLS and source visibility validation remain authoritative.
  A private occasion cannot be referenced by a shared reminder.
- Database does **not** auto-create reminders, send surprises, change
  partner settings, or modify stored task/occasion/budget rows.
- Rollback-only RLS integration test 0020 passed on the current paired
  space: duplicate prevented, next-year allowed, partner may independently
  plan, private reminder hidden, and private surprise cannot be revealed.
- No SQL test data persists.

##### Automated tests
`test/occasion_preparation_rules_test.dart`
- Four preparation phases and personalized lead time
- Annual rollover including Feb 29
- Expired one-off occasions produce no reminders
- Future-only suggested alerts
- Per-user/per-year duplicate selection.

##### Phone acceptance after signed APK
1. Upgrade over the existing preview—do not clear app storage.
2. Open Remember → Occasions & surprises → any active occasion.
3. Review each phase's recommendation, including an already-past
   savings recommendation, without creating an overdue notification.
4. Create a private research reminder, select a specific date/time,
   then reopen the occasion to confirm a saved reminder link.
5. Open it; verify Reminders opens and the original Occasion is linked.
6. If the occasion is shared, explicitly choose Both for another
   reminder. Confirm both partners see it and each retains independent
   completion/snooze outcomes.
7. Confirm a private occasion does not offer a Both option.
8. Test duplicate tap, edit, archive, new annual occurrence and
   timezone differences between the phones.
9. Verify the existing gift comparison, saved proposals, budget,
   Activity links, and shopping statuses remain unchanged.
10. Recheck To-do and Checklists expansion after R34, plus Firebase
    love gestures on both phones.

##### Release policy
This is **source-only** until Flutter analyze/tests and signed preview
build succeed. Do not claim physical two-device acceptance until tested.

### A49. OneSpace · `docs/R36_THOUGHTFUL_LOVE_SELF_REMINDERS_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R36 — A Little Love: personal thoughtful reminders

##### Product goal
Help people remember to show love at meaningful moments without
automatically impersonating them or notifying their partner before
the person has chosen the gesture.

##### Built
- A Little Love now offers **Remind me to send a little love**.
- The sender selects a gesture category (or any thoughtful gesture),
  morning/after-work/evening suggestion, or a fully custom date/time.
- Suggestions use the *current user's phone local calendar/time*, not
  the partner's timezone. No reminder is created until Save.
- The screen shows when the user last intentionally sent a gesture.
  Only the sender's own sent history contributes.
- The new record uses existing `reminders` and server-side reminder
  delivery; visibility is always **private**, audience always **me**.
- Reminders now display **Open A little love**, opening the gesture
  screen without automatically sending anything. The user still selects
  and manually sends a gesture.
- Existing personalized gesture message pools and random selection
  remain untouched. Existing automatic-ritual settings also remain
  untouched; this is a new opt-in alternative, not a silent migration.

##### Security / integrity
- Migration `supabase/schema/0100_r36_personal_love_gesture_reminders.sql`
  adds two nullable intent columns and check constraint to existing
  reminders table. Existing rows remain untouched.
- Database enforces private/me scope, no source object reference,
  and unique active prompts of the same kind at exactly the same instant.
- No new tables, no new FCM credentials, no extra notification channel,
  no ingestion of private mood or private daily check-ins.
- Rollback-only RLS test
  `supabase/tests/0021_r36_personal_love_prompt_rls.sql` passed:
  another partner cannot view the prompt; accidental shared reminder
  is rejected; exact duplicate is rejected; no test rows persisted.

##### Two-phone acceptance after verified APK
1. Keep existing two accounts, pairing and FCM registration.
2. On phone A open A little Love, tap Remind me to send a little love.
3. Select a category, the After work suggestion, then a custom future
   date/time. Save without actually sending a gesture.
4. The partner on phone B must see **no gesture** and **no reminder**.
5. At its due time, phone A receives a normal personal reminder.
6. Tap Open A little love; A selects a gesture and sends it manually.
   B then receives the existing gesture push.
7. A's last-sent indicator updates; B's own sender history remains
   independent of A's.
8. Existing one-tap gestures, personalized pools, manual history,
   check-ins, calendars and checklists still work.
9. Test exact duplicate, offline failure, large text and biometric unlock.

##### Release gate
Flutter analyze and full test suite, signed APK certificate/package/
Firebase checks, and subsequent real phone validation required.
Source commits are not treated as shipped until the release is published.

### A50. OneSpace · `docs/R37_VOICE_CHECKLIST_READOUT_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R37 — Listen to checklists (voice readout)

##### Purpose
Make a daily or recurring checklist useful while someone is getting ready,
carrying a baby, or has their hands occupied. This is a *read-only*
accessibility workflow, not a microphone-driven assistant. Users still
check off items themselves, as agreed for independent partner completion.

##### Scope committed
- An accessible **Listen to checklist** control inside expanded checklists.
- Listen to remaining unchecked items (default) or all active subitems.
- Read all selected subitems in list order, one step, or navigate
  previous/next and replay an individual step.
- Change speech speed (0.75× / 1.0× / 1.25×). Spoken language follows
  the current app locale if supported by the device engine, otherwise
  the phone speech default.
- Speech uses the Android TextToSpeech service or iOS
  AVSpeechSynthesizer over the same first-party MethodChannel.
  No dependency/download, no extra permission, no cloud function.
- Text is read only on explicit user action. The phone's installed
  voice engine *may* use online synthesis depending on its settings.
- Playback stops when the app is backgrounded/locked, the sheet closes,
  or the user taps Stop. Microphone access is never requested here.
- The feature does not automatically mark any task complete, resend
  notifications, change settings, or add data to the backend.
- Existing R34 ExpansionTile PageStorage collision fix stays intact.

##### Safety and privacy
- Uses only the RLS-filtered subitem rows already loaded for the
  expanded checklist, and only current-user/current-cycle completion
  state. Does not read any partner's private data.
- Archived/malformed items excluded, scripts sanitized of control
  characters, names capped at 240 characters and readouts capped at
  200 lines. Android splits long utterances to stay below the TTS
  engine's per-utterance limit.
- Native speech stops on Android onPause/onDestroy and iOS resign-active.
- No server migration is required.

##### Tests
- Pure Dart script tests for item order, old invalid rows, completed
  filtering, empty scripts and item caps.
- Flutter widget tests mock only the platform bridge and verify
  remaining-vs-all selection, native invocation, step navigation,
  and stopping when the sheet unmounts.
- Full Flutter analyze/test required before signed APK. Android
  release pipeline must compile Kotlin too. iOS native code requires
  a subsequent macOS/iOS compile and real-device verification.

##### Two-phone acceptance
1. Install next signed APK over the last working preview, not fresh.
2. Open To-do and Checklists on both devices and expand the old
   and a newer checklist—verify the R34 crash stays resolved.
3. Tap the speaker icon and choose **Still to do**. Completed items
   on this account must not be read; the other account's own
   progress is independent.
4. Read all, read one, switch between steps, adjust speed and Stop.
5. Try a shared checklist, private checklist and recurring instance.
6. Background/lock the phone while reading: speech must stop.
7. Test with airplane mode using an installed offline voice
   and test a phone lacking a compatible engine (recoverable error).
8. Check no item is marked complete by speech and nothing is
   sent to the other partner or Firebase.
9. Recheck normal checklist editing and calendar/gesture pushes.

##### Out of scope
- Hotword activation, always-on microphone, automatic voice commands,
  wake-word assistant, hands-free checkmark changes, and continuous
  playback while the app is locked. Those need a separate product
  and security review.

### A51. OneSpace · `docs/R38_NOTES_LINKED_REFERENCES_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R38 — Notes 2.0 linked source objects and privacy

##### Outcome
A saved rich-text note can reference a real OneSpace object without
duplicating its data or changing the underlying object:
- To-do and Checklists
- Calendar event
- Occasion or surprise
- Shared Experience

The References section in the Quill editor supports selecting an
existing accessible item, displaying its latest live title, opening
its original screen, and unlinking it without deleting the original.

##### User workflow
1. Remember > Notes > open a saved note. New notes must be saved first.
2. Select **Link item**, choose object type, select an accessible item.
3. The link appears below the note. Tap its title to open the actual object.
4. Unlink removes only the reference, never the task/event/occasion/experience.
5. Archived/read-only notes keep their references visible but non-editable.

##### Data and authorization
- Migration 0101 introduces `public.note_references` and
  `private.note_reference_visible`, which verifies note and target
  membership, matching space, and both objects' RLS.
- Migration 0102 prevents a view-only private-note recipient from
  changing its references.
- No link title or private body copied into the reference table.
- A shared note can link only a shared task/event/occasion, never a
  private surprise. A private note may link an authorized private object.
- Both users see references in shared notes, but an inaccessible
  target is not resolved or displayed.
- Live source titles are fetched only through each original table's
  RLS. The reader gets no cached private title when access is revoked.
- Duplicate links are rejected by a unique constraint.
- Existing notes, rich-text deltas, attachments, archived states,
  two-phone pairing, Firebase configuration and checklist data untouched.
- The new table uses SELECT/INSERT/DELETE only, with RLS; it grants
  no UPDATE and no bulk modification of source objects.

##### Verification
- Rollback-only SQL test 0022 passed after migrations 0101 and 0102:
  two accounts, private note hidden, shared note reference visible,
  private surprise cannot enter a shared note, invalid target rejected,
  duplicate rejected, and unlink preserves the source.
- Flutter note_reference_rules unit tests cover allowed object kinds,
  private candidate filtration, archived content and inaccessible targets.
- Full Flutter analyzer, test suite, and Android signed build are
  required before APK release; a native installed test is still needed.

##### Two-device acceptance
1. Upgrade APK in place; do not clear app data.
2. Create one shared rich-text note. Save and reopen.
3. Link a shared checklist, calendar event, occasion, and experience.
4. The other phone sees links and opens the *same* source records.
5. Change a linked object's title; reopen the note and confirm latest title.
6. In a private note, link a private occasion; confirm the other partner
   cannot see the note or reference.
7. In a shared note, the picker must not offer a private surprise.
8. Unlink a checklist; original checklist and all subtasks must remain.
9. Test duplicate linking, archived note read-only mode, revocation
   and opening a source the user has lost access to.
10. Reconfirm R34 checklist expansion and R37 voice controls,
    R35 occasion reminders and two-way Firebase gestures still work.

##### Limitations / next slice
- Backlinks on the source object ("Related notes") and links to
  travel documents will be separate R39 work after the basic
  authorized references pass both-device testing.
- Links are not automatically extracted from note text. The user
  chooses the source explicitly, preserving intention and privacy.

##### Additional R38 notes library UX
- Search Notes by title or plain text while preserving shared/private filters, pin order, and archive visibility.
- Unit tests `notes_library_rules_test.dart` cover case-insensitive search, archive filtering and malformed timestamp resilience.
- Rollback-only SQL test 0023 separately verifies a real private occasion remains inaccessible to shared notes and to the other partner.

### A52. OneSpace · `docs/R39_RELATED_NOTES_BACKLINKS_QA.md`

*First written 14 Nov 2026 · last changed 14 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R39 — Related Notes from original OneSpace records

##### Purpose
Complete the two-way workflow started in R38: a rich text note can link
a checklist, event, occasion or shared experience; now the original
item can also show the accessible notes linked to it.

##### Delivered source
- In expanded To-do and Checklists, a **Related notes** panel, collapsed
  by default, below the checklist. This preserves the R34 repaired
  ExpansionTile/scroll key handling and adds no query until expanded.
- Occasion and Surprise details show a collapsed Related notes panel.
- Shared Experience details show a collapsed Related notes panel.
- Calendar event's action menu offers **Related notes**, which opens an
  on-demand sheet.
- Related note titles are resolved in the original `notes` table under
  the current account's RLS, never stored or copied to the backlink.
- Open a related note through NotesScreen(initialNoteId), which launches
  the existing rich-text editor with the correct read-only permissions.
- Open Notes from the panel to add a link through R38's existing
  Note References picker, then refresh the panel.
- Archived notes are labeled Archived, not silently deleted.
- Read-only panels never modify source object or note data.

##### Privacy and performance
- No extra schema or FCM notifications. The R38 `note_references`
  SELECT RLS enforces that both the note and original target are
  visible; second `notes` query independently applies notes RLS.
- A private surprise note stays invisible to the other partner even
  when its target is otherwise shared.
- No data/permission is cached across sign-ins; each on-demand query
  reads only current authorized rows.
- Lazy loading avoids four extra queries per checklist until user
  explicitly opens the Related notes section.

##### Tests
- R38 SQL rollback tests 0022/0023 passed and still apply.
- New `related_notes_rules_test.dart` covers authorized IDs, pin
  order and missing/inaccessible references.
- Full Flutter source analyzer + automated test suite required.
- Android signed APK and two-phone acceptance will follow only
  after R38 preview source/CI gates are passed.

##### Phone acceptance (R39 release)
1. Link a shared checklist to a shared note from the R38 Notes editor.
2. Return to the checklist, expand Related notes, open the exact note.
3. Verify the other partner sees the same shared note.
4. Link a private note to a private occasion. Verify only its owner
   sees the Related notes entry.
5. Open a shared experience and a calendar event; access linked notes
   through their respective detail/action UI.
6. Change note title. Refresh the source panel to see its new title.
7. Unlink in the Notes editor; original source object remains intact,
   and its backlink disappears after refresh.
8. Check R34 checklist expansion, R37 voice readout and FCM gestures.
