### A61. OneSpace · `docs/R46_BASELINE_HYGIENE_QA.md`

*First written 15 Nov 2026 · last changed 15 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R46 — Baseline hygiene (decided items only)

Branch: `r46-baseline-hygiene` · Date: 15 Nov 2026 · Decisions recorded in the OneSpace claude.ai Project ("OneSpace_Working_Baseline").

##### What changed

| # | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Migration history repaired: schema files 0095–0105 (R30–R44) were live but missing from Supabase's migration log. They are now registered (pointing to their source files). No SQL was re-run. | Supabase `supabase_migrations` |
| 2 | **Automatic love gestures retired** (decision #1). The 5-minute sender job is unscheduled; schedules and consent are switched off; personal gestures no longer carry an automatic trigger; app users can no longer create schedules. Past automated gestures stay in history. | `0107_r46_retire_automatic_gestures.sql`, Settings, Profile, A little love |
| 3 | **Shared Goals removed from the app** (decision #2): Connect hub link, Today shortcut, global search section and screen removed. The `goals` table and its single record are kept untouched (no data deleted). | `lib/` |
| 4 | Tagline set to the approved brand line **"As a couple, we have One Space"** (startup screen, Today; English in both languages as previously approved). | `main.dart`, `onespace_strings.dart` |
| 5 | Stale copy removed: Connect no longer says daily selfies are "planned"; World no longer promises "more of our wider world". | `section_hubs.dart` |
| 6 | Developer performance panel moved out of the Profile list into a small "Troubleshooting" link under Sign out. | `profile_settings_screen.dart` |
| 7 | Meeting counter: a "last time we met" date of today shows **Today** (yesterday shows **Yesterday**) instead of "Coming up". | `shared_space_screen.dart` |

Already compliant, no change needed: Activity chat bubbles show initials only (decision #7).

##### Deliberately NOT in R46
Everything that needs a design decision (Today redesign, filters/history icons, creation flow, share-in Inbox, relationship model) waits for the UX Redesign Specification. The dead "urgent" branch for to-dos in Needs attention will be removed when Needs attention is rebuilt in R47.

##### Two-phone test script (after installing the R46 APK)

1. Open **Settings → Affection & notifications**. Expect only **My love gestures**; no "Thoughtful little rituals".
2. Open **Profile → Schedule & days off**. Expect working days, work hours, bedtime and wake-up; **no** "Allow scheduled gestures" switch. Change a time, save, reopen: the value is kept.
3. **A little love → Settings → Create my own gesture.** Expect name, message and theme only (no "automatic trigger"). Save it; send that type to your partner; the partner receives it.
4. Edit an existing personal gesture; it saves without error.
5. **Connect** tab: no "Shared goals" entry, and no "Daily selfies… are planned" sentence.
6. **World** tab: text reads "People & contacts live in Remember."
7. **⋮ → Search our space:** search for any word; there is no "Goal" filter or result type; other results still open correctly.
8. **Today:** tagline reads "As a couple, we have One Space". In Settings → Our relationship, set *Last time we met* to today → Today shows **Today**; set it to yesterday → **Yesterday**; set 10 days ago → **10 days ago**. Clear it afterwards if you wish.
9. Restart the app (cold start): the splash shows "As a couple, we have One Space".
10. **Profile:** no "App performance" row in the list; a small **Troubleshooting** link appears under Sign out and opens the timings.
11. Wait past a morning/after-work/bedtime moment: **no automatic gesture arrives** for either partner.

### A62. OneSpace · `docs/R47_REMINDERS_FOR_REAL_LIFE_QA.md`

*First written 15 Nov 2026 · last changed 15 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R47 — Reminders for real life

Branch: `r47-reminders-for-real-life` · Date: 15 Nov 2026 · Decisions: D5, D10, D14 in `DECISION_LOG.md` · Spec backlog B-01, B-02, B-03, B-11 (reminders part).

##### What changed

| # | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Repeating reminders**: Every day · Every weekday · Every week on chosen days · Every month · Every year, "every N", up to 6 times a day, optional end date. Times are kept in the phone's time zone, so daylight-saving changes never move them. The 31st falls back to the month's last day; 29 Feb to 28 Feb. | `0108`, reminder editor |
| 2 | **Done for this time**: on a repeating reminder, Done/Follow-up/Snooze apply to the current occurrence only; the schedule continues. Done up to 12 hours early skips that occurrence. | `respond_to_reminder` |
| 3 | **Missed**: if an occurrence is still unanswered when the next one fires, it is recorded as *Missed* in the reminder history. | dispatcher |
| 4 | **Quiet hours**: a reminder your partner set for you waits until your wake-up time (Profile → Schedule, days off respected) and is recorded as *Held until wake-up*. Your own reminders always fire on time. | dispatcher |
| 5 | **Urgent** (partner reminders only): overrides quiet hours; the alert reads "Urgent: …". | editor, dispatcher |
| 6 | **Linked item closed**: if the linked checklist is completed/cancelled, or the event/occasion is removed or finished, the reminder stops and shows "Linked item closed · no more alerts". | dispatcher |
| 7 | **Partner's local time**: when a reminder is for your partner in another time zone, the editor shows "That's 7:00 AM for Kandice". | editor |
| 8 | **Today**: reminders due for you appear in *Needs attention* (hidden while snoozed) and the next occurrence appears in *Coming up together*; tapping opens the reminder. | `onespace_my_attention`, `onespace_coming_up`, Today |
| 9 | Editor header reads **Add new reminder** / **Edit reminder** (D15). | editor |
| 10 | Removed a dead rule in Needs attention ("urgent" to-dos, which cannot exist). | `onespace_my_attention` |

Database: applied live as migrations `r47_reminders_for_real_life`, `r47_reminder_minute_precision`, `r47_hide_snoozed_reminders_in_attention` (all in `0108`). Additive: existing one-time reminders behave exactly as before. Verified on the live database inside rolled-back transactions: twice-daily schedule, weekdays, every 2 weeks, 31st→28 Feb, 29 Feb→28 Feb, New York daylight-saving, end date, quiet-hours deferral to 07:00, Missed record, Urgent at night, linked checklist cancelled, early Done, snooze hiding from Today.

##### Two-phone test script

Before you start: in **Profile → Schedule & days off**, set bedtime and wake-up on **Kandice's** phone (e.g. 23:00 → 07:00). Quiet hours only work when a sleep window is set.

1. **Ahmed:** Remember → Reminders → **+**. The header reads "Add new reminder". Title "Test vitamins", start today in 5 minutes, Repeat = **Every day**, **Add another time** = 5 minutes after the first. Save. The card shows "Every day at … and …".
2. Wait for the first time: the alert arrives; the card shows **Due now**; Today → Needs attention lists it.
3. Tap **Done for this time** → the card shows "Next …" (the second time today). Needs attention no longer lists it.
4. Do not answer the second alert. Tomorrow, after the next alert, open the reminder → Outcome history shows **Missed** for the unanswered time.
5. **Ahmed:** create a reminder for **Partner**, Shared, at a time inside Kandice's sleep window (e.g. 02:00 her time). If her phone is in another time zone, the editor shows "That's … for Kandice". **Do not** tick Urgent. → Kandice gets nothing at 02:00; she gets it at her wake-up time; history shows **Held until wake-up**.
6. Repeat step 5 with **Urgent** on → Kandice is alerted at 02:00 with "Urgent: …".
7. Create a checklist "Test list", then a reminder linked to it (Related item) for 10 minutes later. Cancel the checklist. → No alert arrives; the reminder shows "Linked item closed · no more alerts".
8. Create a weekly reminder: Repeat = Every week on…, tick Mon and Thu, Every **2** weeks, Ends = On a date. The card shows "Every 2 weeks on Mon, Thu at … · until …".
9. Snooze a due reminder by 2 hours → it disappears from Needs attention and comes back when the snooze ends.
10. Today → Coming up together lists your upcoming reminders as "Reminder" / "Repeating reminder"; tapping opens the reminder with its card expanded.
11. Edit an existing one-time reminder created before this update (title only) → it saves and still alerts at its original time.

Clean up afterwards: Archive the test reminders (they stay in history).

### A63. OneSpace · `docs/R48_NOTIFICATIONS_QA.md`

*First written 15 Nov 2026 · last changed 15 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R48 Notifications 2.0: what changed and how to test it

Decisions: D5 (quiet hours), D6 (content push in the recipient's language), D20 (chat batching), D21 (brand line).
Spec: UX Redesign Specification v1, sections 5.1–5.4 and backlog B-04, B-05, B-06.

##### What changed

| # | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Every push says who did what and shows the content, for example "Kay sent you a hug" / "Thinking of you at work today". The name is what you call your partner in Profile (partner label) or their first name. The text follows the recipient's app language (English and Arabic phrases). | `0109_r48_notifications_2.sql` (`compose_notification_text`), Edge Function `onespace-push-delivery` v5 |
| 2 | Plain Activity chat messages now notify (before, only @mentions and replies did). Trip messages and experience comments follow the same rule: **at most one push per item per 10 minutes**; messages in between join the next push as "+N more". The notification list keeps one row per item with the latest message. | `notify_activity_comment`, `notify_trip_message`, `experience_comment_notice`, `post_notice` |
| 3 | Meaningful changes by your partner on shared items push: new to-do, new Activity, new memory, a reminder set for you; date/time moved ("Now Mon 16 Nov, 4:00 PM"); status changed (completed, cancelled, decided, bought, booked, celebrated…); a to-do assigned to you; removed or restored (memories included). Wording, notes and tag edits stay silent. Several quick changes to the same item within 2 minutes share one push. Only-me items never notify. | `notice_item_change` trigger on events, to-dos, Activities, occasions, trips, experiences, memories, reminders |
| 4 | Quiet hours: pushes caused by your partner wait while you sleep (Profile → Schedule: bedtime and wake-up, adjusted on days off) and arrive at wake-up as **one** push: "While you were asleep · 4 updates · latest: …". Your own reminders, and reminders your partner marked Urgent, still ring on time (R47 rule). | `queue_notification_push`, `claim_push_outbox` |
| 5 | If you already opened a notification in the app, its push is not sent. | `claim_push_outbox` |
| 6 | Tapping a push opens the exact item: the Activity scrolled to the conversation, the reminder with its Done / Snooze buttons, the gesture pop-up, the event, to-do, trip, occasion, experience or memory. A wake-up summary opens the notification list. A newer push about the same item replaces the older one in the phone's tray. | `push_registration_gate.dart`, `notifications_screen.dart` (`openNotificationTarget`, `openNotificationById`), `activity_detail_screen.dart` |
| 7 | Notification list rows show the same title and text as the push, plus how long ago ("5 min ago", "Yesterday"). The filter icon is the slider icon (D11). | `notifications_screen.dart` |
| 8 | Brand line shortened to **"Two of us, One Space"** and moved under the OneSpace name in the top bar (also on the splash and sign-in screens); removed from the Today page. | `main.dart`, `onespace_strings.dart`, `shared_space_screen.dart` |

Database changes are non-destructive: new columns on `space_notifications` (`action`, `detail`, `push_title`, `push_body`, `push_due_at`, `batch_count`, `memory_id`) and `private.push_outbox` (`quiet_deferred`, `fold_count`), widened kind checks, replaced functions, new triggers. Existing notifications were given push text without sending anything.

Known limits (planned for later iterations):
- Opening an item your partner has since removed shows the screen's normal view; the "This was removed by Kandice · Restore" banner comes with the status model in R50.
- Arabic push phrases are written in the masculine form; full Arabic review is on the later list.
- "Since you were away" on Today (R49) will show the silent minor edits.

##### Test script (two phones, both on this build)

Call the two phones **A** (Ahmed) and **K** (Kandice). Make sure both allowed notifications when the app asked.

1. **Top bar:** on any main tab, the top bar reads "OneSpace" with "Two of us, One Space" underneath. The Today page no longer shows the line in its body.
2. **Gesture push:** on A, send K a hug with a message. K's phone shows "Ahmed sent you a hug" with the message. Tap it: the gesture pop-up opens.
3. **Chat message:** on A, open a shared Activity and write a plain message (no @). Within about a minute K gets "Ahmed · <Activity title>" with the message text. Tap it: the Activity opens scrolled to the conversation.
4. **Batching:** right after step 3, A sends three more messages. K gets **no** new push straight away. About 10 minutes after the first push, K gets one push showing the latest message and "(+2 more)". The tray shows one entry for that Activity, not four.
5. **Read before push:** A sends another message; K opens the notification list in the app before the next push is due. No push arrives for it.
6. **Moved event:** A moves a shared event to another time. K gets "Ahmed moved <event>" / "Now <new day and time>" in K's own time zone. Tap: the event opens.
7. **Minor edit:** A changes only the event's title or notes. No push.
8. **To-do assigned:** A creates a shared to-do assigned to K. K gets "Ahmed assigned you <to-do>". Then A marks it done: K gets "Ahmed completed <to-do>".
9. **Memory removed and restored:** A removes a shared memory: K gets "Ahmed removed <memory>"; A restores it: K gets "Ahmed restored <memory>".
10. **Reminder for you:** K sets a shared reminder for A. A gets "Kay added a reminder for you" / "<title> · <time>". At the due time A gets "Reminder from Kay"; tap opens the reminder with Done / Snooze.
11. **Quiet hours:** on K, set Profile → Schedule bedtime to a time 5 minutes ago and wake-up 15 minutes from now. A sends K a hug and moves an event. K's phone stays silent. At the wake-up time K gets one push "While you were asleep" / "2 updates · latest: …"; tapping opens the notification list. Clear or restore K's real schedule afterwards.
12. **Only-me stays private:** A creates an Only-me to-do. K gets nothing.

Verified on the live database before release (inside transactions that were rolled back, so no data changed): chat batching and "+1 more", activity decided, to-do completed, silent title edit, quiet-hours hold to wake-up, wake-up summary folding 4 updates into one, read-before-push skip, gesture wording, moved-event wording in the recipient's time zone, memory added/removed, reminder for partner, private to-do silent.

##### R49 — Today 2.0 (spec 3, B-07…B-12)

It runs with your own access rules, so nothing private to your partner can leak. (B-08; "Today one call network" in Troubleshooting timings) | `0110_r49_today_2.sql`, `shared_space_screen.dart` |

| 5 | **Travelling:** when a shared trip is happening today: "Travelling · Paris · back in 4 days". | |

| 8 | **Needs your attention** and **Today and the next 7 days** are always-visible sections with kind empty hints ("Nothing is waiting on you right now.", "A free day…" + Add). The agenda shows Today first, then the next days grouped by day. | `today_attention_section.dart` |
| 9 | **Our space shortcuts:** every feature as a tile in a 3-column grid grouped Remember · Coordinate · Connect · World · Tools, with count badges only where action is needed (Reminders due, unread Letters, Items to sort). Recent discussions, the old countdown card and the 9-tile grid are gone (countdown now sits in the hero). | |

| # | What changed | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Filter panel** on every collection screen (Reminders, Notes, Memories, Occasions, Trips, People, Shared experiences, Activities, Items to sort): slider icon with a dot while filters are on; a panel drops down under the icon (not a bottom sheet), max 70% of the screen; **Status → Removed** is now a filter; the screen's own groups follow; **Clear** and **Apply** pinned at the bottom. Filters reset when you leave the screen. | `record_list_top_actions.dart` |

Rows for removed items have **Restore**. An item's own history button opens the same page filtered to that item. Private items only appear in their owner's log. | `collection_history_screen.dart` |

| 5 | **Editor headers** read "Add new …" / "Edit …" (trip, occasion, preparation step, itinerary item, flight option, experience, gesture, day off, contact, note, event, memory). | |

| # | What changed | Where |

Nothing is saved until there is a title; leaving creates nothing. | `new_activity_screen.dart` + tests |

##### R52 — Share-in 2.0 and "Items to sort" (spec 4, B-16)

| # | What changed | Where |

| 1 | **Save to OneSpace** (when you share a link or text into the app): **Save to Items to sort** is always the first, one-tap choice (private to you). Below it, **up to 3 destinations suggested from what was shared** (shop link → Gift idea · Option in an Activity · To-do; video/music/book → Shared experience; travel site → Trip; maps → Activity/Trip/Event; unknown site → New Activity/Note; text with a date → Reminder/To-do/Event; other text → Note/To-do), then **More…** with every destination. Gift ideas go straight into an occasion's plan; reminders, events and trips open their page pre-filled. | `share_intake_screen.dart`, `share_suggestions.dart` + tests |
| 2 | **Items to sort** tray: private by default, can be shared; each item has **Sort**, **Remove/Restore**, **Change log**; items older than 30 days get a gentle "still want this?"; nothing is removed automatically. | `items_to_sort_screen.dart`, table `items_to_sort` |
| 3 | Placement: a **shortcut tile with a count**, a **"3 items to sort"** row in Needs your attention, and an entry in the top-bar **⋮ menu**. No permanent top-bar icon. | |

Database changes (all applied live, non-destructive, tested in rolled-back transactions):
`0110 r49_today_2` (onespace_today, set_how_we_live), `0111 r50_history_outcomes` (+ `r50_history_allow_completed`), `0112 r52_items_to_sort` (table with row-level security, history, badge).

##### Not done yet (honest list)

- Share-in still accepts **links and text** only (plus contact cards). Photos and PDFs from other apps need Android work and are on the later list.
- The per-item **⋮ menu** (final status first, then Edit, Remove, Change log) and the **status look** in lists (strikethrough/green check/red cross/amber Missed) are not applied to every screen yet; history and Remove wording are.
- The "Showing: … ✕" line exists as a component but is only wired on Items to sort.
- To-do creation still uses its bottom sheet (no dialog); events, notes, reminders, trips, memories already used full pages.
- Activity purpose starters fill an outline in the details; they do not yet pre-add option/checklist sections.
- Activities have their own older audit trail, so the Activities screen has no clock icon yet.
- Seasonal theme preview stays on Today; "On this day" memories are not added yet.

##### Test script (two phones on this build)

1. **Today opens instantly:** close and reopen the app. Today appears straight away from the saved copy, then refreshes. Turn on flight mode and reopen: you still see Today with "Last updated … · Retry".
2. **Greeting:** "Good morning/afternoon/evening, <name>" and "Your space with your …".
3. **How we live:** tap the house/plane icon on the hero, choose the other option. It saves; **Undo** appears for 10 seconds; your partner gets a push. Switching Apart → Together asks to save "Our journey apart"; check Memories. Switch back with Undo.
4. **Good time to talk:** (only shown when your time zones differ) set work and sleep hours in Profile for both of you; the hero shows the next shared free window.
5. **Since you were away:** on K, send A a hug and add a to-do. On A, open Today: both appear under "Since you were away"; tap one, it opens. Reopen Today: the section folds to one line.
6. **Shortcuts:** every feature has a tile; Letters/Reminders/Items to sort show a count only when something waits.
7. **Quick add:** tap "+ Quick add" → To-do opens the to-do editor; Something to research opens the new Activity page.
8. **New Activity:** in Coordinate tap +, pick "Buy something" (outline appears), type a title, Create. Then tap + again and go back without a title: nothing is created.
9. **Filter panel:** in Notes, tap the slider icon: the panel drops down under it; tick **Removed**, Apply: removed notes show; Clear resets.
10. **Change log:** in Notes tap the clock: see who did what; tap "Me", then "Removed"; Restore a removed note from its row.
11. **Wording:** item menus say Remove/Restore, not Archive; editors say "Add new …"/"Edit …".
12. **Share-in:** from Chrome share an Amazon product to OneSpace: "Save to Items to sort" first; suggestions include Gift idea and Option in an Activity. Tap Save to Items to sort → the tray opens. On Today the tile and "1 item to sort" appear. Tap Sort → Gift idea → choose an occasion → it lands in that occasion's plan and leaves the tray.
13. **Privacy:** the tray item is invisible on the partner's phone until you choose "Share with my partner" in its ⋮ menu.

### A65. OneSpace · `docs/R53_R54_QA.md`

*First written 15 Nov 2026 · last changed 15 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R53–R54: Links between items, and polish

Spec: UX Redesign Specification v1, section 6 (links), 2.3/2.6 (filters, statuses), 3.5 (On this day); backlog B-17.
Decisions: D10 (links), D29–D31 in `docs/DECISION_LOG.md`.

##### R53 — Links between items

| # | What changed | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Create from this** (link-plus icon on Activity, Trip, Occasion and Experience pages; "Create from this" in a calendar event's menu): Create reminder · Add to calendar · Make a to-do · Add a note, plus type-specific actions: Trip → **Leave-for-airport reminder** (trip day 6:00 AM) and **Online check-in reminder** (day before, 9:00 AM); Activity → **Turn into trip**; Occasion → **Preparation reminder** (a week before, 9:00 AM); Experience → **Plan a date**. The new item is pre-filled (title, date, shared like its source) and **linked automatically**. | `lib/core/linked_items.dart` |
| 2 | **Linked items strip** under the title of Activities, Trips, Occasions and Experiences, inside each reminder, and as "Linked items" in a calendar event's menu. Tap a chip to open the item; **long-press to Unlink** (the item itself stays). A reminder's built-in source (to-do, event, occasion) shows here too, both ways. | |
| 3 | **Privacy:** a link to something the viewer cannot see (the partner's Only-me item) is never shown, not even as "hidden item". Tested on the live database. | table `item_links` with row-level security, `item_linked()` |
| 4 | **Trip end → last time we met:** when you live apart, marking a shared trip **Completed** updates "Last time we met" to the trip's end date (never moves it backwards). | trigger `r53_trip_end_last_met` |
| 5 | Reminder and calendar change logs now open the shared change log page (R50) instead of the old pop-up. | |

##### R54 — Polish

| # | What changed |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Activities have a change log** like every other list (clock icon on Coordinate): Created, Edited, Completed (completed/bought/booked), Cancelled, Removed, Restored. |
| 2 | **On this day** on Today: at most one past memory from today's date at the top of "Today and the next 7 days" ("On this day · 2 years ago"); tap opens it; ✕ hides that memory from Today on this phone. |
| 3 | **"Showing: Removed ✕"** line under the title on Reminders, Notes, Memories, Occasions, Trips, People, Shared experiences and Items to sort while the Removed filter is on; ✕ clears it. |
| 4 | **Search** now finds **People (contacts)** and **Items to sort**; its filter uses the slider icon with a dot. |
| 5 | **Status look** (spec 2.6) in the To-do, Activities, Occasions and Trips lists: completed/bought/booked/celebrated rows are struck through and muted with a green check; cancelled rows with a red cross. |

Database changes (applied live, non-destructive, tested in rolled-back transactions):
`0113 r53_links` (+ `r53_trip_end_updates_last_met`), `0114 r54_polish_activity_history_on_this_day`.

##### Still open after this round
- The single ⋮ menu per item (final status first, then Edit, Remove, Change log) is not unified on every screen yet.
- Photos and PDFs shared from other apps; to-do as a full page; Activity purpose starters that pre-add sections; Arabic for the newer screens; iOS.

##### Test script (two phones)
1. Open a shared **Trip** → tap the link-plus icon → **Leave-for-airport reminder**: the reminder page opens with "Leave for the airport · <trip>" at 6:00 AM on the trip day, shared with both. Save. Back on the trip, the **Linked** strip shows the reminder. Open the reminder: its strip shows the trip (B-17).
2. On the trip, **Create from this → Add a note** (it opens linked); **Make a to-do** (added and linked).
3. Long-press a chip in the strip → **Unlink** → the chip disappears; the item still exists.
4. On A, create an **Only-me** note from a shared trip. On K, open the trip: the note link does **not** appear.
5. In Calendar, open an event's menu → **Create from this → Create reminder**; then **Linked items** shows it.
6. With How we live = Apart, mark a shared trip **Completed**: Today's "Last time we met" becomes the trip's end date.
7. In Coordinate, tap the clock: Activity history with chips. Mark an Activity **Bought**: a "Completed" row appears.
8. In Notes, turn on the **Removed** filter: "Showing: Removed ✕" appears under the title; ✕ turns it off.
9. In Search, type a contact's name: it appears under Contact.
10. In To-do, complete a list: it shows struck through with a green check; cancel one: red cross.
11. If you have a memory dated on today's day and month in a past year, Today shows it as **On this day**; ✕ hides it.

### A71. OneSpace · `docs/R61_QA.md`

*First written 15 Nov 2026 · last changed 15 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R61 QA — Logo, Me tab, Voice 2.0

Install 0.1.4xx as an in-place update. Phone language English first, then Arabic.

##### Shell and logo
1. The launcher icon is sharp (no blur) on the home screen and in Recents; themed icons (Android 13+) show the mark in one colour.
2. Top bar: mark, "OneSpace" and the full tagline are readable on a small phone; mic, bell and ⋮ fit.
3. Bottom bar: Today · Remember · Coordinate · Connect · Me. Me shows profile, Settings, Language, Search, Items to sort, Your data.
4. Coordinate has two tabs: Activities and Trips. Links that used to open World/Trips land on Coordinate → Trips.
5. No "Read aloud" item in any ⋮ menu, letter or checklist.

##### Voice (tap the mic)
The sheet opens and listens straight away; words appear as you speak; it answers aloud and listens again. Say "no" / "that's all" or stay quiet to close.
1. "What's on today?" → spoken summary. Then "and tomorrow?" (or "what's on tomorrow").
2. "Read the groceries list" → open items read out. "Check off milk on the groceries list" → confirmed; the list shows it ticked.
3. "Add bread and eggs to the groceries list" → both added.
4. "Remind me to call the clinic tomorrow at 5 pm" → sheet closes, reminder page opens pre-filled; nothing saved until Save.
5. "I took my vitamins" (with a Vitamins reminder) → marked done. "Snooze the rent reminder for an hour" → snoozed.
6. "I'm feeling great today" → Daily connection shows today's mood.
7. "Send Kay a hug" → partner gets the gesture notification.
8. "How many days until our anniversary?", "When's our next trip?", "When do we see each other?"
9. "Read Kay's last letter", "Read the Paris trip", "Read the <activity name> activity".
10. Something unclear ("milk") → chips Reminder · Note · Event · To-do list.
11. Long-press the app icon → "Voice command" opens the app straight into the sheet.
12. Mic permission denied → clear message in the sheet, no crash. Airplane mode → "network" message.

##### Arabic (switch app language to العربية)
Sheet is right-to-left, replies are spoken in Arabic.
- «ماذا لدينا اليوم؟» · «اقرأ قائمة المشتريات» · «أضف حليب وبيض إلى قائمة المشتريات»
- «اشطب الحليب من قائمة المشتريات» · «ذكرني بالدواء بعد نص ساعة» · «ذكرني أن أتصل بالعيادة بكرة الساعة 5 المساء»
- «أنا مبسوط اليوم» · «أرسل لها حضن» · «كم يوم باقي على ذكرى زواجنا؟» · «متى رحلتنا القادمة؟» · «خلاص» (closes)

### A75. OneSpace · `docs/ROADMAP_GAP_REVIEW_R45.md`

*First written 15 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical — the app as built at R45 (15 Nov 2026).*

> Generated 15 Nov 2026 by Claude from code ae0870b (R45). PDF version shared with Ahmed. Later decisions: see DECISION_LOG.md.

#### 1. Summary

This document does three things:

- **Part A** turns **RoadMap.docx** (the original vision, all later feedback and clarifications) into bullet-point requirements. It keeps the *latest* instruction where you changed your mind, and classifies each one against the **actual code and live database** (commit `ae0870b`, R45).
- **Part B** reviews **OneSpace_Roadmap_Reviewed_Requirements_R45.docx** (ChatGPT's own self-assessment of the same roadmap).
- **Part C** sets out how I propose we continue working together.

> **Headline.** My independent count is lower than ChatGPT's. The R45 document marks 108 of 179 items "Fully implemented" (60%). Using a stricter test ("does it do what Ahmed actually asked, end to end?"), about **53%** of the product requirements are fully met. The difference is mainly items where *code exists* but the specific behaviour you asked for is missing (timing rules, notifications, search coverage, labels). The R45 document also misses several of your original pain points entirely.

**Status key:** [FULL] does what was asked · [PARTIAL] part of it · [DIFF] built, but not the way you asked · [NOT] not built.


#### Part A: RoadMap.docx organised and checked against the code

##### A1. The original problems OneSpace must solve

| ID | Problem you described | Status | What the code does today |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-1 | Two separate lives; I forget details of her life | [PARTIAL] | Notes, contacts, occasions and memories exist. Contacts are not in global search, and private "partner preferences" have no dedicated structure (only free notes). |
| P-2 | Different time zones make appointments hard | [PARTIAL] | Clocks per partner; events with zones; reminders delivered at the right instant. No pre-event alerts, no "her local time" shown when you create an alert for her. |
| P-3 | Requests made while I sleep/work get dropped | [PARTIAL] | Activities and Reminders capture requests, but a plain chat message in an Activity does **not** notify, and reminders cannot repeat. |
| P-4 | Phone alarms to wake her or be free for her call broke at a time change | [PARTIAL] | Reminders are absolute instants (DST-safe). There is no recurring "call/wake" alarm that follows *her* time zone, and no quiet-hour awareness. |
| P-5 | Remember her friends' and neighbours' names | [PARTIAL] | People & contacts with notes, import from phone. **Not globally searchable**, and no "relationship to Kandice" field (notes only). |
| P-6 | She asks for research (heater, pillows), forgets to review, then gets upset | [FULL] | Coordinate Activities with options, comparison, likes, decisions and outcomes cover this workflow well. The silent-chat gap (P-3) is the remaining weak point. |

##### A2. Shared schedules, routines and everyday tasks

| ID | Requirement (latest intent) | Status | Evidence / gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | Coordinate schedules, appointments, wake-up times and calls across time zones | [PARTIAL] | Clocks, zoned events, reminders. No wake-up/call planner and no partner-local-time preview. |
| R-2 | Save important notes, links, hiding places, reminders | [FULL] | Notes 2.0 (rich text, images, links), Reminders. |
| R-3 | Track last menstrual cycle | [NOT] | No cycle tracking anywhere (not mentioned in the R45 document either). |
| R-4 | Medication times, pick-ups, gas, car wash, laundry | [PARTIAL] | One-off Reminders or recurring checklists only. **Reminders cannot repeat.** |
| R-5 | Shared to-do lists and reusable checklists (car items, before going out) | [FULL] | Checklists with sub-items, per-person ticks, recurrence. |
| R-6 | Automated reminders, morning check-ins, safety alarm | [PARTIAL] | Server reminders (one-off); check-ins are manual; **no safety alarm**. |
| R-7 | Task priority without a due date unless one applies | [FULL] | Priority with optional target date. |
| R-8 | To-do title "To-do and Checklists" | [FULL] | Screen title matches. |
| R-9 | Button "+ Add to-do list" | [DIFF] | Button reads "Create checklist". |
| R-10 | Lists with items inside that can be ticked | [FULL] | Sub-items with ticks. |
| R-11 | Remove the parent checkbox (concept changed to sub-items) | [FULL] | Parent completes via status; items are ticked. |
| R-12 | Filters (visibility, status) moved to a filter icon at top | [FULL] | Filter icon in To-do header. |
| R-13 | Cancelled = red strikethrough; completed = strikethrough, not red | [FULL] | Styling implemented (verify colours on all palettes). |
| R-14 | Switch between all to-dos and "what to do today" | [FULL] | "Due today / overdue" filter. |
| R-15 | Recurring checklist opens fresh each cycle, closes the previous one, keeps history | [FULL] | Per-cycle, per-user completion rows; history retained. |

> "All items" includes the 6 original problem statements (A1) and the 10 local-testing checks (A18). The R45 document counted 179 consolidated items with 60% Fully. My register is more granular (your later UI feedback is listed item by item) and uses a stricter test, giving **53% Fully** for product requirements.


#### Part B: Review of "OneSpace_Roadmap_Reviewed_Requirements_R45"

##### B1. What the document does well

- **Traceability:** 179 numbered requirements (OS-001…OS-179) grouped in 19 domains, each with evidence pointers. This is a good structure to keep.
- **Honest release boundary:** it correctly states that R45 is not in APK 0.1.417 and that "fully" does not mean device-tested.
- **Respects your later decisions:** legacy lists retired, games as links, server move deferred, import/export instead of contact sync.
- **Sensible priority tiers (P0–P3)** at the end, with privacy and device acceptance first.

##### B2. Where it is too generous

The main weakness is the definition of **"Fully implemented"**. In practice it means "an identifiable screen and table exist", not "it behaves the way Ahmed asked". The builder is also grading its own work. Items I would reclassify:

| R45 item | R45 says | I say | Why (from the code) |
|---|---|---|---|
| OS-005 Living together / apart / LDR | Fully | Differently | UI writes only 2 of 4 modes; "apart, same time zone" cannot be chosen. |
| OS-022 Needs attention | Fully | Partial | Excludes first-class Reminders, routines and letters; dead "urgent" branch for to-dos. |
| OS-023 Coming up (7 days) | Fully | Partial | Excludes Reminders, holidays, days off and recurring routines. |
| OS-042 Recurrence time zone and holiday exclusions | Fully | Partial | Holidays hard-coded US federal only; typed IANA zone. |
| OS-055 Server-side reminder delivery | Fully | Partial | No recurrence, no quiet hours, no source re-validation. |
| OS-070 Activity conversation | Fully | Partial | Plain messages never notify; the chat shows "You"/full name although you asked for initials. |
| OS-128 Preparation-phase reminders | Fully | Partial | Fixed lead days; your timing rule not implemented. |
| OS-144 Push through Firebase | Fully | Partial | Generic, unlocalised body; push tap opens the list, not the item. |
| OS-157 Central settings separate from profile | Fully | Partial | Weather city, schedule, consent and language duplicated in Profile. |
| OS-171 Preserve records through RLS | Fully | Fully (with a caveat) | RLS is solid, but 11 live migrations are missing from the migration history. |
| OS-097 Self-reminders | Fully | Partial | Fixed 9/18/21 h; none of the smart conditions you described. |

##### B3. What it missed entirely

1. **Menstrual-cycle tracking** and **medication times**: in your first page of requirements, absent from the 179 items.
2. **Recurring reminders**: the most important missing capability for daily life.
3. **Silent Activity chat**: the core collaboration loop does not alert the partner.
4. **Contacts missing from global search**: it notes search is partial but doesn't name the gap most relevant to your original problem.
5. **Quiet hours for reminders and push** across time zones.
6. **Migration-history drift** (0095–0105 applied but not recorded).
7. **Generic push content.**
8. **"Messages to see when she wakes up"** (scheduled letters/gestures).
9. **Stale and duplicated UI copy** (Connect hub says selfies are "planned"; two taglines; developer panel in Profile).
10. **Goals module**: built without request and not discussed.
11. **Chat identity and voice-note behaviour** that contradict your explicit feedback.
12. **Contact purge never runs** despite a 30-day retention date.
13. **Arabic coverage not quantified** (≈25%).

##### B4. Overall verdict on the R45 document

It is a **useful, well-structured traceability register** and a fair *engineering* inventory. As a *business acceptance* document it **overstates completion by roughly 7 percentage points overall**, and by much more in the areas that matter most for daily use (alerts, reminders, search). It also frames gaps softly ("needs device review") where the code shows a definite gap. Keep its ID scheme and P0–P3 tiers. Replace its "fully" test with **"does the behaviour match Ahmed's latest instruction, end to end, including alerts?"**, and add a severity per gap.


#### Part C: How we continue: working agreement and plan

##### C1. Working agreement (what I propose)

| Topic | Proposal |
|---|---|
| Approval | I never change code, the database or settings without your explicit "go" on a written scope. Reviews are read-only. |
| Source control | One branch and one Pull Request per iteration. `main` changes only when you approve the merge. |
| Database | Every change is a numbered migration file **and** a recorded Supabase migration (fixes the drift). Destructive changes are always shown to you first. |
| Builds | Keep the existing policy: CI verification on every push, one signed APK per approved batch. |
| Acceptance | Each iteration ships with a short two-phone test script (10–20 steps) written for you, not for developers. |
| Single source of truth | The Handbook (this baseline) + a Decision Log + a Backlog stored in this claude.ai Project, updated each iteration. |
| Severity | Every finding is rated Critical/High/Medium/Low and fixed in that order unless you decide otherwise. |
| Language | Business explanations in plain language; technical notes in a separate appendix. |

##### C2. Decisions I need from you before building

1. **Automatic love rituals:** retire them (self-reminders only) or keep them as an explicit opt-in? *My recommendation: retire, and make self-reminders smart.*
2. **Shared Goals:** remove, hide, or repurpose (e.g. "savings for our next visit")?
3. **Relationship mode:** confirm three choices: Together · Apart, same time zone · Long distance.
4. **Edits to shared items:** should the partner be notified when a shared event, trip or reminder *time* changes? *Recommendation: yes, for date/time changes only.*
5. **Quiet hours default:** hold non-urgent alerts during the recipient's sleep window and deliver at wake time? *Recommendation: yes, with an "urgent" override.*
6. **Push privacy level:** show type and partner name (e.g. "Kandice sent you a letter") or keep it generic? *Recommendation: type + name, never content.*
7. **Chat identity:** do you still want initials only instead of "You"/name?
8. **Mutual deletion of shared memories:** decide now or keep archive-only?
9. **Cycle tracking:** do you still want it? It is sensitive health data, so it needs a private-by-default design.

##### C3. Proposed iteration plan (from the code, in priority order)

| Iteration | Theme | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| **R46** | Baseline hygiene (no visible change) | Record missing migrations; enable leaked-password protection; remove stale copy; one tagline; hide developer panel; fix "last met today" text; align to-do priority scale. |
| **R47** | Reminders that work for real life | Recurring reminders; quiet hours & days-off awareness; show recipient's local time; stop alerts for cancelled/archived sources; Reminders + today's routines in Today. |
| **R48** | Never miss a message | Notify on every Activity message (batched); type-specific, localised push with deep links (incl. gesture pop-up); edit notifications for date/time changes. |
| **R49** | Find anything | Contacts, checklist items, chat, comments, photo tags in global search; one shared @-autocomplete with first-name/label matching. |
| **R50** | One calendar | Calendar shows reminders, trips, Activity dates, experiences as toggleable layers; true week grid; optional pre-event alert. |
| **R51** | Travel-ready | Offline encrypted documents, travel-day card on Today, check-in/leave reminders, more document types, trip end updates "last met". |
| **R52** | Thoughtful love & occasions | Smart self-reminders (last sent, schedule, days off, shared mood ≤3); your occasion timing rule + salary month; three-mode relationship selector. |
| Later | Expansion | Complete Arabic; settings consolidation; posters/genres; scrapbook templates; iOS; AI level 2; voice; account deletion/export. |

Each iteration is roughly one focused build plus one APK. R46–R48 alone would remove most of the "dropped request" risk that motivated OneSpace.

##### C4. What I already have and what I need from you

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| GitHub repository access | Connected (read and push), private repo `BoyofKay/OneSpace`. |
| Supabase access | Connected to project OneSpace (Seoul); read-only queries used so far. |
| Firebase | Not needed for review. Push credentials already live in Supabase secrets; only needed if we change the Firebase project. |
| Phones | Only you can install APKs and run two-phone tests. I will write the test scripts. |
| Your input | Answers to the 9 decisions in C2, and your go-ahead to start R46. |

### A82. OneSpace · `docs/R74_SETUP.md`

*First written 16 Nov 2026 · last changed 16 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R74 — what only Ahmed can set up

##### 1. Google sign-in (about 15 minutes)
1. https://console.cloud.google.com → create/choose a project → APIs & Services → OAuth consent screen: External, app name "OneSpace", your email, add the 4 tester emails as test users (or publish).
2. Credentials → Create credentials → OAuth client ID → **Web application**.
   Authorized redirect URI: `https://ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback`
3. Supabase dashboard → Authentication → Sign In / Providers → Google → enable, paste Client ID and Client secret → Save.
4. Supabase → Authentication → URL Configuration → Redirect URLs: make sure `onespace-preview://auth-callback/` is listed.
The "Continue with Google" button appears in the app by itself once step 3 is saved.

##### 2. Apple sign-in (needs the Apple Developer account, $99/year)
1. developer.apple.com → Identifiers → App ID with "Sign in with Apple"; then a **Services ID** (e.g. com.onespace.signin) with Sign in with Apple, domain `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo.supabase.co`, return URL `https://ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback`.
2. Keys → new key with Sign in with Apple → download the .p8 (once).
3. Supabase → Providers → Apple → enable; Services ID, Team ID, Key ID and the .p8 (Supabase generates the secret; it expires every 6 months — set a reminder).
Apple's rule: an iPhone app that offers Google sign-in must also offer Apple.

##### 3. Nightly database backups
GitHub → BoyofKay/OneSpace → Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → New repository secret:
- `SUPABASE_DB_URL` = Supabase → Connect → **Session pooler** URI (replace [YOUR-PASSWORD]).
- `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE` = a long passphrase you keep in your password manager.
Then Actions → "Back up OneSpace database" → Run workflow once to check. Backups are kept 14 days.

##### 4. Supabase settings worth switching on
- Authentication → Attack protection → Leaked password protection (Pro plan only; skip on Free).
- Authentication → Email: keep "Confirm email" on.

##### 5. Where to look during the test
- Messages from testers: tables `support_requests` (signed in) and `public_support_requests` (from the sign-in screen).
- Automatic error reports: table `client_errors` (newest first).
- Supabase → Reports for usage; Firebase console → Cloud Messaging for push.

### A86. OneSpace · `docs/R83_QA.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R83 — Add a place by pinning the map (QA acceptance)

Scope: **Our places → Our map**. QA first, then production only after phone acceptance.
Existing **Edit → Find** and calendar sync must remain unchanged.

##### Main workflow

1. Open Remember → Our places → Map. Existing hearts and bucket-list pins remain visible.
2. Tap **Pin a place**, then tap an empty spot on the map. A pin appears at the precise tapped coordinates.
3. Tap **Been here**. The normal **New place** editor opens (not a popup), pre-selected to Been there, with latitude/longitude, and any available place name, city, country and address.
4. Enter or correct the required name; change privacy, category and optional visit date; save. The new heart appears at the pinned coordinates; it is listed under Been there.
5. Repeat by long-pressing anywhere on the map and selecting **Add to bucket list**. The normal **New dream place** editor opens pre-selected to Bucket list; save. The compass pin appears.
6. Use **Remove pin** or Cancel in the editor; no place should be saved. A name/address lookup failure must not prevent manually naming and saving the pinned location.
7. Select an existing pin → details. Switching All/Been there/Bucket list filters, panning, zooming and attribution still work.

##### Regression / two-phone checks

- In a place without coordinates, use **Edit → Find**; the existing marker and message should update.
- Test existing Google Maps sharing to OneSpace, open a place mini-map, and open in Google Maps.
- Save a **shared** pinned place on Phone A; it appears on Phone B. Save **Only me** on A; Phone B cannot see it.
- Select an existing marker before creating a new pin; the cards must not overlap.
- Test Arabic UI and both a large and a narrow Android phone; confirm buttons and editor are readable.
- Verify Google Calendar's existing OneSpace calendar remains visible when enabled; R83 does not change calendar integration.

##### Release gate

R83 source passed the GitHub **Verify OneSpace iteration** Flutter analysis/tests workflow
[run 44159377150](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/actions/runs/44159377150).
QA APK must compile and be tested on both phones before building/promoting a new production Play bundle.
No Supabase migration is required by this change.

### A87. OneSpace · `docs/R84_QA.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### R84 — Counters are opt-in; clearer places map

##### Product rule
**Counters are a personal/shared choice, not a mirror of every dated object.**
The Counters screen loads only non-archived rows saved in `space_counters`. It must NOT independently create/display birthdays, anniversaries, custom special dates, calendar events, occasion plans, trips, flights, or reunion dates. Other features retain their own date display and reminders.

##### QA — add from the source
1. Open Remember → Counters. Confirm existing **saved** counters remain; unrelated upcoming dates are not injected. Refresh; nothing appears until the user creates a counter.
2. Create a Calendar event with a date → event `⋮` → **Add to counters** → full **New counter** editor (title/date prefilled). Cancel: no counter. Repeat and Save: one counter appears.
3. Occasion plan (list and detail) → `⋮` → Add to counters. Annual occasion defaults to Every year; privacy defaults to source visibility.
4. Settings → birthdays, anniversary and special dates: each saved date's `⋮` → Add to counters. Verify correct date and annual behaviour.
5. Trip (list and detail) with leaving date → `⋮` → Add to counters. A trip *without* a date must not offer a misleading action.
6. Flight option with departure date → `⋮` → Add to counters. Try private trip and verify resulting counter defaults to **Only me**.
7. Dated itinerary item (including flight) → `⋮` → Add to counters.
8. Counter **+** → **Choose from calendar, dates, occasions, flights or trips** → grouped list of dated entries (including existing last/next meet). Pick a record; verify the counter editor is filled and can Save without extra changes.
9. Edit any source after saving its counter. The counter is an **independent snapshot** and must not silently change. Remove the source: the saved counter remains. Repeating action requires a second explicit Save; it should never happen on loading the tab.
10. Two-phone privacy: private sources never appear to the other account in the picker, and shared counters appear on both devices.
11. Calendar/Google Calendar synchronization, scheduling, push notifications, and all item history actions still function unchanged.

##### QA — map
1. Open Remember → Our places → Map. Been there markers are **coral circles**, bucket-list markers **teal rounded squares**. Their central icon identifies category (e.g. cafe, restaurant, hotel).
2. Shared pins have two small differently coloured initials for the people who can see them. Private pins show the owner's initial. On a one-person space, no phantom partner badge appears.
3. Filtering, existing-pin detail, panning/zooming and long-press to create a **new pin** continue working.
4. Save an existing private place on A; verify partner B cannot see it. Save shared on A; B sees it with the two badges.
5. Open place detail's mini map; it shows the same category icon/status treatment.
6. Check accessibility labels and both English and Arabic on a small Android phone.

##### Release
QA first. Never trigger production or modify Supabase schemas/data for this change. No database migration required; a counter copies the chosen source date into its own existing row.

### A88. OneSpace · `docs/RELEASE_ENVIRONMENTS.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Active reference (summarised in §1).*

#### OneSpace environments: QA and Production

Since 17 Nov 2026 OneSpace exists as **two separate apps with two separate backends**.
They install side by side on one phone and never share data.

| | **OneSpace QA** | **OneSpace** (production) |
|---|---|---|
| Who | Ahmed and testers checking each new build | Real couples (Founders first) |
| Android package | `onespace.qa` | `live.onespace.app` (permanent once on Play) |
| Name / icon | "OneSpace QA", amber icon with a QA badge | "OneSpace" |
| Backend | Supabase project **OneSpace** (`ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`, Seoul) — test users and demo data only | Supabase project **OneSpace Production** (`letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz`, Frankfurt) — clean start |
| Sign-in callback | `onespace-preview://auth-callback/` | `onespace://auth-callback/` |
| Signed with | Test key in `tools/dev-signing/` (public, never for Play) | Play **upload key** from GitHub secrets; Google re-signs with the Play app signing key |
| Built by | `.github/workflows/android-dev.yml` → APK in GitHub Releases (`qa-0.1.N`) | `.github/workflows/android-play.yml` (manual) → `.aab` in the run's Artifacts |
| Distributed | GitHub Releases APK | Play Console: closed testing (Founders = UAT) → production |

UAT is **not** a third app: it is the exact production build on the Play closed-testing
track. When it passes, the same build is promoted to production.

##### How the app picks its backend

`lib/core/app_env.dart` holds both backends. The build passes
`--dart-define=ONESPACE_ENV=qa` or `--dart-define=ONESPACE_ENV=production`; anything that is
not `production` is QA, so a build can never reach real couples by accident. The Android flavor
(`--flavor qa` / `--flavor prod`) sets the package, label, icon and callback scheme.

```bash
# QA APK
flutter build apk --release --flavor qa --dart-define=ONESPACE_ENV=qa
# Production bundle for Play (needs the upload key environment variables)
flutter build appbundle --release --flavor prod --dart-define=ONESPACE_ENV=production
```

`android-play.yml` refuses to build while `app_env.dart` still has placeholder production
values, without the upload key, or (unless you tick *allow_without_push*) without a Firebase app
for `live.onespace.app`.

##### One-time setup checklist (Ahmed)

###### GitHub secrets — `BoyofKay/onespace` → Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions
| Secret | Value |
|---|---|
| `ONESPACE_UPLOAD_KEYSTORE_BASE64` | content of `onespace-upload.jks.base64.txt` (sent privately) |
| `ONESPACE_UPLOAD_STORE_PASSWORD` | from `README-upload-key.txt` |
| `ONESPACE_UPLOAD_KEY_PASSWORD` | from `README-upload-key.txt` |
| `ONESPACE_UPLOAD_KEY_ALIAS` | `onespace-upload` |
| `ONESPACE_GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON_BASE64` | update after adding the two Firebase apps below |

###### Firebase (same Firebase project for both apps)
Add two Android apps, then download the new `google-services.json` (it covers all apps) and
replace the secret with its base64:
- `onespace.qa` — SHA-1 `A0:47:86:BF:75:A5:EA:CA:89:DF:43:83:91:3D:08:FF:51:CF:49:16`
- `live.onespace.app` — upload key SHA-1 `D1:6A:CF:3A:DE:96:A3:33:AA:69:52:23:9B:E6:4E:EE:09:F3:DB:85`,
  plus the **Play app signing** SHA-1 shown in Play Console → Setup → App signing (after the first upload).

Until then QA builds still work, with in-app notifications only.

###### Supabase — OneSpace Production project (dashboard)
- **Edge Function secrets:** `ONESPACE_FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` (same Firebase service account
  JSON as QA), `RESEND_API_KEY`, `FOUNDER_ADMIN_KEY`, `GOATCOUNTER_TOKEN`.
  (No `ONESPACE_PUSH_CRON_SECRET` needed: the push and account-purge workers read the shared secret
  from Vault through `public.push_worker_secret()`, service role only — migration 0136.)
- **Authentication → URL Configuration:** Site URL `https://onespace.live`; Redirect URLs
  `onespace://auth-callback/`.
- **Authentication → Providers:** Google (and Apple later) with an Android OAuth client for
  `live.onespace.app` and the Play signing + upload SHA-1s.
- **Authentication → Emails / SMTP:** same sender setup as QA (e.g. Resend SMTP) so confirmation
  emails come from onespace.live.

###### Supabase — OneSpace (QA) project
Nothing changes: it keeps `onespace-preview://auth-callback/`, its users and data. Optionally
rename it to "OneSpace QA" in Settings → General.

##### Database changes from now on
Every migration is applied to **QA first**, tested with the QA app, and only then to Production.
Never test a migration on Production.

##### Moving testers
The old preview app (`com.onespace.onespace.preview`) stops receiving updates. Testers uninstall it
and install **OneSpace QA** from the newest `qa-0.1.N` GitHub release.

##### How Production was created (17 Nov 2026)
- The 149 QA migrations were replayed in order into the new project (project URL rewritten), then a
  catalog comparison against QA found objects that had been made in QA outside migrations
  (tables `record_action_history`, `note_references`, `partner_checkin_acknowledgements`,
  `shared_experience_interests`, some columns, constraints, triggers, policies, function grants).
  They were copied exactly, and the final comparison showed no differences. Storage buckets,
  storage policies and the 7 pg_cron jobs match (jobs point at the production URL).
- No app data was copied. Only the Founder waitlist (couple #1) moved from QA to Production.
- **Lesson:** every database change must be a migration. Changes made with ad-hoc SQL do not reach
  Production.

##### Public website URLs for Google Play and iOS listings

Use the canonical clean URLs in store consoles, release notes, marketing and any future in-app external links:

- Privacy policy: `https://onespace.live/privacy/`
- Terms of use: `https://onespace.live/terms/`
- Account deletion: `https://onespace.live/delete-account/`
- Customer support: `https://onespace.live/support/`
- Frequently asked questions: `https://onespace.live/faq/`
- Founder signup: `https://onespace.live/join/`

The website serves localized clean links under `/ar/`, `/es/`, `/fr/`, and `/de/`, and keeps the previous `.html` addresses as backward-compatible redirects. In-app Terms and Privacy screens are native and must remain consistent with these hosted policies. These documentation-only updates do not change the signed Android AAB or iOS build.

##### iOS identity and release isolation (18 November 2026)

The iOS release branch ios/launch-readiness-20261118 adds separate native QA
(com.onespace.onespace.qa) and production (com.onespace.onespace) identities,
matching auth callback schemes and a fail-closed startup check before the app
opens Supabase. See docs/IOS_SETUP.md for the explicit environment command,
manual unsigned QA build, manual signed production/TestFlight pipeline and
Apple/Firebase prerequisites. New iOS code still requires compile, signing
and real-device acceptance before merging/releasing.

### A90. OneSpace-Command · `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Active reference (Command Center runbook).*

#### OneSpace Command — Current Deployment and Remaining Connections

**Current deployment:** [Cloudflare OneSpace Command](https://onespace-command.ph-ahmed-rezk.workers.dev), protected by Cloudflare Access. The dashboard opened in the authenticated owner's screenshot. This does **not yet verify live metrics or alert sending**.

**Ownership and isolation:** this repo alone. Do not change the OneSpace mobile application, its shared database schemas, or the website.

##### 1. Security — deployed but independently recheck

- Cloudflare Zero Trust team domain: `frosty-disk-bccd.cloudflareaccess.com`.
- Worker Access policy is **All traffic**. The policy currently allows members of the Cloudflare account, not just one individual.
- Worker checks signed Cloudflare Access JWT (audience, issuer) and the single authorized `ADMIN_EMAIL` using `jose`.
- The following **Cloudflare Worker secrets** must exist in Production: `CF_ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN`, `CF_ACCESS_AUD`, `ADMIN_EMAIL`. They should NOT be committed.
- Verify a private/incognito visit is blocked by an Access login challenge. Also check that an unrelated Cloudflare account member cannot access the Worker. Keep the worker-level exact admin-email check.
- Preview deployments need the same secrets/protection if enabled. They must not silently fall back to production credentials.
- No public, unauthenticated endpoint should return metrics.

##### 2. Supabase — existing *shared* project, read only

Current Supabase project id: `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`. It contains both mobile app data and the website Founders tables; **it is not an isolated QA database**.

The Worker expects one scoped, project-specific personal access token with **Database → Read**, stored as Production Cloudflare secret **`SUPABASE_READONLY_PAT`**. Never send it in a chat or commit it to GitHub.

After saving that secret in **Cloudflare > Workers & Pages > onespace-command > Settings > Variables and Secrets**, click Deploy and reopen your dashboard > **Infrastructure**, then Reload.

Expected once authorized:
- Database size in MB compared with the currently configured 500 MB per-project limit. Confirm your actual Supabase plan still has this limit.
- Registered user count from `auth.users`.
- Stored object count and size from `storage.objects`. Total organization quota remains unavailable; no fake percentage.
- Existing Founder website data: number of registrations, confirmed members, Founder couples, waiting couples, platform preferences and `founder_email_log` sent/failed counts. These are read-only aggregate queries. No emails, names or tokens returned.
- The Founder couple identifier is one per *couple*, not one per person; maximum Founder couples is 250.

If the dashboard displays "Supabase … not configured", check that the token was actually saved and deployed in **Production**. If it shows an API error, check the scoped token's **Database Read** permission, correct project, expiry, and worker's configured query path. Do not grant Read-write.

##### 3. GoatCounter — optional read-only web traffic

The Worker includes a read-only adapter to `GET https://onespace.goatcounter.com/api/v0/stats/total`. To activate:

1. Create a GoatCounter API token limited to **read-only statistics** (not write/count).
2. Save it as Worker **Production secret** `GOATCOUNTER_READONLY_TOKEN`.
3. Optional: set `GOATCOUNTER_SITE_CODE=onespace` as nonsecret variable. If omitted, `onespace` is the default.
4. Deploy and Reload. The UI shows last **30 days of pageviews**, excluding custom analytics events where the API exposes both values; this is **not unique visitors**.
5. If it reports an error, review GoatCounter token authorization; no fake visits are shown.

##### 4. Alerts — currently NOT enabled until all conditions met

The code contains an hourly Cloudflare Cron Trigger and 60/75/90% thresholds, **but sending alerts requires durable state and a separate sender**.

1. The repo's `wrangler.jsonc` now declares `"kv_namespaces": [{ "binding": "ALERT_STORE" }]`. **Cloudflare Wrangler 4 auto-provisions the KV namespace** on the next successful Cloudflare Workers Builds deployment if it does not already exist. Check **Workers & Pages → onespace-command → Settings → Bindings** to verify `ALERT_STORE` appears. If provisioning fails, check the deployment log; do not guess or manually duplicate namespaces.
2. Create a **new Resend sending-only key** for system alerts, restricted to `onespace.live` if available. Do not reuse or reveal the website's key.
3. Save these **Production secrets**: `RESEND_ALERT_API_KEY`, `ALERT_FROM_EMAIL`, `ALERT_TO_EMAIL`. For example, sender `OneSpace Command <support@onespace.live>` and recipient an administrator-controlled inbox.
4. Verify the Cron Trigger `0 * * * *` is present in Cloudflare **Settings > Triggers**.
5. Deploy; the Alerts page changes from "not active" to **configured** once KV and all credentials are present. "Configured" does **not** prove delivery.
6. Perform a controlled isolated test of the alert state machine and inspect the Resend log before relying on alerts. Never artificially fill the real database just to test.
7. Hourly scheduling may need higher frequency later; currently the alert isn't instant. Quota alerts will only operate on resources with a verified used/limit pair (initially database).

Cloudflare KV is eventually consistent; this v0.2 implementation avoids rapid writes to the same key, but not exactly-once guarantees in all outage scenarios. Resend idempotency reduces duplicates.

##### 5. Other metrics not yet integrated

- Supabase organization's monthly bandwidth/egress, Storage allotment, MAU, Edge Functions and Realtime: each requires appropriate organization-level billing data and validated quotas. Do not infer from DB size alone.
- Resend account-wide `GET /usage` requires a **full-access API key** (official Resend API), which is broader than the current sending-only key. **Do not create one without deciding whether the benefit outweighs the risk**. Founder send/failed log counts are available via read-only Supabase already.
- Zoho Mail Lite incoming/outgoing counts may need a separate read-only OAuth authorization; verify available scopes and plan before implementation. A shared inbox alias is not a separate mailbox.
- Firebase app crashes, active couples and app store installations require their respective reporting connections and definitions.
- Production app isn't deployed to a distinct Supabase project yet. The Production selector must show unavailable metrics, not cloned QA data.

##### 6. Phone installation

1. Open the protected Worker URL above on Android Chrome, authenticate through Cloudflare Access.
2. Open Chrome menu (three dots) and choose **Install app** or **Add to Home screen**.
3. Install / accept. The screen adapts to mobile width and shows app navigation. A shortcut may be offered instead of full PWA installation depending on browser/manifest; check the offered behavior.
4. Sign in through Cloudflare Access on the phone. Do not enable public anonymous dashboard access for easier installation.

##### 8. Scheduled check visibility

The Alerts tab now reads a private, authenticated `GET /api/alerts/status` endpoint from Cloudflare KV:
- **Last scheduled check:** UTC scheduler heartbeat displayed in the viewer's local timezone. Missing means never recorded or the new deployment has not run.
- **Check result:** `ok` requires at least one real usage measurement with a valid denominator, `error` indicates failure/no comparable measurements, `inactive` means the alert email configuration is incomplete.
- **Resources checked:** Count of metric usage/quota pairs genuinely evaluated during the last successful scheduled run.
- **Last test accepted:** Timestamp of the controlled Resend test, read from `test-alert:last`.

The Worker uses `0 * * * *` (minute 0 every UTC hour). **Do not mistake a successful manual test for verified cron execution.** After the latest Cloudflare deployment, inspect the Alerts tab after an actual hourly trigger. Confirm it reports `Latest scheduled check completed` and at least one comparable resource. Check Cloudflare trigger events if it does not.

Only administrative metadata and aggregate counts are exposed. No recipient emails, provider keys or member data are returned.

##### 7. Verification matrix

| Test | Expected |
|---|---|
| Unauthenticated incognito opening | Cloudflare Access challenge / blocked |
| Authorized administrator opening | Dashboard loads |
| Production selector without project setup | Unavailable data (not QA values) |
| Live Supabase token configured | Aggregates visible and fresh timestamp |
| Supabase revoked or expired | Error state, no fabricated readings |
| Founder aggregates query | Totals and platform demand only, no personal data |
| GoatCounter without token | Pending, not 0 visits |
| Alerts without KV/Resend | Explicitly "NOT active" |
| Configured alerts | Explicitly "configured"; verify a test send before treating as operational |
| Android Chrome install | Home-screen icon / standalone presentation if supported |

###### External API references

- [Supabase read-only Management query](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/api/v1-read-only-query)
- [Supabase scoped access tokens](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/personal-access-tokens)
- [GoatCounter API](https://www.goatcounter.com/help/api)
- [Cloudflare Workers Access](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/cloudflare-access/)
- [Cloudflare KV configuration](https://developers.cloudflare.com/kv/)
- [Resend Usage API requires full access](https://resend.com/changelog/account-usage-api)

##### How merges go live

There is one deployer: the Cloudflare Workers Builds git connection (Workers & Pages → onespace-command → Settings → Builds). Every push to `main` runs `npm test && npm run build`, then `npx wrangler deploy`. If any test fails, nothing is deployed. Other branches get a preview build with no secrets. The GitHub workflow `ci.yml` still runs the tests on pull requests; there is no GitHub deploy workflow.

### A91. OneSpace-Command · `docs/DISK_CAPACITY_EVIDENCE_20261117.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Supabase disk capacity — manual evidence and safe monitoring

**Evidence date:** 2026-11-17 (date only; screenshot capture time not verified).
**Provenance:** Screenshots of both projects' Supabase → Settings → Infrastructure views, supplied by the project owner. Stored as structured **reference values** in `src/lib/disk-evidence.ts` (no raw screenshots or private tokens in this repository).

| Field | QA (Seoul) | Production (Frankfurt) |
|---|---:|---:|
| Project reference | `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo` | `letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz` |
| Disk occupied | 0.34 GB | 0.31 GB |
| Disk provisioned | 2 GB | 2 GB |
| Supabase display utilization | 17% | 16% |
| Database component on disk | 41.4 MB | 33.2 MB |
| WAL | 128 MB | 112 MB |
| System | 174.4 MB | 167.9 MB |
| CPU utilization | 8% | 3% |
| Memory utilization | 63% | 35% |
| Disk IO balance | 99% | Not reported |
| GP3 disk included by Pro | 8 GB/project | 8 GB/project |

**Important distinctions:**

1. **Historical UI snapshot:** The new Infrastructure card deliberately shows the captured values and date even after future app reloads. It is **not refreshed** by the Cloudflare dashboard, Supabase Management API, or hourly cron. No inferred live readings or alert emails.
2. **Used vs. provisioned:** Disk-occupied GB compared with the current 2 GB provisioned size is an **operational utilization indicator**, distinct from the **8 GB GP3 included billing allowance**. Project disk autoscaling is configured in Supabase and does not imply an immediate billable overage.
3. **SQL measurement:** `pg_database_size(current_database())` reports data size, not total attached disk occupied. It omits WAL and system files and must not be compared with a 2 GB provisioned disk as if it were total usage.
4. **Sum rounding:** The displayed component MB values need not exactly sum to the displayed rounded GB total. Never recompute a falsely precise "live" percent from mixed or rounded quantities.
5. **Alerts:** `command-quotas` intentionally remains a **missing live measurement** in Systems & Connections until matching-scope, fresh total disk occupancy **and** provisioned bytes are retrieved through a narrowly scoped read-only provider interface. A screenshot must **never** feed a monitoring threshold or simulate a current alert.
6. **Privacy:** No credentials, secrets, customer records or private image bytes are included in the dashboard reference file.

**Operator procedure:** For current disk status, open `https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/{project-ref}/settings/infrastructure`; select the correct QA or Production project. To update reference evidence, obtain a dated screenshot for each environment, edit `src/lib/disk-evidence.ts` and its tests together, run CI, and deploy. Consider integrating the Supabase Metrics API only after verifying its authentication scope and exact provisioned/used disk series for each project.

**Source docs:** https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/database-size and https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/manage-your-usage/disk-size

### A92. OneSpace-Command · `docs/FIREBASE_RESEND_MONITORING_V07.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Command v0.7 — Connect Firebase and Resend

The Command Center has two new **read-only reporting adapters** and no changes to OneSpace's existing mobile app, Firebase sender, website, Founder waitlist, Zoho mailbox, or Supabase tables.

##### Architecture

- **Resend:** GET `https://api.resend.com/usage` (existing adapter) and GET `https://api.resend.com/emails/metrics` (new). Daily/monthly **account-wide** quotas flow to Overview/Infrastructure and the existing 60/75/90% scheduler. Delivery, bounce, complaints, unsubscribes and failures are shown under **Communications** for the last rolling 30 days. The new adapter does not enumerate individual emails or recipients. The API key is server-side only.
- **Firebase:** A **new, dedicated Google service account** with Firebase Cloud Messaging API Viewer role uses its private key on the Worker to create short-lived OAuth access tokens. GET `https://fcmdata.googleapis.com/v1beta1/projects/{projectId}/androidApps/{appId}/deliveryData`. Aggregate Android FCM diagnostic counts and percentages are displayed under **Mobile App**. Google's FCM Data API provides a **7-day historical window, delayed by up to five days**, with privacy thresholds, so an empty diagnostic report is possible even when the integration is working, especially during a two-device QA test. No send/push-management endpoints are called.
- All operational endpoints are protected by the existing Cloudflare Access admin identity. The Worker never returns OAuth tokens, private keys, raw provider records, emails, names, devices or message bodies to the browser.
- All values are unavailable until the upstream service returns a valid response. Reporting is independent of the app's sending infrastructure.

##### 1. Resend configuration — in Cloudflare

The *existing* `RESEND_ALERT_API_KEY` is sending-only; **do not replace or repurpose it**.

Resend currently requires a separate **Full access** API key to retrieve account usage and email metrics (no read-only API key permission). This is broader than ideal; only create it if you accept the risk, and use a unique key named **OneSpace Command Reporting**. Leave the key's existing sender alone.

Go to **Cloudflare Dashboard → Workers & Pages → onespace-command → Settings → Variables and Secrets → Add**:

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Environment | Production |
| Type | Secret |
| Key | `RESEND_USAGE_API_KEY` |
| Value | The newly-created *reporting* API key from Resend (do not share via chat) |

Select **Add variable and deploy**. Never put the key in GitHub source, frontend, screenshots, or this document. If the existing `RESEND_USAGE_API_KEY` is already present, do not generate another key merely for this release.

To validate, visit Command → Communications → **Email delivery and reputation**, then refresh the page. On success the account-wide Usage and Metrics endpoints return figures. If the API returns permission failure, the UI remains **Error/Unavailable**; no synthetic values.

##### 2. Firebase configuration — in Google Cloud and Cloudflare

There are two Google/Firebase projects in your wider environment. **Choose the Firebase project connected to the OneSpace Android app under test** (do not guess). In Firebase Console → Project settings → General, record:

- **Project ID** (not numeric project number).
- **Android app ID** (Firebase's app ID, shaped `1:123456:android:abc123`; NOT the Android package name).
- Verify the OneSpace Android app is already using that Firebase project. Do not upload/reconfigure `google-services.json` for this monitoring setup.

In **Google Cloud Console**, select that same project:

1. Go to **APIs & Services → Library**, enable **Firebase Cloud Messaging Data API** (`fcmdata.googleapis.com`) if required.
2. Go to **IAM & Admin → Service Accounts**, create a **new** reporting-only service account, e.g. `onespace-command-monitor`.
3. Grant it **Firebase Cloud Messaging API Viewer** (`roles/firebasecloudmessaging.viewer`). Do NOT grant Owner, Editor, Firebase Admin, or FCM API Admin, and do not reuse the existing FCM push-sending service account.
4. Under the new account's **Keys** create a new JSON private key, *only if service-account keys are permitted in your organization*. Treat the downloaded file as a highly sensitive credential. If key creation is forbidden, stop rather than weakening policy; a keyless identity mechanism would be a separate integration.
5. In Cloudflare **Workers & Pages → onespace-command → Settings → Variables and Secrets**, create three **Production** settings:

| Type | Key | Value |
|---|---|---|
| Variable | `FIREBASE_REPORTING_PROJECT_ID` | Firebase project ID |
| Variable | `FIREBASE_REPORTING_ANDROID_APP_ID` | Firebase Android App ID |
| **Secret** | `FIREBASE_REPORTING_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` | Entire new service-account JSON text including private key |

Select **Add variable and deploy**. Keep the JSON only in Cloudflare secrets. Do not paste JSON/private key into ChatGPT, GitHub, screenshots or the Flutter app.

The Worker uses the signed service-account JWT solely to ask for an OAuth access token, then executes a GET to the FCM Data API. The service account's viewer IAM prevents sending notifications even though the OAuth endpoint requires the `cloud-platform` scope.

**Validation:** Command → Mobile App → **Android notification diagnostics**. The status changes to connected only after a valid FCM response. Some Android aggregates require app/device diagnostic data collection, are not a full count of notifications sent, and may be delayed. iOS is not included.

###### Crashlytics and app usage

The current Flutter repo contains no evidence of the Firebase Crashlytics or Firebase Analytics SDKs being installed. So **crash-free sessions, true DAU/MAU, app retention, screen views and iOS push delivery are not claimed to be available** from this integration. These need a separate, carefully reviewed application instrumentation release or provider integration.

The pre-existing Supabase `client_errors` reports remain available under Mobile App and are not relabeled as Firebase crash counts.

##### 3. QA checks

- Resend without new secret → **Pending**, no requests made, no false zeros.
- Resend with valid key → **Connected** and real 30-day provider metrics; no customer email data.
- Firebase without reporting config → **Pending**; no Google token requested.
- Firebase with matching project/app/Viewer identity → **Connected** with Android delivery diagnostics, if available.
- Wrong Firebase project, missing API, or IAM failure → **Error**, not zero or connected.
- Existing Supabase metrics and Zoho/GoatCounter remain unchanged.
- Existing `RESEND_ALERT_API_KEY` continues to send alerts independently; do not delete it.
- All configured keys remain in Cloudflare server-side Secrets; secrets are not exposed to browser responses.
- Cloudflare Access remains required for both routes.
- Scheduled Resend quota alerts remain at 60/75/90%; this release does **not** silently add bounce/complaint alert thresholds, because those require separately defined reputation policies.

##### Provider documentation

- Resend usage: https://resend.com/changelog/account-usage-api
- Resend metrics: https://resend.com/changelog/email-metrics-api
- FCM Data: https://firebase.google.com/docs/reference/fcmdata/rest/v1beta1/projects.androidApps.deliveryData/list
- FCM Viewer role: https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/roles-permissions/firebasecloudmessaging
