### A93. OneSpace-Command · `docs/FOUNDER_CAMPAIGNS_PROPOSAL.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Proposal / draft — not an approved decision. Founder-number content ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by D81.*

#### OneSpace Command — Founder Campaigns Implementation Proposal (approval draft)

**Status:** Architecture proposal only. **No SQL migrations applied, no app or website files changed, no recipients contacted.**
**Owner:** OneSpace Command. **Existing Founder website owner:** Claude.
**Source audit:** 2026-11-17. Reviewed website `backend/founders/functions/_lib.ts`, `founder-admin.ts`, `founder-join.ts`, `founder-confirm.ts`, `0134_founder_members.sql`, and read-only live Supabase schema/aggregate counts.

##### 0. Verified baseline

- Correct Command repository: `BoyofKay/OneSpace-Command` (private).
- Website repository: `BoyofKay/onespace-app`, not to be edited.
- Shared existing app + website Supabase project: `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`.
- `private.founder_couples`, `private.founder_members`, `private.founder_email_log`, `private.founder_attempts` exist. No command-specific tables exist yet.
- Existing `public.founder_*()` functions are SECURITY DEFINER and EXECUTE limited to `service_role`.
- Read-only aggregate on 2026-11-17: 1 founder couple, 1 member confirmed/consented/not-unsubscribed, 0 waitlist couples, 2 email log records. **Not enough for a 20-couple wave**.
- Founder number attaches to *couple*, the first 250 confirmed couples obtain Founder status. A partner shares the number.
- Confirmed/consenting/unsubscribed checks must be repeated **at actual send time**, not only during preview.
- Current website's mail helper offers a branded EN/AR HTML shell, unsubscribe footer and List-Unsubscribe headers; templates must match it.
- Current Cloudflare Command Worker has a scoped Supabase Monitoring PAT. **Do not reuse this PAT for campaigns or expand its permissions.**

##### 1. Separation of concerns and security

```text
Cloudflare Access (all traffic, authorized administrator only)
    |
OneSpace Command Worker UI / API (CSRF, approvals, audit)
    |  campaign-specific secret; no service_role in Worker/browser
    v
Dedicated Supabase founder-campaign Edge Function [new, approval needed]
    |  secrets kept inside Supabase Edge runtime; strict action allowlist
    v
New service_role-only public RPC functions for NEW private campaign tables
    |
private.founder_couples, private.founder_members (READ only)
private.founder_email_log (append via existing founder_log_email)
private.founder_waves, founder_wave_members, founder_campaign_outbox,
private.founder_surveys, founder_survey_responses,
private.founder_campaign_settings (new only after approval)
    |
Resend → founders@onespace.live (sender); Zoho receives replies
```

- Keep Supabase Management `SUPABASE_READONLY_PAT` for operational monitoring, project-scoped `Database → Read`.
- No service_role in the frontend, GitHub, or Cloudflare Worker. New server-only campaign Edge Function could use project-internal service_role for narrowly enumerated RPC calls; review before deploying.
- Protect Edge Function with a strong separate secret/HMAC; no public business endpoints except a separate opaque-token survey receiver that has its own narrow security controls. Prefer replay-resistant signed requests and audit every write.
- In Cloudflare Worker, require verified Access JWT, exact administrator email, CSRF protection for state-changing requests, no arbitrary SQL or function names accepted from client.
- Preserve `private.founder_attempts` completely. No read/write to app tables (spaces, profiles, tasks, etc).
- Campaign previews contain emails and invite codes and must remain protected, ephemeral, not stored in browser persistent storage or logged.

##### 2. Proposed *new* tables (future migration; do not run yet)

1. `private.founder_waves`: id uuid, name, status (draft/previewed/approved/sending/paused/completed/cancelled), target platform (android/iphone), audience_filter jsonb, requested_couples int, snapshot_created_at, approval_by, approved_at, launch_window, created_at/updated_at; idempotent version, dates, audit metadata.
2. `private.founder_wave_members`: wave_id FK, member_id FK, couple_id FK, member role, language, eligibility snapshot fields, invite status (queued/sending/sent/failed/skipped), invited_at, nudge_count (0..2), last_nudge_at, installed_at nullable, partner_joined_at nullable, last_status_reason; unique (wave_id, member_id). Missing installed_at is **unknown**, NOT "not installed".
3. `private.founder_campaign_outbox`: id, wave_id, member_id, kind (wave_invite/wave_nudge_1/wave_nudge_2/survey), unique (wave_id, member_id, kind), idempotency_key, status (queued/leased/sent/failed/skipped), attempt_count, retry_after, provider_id, last_error, created_at, updated_at, claimed_until. Supports transactional enqueue, retry, and concurrent-worker safety.
4. `private.founder_surveys`: id, wave_id, member_id, opaque_token_hash, scheduled_at, sent_at, completed_at, status, created_at. Unique per member/campaign.
5. `private.founder_survey_responses`: survey_id FK, question keys + controlled response values, submitted_at (no names/emails in link). If free text used, restrict length and retention.
6. `private.founder_campaign_settings`: keyed, server-side approved values for Android closed-test link, iPhone TestFlight link, marketing postal address, optional Founder benefits text. **Never auto-populate with fabricated links, benefits or address.**
7. `private.founder_campaign_audit`: command admin actor, action, wave, timestamp, minimal structured metadata. No tokens/emails in audit payload.

Migrations follow the repo's official timestamped Supabase CLI conventions, are reviewed before application, and include reverse/rollback instructions where safe. Tables remain in `private` with RLS and no public API grants. New `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs: hardened `search_path = ''`, fixed SQL, qualified identifiers, no generic execute SQL, `revoke execute from PUBLIC, anon, authenticated`, `grant execute to service_role`. Prefer definitions in unexposed private schema with a narrowly exposed service-only RPC facade as required by the API.

##### 3. New RPC/business operations (future, pending approval)

- `founder_campaign_preview(filter, count, target)`: read-only, couple-level deterministic selection with member eligibility. Return admin-only preview including couple IDs, chosen recipients, languages, inferred platform compatibility, and rendered email previews; do not send.
- `founder_campaign_create_draft(...)`: save draft and frozen audience definition; no send.
- `founder_campaign_approve(wave_id, snapshot_hash, admin_identity)`: verify identical candidate snapshot, settings, consent, launch links and address; stage outbox records transactionally.
- `founder_campaign_claim_batch(wave_id, limit)`: race-safe claim (row locks, SKIP LOCKED), **re-evaluate eligibility** and duplicates at send time; never return recipients in public API.
- `founder_campaign_record_delivery(outbox_id, provider_id, status)`: append to existing `public.founder_log_email`, update status idempotently; preserve sent vs delivered distinction.
- `founder_campaign_nudges_due(...)`: schedule only for known eligible conditions, maximum two per wave, approved policy.
- `founder_campaign_survey_due(...)`: only after known activation/usage date >=7 days, or specifically verified manual activation; no automatic guesses.
- `founder_campaign_stats(...)`: aggregates only, per-wave sent/failed/opted-out and nullable activation.

An identity-verified server action, not client-side state, must authorize approval and sending. Do not send on a mere GET or page load.

##### 4. Audience selection and wave lifecycle

- Build candidate **couples** ordered by confirmed couple/Founder number and creation time, not by people.
- Filters: status founder/waitlist, platform android/iphone/both/unsure, language en/ar, living together/apart/both/soon/unknown, confirmed-only.
- Define platform eligibility at recipient level (e.g. Android release targets android and both; unsure can be an opt-in inclusion rule approved in UI). For a couple whose members prefer different platforms, explicitly show the mismatch, never silently invite an iOS-only recipient to Android.
- A person is emailable ONLY when `confirmed_at IS NOT NULL AND unsubscribed_at IS NULL AND consent_launch IS TRUE`. One email to each eligible confirmed member; first member receives partner invite link only when partner hasn't confirmed.
- Preview exact couple count, member count, skipped users by reason, selected EN/AR templates, install URLs, partner links, and projected Resend sends.
- Save a frozen snapshot with a hash. Revalidate on approval and **again just before every send**; if an eligible member later unsubscribes, skip them.
- Explicit confirmation screen: “Send N messages to M couples” with irreversible action, plus a typed confirmation.
- Defaults: 20 couples per first wave if 20 exist; current eligible capacity is smaller; never pad with fictitious recipients.
- No duplicate sends: enforce outbox unique key and stable Resend idempotency key `wave-<waveId>-<memberId>-<kind>`, use rate <=5 requests/s or lower actual account rate, and backoff on 429/5xx. Record failed vs provider-accepted vs delivered accurately; delivery requires signed Resend webhook.
- Cancellation stops unsent queue; do not claim it can recall delivered email.

##### 5. Nudges

- Day 3 and Day 7 after invitation, **hard cap 2 nudges per wave** and never the same kind twice.
- Partner-not-joined criterion can be verified from currently confirmed partner membership; mention partner invite when applicable and approved.
- Installation/activation is **unknown** until the app is linked or the admin explicitly records verified status. Null `installed_at` NEVER proves noninstallation. Therefore no automatic “you have not installed” nudges under unknown status.
- Every nudge checks current unsubscribe, consent, account status, wave status and duplicate prevention.
- Disable all automated nudges until the policy and mail templates are approved. A daily job is sufficient.
- Disallow marketing nudges to members who have unsubscribed even if a previous wave was approved.

##### 6. Surveys

- Post-use survey after 7 days of **verified app activation**, not merely 7 days after invite. Until app linking exists, only verified manual activation triggers surveys.
- Three optional questions: onboarding ease, shared usefulness, priority improvement; consent and nonidentifying opaque token. Responses protected in private tables.
- Token is random, unguessable, single-use/expiring as designed, never includes emails or member ID in query string. No user PII sent to GoatCounter or logs.
- Surveys pause if unsubscribed or lacking consent. Include marketing address/unsubscribe headers.

##### 7. Screens to add in Command

1. Founder overview: verified couple counts, confirmed member counts, wave count, pending/failed/opted out.
2. Waves: create, filter, preview exactly who, bilingual rendered email, dry-run, explicit approval, progress and pause/cancel.
3. Nudge queue: confirmed conditions, eligible/ineligible reasons, due dates, max count and delivery history.
4. Surveys: template preview, approved 7-days-after-use scheduler, aggregate responses, export without PII by default.
5. Audit/history: actor/action/time, errors, resend provider IDs and bounded retention.
6. Settings: install link (Android/iOS), sender identity, required physical address, timings, pause-all switch, Founder-benefits optional and disabled until approved.

##### 8. Tests and QA gates

- Exact per-member eligibility and per-couple selection; language, platform, join role, duplicate registration.
- No sends to unconfirmed, unsubscribed, consent-false, wrong-platform, or recently unsubscribed recipients.
- UNIQUE(outbox wave/member/kind) prevents duplicates; retries on failure not on confirmed success; prove using mocked Resend.
- Simulate 429 and server timeout, verify backoff and idempotency.
- EN and Arabic RTL rendering including unsubscription and reply-to plus physical mailing address.
- Verify all user-triggered writes have CSRF + admin authorization; no email/token leak to GoatCounter.
- Ensure manual app activation is explicitly marked verified; unknown never triggers noninstallation nudges/survey.
- Validate audit log and failure handling; no app data tables queried.
- Read-only dry-run first. Never send live email as part of CI.

##### 9. Pre-implementation gates needing owner approval

1. Approve this data model and explicit Admin send/approve workflow **before any database migrations**.
2. Supply the *real* Google Play closed-testing opt-in link and TestFlight link when available.
3. Supply a valid physical mailing address for campaign email compliance; cannot send marketing campaigns before that.
4. Approve all ten EN/AR templates in [FOUNDER_EMAIL_TEMPLATES_DRAFT.md](FOUNDER_EMAIL_TEMPLATES_DRAFT.md).
5. Confirm that public Founder website implementation is stable at the time of migration; agree on no changes to Claude-owned database objects.
6. Require minimum viable audience availability; currently there is only one eligible confirmed member in one Founder couple.

##### 10. Delivery phases

A. Approval-ready design, source audit and email-copy drafts. **Current phase: done.**
B. Migration files plus static UI with mock data, tested in isolated local DB; propose migrations for review (not apply).
C. Apply reviewed additive migrations to the shared Supabase project only after explicit authorization.
D. Build and secure isolated campaign Edge Function, outbox and dry-run routes; inspect 20-couple dry-run when enough candidates exist.
E. Approve and run first real wave. Audit logs, bounces and opt-outs.
F. Enable conditional nudges, then verified activation-based surveys; link app later with a separately approved read-only mapping.

Only show this if the sender is the first confirmed member, the partner is not yet confirmed, and the invite link is still valid. If the partner is confirmed, omit the block. Do not include private invite codes in analytics.

### A95. OneSpace-Command · `docs/FOUNDER_OUTREACH_PLANNER_V09.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Founder Outreach — Production wave planning (v0.9 integration)

**2026-11-17. Initial workflow: preparation only. NO live emails are sent.**

##### Source alignment
- Based on merged PR #32 (latest main), `docs/WEBSITE_TAB.md`, `docs/FOUNDER_CAMPAIGNS_PROPOSAL.md`, `docs/FOUNDER_EMAIL_TEMPLATES_DRAFT.md`, and the existing website `backend/founders/functions/founder-admin.ts`.
- Production alone owns Founder couples/members/email history: `letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz` (Frankfurt). QA `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo` must NEVER be queried for real Founder audiences.
- Existing `founder-admin` POST `resend_pending` sends *transactional confirmation* email; **not** authorized as campaign sender, and deliberately not called.
- No changes to `private.founder_*`, website SQL, Flutter app or Resend; no schema migration needed for this planning phase.

##### Page
New **Founder Outreach** in Command Center navigation after **Website**, Production only with no QA selector.

- Live audience eligibility aggregate from Production-scoped **Database Read** PAT via fixed server SQL (no caller-provided SQL, no PII).
- Eligible member rule: `confirmed_at IS NOT NULL`, `consent_launch IS TRUE`, `unsubscribed_at IS NULL`. Target platform is determined from member preferences. One eligible couple counts as one potential wave slot.
- Counters: Founders, eligible couples, eligible members, pending confirmations, no-consent/opted out.
- Select wave name, Founder or waitlist or all, Android/iPhone/either, communication kind, max 1–100 couples; audience preview shows *possible couples only*, never reserves recipients or estimates individual sends.
- Save/cancel **planning drafts** to existing operational Cloudflare `ALERT_STORE` KV (max 30). Drafts have creator/date, no email addresses, no personal tokens, and no ability to send.
- EN/AR text previews for invite waves and Day 3/Day 7 followups, survey and product update placeholder, with a visible warning that templates are not approved and may contain unresolved placeholders. No HTML emails sent. Copy-to-clipboard is manual.
- Clearly visible nudge rules: Day 3 and Day 7 maximum 2 per wave; never infer non-installation from an unknown usage state. Survey only after verified activation.
- The **Send wave** button is always disabled until the actual sender is delivered and all legal/operational gates pass. The browser has **no credentials or recipient addresses**.

##### Actual sending: explicitly NOT implemented
The previous proposal is an approval draft, not an existing service. Do not display drafts as queued/scheduled/sent, and do not call Resend from Cloudflare using `RESEND_ALERT_API_KEY`.
To safely enable send-through-Command, a separate implementation is required:

1. Approve EN/AR templates and any announcements. Provide verifiable Play closed-test opt-in link (and iOS TestFlight URL when relevant). Provide actual business postal address for footer.
2. Create **additive migration files in the Flutter/Supabase-owned repository**, apply to QA, test, then Production. New `private.founder_waves`, frozen recipient snapshots/outbox, per-wave nudge audit and public service-role-only RPC facade. Never edit legacy founder tables or bypass their ownership.
3. Deploy a dedicated `founder-campaign` Production Edge Function with server-only service role; Cloudflare gets a **separate campaign API secret**, no service role. Enforce Access auth + same-origin POST + explicit dual-confirmation on send; recipient and consent rechecked *at the instant of dispatch*, never just at preview.
4. Outbox claim must be race-safe/idempotent by wave/member/kind; rate-limit and retry; record every provider attempt and append using the existing `founder_log_email` RPC.
5. Approval must show exact recipient-level preview inside authenticated Command (PII never goes into browser storage, analytics or logs); maintain send/pause/cancel/history.
6. Only after actual invitation timestamps exist, schedule Day 3/7 nudges with strict max 2/wave; never nudge unconfirmed/unsubscribed/non-consenting.
7. Surveys require *verified* activation and secure opaque single-use tokens; no guesses from lack of activity.

##### Manual use
1. Open Founder Outreach. Check the Production-only source ribbon and audience eligibility counters.
2. Choose your wave scope and maximum couples. The initial recommended invite wave is **up to 20 Founder couples**, but never pad a smaller real audience.
3. Preview both EN and AR copy. No deployment triggers sends.
4. Save a planning draft. Reopen to review it, or cancel it.
5. Use the locked Send readiness checklist as the work backlog. Work with the product owner on templates, links and mailing address before adding the actual send backend.

##### Readiness status vs exact sent
- Database aggregates prove eligible *potential* recipients only.
- Drafts prove planned intention; they are not outbox items, not approved sends, not delivered messages.
- Resend provider accepted `sent` is not delivered; delivery requires provider event/webhook verification.
- When user count is low, a 20-couple wave cannot be truthfully presented as having 20 recipients.

### A96. OneSpace-Command · `docs/FOUNDER_WAVE_001_READINESS.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Founder Invite Wave 001 — Read-only Readiness Report
Date: 2026-11-17
Status: **NOT READY TO SEND** — no wave created, no database changes, no messages sent.

##### Live audience summary (read-only aggregate SQL)

| Metric | Count |
|---|---:|
| Founder couples registered | 1 |
| Waitlist couples | 0 |
| Total registered members | 1 |
| Confirmed members, consent_launch=true, unsubscribed_at is null | 1 |
| Pending email confirmations | 0 |
| Prior waitlist email log entries | 2 |
| Requested first wave capacity | about 20 couples |

This is **not** a 20-couple dry-run recipient list. Only one eligible confirmed member exists. The single couple is not necessarily complete, and we have not retrieved identifying recipient details.

##### Prerequisites

- [x] Founder schema and existing campaign-related website code reviewed.
- [x] Aggregate eligibility counted from shared Supabase project using SELECT only.
- [x] Resend verified domain is described in existing website code.
- [ ] User approves campaign architecture and additive migrations.
- [ ] Real Google Play closed-testing installation / opt-in link supplied and verified.
- [ ] Postal mailing address for compliance footer provided.
- [ ] English/Arabic draft templates approved.
- [ ] Secure campaign Edge Function and DB migration reviewed and deployed.
- [ ] Consent and unsubscribe state revalidated during send.
- [ ] Enough eligible couples to reach 20, or user selects a smaller approved wave.
- [ ] Exact recipient/email preview generated and approved inside access-protected Command.
- [ ] Separate explicit “Send” confirmation issued.

##### Suggested wave defaults once eligible

`Wave 001`: Android, up to 20 *couples*, first confirmed eligible members in deterministic registration order; partner receives email only if separately confirmed, consented and unsubscribed=false. Platform choice is filtered per recipient, not assumed from a couple’s first member. Continue to show an approximate maximum count until audience reaches that size.

##### What NOT to do now

- Do not call existing `founder-admin` POST `resend_pending`.
- Do not invoke Resend, Founder write RPCs or customer-facing Edge Functions.
- Do not create placeholder couples or use test email addresses to pad campaign numbers.
- Do not infer installations from `installed_at IS NULL`.
- Do not confuse Resend API accepted/sent with delivered: only verified webhooks can establish delivery.

##### Safe next actions

Approve the proposed [data and workflow design](FOUNDER_CAMPAIGNS_PROPOSAL.md), review [draft EN/AR templates](FOUNDER_EMAIL_TEMPLATES_DRAFT.md), and keep infrastructure monitoring independent using the `Database → Read` scoped PAT.

### A97. OneSpace-Command · `docs/GOATCOUNTER_TROUBLESHOOTING.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Active reference (Command Center runbook).*

#### GoatCounter → OneSpace Command diagnostics

**Status:** Revised connector code committed; real credentials are never stored in GitHub.

##### Established facts (2026-11-17)

- GoatCounter website `onespace.goatcounter.com` exists and has a token with **Read statistics** permission limited to that single site.
- OneSpace Command's Communications page currently shows **GoatCounter: Error** while Supabase, Founder and Zoho connections are live.
- The previous adapter did not distinguish network/API/schema issues. No evidence the token's permission is incorrect; do not regenerate it unnecessarily.

##### Fix in the Worker

- Uses `GET https://onespace.goatcounter.com/api/v0/stats/total` with a 30-day UTC date-time rounded to the hour.
- Follows GoatCounter documentation: **Bearer** authorization, `Accept: application/json`, **`Content-Type: application/json`**.
- Accepts nonnegative safe integer totals (including numeric string forms) and subtracts reported `total_events` from `total`.
- Shows zero only when the API actually reports zero pageviews.
- Sanitized failure reasons are returned without upstream bodies, keys, or personal information.

##### How to verify

1. Check **Cloudflare → Workers & Pages → onespace-command → Deployments**, latest GitHub deployment **Ready**.
2. Check **Settings → Variables and Secrets → Production** contains **`GOATCOUNTER_READONLY_TOKEN`** as a secret (not preview only). Do not reveal the value.
3. Open OneSpace Command → **Communications → Reload**.
4. **Live** means the provider reported valid totals. **Error** now indicates one of:
   - `HTTP 400`: malformed request, check date parameters and redeploy latest code.
   - `HTTP 401`: token rejected, inspect correct token saved in Cloudflare production; no need to send it in chat.
   - `HTTP 403`: token does not grant Read statistics to this specific site.
   - `HTTP 429`: GoatCounter rate-limited, retry later.
   - Unexpected totals: API returned fields different from the documented `total`, `total_events`; never substitute made-up counts.
   - Timeout/network: external API unreachable.

No changes to website tracking or GoatCounter privacy settings are necessary for this read-only connection.

Official documentation: https://www.goatcounter.com/help/api and https://www.goatcounter.com/api.json

---

### A98. OneSpace-Command · `docs/INTEGRATIONS.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Command — Integration contract and safe deployment

##### Status
The v0.1 React app is a **development-only, read-only prototype**. It contains a dated QA snapshot from November 17, 2026 (OneSpace Supabase project: database 26 MB, 4 registered users, 59 stored objects, 30.47 MiB files). No fake production numbers are rendered. **No actual periodic metrics collection, automatic alert sending, authentication, or deployment has been implemented.**

##### Future secure API

The frontend calls the same origin:

GET /api/overview?environment=qa
GET /api/overview?environment=production

- Require administrator authentication **and** authorization on every request.
- Use secure, HttpOnly, SameSite cookies with session expiration; require multi-factor authentication at the identity provider.
- Deny cross-origin API access by default, apply rate limits and security headers.
- Never return or log third-party access tokens or personal couple content.
- Return status 401/403 when unauthorized; the client shows an error and no metrics.
- Do not substitute demo data in a production build when the backend fails.

Response interface is defined in src/lib/data.ts. The response must have mode="live", environment matching the requested value, a trustworthy capturedAt timestamp, and aggregates only.

###### Measurements

| Source | Desired values | Caveat |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Supabase Management API | Database size, per-org storage, bandwidth, MAU, function/realtime usage | Organization vs project scope and monthly billing windows must match each limit |
| Supabase SQL (read-only) | Table/database size; user count; object count | SQL cannot infer bandwidth, authentic MAU or total organization storage |
| Resend | Sending totals, delivery/bounce counts | Use restricted server-side key; confirm reporting availability for plan |
| Zoho | Inbox/outgoing counts by owned mailbox/aliases | Check Zoho Mail Lite API access and OAuth scopes first; never scrape or store mail bodies |
| GoatCounter | Pageviews/referrers and event aggregates | Confirm reporting permissions; keep personally identifying data out |
| Founder waitlist | Registrations, founders 1–500, platform preference | Await Claude's actual schema/API; use read-only credentials and aggregate responses |
| Firebase | Crash trends, push status, app versions | Avoid exporting per-user identity data |

###### Alert policy

- Advisory: 60% or greater.
- Warning: 75% or greater.
- Critical: 90% or greater.
- Do not send a repeat alert at every polling cycle.
- Alert on first trusted baseline using an explicit startup policy; the browser helper deliberately does not alert on a comparison with an unknown previous reading.
- Recovery only after falling at least five percentage points below the active threshold.
- Server collector must persist prior metric levels, send acknowledgements, deduplicate notifications, and record retry failures.
- Monitor **data freshness** and collector failures separately from utilization; missing data must never be reported as zero.
- Apply no quota threshold unless measurement and limit have the same scope, units, and billing period.

##### Separation and deployment

- Existing GitHub repos BoyofKay/OneSpace (mobile) and BoyofKay/onespace-app (marketing) remain untouched.
- Existing OneSpace Supabase project remains untouched.
- Production and QA environments must stay segregated: unique credentials and environment-specific endpoints.
- Never deploy dashboard on public GitHub Pages without authenticating access. The repository's privacy flag does **not** protect a published web app.
- Choose hosting and identity provider before deploying (e.g. Cloudflare Access protecting a private hostname, backed by a locked-down serverless API).
- Require administrator MFA and server logs for access to metrics.
- Keep secrets server-side only, encrypted at rest, and rotate when they change.

##### Integration milestones

1. Verify one protected metrics endpoint using a non-production read-only identity.
2. Show live Supabase quotas and a freshness timestamp.
3. Add scheduled collector and durable alert transitions with email/push.
4. Add Resend, then Zoho and GoatCounter if API access supports it.
5. Connect Claude's waitlist only after its backend contract is finalized.
6. Verify mobile installation, alerts, rate limits and disaster-recovery documentation.

---

### A99. OneSpace-Command · `docs/INTEGRATIONS_V03.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Command v0.3 — Monitoring integration guide

**Boundaries:** The Command Worker is an administrator-only service. No mobile app, public website, Founder table, or schema changes are needed. Never commit, screenshot or paste provider secrets in chat. All new connectors are optional and *off by default*.

##### Already verified by the project owner

- Cloudflare Access with matching Worker authentication: live dashboard accessible only to administrator.
- Supabase read-only project-scoped monitoring: live database size, registered and recently signed-in accounts, stored objects and Founder counts.
- Cloudflare KV binding: `ALERT_STORE` attached to `onespace-command-alert-store`.
- Test email through dedicated Resend sending key delivered to Zoho support inbox on November 17, 2026.
- **Not yet verified:** live hourly Cron execution and actual threshold breach notification. Test delivery is separate.

##### v0.3 enhancements

- `Alerts > Run safe check now`: Access-protected, origin-checked POST; reads trusted Supabase aggregates, stores a separate manual-check timestamp in KV. **Never sends email or pretends to be Cron.**
- `Alerts > Scheduled monitoring`: shows actual recorded hourly run, failure states, last test and manual check.
- `Alerts > Recent alert events`: bounded 30-entry audit of Resend accepted/failed and threshold recovery (not proof of inbox delivery).
- `Infrastructure > Recorded history`: latest daily aggregate for each of the last 14 days, kept for a maximum of 21 days in Cloudflare KV. Trends start with successful scheduler observations; historical values aren't invented.
- `Resend account quotas`: optional full-access-key GET /usage connection with daily/monthly limits, not the alert sending key. Broad credentials need separate owner approval.
- `Zoho Mail`: optional read-only Inbox and unread counts (NOT entire mailbox lifetime counts) with hard pagination cap 1000. The address aliases are one mailbox. Count never estimated when exceeding cap.
- `GoatCounter`: 30-day pageviews excluding counted custom events, not unique people; ISO datetime parameters aligned to documentation.

##### Scheduler verification

Worker `wrangler.jsonc` declares `0 * * * *` (every hour at UTC minute zero). On the Worker page, use **Settings → Trigger Events → View events** to confirm Cron invocations, or check the `Alerts` tab for `Latest scheduled check completed`. Cron history may take time to appear after creation or change.

If the last scheduled run reads `No scheduled check recorded yet`, do not interpret that as success or failure. Check the most recent deployed version and whether Cloudflare actually invoked the trigger. `Run safe check now` can verify the monitoring path without altering the Cron heartbeat.

If the last scheduled run says `error`, verify: `SUPABASE_READONLY_PAT` still valid (project-scoped Database Read), `QA_PROJECT_REF` matches, `QA_DATABASE_LIMIT_MB` reflects the actual plan, and the Worker has `ALERT_STORE` binding.

##### GoatCounter (optional and recommended next)

Create a read-only GoatCounter API token (view statistics for site `onespace`, no write/count permissions). Save Cloudflare Production secret `GOATCOUNTER_READONLY_TOKEN`. Optional `GOATCOUNTER_SITE_CODE` defaults to `onespace`. After deployment reload `Communications`. The total is *pageviews over 30 days*, excluding custom events; not unique visitors.

Official docs: https://www.goatcounter.com/help/api

##### Resend account quota reports (optional / broader access)

Resend's `GET https://api.resend.com/usage` **requires a full-access API key**. Do **not** reuse the sending-only alert key. If and only if the broader permission is approved, save the new key as Cloudflare Production secret `RESEND_USAGE_API_KEY`. The adapter only makes GET /usage and does not send or modify messages; however the credential itself could grant more permissions if stolen.

Daily/monthly quotas are **account-wide**, not tied to the app's QA or production environment. They appear only on `Current shared project` to prevent double-counting. `null` limits are shown as unavailable, not 0.

Official docs: https://resend.com/changelog/account-usage-api

##### Zoho Mail Lite Inbox counts (optional / requires explicit OAuth permission)

The website's `hello@`, `founders@`, `privacy@` and `support@` aliases all arrive at the same Zoho Mail Lite mailbox. Do not try to fetch separately per alias.

The Command adapter requires an OAuth application with `ZohoMail.folders.READ` and `ZohoMail.messages.READ` and server-side **Production secrets**:

- `ZOHO_ACCOUNT_ID`: Zoho numeric mail account ID (not an email address).
- `ZOHO_CLIENT_ID`: OAuth client identifier.
- `ZOHO_CLIENT_SECRET`: OAuth secret.
- `ZOHO_REFRESH_TOKEN`: OAuth refresh token with read-only scopes.
- `ZOHO_REGION`: optional `com` / `eu` / `in` / `com.au`. Default `com` matches `mail.zoho.com` accounts. **Use your account's actual data-center region.**

The Worker exchanges the refresh token server-side, reads folder metadata and the Inbox message listing, and only returns numeric counts. **No names, emails, subjects, recipients, message bodies or OAuth tokens enter the dashboard or KV.** If Inbox exceeds 1,000 messages, the adapter shows unavailable instead of pretending the count is complete. These are current-Inbox counts, not all-time received counts, and therefore omit archived or moved messages.

Read-only OAuth scope can technically permit access to email content if the credential is misused. **Do not connect this without explicit approval.** The code does not fetch message bodies.

Official docs:
- https://www.zoho.com/mail/help/api/get-all-folder-details.html
- https://www.zoho.com/mail/help/api/get-emails-list.html

##### Unconnected by design

- Supabase organization-wide storage/egress/MAU limits require matching billing-period consumption and quotas, not a project database query.
- Firebase Crashlytics/Cloud Messaging analytics requires suitable reporting credentials and correct environment setup.
- Separate production Supabase monitoring requires a production project reference and read-only credential authorized for that project. Do not represent the current shared project as independent QA/production.
- Real Founder invitation waves, nudges and surveys are a separate approval-gated business workflow and have not been deployed in this iteration.

##### Billing and safety

The current design uses Cloudflare Workers, KV and separate read-only Supabase API queries. The presence of a Free tier does not guarantee all usage stays free forever. Track actual provider billing pages, API traffic and rate limits. Each monitoring record contains only aggregate usage numbers and timestamps. `Never artificially fill your database to test capacity alerts.\n

### A100. OneSpace-Command · `docs/MANUAL_VERIFICATION_V08.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Manual verification workflow — OneSpace Command

Manual verification supplements provider probes. It **never** overrides Live, Missing, Error, Retired, Excluded or Planned statuses.

##### What an administrator can do

1. Open **Systems & Connections**, clear **Needs attention only** if necessary.
2. Find a row labeled **Needs verification** and click **Review / Verify**.
3. Follow the task-specific checklist. Open the linked release/provider screen and actually perform the checks.
4. For Android build/release rows, use **Record release change** to record the version/tag you inspected.
5. Describe what you personally tested (at least 10 characters), optionally link to the evidence (HTTPS only), affirm the checkbox and select **Confirm manual verification**.
6. The row becomes **Manually verified** (not Live) and includes who, when, release and the latest evidence.
7. Use **Reopen for verification** after meaningful changes or failed checks. Events are kept in a per-component KV history.

Release-specific sign-offs are invalidated when the administrator **records a different release version**. Because `BoyofKay/onespace` is private and no GitHub release-feed credential is configured in the Worker, the Command Center cannot **automatically detect** new GitHub releases at present. Adding a trusted, authenticated GitHub release signal is a separate integration. Do not describe this as release-change autodetection.

##### Scheduled monitoring, verified from the relevant screen

Under **Alerts → Production → Scheduled monitoring**, click **Refresh status**. The existing **Run safe check now** is only a manual read: it does not execute the scheduled Cron or send alerts.

If the recorded scheduled run is `ok`, checked one or more comparable resources, and is less than two hours old, **Confirm I reviewed the latest scheduled run** records a separate human attestation of those facts. The server independently checks the same conditions before saving. Manual review cannot turn a failed or stale Cron into Live, and cannot prove email delivery. The audit is visible in **Systems & Connections → Hourly monitoring Cron**.

##### Security / storage

- Cloudflare Access cryptographically authorizes every request; POST also requires same-origin `Origin` and a small validated JSON body.
- Attestations are stored in the existing Cloudflare `ALERT_STORE` KV namespace, under `manual-verification:v1:<known-id>`; no Supabase database changes or migrations required.
- Only eligible, known system IDs are writable. Administrator identity is the configured, authenticated `ADMIN_EMAIL`, never a client-supplied value.
- Notes limited to 600 characters, HTTPS evidence links without embedded credentials, release IDs limited to 90 characters with a conservative character whitelist. No secret values or log bodies returned.
- Each item retains the latest 20 timestamped historical events. Cloudflare KV is eventually consistent and does not provide strong transactional audit semantics; this is a single-admin operational verification history, **not** an immutable compliance-grade audit ledger.
- Version and verification changes must be recorded independently per environment (QA vs Production).

##### Status meanings

- **Live:** Provider request succeeded or current automatic monitoring evidence passed.
- **Configured:** Settings are present but behavior not fully established.
- **Needs verification:** Manual/procedural evidence still required.
- **Manually verified:** Administrator attested checks with date and evidence; not a provider connectivity probe.
- **Needs setup / Error:** Actual configuration problem or failed automatic probe, never overridden by a manual click.

##### Future improvement

To automatically invalidate app attestations on every new GitHub QA release or Play build, connect the private repository through a least-privilege authenticated release-feed or signed deployment event. This is not silently enabled and should not use the broad Cloudflare deployment token as an app-release webhook credential.

### A101. OneSpace-Command · `docs/MARKETING_POSTS_V12.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Marketing Posts — manual social campaign library (v1.2)

This is a **separate tab** in OneSpace Command: \`Marketing Posts\`, placed next to Website and Founder Outreach.

##### Purpose and scope

- 10 ready-made, concise social posts (2 lengths each): Reddit LDR/relationships; Facebook LDR/relationships/immigration EN+Egyptian Arabic; WhatsApp immigration EN+Egyptian Arabic; Telegram immigration EN+Egyptian Arabic.
- Egyptian colloquial Arabic for community posts rather than Modern Standard Arabic. Existing Founder Outreach **email templates are not changed**.
- Four filters (platform, audience, language, length), search, edited draft text, copy controls, manually marked posting status.
- All content is intended to be reviewed and posted **manually**, subject to subreddit or group promotion rules. No social authentication, auto-posting, email, scheduling, or data requests.
- All campaign links lead to \`https://onespace.live/\` with \`utm_source\`, \`utm_medium\`, \`utm_campaign\`; each post has its own unique campaign code.
- Post copy and campaign names are sourced from \`src/lib/marketing-posts.ts\`.

##### Copy actions and platform limitations

- **Reddit / Telegram:** “Copy with embedded link” puts \`text/html\` on the clipboard with a normal clickable anchor, and \`text/plain\` Markdown fallback; verify the editor preserved the clickable link. Reddit’s Markdown editor supports Markdown link syntax. Telegram support varies by client/editor. Do not promise hidden links survive every paste.
- **Facebook / WhatsApp:** “Copy clean post” uses only \`onespace.live\`. This intentionally does NOT preserve the individual campaign UTM. The separate “Copy with tracked URL” and “Copy link” actions preserve UTM but expose the full address. Getting both a pretty short URL and guaranteed UTM requires **future short-link redirects configured on the website/domain**. No made-up \`/go/\` routes.
- Only the established homepage and existing public Founder signup are used. **No website code, URLs or database objects were modified**.

##### Privacy, persistence, and meaning of status

- Edits and manually marked posting status use browser \`localStorage\` on the Command Center domain; they **do not sync across browsers/devices**, do not prove a post was actually published, and do not count as campaign performance analytics.
- On a new browser or cleared storage, the original drafts appear. Reset version restores the original shipped text.
- No secrets, user personal data, posts, or recipient lists are sent to Supabase, Cloudflare KV, Resend or social platforms by the tab.
- Existing Founder Outreach approvals and send-lock protections are unaffected.

##### Acceptance tests

1. \`npm test\` verifies 10 unique posts, both lengths, Egyptian wording, UTM/URL construction, and HTML escaping.
2. \`npm run build\` verifies React and TypeScript compile.
3. Manual checks: light/dark, phone/desktop, language filter, edit and reset, clipboard fallback, no automatic sends, plus navigation from Website → Marketing Posts → Founder Outreach.
4. Production deploy happens through the Cloudflare Workers Builds git connection (tests run before every build) and must remain behind Cloudflare Access.

##### Follow-up feature (optional)

A small, separate \`onespace.live/go/\` redirect system can enable genuinely short branded URLs for WhatsApp/Facebook while retaining tracking; this would require **a separately approved website change**, and should not be implied to exist today.

### A102. OneSpace-Command · `docs/MOBILE_CUSTOMER_HEALTH_V06.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Command v0.6 — Mobile customer health and lifecycle

##### Scope

Extends **Mobile App**, not the marketing website. Existing Founder signups and GoatCounter reports are unchanged. Uses **only fixed, read-only, aggregate SQL** against the existing shared QA Supabase project. No new tables, permissions, app telemetry, backend triggers, subscription integration, production modifications, customer email or mobile releases.

##### Actual measurements

| Metric | Definition, source and caveats |
|---|---|
| Couples living together | Paired `public.spaces` with `relationship_mode = 'living_together'` |
| Long distance, same timezone | Paired spaces with `relationship_mode='apart_same_tz'` |
| Long distance, different timezones | Paired spaces with `relationship_mode='apart_different_tz'` |
| Mode not selected | Paired spaces in `not_set` state |
| Unpaired spaces | All spaces minus paired (at least two distinct members) spaces. Not confirmed abandoned onboarding |
| No sign-in for 10/20/30 days | Auth accounts **older than the window** whose `last_sign_in_at` is older than the window or null |
| Never signed in | All currently non-deleted, non-anonymous Auth accounts with null `last_sign_in_at` (regardless of age) |
| No contribution for 10/20/30 days | Auth accounts older than window with no newly created supported feature or collaboration records during window |
| Suggestion counts | `kind='idea'` in `public.support_requests`; pre-sign-in support table does not support ideas |
| Technical issue counts | `kind='issue'` across authenticated and pre-sign-in support tables; **not necessarily customer complaints** |
| General inquiry and account help | `kind='contact'` and `kind='account'`, respectively, across support tables |
| Open backlog | `status in ('new','in_progress')` across both support tables |
| Answered/closed | `status in ('answered','closed')` |
| Support requests last 30 days | Requests created in last rolling 30 days |
| All support requests | Authenticated + pre-sign-in mobile help requests (may also include other pre-login help channels using the same table) |

The inactivity windows are **cumulative**, not mutually exclusive. A person is included only if their **account age** exceeds the threshold to avoid classifying brand-new accounts as churned. A user may open the app on a restored session without generating an Auth sign-in; a user may read content without creating anything. **These are risk indicators, not verified inactive accounts.**

The new public-support source may also be used by other pre-authentication entry points. We cannot reliably attribute each entry to a platform if no platform is stored. All support counts are requests rather than unique customers.

##### KPIs that cannot be truthfully reported today

| Requested KPI | What is missing | Future design |
|---|---|---|
| **User country** | No user-declared country in customer profile; saved places / configured holidays do not indicate residence | Explicit opt-in country selection (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2) at onboarding, editable, one value per user, aggregate only |
| **Average concurrent active users** | No live online/offline presence or session heartbeat; database Auth sessions may survive closed apps | Privacy-aware authenticated presence with bounded TTL (e.g. 5-minute expiry), sample online accounts every 5 minutes, compute mean over a specified period; display country only separately |
| **Truly inactive users >10/20/30 days** | No last foreground app activity | App foreground event / last-seen timestamp with consent and cutoff rules |
| **Explicit complaints** | Mobile support form only classifies `contact`, `issue`, `idea`; no complaint type | Add `complaint` to mobile form + schema under separate approved app migration |
| **Cancelled / non-renewed subscriptions** | No subscription or billing provider records are connected | Use authoritative Google Play / App Store / subscription service webhooks; distinguish cancellation requested from access expiry, non-renewal and involuntary payment failure |
| **Churn, renewal and recovery rate** | Same as above | Report active paid subscriptions, cancels, expirations, billing failures, restores, renewals, MRR and cohort retention once pricing launches |

Privacy notes: never infer country from IP or timezone alone; never read or expose precise location. Do not put subscription metadata, support message content, usernames, email addresses, customer IDs or private couple records in Command API responses.

##### Other recommended future KPIs

- Invitation-to-pairing conversion and elapsed time to partner joining
- Time to first shared activity, both-partner activation in week one
- First-session drop-off and onboarding completion
- Weekly active couples, weekly active users, true DAU/MAU and DAU/MAU stickiness
- D1/D7/D30 retention and cohort-based resurrection
- Feature **opened** versus **record created** funnels
- Crash-free users and crash-free sessions, error rate by version and platform
- Support first-response time, resolution time, complaint rate per 1000 active users and satisfaction
- Subscription trial conversion, cancellations vs expired subscriptions, voluntary and involuntary churn

##### Testing and release

1. Open Command → Mobile App → **Couple segments**. Counts must reconcile to paired spaces.
2. Inspect **Customer inactivity risk**. Confirm cumulative 10/20/30-day counts and never-signed-in values.
3. Inspect **Voice of customer**. The app's `idea` and `issue` categories are separate. **Explicit complaints** must display unavailable rather than assume each technical issue is a complaint.
4. Inspect **Geo & customer lifecycle**. Country, average concurrency, true activity and subscription churn remain unavailable.
5. Verify website KPIs and other Command tabs remain unchanged.
6. Verify switching to unconfigured Production cannot reuse QA counts.

**No customer emails, file contents, detailed tickets or personal identifiers are returned in this API.**

### A103. OneSpace-Command · `docs/MOBILE_PRODUCT_KPIS_V05.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Command v0.5 — Mobile product intelligence

##### Scope and boundaries

**This dashboard measures the mobile product, not the public website.** The existing Founder waitlist and GoatCounter integrations remain independent.

Data source: existing OneSpace Supabase project using the current *project-scoped, read-only Management API token*. All SQL in `worker/mobile-kpis.ts` is fixed and aggregate-only.

No database schema changes, app releases, triggers, new SDKs, customer communications, or alteration of the mobile app were performed.

The current selected project is **Current shared project / QA**, even if it contains working user data. Do not present those figures as publicly launched production customer growth.

##### Exact KPI catalog

| KPI | Definition | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Registered app accounts | Non-anonymous, non-deleted Supabase Auth users | `auth.users` |
| New accounts, 7/30d | Such accounts created during rolling windows | `auth.users.created_at` |
| Spaces | All created shared spaces | `public.spaces` |
| Paired couples | Spaces with at least two distinct space members | `public.space_members` |
| New spaces, 30d | Shared spaces created during last 30 days | `public.spaces.created_at` |
| Contributing couples, 7/30d | Distinct *paired spaces* with at least one supported user-created feature record | 13 feature tables with `created_at` |
| Contributing members, 30d | Distinct author account IDs that created a supported record in a paired space | Same tables |
| Both-partner contributing couples, 30d | Paired spaces with at least two distinct contributing authors, 30d | Same tables |
| New feature records, 30d | Number of new top-level objects across supported features in paired spaces | Same tables |
| Feature adoption | Paired spaces with at least one feature record since records began | 13 feature tables |
| Feature creations, 30d | Newly created records in each feature, paired spaces only | 13 feature tables |
| Collaboration actions, 30d | Created activity comments/components/decisions/reactions, shared-experience comments and trip messages in paired spaces | 6 interaction tables |
| Collaborating members, 30d | Distinct accounts who made one of those interaction records | 6 interaction tables |
| Audit actions, 30d | Logged record actions in paired spaces; **can overlap** with other activity counts | `record_action_history` |
| Daily chart | UTC day of new feature records, contributing paired spaces, or registration | Timestamp aggregates over last 14 UTC days |
| Client error reports, 7/30d | Number of app client-error report rows in rolling windows; not unique crashes | `client_errors` |
| Push-enabled accounts | Distinct user IDs with at least one enabled registered push device | `push_devices` |
| Android/iOS push accounts | Distinct accounts with enabled push devices of each platform; same person may appear in both | `push_devices.platform` |

**Automated love gestures are excluded** from feature creations, so they do not inflate human-generated actions.

All dates use rolling intervals for the summaries and UTC calendar days for the 14-day chart. Aggregate counts are recalculated on dashboard refresh. Creation of a record is not proof of an app opening that day; editing the same object is not an additional creation. A couple may use OneSpace without creating objects in the measured window.

##### Not measurable yet — do not invent

- True daily/monthly active users (DAU/MAU), session duration or frequency.
- D1/D7/D30 returning cohorts and churn.
- Screen visits, search conversion, feature opening, taps and time in screen.
- Install/uninstall counts or store-page-to-activation conversion.
- Notification delivered/opened rate.
- Paying subscribers, ARR, churn, and revenue.

Supabase billable Auth MAU is also different: it counts distinct users making an Auth action within the **billing cycle**, not distinct creators of content over a rolling 30-day period.

##### Future instrumentation (requires owner approval before app change)

Implement a privacy-preserving event vocabulary **only after an explicit mobile app change request**:

- Session: `app_opened`, `session_started`, `session_ended` with coarse durations.
- Activation: `account_created`, `space_created`, `partner_invited`, `partner_joined`, `first_shared_action`.
- Feature funnels: `feature_opened`, `item_created`, `item_completed`, `share_attempted`, `share_success`.
- Reliability: `app_startup_failed`, `navigation_error` with stackless categorization; link to existing client errors without collecting private text.
- Notification: delivery receipt only if supported; `notification_opened` without message contents.
- Privacy: no raw note text, partner messages, contacts, image files, trip details, location, IP address, sensitive relationship categories or precise device identifiers.

Ensure consent/opt-out, restricted retention, minimum data, couple/partner privacy controls, and event-schema versioning. The initial Command dashboard reads existing tables only.

##### QA verification

1. Go to Command → **Mobile App** (new top-level tab).
2. Choose **Current shared project** and confirm the environment banner appears.
3. Compare registrations with Supabase Auth, paired spaces with `space_members`, and client error count with the `client_errors` table.
4. Switch the daily chart metric and inspect feature adoption and collaboration breakdown.
5. Select **Production** without a configured production reference. It must show *Unavailable*, never copy QA numbers into production.
6. Confirm no email addresses, profile names, message bodies, file paths, phone contacts, tokens or per-user rows are visible.
7. Website and Founder tabs must behave exactly as before.

##### Performance caution

The live aggregate uses a fixed SQL CTE over existing feature tables. It is appropriate for current small QA volumes but needs scheduled pre-aggregation/caching and a query-plan review before many thousands of couples arrive. Never add indexes or change the shared app schema without a reviewed migration and explicit owner authorization.

##### Source of truth

- Read-only query: `worker/mobile-kpis.ts`
- Protected API: `GET /api/mobile/kpis?environment=qa`
- Frontend: `Mobile App` tab in `src/App.tsx`
- Tests: `worker/mobile-kpis.test.ts`

Provider reference: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/manage-your-usage/monthly-active-users

### A104. OneSpace-Command · `docs/NEXT_STEPS_V11.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Command — v1.1 handover (17 Nov 2026)

##### Read first (anyone changing this repo, including ChatGPT)
1. `git pull` the latest `main` before any change. v1.0 (PR #36) and v1.1 are merged and deployed.
2. Don't re-add what v1.0 removed: the **Audience** tab, the Founder/website stats on **Communications**, and the **Alerts** slider.
   The Website tab and Systems page already cover them. Details: `docs/REVIEW_V10_APP_USAGE.md`.
3. Shared chart pieces live in `src/lib/charts.tsx` (`Bars`, `DayBars`). Every chart shows the count **and** the %.
4. `/api/mobile/kpis` returns `{...kpis, engagement, usage, complaintsTotal}`. Engagement and usage are read separately,
   so a failure in one never blanks the page.
5. Every database change is a migration, applied to QA first and then Production. App migrations go in `BoyofKay/onespace`
   (`supabase/schema`); website/Founder migrations go in `BoyofKay/onespace-app` (`backend/founders`).

##### v1.1 — what was added
| Item | Status | Where |
|---|---|---|
| In-app usage: session length, sessions, total time, tabs, top screens, notification open rate | **Built.** Data starts with app build R81. | App `lib/core/usage_events.dart`, migration `0137_app_usage_events.sql` (QA + Prod), `worker/app-usage.ts`, Mobile tab "In-app usage" |
| Complaint category | **Built** | App support form topic "Complaint", website topic "Make a complaint", migration `0138` (QA + Prod), Mobile tab "Voice of customer" |
| Production secrets rows verified automatically | **Built** | Website repo migration `0139` (cron every 30 min → `private.ops_secret_status`), `worker/secret-status.ts`, Systems rows turn green or red |
| Google Play installs / uninstalls / store conversion | **Placeholder** | `worker/play-reports.ts` (5 setup steps in the header), Systems row `play-reports` |
| Subscriptions / churn / revenue | **Not applicable** (no paid plans) | Systems row `billing` = excluded. When plans exist: Google Play Real-time Developer Notifications + App Store Server Notifications → an Edge Function → `private.subscription_events`. |

###### Privacy rules for usage events (decision D78)
- Only an event name (`session_start`, `session_end` + seconds, `tab/<name>`, `screen/<WidgetClass>`, `push_open/<type>`), a number and a time. Never content.
- On by default. People can switch it off in app Settings → Privacy → "Share usage statistics".
- At most 200 events per call and 3,000 per day per person. Kept 180 days, and deleted with the account.
- TODO (privacy page / data export): add one line about usage statistics to the app privacy policy, and include the
  `app_events` row count in `export_my_data` in the next app iteration.

##### Founder email automation — design (not built; needs approvals listed at the end)
Today the Founder Outreach page plans waves and previews emails, but sending is locked (`canSend: false`).

###### What has to be linked
1. **Sender stays in Supabase Production.** It reuses `RESEND_API_KEY` and the email templates in `backend/founders/functions/_lib.ts`.
   The Command Center never holds the sending key.
2. **New migration (QA first):** two tables.
   - `private.founder_campaigns`: id, kind, cohort, platform, limit, status (draft → approved → sending → sent/cancelled), approved_by/at, template version.
   - `private.founder_campaign_recipients`: campaign_id, member_id, status, provider_id, sent_at. It has `unique(campaign_id, member_id)`, so nobody gets the same email twice.
3. **New Edge Function `founder-campaign`:** `preview`, `approve` and `send-test` (to the admin only) endpoints.
   - It re-checks consent, confirmation and unsubscribe status at send time.
   - It adds `List-Unsubscribe` headers, as the existing sender already does.
4. **Link Command Center → Supabase:** one shared secret, `COMMAND_CAMPAIGN_KEY`, set as both a Cloudflare secret and a Supabase secret.
   The Worker calls the function server-side behind Cloudflare Access plus the admin-email check.
5. **Queue drain:** a pg_cron job every 5 minutes sends queued recipients at a safe rate. Day-3 and day-7 nudges are queued automatically.
6. **Delivery feedback:** a Resend webhook goes to an Edge Function `resend-webhook`, verified with `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET`.
   It records delivered, bounced and complained, and suppresses bounces and complaints automatically.
7. **Who gets the nudge:** "only people who haven't installed yet" needs the Founder ↔ app-account email match (decision D76, pending).
   Until then, nudges go to everyone in the wave who hasn't clicked their personal install link (tracked by a redirect `onespace.live/go/<token>`).

###### Inputs needed from Ahmed before sending is unlocked
- ~~Postal address~~ — provided 17 Nov 2026 (`MAILING_ADDRESS`).
- The Play closed-testing opt-in link (`{{APP_INSTALL_LINK}}`).
- ~~Final approval of the EN/AR templates~~ — approved 17 Nov 2026.
- Approval of D76 (Founder ↔ app match), or acceptance of the click-based nudges.
- **Resend plan (decided 17 Nov 2026):** stay on Free. Sending is Founders-only and temporary (it retires with the website's
  "Join as a Founder" button). Pace invites at about 70/day so normal confirmations and support emails keep headroom under the 100/day cap.

##### Email allowance KPI (17 Nov 2026)
`GET /api/email/allowance` → Resend `GET /usage` (needs Cloudflare secret `RESEND_USAGE_API_KEY`, a full-access key used only to read).
Shown on **Founder Outreach** (with "this draft needs N emails ≈ D days") and **Communications**. "Safe to send now" keeps 30/day and
300/month for confirmations, password resets, support and alerts. The future sender must read the same numbers and stop at that line.

### A105. OneSpace-Command · `docs/QA_PRODUCTION_SPLIT_V08.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Active reference (Command Center runbook).*

#### OneSpace Command v0.8 — QA / Production isolation (17 November 2026)

##### Approved environment map (decision D77)
| | QA | Production |
|---|---|---|
| App package | `onespace.qa` | `live.onespace.app` |
| Supabase | `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo` (Seoul) | `letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz` (Frankfurt) |
| Use | Development and test records | Real couples, Google Play closed-testing Founders, live Founder waitlist |
| Firebase | One shared Firebase project `onespace-b70a2`, **QA Android registration** | Same Firebase project, **Production Android registration** |
| Founder authoritative data | **Not read** | `private.founder_couples`, `private.founder_members`, `private.founder_email_log` |

**Fail-closed behavior:** Worker validates the exact approved project refs on every overview, mobile KPI and infrastructure query. Production cannot silently use QA. Read-only access tokens are also isolated per environment. Cloudflare's Access JWT and admin email allowlist protect every request. A missing Production token produces unavailable data, not fake zeroes or QA counts.

The Command Center is a separate operations application. It does not modify app databases, migrations, website, Founder tables, or mobile releases. No Founder send, invite wave, nudge, survey or billing operation is enabled by this release.

##### Required Cloudflare Worker settings

Use **Workers & Pages → onespace-command → Settings → Variables and Secrets → Production**.
`wrangler.jsonc` contains the two *public project references*; ensure the **deployed Worker** has the same values. Deploy this release before using the new Firebase parameterized endpoint.

| Setting | Kind | Value |
|---|---|---|
| `QA_PROJECT_REF` | Variable | `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo` |
| `PRODUCTION_PROJECT_REF` | Variable | `letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz` |
| `SUPABASE_READONLY_PAT` | **Secret** | Existing scoped **QA** Management API PAT, Database: Read only |
| `PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_READONLY_PAT` | **Secret** | **New** Production-scoped Management PAT, Database: Read only |
| `FIREBASE_REPORTING_PROJECT_ID` | Variable | `onespace-b70a2` |
| `FIREBASE_REPORTING_QA_ANDROID_APP_ID` | Variable | Actual **Firebase App ID** for package `onespace.qa`, format `1:...:android:...` |
| `FIREBASE_REPORTING_PRODUCTION_ANDROID_APP_ID` | Variable | Actual **Firebase App ID** for `live.onespace.app` |
| `FIREBASE_REPORTING_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` | **Secret** | Existing dedicated Google **Viewer** account JSON; **never** the push sender |

Other current secrets (Cloudflare Access, alerts, Resend, Zoho and GoatCounter) remain as-is. The old `FIREBASE_REPORTING_ANDROID_APP_ID` variable is obsolete and deliberately ignored. Remove it *after* confirming the new two IDs; **do not remove** the reporting service-account JSON. Do not paste any key into GitHub, ChatGPT, docs, screenshots or frontend.

###### Create Production read-only PAT
In **Supabase Dashboard → Account → Access Tokens**, create a new scoped token limited to **OneSpace Production** (`letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz`) with **Database → Read**. Do **not** use a classic all-project token, QA token or service_role key. Store it only as Cloudflare Production Secret `PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_READONLY_PAT` and deploy. The original `SUPABASE_READONLY_PAT` continues monitoring QA.

Do not automatically reuse an old 500 MB **Free-plan** capacity threshold. The organization is now on **Pro**, and this release removes the default QA 500 MB limit from `wrangler.jsonc`. Inspect and remove any stale Cloudflare `QA_DATABASE_LIMIT_MB=500`; configure `QA_DATABASE_LIMIT_MB` / `PRODUCTION_DATABASE_LIMIT_MB` only if the actual per-project allocation is verified. Until then, the dashboard reports usage with **unknown quota**, and no unverified percentage alerts.

###### Firebase registration
In the existing Firebase project `onespace-b70a2`, register Android packages `onespace.qa` and `live.onespace.app` separately. Copy each **Firebase App ID**, NOT package name or Firebase project number. Put these two IDs in the corresponding Cloudflare **Variables** above. Enable **Firebase Cloud Messaging Data API** and keep `onespace-command-monitor` at **Firebase Cloud Messaging API Viewer** only.

The existing service-account JSON for sending pushes belongs in **Production Supabase Edge Function Secrets** as `ONESPACE_FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`; it is distinct from Command's Viewer JSON. App-side new `google-services.json` and GitHub `ONESPACE_GOOGLE_SERVICES_JSON_BASE64` are **the mobile team's separate release task**. Command cannot fix app-side push registration by itself.

FCM Data API reports sampled, delayed Android diagnostics (not push opens, true DAU/MAU or all sends). Each dashboard environment now has a separate GET endpoint `/api/integrations/firebase?environment=qa|production`. Errors distinguish invalid format, OAuth, 403 API/IAM, 404 app registration, 429 and upstream issues without returning secrets or raw Google payloads.

##### Validation / release gates

1. Open the feature PR, verify `npm run build` and `npm test` in **Command CI**. **Merge only after passing.** CI does not contact Supabase or send mail.
2. Deploy the Command Worker using the repo workflow. Confirm Cloudflare Access still denies unauthenticated visitors.
3. **Production** (default): Overview Supabase connected to Frankfurt; Founder waitlist and email logs show Production; Mobile App should show newly empty real-user metrics until Founders activate; Firebase can show **Pending** until the production Android registration exists.
4. Switch to **QA · Seoul**: app metrics return test users; Audience explicitly says **Production-only**; Founder email totals are hidden, and no QA `private.founder_*` query occurs.
5. For each environment: refresh **Mobile App → Android notification diagnostics** and verify it uses that environment's registered App ID. Low volume can result in connected with no diagnostic samples.
6. **Alerts → Run safe check now** uses selected environment with separate QA/Production manual check history; no outgoing email. Hourly scheduler can check each connected environment independently; any historical QA Founder samples are masked.
7. Confirm existing Resend, Zoho and GoatCounter integrations. Their reports are **account/site-wide**, not split into QA/Production.
8. Check the website PR #30 independently before pointing Founders to the new backend. **Command does not merge or deploy the website.**

##### Deliberately not part of this change
- Founder invite waves, nudges, surveys, billing/subscription churn: **design/approval pending**. No new tables or migrations.
- Production app analytics instrumentation, Crashlytics, GA4, country, true concurrent sessions or installed-device counts: requires product instrumentation and explicit measurement/consent design.
- Android push setup in the two Flutter flavors and Google Play closed testing: separate repository/workflow.
- Apple/iOS FCM diagnostics: Android-only API.

##### Systems & Connections inventory (Command v0.8)
The new **Systems & Connections** sidebar tab is a searchable, read-only operating register, including QA, Production, shared, retired and future systems. It includes mobile apps, Google Play closed testing, Supabase environments, Founder-only Production data, GitHub repositories, APK/AAB workflows, Firebase client registrations, FCM reporting/sending identity separation, Cloudflare Worker/Access/KV/Cron, website/email analytics, and provider-scoped tokens.

**Status semantics**:
- **Live**: the protected Command Worker just received a successful provider response (not proof that every end-user workflow works)
- **Configured**: required Cloudflare setting is present, or source is observed; may still need functional verification
- **Needs setup / Error**: missing setting or failed/mismatched connection, respectively
- **Needs verification**: GitHub, Google Play, public website or Supabase Edge secret states that the Command Worker cannot safely read
- **Planned / Retired / Excluded**: intentionally absent or not part of this environment

The inventory never returns any secret values, OAuth tokens, service-role keys, email bodies or individual people. It is protected by the same Cloudflare Access administrator check as all API routes; GET `/api/systems` does not write or send. **Refresh** performs read-only diagnostics. The status page must never claim a Google Play release or Supabase Edge secret is verified simply because a related Cloudflare variable exists.

The table is independent of the selected environment and remains accessible even if Production Supabase is not connected. Filter by Production/QA/Shared/Future and show unresolved items only. For a public website or externally managed credential, follow its management-console link to verify manually.

##### Page ownership and selection behavior (navigation fix)

- **Overview, Mobile App, Infrastructure, Alerts**: page-level QA / Production selector. It lives next to Reload and is **not** a universal top-bar setting. Selection remains in memory when moving between pages, and resets to Production after a browser reload. A visible source ribbon names the exact application, project and location.
- **Audience**: Production-only Founder data. No QA selector. QA Founder tables are not queried.
- **Communications**: global Resend/Zoho/GoatCounter reporting, plus **Production-only** Founder email-log aggregates. No QA selector or duplicate environment-specific accounting.
- **Systems & Connections**: covers all environments and current/pending/future sources in one table. No QA selector.
- Storage explorer: ranked descending by actual bytes across PostgreSQL table+index and storage bucket aggregate components. Relative bars are measured against the largest listed component, **not** common quotas or equivalent billing units.
- Feature adoption: sort selector for adopting couples, 30-day created records and all-time created records, with highest-first/lowest-first toggle. Bars use the selected measure.

###### Why the live page might still show v0.7
PR #21 is a GitHub draft branch and **does not deploy itself**. Main branch and Cloudflare Worker must be updated in separate stages.

1. After **Command CI** passes, review PR #21 and merge to main.
2. For first use of the repo's manually triggered **Deploy OneSpace Command to Cloudflare** workflow, add the needed GitHub Actions **repository secret** under `BoyofKay/OneSpace-Command → Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions`:
   - `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` — newly created Cloudflare API token scoped to this account's **Workers Scripts: Edit** with necessary additional binding permissions (e.g. KV edit only if namespace provisioning requires it); never a global API key
3. Configure the **Cloudflare Worker secrets** separately from these GitHub deployment secrets. The Worker needs its existing Access JWT and Resend/Zoho secrets; add `PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_READONLY_PAT` and the two registered Firebase app IDs for the new architecture. Do not paste values into GitHub code or chat.
4. In **GitHub → Actions → Deploy OneSpace Command to Cloudflare → Run workflow** select **main**. It runs build+tests before publishing. The workflow fails closed if either deployment credential is absent.
5. Visit the protected URL and verify footer version **v0.8**, Systems & Connections sidebar, page-only selector, and active source ribbon. Compare QA and Production independently. If a Production token or Firebase App ID is missing, the dashboard correctly displays an unavailable status rather than borrowing QA metrics.

Do not attempt to run the deployment workflow from a feature branch or merge a PR with failed CI. Never replace the original Firebase sending service account with the read-only reporting JSON.

**Cloudflare deployment safety:** Wrangler configuration now sets `keep_vars: true` so nonsecret dashboard variables (such as the Firebase App IDs) are not discarded when the Worker code is deployed. Cloudflare preserves existing encrypted secrets automatically; still verify that the correct Worker and account are selected before deploying.

###### 17 Nov 2026 deployment Account ID correction
Cloudflare rejected the previous GitHub `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` secret as malformed (the CI output masked the original). The Cloudflare account ID is not a secret and is now pinned in `wrangler.jsonc` as `account_id=0b38c8377a99638b6028d8d1aae777eb`, grounded in the logged-in account's Cloudflare dashboard URL. GitHub deployment now requires **only** the restricted `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` secret. A pre-existing GitHub `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` secret may remain but is ignored; removing it is optional. No app/API/provider secrets are touched by this change.
