### A106. OneSpace-Command · `docs/RELEASE_VERIFICATION_SYNC_20261117.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Command — Manual verification labels and optional automatic release discovery

##### Verification UX

Every eligible QA, Production or Shared row now carries a subordinate **Manual verification** label beneath its environment badge.
Recording a release tag is **step 1**: it does not attest to installing and testing an app.
The administrator still needs to open **Review / Verify**, perform the listed checks, enter test notes, tick the checkbox, and select **Confirm manual verification**. That changes **Needs verification** to **Manually verified**, never to Live.

The Supabase Pro billing/provisioning row can be attested once its **current** allowance and disk configuration have been checked. 'Recheck when provisioning changes' means reopen it only if configuration later changes; there is no need to wait for a change.

##### Optional private GitHub version detection

The `BoyofKay/onespace` repository is private; OneSpace Command cannot fetch its releases without explicit server-side authorization. To enable release detection, a repository owner can create a fine-grained personal access token:

- Resource owner: `BoyofKay`
- Repository access: **Only select repositories** → `onespace`
- Repository permissions: **Contents: Read** (release metadata and APK assets), **Actions: Read** (Play workflow run + artifact metadata), **Metadata: Read** automatic only
- No write permissions or administration access
- A rotation/expiry period such as 90 days

Save this credential ONLY as a Cloudflare Worker encrypted Production Secret:
`ONESPACE_GITHUB_RELEASE_READ_TOKEN`.
Do not place it in `wrangler.jsonc`, Github files, mobile builds, browser local storage or chat.
Do **not** reuse the Cloudflare deployment token or a GitHub PAT with write/admin rights.

When **Systems & Connections → Recheck connections** loads:

1. Authenticate read-only against the private GitHub repo's latest releases and successful Play build workflow runs.
2. Identify the latest `qa-0.1.N` release that includes a nonempty `OneSpace-QA-0.1.N.apk` asset.
3. Identify the newest successful **main-branch** Play workflow with a non-expired nonempty `OneSpace-Play-X.Y.Z-N` artifact, **excluding** any `TEST-NOT-FOR-PLAY-` prefixed artifacts. This is build evidence, not Google Play approval.
4. On a changed verified version, update the corresponding KV verification record and clear the previous manual sign-off. Keep QA and Production independent.
5. On an unchanged version, preserve prior approval. A failed/expired GitHub token does not invent newer versions or erase records; the GitHub feed status must be repaired and should not be mistaken for current evidence.

The optional token does NOT affect working Supabase, Firebase, Resend or Cloudflare alert connections. Without it, manual release version recording remains available.

**Event latency:** This is authenticated polling on inventory refresh, NOT instantaneous webhook delivery. After a new QA GitHub Release or signed AAB build, click Recheck connections. Fully event-driven notification would require a signed webhook or dedicated GitHub Actions sender credential under Cloudflare Access and is not implemented here.

##### Verified status before Play Console

- QA GitHub release `qa-0.1.446` exists with APK asset and successful QA build.
- Production Play GitHub Action run `44064622211` passed and has signed `OneSpace-Play-1.0.0-3` artifact; earlier test-only build is deliberately excluded.
- Google Play Console app creation, bundle acceptance, policy declarations, closed-testing testers, and Play App Signing SHA-1 are **external** and require the owner's console evidence.
- Google Play currently expects Android 16/API 36+ for new Android apps after 31 August 2026; QA APK logging shows API 36. Console must validate production AAB.

### A107. OneSpace-Command · `docs/RELIABILITY_V04.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace Command v0.4 — alerting and infrastructure reliability

**Scope:** Command Center only. No changes to OneSpace mobile/website, Supabase tables or policies, Zoho, or Firebase.

##### What is monitored

- Database size: measured PostgreSQL database size, MB (decimal). Alert comparison uses **the per-project 500 MB limit currently configured in `QA_DATABASE_LIMIT_MB`**. Confirm this against the Supabase plan if it changes.
- File storage: size and count of objects in the current project, shown as observed MiB, with top ten bucket totals.
- Top ten PostgreSQL tables: `pg_total_relation_size` includes table data, indexes, and TOAST. It is not Postgres disk volume / WAL and not all project storage.
- Organization-level billing limits, egress, monthly Auth users and Edge Functions remain **unavailable**. They must not be estimated from registered users or a single project's storage-object sizes. Supabase billing is organization-wide for these items.

**No file paths, photo contents, messages or user rows are retrieved** for the breakdown. Uses fixed, aggregate-only SQL through the existing scoped read-only Supabase Management token. The query is available as `worker/infra-breakdown.ts` and tested against existing OneSpace tables/buckets without modifying them.

##### Thresholds

- Advisory at 60%, warning at 75%, critical at 90%.
- A severity falls when utilization decreases **below** that tier's cutoff minus 5 percentage points. For example, critical clears below 85%.
- Null or unverified usage/denominator **does not generate an alert**.
- An hourly Cloudflare Cron is configured in `wrangler.jsonc` as `0 * * * *`, i.e., at minute 0 UTC.
- A manual **Run safe check now** reads real aggregates but does **not** claim to be a Cron invocation and sends no email.
- **Verify policy** runs a fixed synthetic sample sequence, does not access external data or write KV and sends no email.

##### Delivery and duplicate control

- New threshold notifications persist a pending per-resource KV record before sending.
- After Resend accepts the message, the Worker writes a distinct acknowledgement key. On the next scheduled check, the Worker clears the pending state **without sending again**.
- An accepted-alert email uses an Idempotency-Key and is never printed to browser logs.
- Resend's provider idempotency key retention is 24 hours; a rare crash between Resend acceptance and KV acknowledgement (or multiple concurrent Cron invocations) can still produce duplicate attempts. The Command Center therefore provides *best-effort* deduplication, **not exactly-once delivery**.
- Recovered resources cancel unsent obsolete notifications.
- Each sent/failed/recovered event records only a timestamp, environment, resource and status; recent history stays capped.
- The Worker does not store message contents in the UI or share secrets with the browser.

##### How to verify safely

1. Confirm latest GitHub merge is deployed **Ready** in Cloudflare Workers & Pages → onespace-command → Deployments.
2. Open OneSpace Command → **Alerts** → **Verify policy**. Expect a sequence of 60%, 75%, 90% escalations and hysteresis-based recoveries, with **zero real sends**.
3. Open **Alerts → Run safe check now**. Confirm a manual timestamp appears; this proves the read-only data path but **does not** substitute for an actual scheduled run.
4. Go to Cloudflare → onespace-command → Settings → Trigger Events → **View events**. Verify there are actual Cron invocations since the deploy. Cron trigger changes may take up to 15 minutes to propagate.
5. Open **Alerts → Refresh status**. Last scheduled run must be recent (within 2 hours) and have a nonzero resource count. A stale warning requires investigation of actual Cloudflare Cron events.
6. In **Infrastructure**, confirm the largest database table and storage buckets appear. These are current values, not monthly billing totals.
7. Actual 60/75/90 delivery can be verified only if real quotas are approached or in a separate isolated test environment. **Never intentionally fill the actual database to generate alerts**.

##### Known limits / next steps

- Cloudflare KV is eventually consistent; concurrent scheduler invocations may require stronger synchronization (e.g. Durable Objects) for exactly-once guarantees.
- Cron-based monitoring is hourly, not instantaneous. Use Cloudflare Cron Events for independent evidence of execution.
- Organization-wide billing quota integration needs an explicitly documented/authorized billing usage data source. No unverified endpoint or broad access token should be introduced.
- Separate QA and Production monitoring awaits a dedicated Production Supabase project; the current shared project must not be duplicated as independent usage.
- Alert recipient remains the configured administrator mailbox; a mobile notification channel can be added in a later version with explicit authorization.

Documentation:
- https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/cron-triggers/
- https://developers.cloudflare.com/kv/api/write-key-value-pairs/
- https://resend.com/changelog/idempotency-keys
- https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/billing-on-supabase

---

### A108. OneSpace-Command · `docs/REVIEW_V10_APP_USAGE.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Command Center v1.0 review (17 Nov 2026)

##### New: app usage measured from existing Supabase records (no app change)
Source: `worker/mobile-engagement.ts` (read-only, aggregates only), shown on **Mobile App**.

| KPI | How it is measured |
|---|---|
| Active today / 7 / 30 days, stickiness | Accounts with a sign-in or automatic session refresh (the app refreshes its 1-hour token when opened or resumed) |
| Active users per day (14 days) | Same evidence per UTC day; days older than Supabase's retained token history show "—" (unknown, not zero) |
| Peak users in one hour (7 days) | Distinct active users in the busiest hour — the honest stand-in for "online at the same time" |
| Came back after day 1 / 7 / 30 | Accounts at least N days old that opened the app on or after day N |
| Activated in first 7 days | Created at least one item within a week of signing up |
| Partner joined, median time to pair | Second member joining a space vs. space creation |
| Accounts by country | Phone time zone saved by the app (`profiles.time_zone`) mapped with the IANA zone table |
| Notifications sent, send success, median time to send | The app's own push queue (`private.push_outbox`: sent / dead / waiting) |
| Accounts going quiet (not opened 10/20/30 days) | Replaces "no sign-in", which was misleading (people stay signed in for months) |

Overview "Active in app · 30 days" and the Infrastructure **MAU** card (vs the 100,000 MAU Pro allowance, shared by the organisation) use the same evidence.

##### Still not measurable (needs a consent-aware app event log)
Session length, screens/funnels, notification open rate, installs/uninstalls (Google Play reports once the Play Console exists),
exact concurrent users, explicit complaint category. Billing churn is not applicable (no paid plans).

##### Removed duplicates
- **Audience** tab (Website tab shows the same Founder numbers with more detail).
- Communications: Founder email logs, website page views and the connection list (they live on Website and Systems).
- Alerts: the slider simulator (the server dry-run and threshold cards cover it).
- Infrastructure: the 17 Nov manual disk snapshot is collapsed under history (the live disk reading replaces it).
- Systems: stale rows (PR #21, PR #30, "mobile analytics: planned", billing) updated.

### A109. OneSpace-Command · `docs/SUPABASE_LIVE_DISK_V08.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Active reference (Command Center runbook).*

#### OneSpace Command — live Supabase disk monitoring (optional)

The manual 17 November 2026 snapshots remain as a dated historical reference.
This integration uses **separate read-only Management API tokens** for QA and Production.
Never use existing Database Read PATs, Supabase `service_role`/secret API keys,
or app credentials for this purpose.

##### Official read-only provider APIs
- `GET https://api.supabase.com/v1/projects/{ref}/config/disk/util`
  returns `timestamp`, `metrics.fs_size_bytes`, `metrics.fs_used_bytes`, `metrics.fs_avail_bytes`.
- `GET https://api.supabase.com/v1/projects/{ref}/config/disk`
  returns `attributes.size_gb` and `attributes.type`.

Supabase fine-grained token scope for both: **`infra_disk_config_read`**.
Reference: https://supabase.com/docs/reference/api/v1-get-disk-utilization and
https://supabase.com/docs/reference/api/v1-get-database-disk.

##### Minimal-privilege configuration (must be performed by account owner)

Under [Supabase Access Tokens](https://supabase.com/dashboard/account/tokens), create two *separately scoped* PATs:
1. `onespace-command-qa-disk-read` — resource **Project: OneSpace / `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`**, permission **Infrastructure / Disk configuration: Read** (`infra_disk_config_read`) and nothing else.
2. `onespace-command-production-disk-read` — resource **Project: OneSpace Production / `letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz`**, same single permission.

Choose a practical expiration/rotation schedule (e.g. 90 days) and store the raw generated tokens **only** in encrypted Cloudflare production Worker secrets:

| Secret name | Project |
|---|---|
| `QA_SUPABASE_DISK_READ_PAT` | QA Seoul |
| `PRODUCTION_SUPABASE_DISK_READ_PAT` | Production Frankfurt |

Do not put tokens in `wrangler.jsonc`, GitHub repository files, browser query strings,
or OneSpace mobile/website clients. Avoid sharing token values with ChatGPT.

After secrets are saved/deployed, use Command → Infrastructure / QA and Production
and Systems & Connections → Recheck connections. The API is inspected
only with the expected hard-coded project references; a wrong reference does not cause a QA fallback.

##### Monitoring behavior and safety

- **Live disk utilization** = `fs_used_bytes / fs_size_bytes × 100` from the **same response**.
- `attributes.size_gb` is the **provisioned** disk size, needed for context and
  billing risk, not the filesystem-utilization denominator.
- An invalid response, an HTTP error, impossible values, missing token, or
  data more than **48 hours old** is marked unavailable. It may not feed alerts.
- Only the fresh comparable `disk-filesystem` metric feeds automatic 60%, 75%, 90%
  thresholds (in addition to existing independently measured quotas).
- Disk read permission does **not** grant database SQL or table access.
- The hourly Cron must still be operational and Resend must accept alert sends;
  configured credentials alone do not prove end-to-end alert delivery.
- Existing screenshot values stay manual/historical, even when the live API connects.
- Credentials expire: plan rotation before expiry to preserve live coverage.

### A110. OneSpace-Command · `docs/WEBSITE_TAB.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 19 Nov 2026 · Status: Active reference (Command Center runbook).*

#### Website tab (v0.9)

**What:** onespace.live traffic + the Founder waitlist, inside the protected Command Center's standalone **Website** tab.
Uses the Production `founder-admin` aggregate API as the single source of truth; the former public website admin page is retired.

**How it works**
- Browser → `GET /api/website?days=7|30|90` (Cloudflare Access protected, same-origin).
- Worker (`worker/website.ts`) → Supabase **Production** edge function `founder-admin`
  (`https://letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz.supabase.co/functions/v1/founder-admin`) with header `x-admin-key`.
- That function returns Founder aggregates (no email addresses) + GoatCounter traffic, paced under
  GoatCounter's 4 requests/second limit and cached for 5 minutes.
- The Worker scrubs any email-looking text from provider error messages before returning.
- Production only. No QA data. No numbers are invented when a source fails.

**Setup (one Cloudflare secret)**
- Cloudflare → Workers & Pages → `onespace-command` → Settings → Variables and Secrets →
  add **Secret** `WEBSITE_ADMIN_KEY` = the same value as `FOUNDER_ADMIN_KEY` in Supabase Production.
- Then run the GitHub workflow *Deploy OneSpace Command to Cloudflare*.

**Charts:** every bar shows the count and the percentage (share of the total shown, or of the period
for daily bars; funnel steps show % of visits and % continued from the step before).

**Counting rules shown in the UI:** GoatCounter counts each page once per person per visit, without
cookies; breakdowns (sources, countries, devices, systems) also count clicks, so their percentages are
the reliable comparison. Time-on-page and read-to-end are measured from 17 Nov 2026.

##### 19 November 2026 — clean URL and metric continuity

- Canonical URLs are now directory routes such as `/faq/`, `/privacy/`, `/join/` and their five localized counterparts. Historic `.html` visits remain in GoatCounter and are **never deleted or overwritten**.
- `src/lib/site-paths.ts` normalizes and combines the legacy and current paths for the Command Center's top returned-page chart, Founder landing-page chart, and event labels. The raw provider history and total counts remain unchanged. Since the backend reports only a top subset of paths/landings, this normalized chart is **not** an exhaustive per-page count.
- The main traffic count from GoatCounter is page visits, not unique individuals. The formerly named “Visits that sign up” card now accurately says **“Sign-ups per 100 page visits”**: registered Founder form submissions divided by page visits. It is **not** a unique-user conversion rate and can be over 100% in unusual repeat-attribution cases. The chart with daily visits uses up to the 100 top GoatCounter paths and may be partial; this caveat is displayed in the UI.
- Signup form confirmation events from GoatCounter are not equivalent to email-confirmed people from the Production Founder database. The funnel uses event counts; people can click more than once. The separate verified Founder counts retain their own period boundaries.
- UTM source/medium/campaign parameters are retained in first-touch `sessionStorage` for the website registration payload; GoatCounter also automatically detects `utm_campaign` and `utm_source` in its campaign widget. Do not include user-entered values, invitation codes or email-confirmation hashes in tracking events. New Command Center marketing links already use the root URL with UTMs.
- The Command Center's `/api/website` remains protected and reads Production-only Founder aggregates plus GoatCounter through the server-side `founder-admin` Edge Function. No QA fallback and no new analytics access permissions were added.

### A111. OneSpace-Command · `docs/ZOHO_CONNECT.md`

*First written 17 Nov 2026 · last changed 17 Nov 2026 · Status: Active reference (Command Center runbook).*

#### Connect Zoho Mail to OneSpace Command

**Status:** Secure OAuth connect flow implemented in the private Command Worker, pending owner setup and live confirmation.

##### Scope

- Account: `support@onespace.live` hosted in Zoho Mail US data center (`mail.zoho.com`).
- Aliases `hello@onespace.live`, `founders@onespace.live`, `privacy@onespace.live` are all one mailbox.
- OneSpace Command displays Inbox message count and unread count. No messages, subjects, sender addresses or bodies are copied into Cloudflare KV, frontend, logs or Supabase.
- OAuth permissions: `ZohoMail.accounts.READ,ZohoMail.folders.READ,ZohoMail.messages.READ`.
  - Zoho `messages.READ` *technically grants access to message contents*; we intentionally call only list-without-body endpoints. Evaluate this consent before connecting.
- OAuth refresh token is AES-GCM encrypted using a separate secret before saving to Cloudflare KV. Decryption is server-side only. Audit carefully before allowing other Cloudflare account members administrator access.

##### Step 1: Register a Zoho **Server-based Application**

Open https://api-console.zoho.com/ while signed in to the OneSpace Zoho account.
Click **Add Client** / **Get Started** → **Server-based Applications**.

- Client Name: `OneSpace Command`
- Homepage URL: `https://onespace-command.ph-ahmed-rezk.workers.dev`
- Authorized Redirect URI (must match character-for-character):
  `https://onespace-command.ph-ahmed-rezk.workers.dev/api/zoho/callback`

Create the client. Privately copy **Client ID** and **Client Secret**. Never send them in chat.

##### Step 2: Save 3 Production secrets in Cloudflare

Go to Cloudflare → Workers & Pages → `onespace-command` → **Settings → Variables and Secrets**.

| Secret name | Secret value |
|---|---|
| `ZOHO_CLIENT_ID` | Client ID from Zoho API Console |
| `ZOHO_CLIENT_SECRET` | Client Secret from Zoho API Console |
| `ZOHO_VAULT_KEY` | A fresh cryptographically random secret, at least 32 characters (ideally 64 hex characters) |

On Windows PowerShell 7, generate the random 32-byte key **locally** using:
`[Convert]::ToHexString([System.Security.Cryptography.RandomNumberGenerator]::GetBytes(32))`

On older Windows PowerShell, use a password manager's generator to generate a 40–64 character high-entropy secret. The vault key is never sent in chat.

Optional nonsecret variable: `ZOHO_REGION=com`, matching the actual URL `mail.zoho.com`.

**Do not** configure `ZOHO_ACCOUNT_ID` or `ZOHO_REFRESH_TOKEN` for this new flow: the account ID is discovered and the refresh token is saved encrypted automatically.

Deploy the latest Worker build that contains `/api/zoho/connect`, `/api/zoho/callback`, and `/api/zoho/status`. Cloudflare may require a new deployment after adding secrets.

##### Step 3: Connect from Command

1. Visit https://onespace-command.ph-ahmed-rezk.workers.dev and authenticate with Cloudflare Access.
2. Open **Communications**.
3. The **Zoho Mail · OneSpace Support** panel should show *Ready to authorize*.
4. Click **Connect Zoho Mail**.
5. Review Zoho's read-only permissions carefully, then approve.
6. Zoho redirects to the same protected Worker, where it exchanges its short-lived code for a refresh token.
7. The Worker selects only the primary `support@onespace.live` mailbox, encrypts the refresh token in KV, then returns to Communications.
8. Refresh to see *Zoho Mail Connected*, the Inbox message count, and unread count.

If the message list exceeds 1000 items, the connector deliberately shows unavailable rather than displaying an incorrect exact count. Inbox counts do not measure incoming messages sent since a particular date; they are a snapshot of currently stored Inbox messages.

##### Verification and troubleshooting

- **Not configured**: ensure all 3 secrets are saved in Production, and the new code has been deployed.
- **Redirect URI mismatch**: verify exact path and HTTPS in Zoho API Console.
- **Connected different mailbox**: ensure you authorize the Zoho account that owns the primary `support@onespace.live` mailbox. The code rejects other accounts.
- **Not receiving refresh token**: user consent may need reauthorization; the flow requests `access_type=offline`.
- **Inbox counts unavailable**: verify account plan/API access, the Zoho data center, OAuth scopes, and message count pagination.
- **Revoking**: remove OAuth authorization in Zoho Accounts Connected Apps. The Command endpoint `POST /api/zoho/disconnect` deletes the stored encrypted token but **does not itself revoke Zoho's provider token**.
- **Rotating the vault key**: existing ciphertext becomes undecryptable; reconnect through OAuth after rotation. Don't rotate without planning reconnection.
- **Privacy**: no account details or actual OAuth refresh tokens are returned to the UI; do not screen-share the Zoho API Console secrets.

Official documentation:
- https://www.zoho.com/mail/help/api/using-oauth-2.html
- https://www.zoho.com/mail/help/api/get-all-users-accounts.html
- https://www.zoho.com/mail/help/api/get-all-folder-details.html
- https://www.zoho.com/mail/help/api/get-emails-list.html

### A112. OneSpace · `docs/LAUNCH_SECURITY_GATE_2026-11-18.md`

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace launch security, recovery and release gate — 18 Nov 2026

Status: PARTIALLY VERIFIED. This is an engineering audit, not a penetration-test certification.

##### Separate environments
- QA: `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo` (4 Auth users, 2 spaces at review)
- Production: `letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz` (0 Auth users, 0 spaces at review)
- User confirmed leaked-password protection enabled on both; connector does not expose the Auth configuration toggle.

##### Database authorization
- QA: 8 cross-space RPC negative tests PASS, rolled back: `create_scrapbook`, `create_trip_preparation_checklist`, `invite_scrapbook_partner`, `remove_scrapbook_page`, `record_activity_decision`, `respond_to_reminder`, `save_occasion_proposal`, `respond_scrapbook_invite`.
- QA: 12 remaining signed-in privileged RPC safety probes PASS, rolled back: `accept_terms`, `cancel_account_deletion`, `demo_reset_space`, `disable_push_device`, `export_my_data`, `log_app_events`, `log_client_error`, `register_push_device`, `request_account_deletion`, `sealed_letters`, `set_how_we_live`, `submit_public_support`. The latter are scoped-operation/invalid-input tests, not 12 additional cross-space mutation tests.
- Security Advisor: QA and Production each still have 20 authenticated privileged-function WARN notices and 1 intentionally callable anonymous support-function WARN. These must not be dismissed solely to make the count zero.
- All 20 function names/argument signatures are present in both environments. Function definitions were byte-for-byte identical for 18; `log_client_error` and `submit_public_support` contain formatting/comment differences with matching security-relevant logic, owner and guards.
- Last change on both databases: migration `0140_security_scrapbook_current_membership.sql`, requiring current space membership for scrapbook invitations.
- 76/76 public tables in each project have RLS; all Storage buckets are private. Real-device and third-party penetration tests remain open.

##### Public support abuse
- Website's `founder-join` and `site-contact` Edge Functions require server-verified Turnstile in QA and Production.
- Mobile pre-sign-in `submit_public_support` intentionally remains anonymously callable; database bounds inputs and limits global intake to 30/hour and 3/hour/email. Direct database RPC calls can bypass the website's Turnstile. This creates a residual quota-exhaustion risk.
- Pre-launch remediation: separate website and mobile intake with appropriate independent app/device verification, IP/device-level quotas and failure monitoring, while preserving help before sign-in. Do not revoke the RPC before mobile clients have moved.

##### Backups and restoration
- 17 and 18 Nov GitHub QA backup workflow runs showed SUCCESS but their dump steps were SKIPPED and no encrypted artifacts existed. They are **not proof of backups**.
- The workflow now fails on missing `SUPABASE_DB_URL` or `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE`, and verifies that each encrypted dump can be decrypted into a valid gzip stream.
- **No successful GitHub encrypted backup artifact or restore has yet been demonstrated.** Configure the secrets and manually run the workflow. Never restore a test into QA or Production.
- Supabase Pro includes automatic daily database backups (usual 7-day retention), but availability for these two specific projects has not been independently inspected through the connector.
- Database backups only save Storage object metadata, not binary images, audio, or documents. At the audit: QA has 59 private stored objects; Production has 0. A separate secure binary-object backup and isolated restore drill are necessary.
- An actual restore acceptance test requires an isolated, separately billed Supabase clone or a correctly configured compatible staging system, plus a sampled protected Storage-object restore. It has NOT been performed.

##### Sessions, deletion and export
- QA and Production each have an active daily account-purge Cron, plus events/client-error cleanup and push-delivery jobs. Active schedule does not prove successful end-to-end purge.
- QA account deletion and `export_my_data` were exercised in a rolled-back functional safety probe. No real account was deleted.
- `export_my_data` exports matching database records as JSON, not the underlying Storage file bytes or a partner's independently created shared items. Full portability requires a separate media-export workflow.
- Existing mobile code documents local OS biometric/PIN fallback, immediate background shield, 5-minute idle and 30-second return lock. Real-device verification and server-side maximum-session/inactivity settings remain outstanding.

##### Android
- Previous Google Play workflow run `44064622211` succeeded and produced signed `OneSpace-Play-1.0.0-3` AAB (17 Nov).
- Fresh Production signed build requested by explicit marker `release/PRODUCTION_READY_TO_BUILD.md`, run `44353582051` (18 Nov). At time of this document, it is running analyzer/tests/build; no Play upload has occurred.
- Manual Play Console closed testing and actual phone tests are not completed by producing an AAB artifact.

##### Command Center
- Production and QA mobile KPI aggregate queries executed successfully; Production empty account results are grounded, not copied from QA.
- False-zero inactivity windows fixed in merged PR #46. Insufficient refresh-token history now means unknown; CI passed. Merged source requires Cloudflare deployment.
- Production Founder-app aggregate attribution (confirmed email match, push-enabled proxy, paired app space) added in merged PR #47; read-only SQL and CI passed. Counts only leave Production. Still requires Cloudflare deployment.
- Firebase delivery reports use the delayed Android FCM Data API; no direct Firebase provider live verification or app analytics/Crashlytics integration was performed in this audit. Peak users per hour is NOT a concurrent-user metric.

##### Do not close P0 until
1. Independently confirm Supabase automatic daily backup inventory for both environments and establish offsite encrypted backups.
2. Successfully decrypt and **restore** to an isolated target, including selected private Storage files.
3. Complete cross-user QA regression and real-device auth/biometrics/offline/notification tests.
4. Verify account deletion end to end on a disposable QA identity and validate data export completeness.
5. Complete signed Production AAB build, install/closed-track validation, pairing and Firebase delivery.
6. Verify server session policy, public support abuse controls and independent security review.

### A113. OneSpace · `docs/SECURITY_HARDENING_2026-11-18.md`

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Supabase security hardening — 18 November 2026

Applied live to **OneSpace** mobile (`ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`) and **OneSpace Production** website/founder (`letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz`).

##### Verified changes
- Anonymous `public.log_client_error` RPC EXECUTE privilege removed, while authenticated diagnostics work.
- Unauthenticated `public.submit_public_support` remains operational; inputs and quotas are bounded, with global/per-email throttles serialized under an advisory transaction lock.
- Both RPC owners are dedicated NOLOGIN, NOBYPASSRLS roles with column-level permissions and RLS policies (no privileged `postgres` owner).
- Explicit RESTRICTIVE deny policies for `anon`/`authenticated` on internal-only RLS tables; no direct client read grants added.
- Private trigger handlers and six internal helper functions lose unnecessary client EXECUTE grants; existing triggers and privileged internal procedures continue to call them.
- Tests: anonymous support + authenticated error report both succeed within transactions rolled back; no test rows persist.
- Security advisor re-run: missing-policy informational lints 0 in both projects; 1 anonymous definer warning remaining by design, 20 authenticated definer warnings requiring per-object authorization audit.
- On the active mobile project the Auth security advisor also flags leaked-password protection, a Supabase Pro-enabled Auth dashboard setting. This requires updating Auth service configuration, not database RLS.
- `Give it up, Boy! beta version` is a separate legacy project and was not modified.

##### About the remaining warnings
Supabase flags **any** deliberately exposed SECURITY DEFINER RPC, even when bounded by ownership checks. An unverified warning must not be marked closed or accepted solely because the function has an `auth.uid()` reference. The open task is a two-account, wrong-space/wrong-object IDOR regression suite against all 18 signed-in-only functions. Do **not** blindly revoke them or flip them to SECURITY INVOKER: doing so breaks core flows.

The one public-warning function handles signed-out support and now executes under a restricted, non-bypass role. It remains an intentional exception and deserves abuse/rate-limit monitoring.

##### Apply / verify
- The following SQL is the source-controlled representation of the migrations already applied in Supabase. On a new clone run `supabase/schema/0139_security_least_privilege.sql` **after** earlier schema scripts.
- Run `supabase/tests/0028_security_least_privilege.sql` to check privilege invariants (read-only, transaction rolled back).
- Run Supabase Security Advisor and verify the same warning categories; do not interpret any linter count as a penetration test.

Reference: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/functions and https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/postgres/row-level-security.

### A115. onespace-app · `docs/ASO_PRELAUNCH.md`

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — prelaunch app store listing copy (REVIEW / NOT PUBLISHED)

**Status:** Prepared for store account setup; NOT submitted or uploaded to either store.
**Platforms:** Google Play production package `live.onespace.app`; iOS bundle identifier to be finalized before signing.
**App UI languages:** English and Arabic. Website languages do not imply mobile availability.
**No claims:** no invented testimonials, ratings, downloads, rankings, guaranteed discounts, free lifetime subscriptions, or unavailable iPhone share-sheet extension.

##### Google Play — English
- **App name (30 max):** OneSpace
- **Short description (80 max):** A private space for couples: shared plans, lists, love notes and memories.
- **Full description:**

One life. Two people. One shared space.

OneSpace helps couples keep everyday plans, conversations, little gestures and meaningful memories together — whether you live in different cities or share the same home.

Plan together. Create shared activities, compare options, discuss what matters and record the decisions you make as a couple.

Remember what matters. Organize a shared calendar, reminders, checklists, notes, important dates and contact details. Keep personal items private when you choose.

Explore together. Save places you've visited and places you want to go. Prepare trips with flight options, packing lists and travel details. Create counters for moments that matter — only when you choose to add them.

Stay connected. Send little love gestures, write letters, check in with each other and keep shared memories and scrapbooks.

Your space belongs to you two. Each partner has their own account, with shared and private items controlled by their permissions.

OneSpace supports English and Arabic in the mobile app. Some features and availability differ by platform while the iPhone app is being prepared.

Learn more and register for Founder invitations at https://onespace.live.

##### Google Play — Arabic
- **App name:** OneSpace
- **Short description:** مساحة خاصة لشخصين تجمع الخطط والقوائم والتذكيرات ورسائل الحب والذكريات.
- **Full description:**

حياة واحدة، وشخصان، ومساحة مشتركة.

يساعدكما OneSpace على جمع خطط الحياة اليومية والمهام والقرارات والذكريات في مكان واحد، سواء كنتما تعيشان في مدينتين مختلفتين أو تحت سقف واحد.

خطّطا معًا: أنشئا أنشطة مشتركة، وقارنا الخيارات، وتناقشا في التفاصيل، واحتفظا بالقرار النهائي.

تذكّرا ما يهم: تقويم مشترك، وتذكيرات، وقوائم مهام، وملاحظات، وتواريخ مهمة، وبطاقات لأشخاص من حياتكما.

اكتشفا أماكنكما: احفظا الأماكن التي زرتماها وما تريدان زيارته، ونظّما الرحلات والتجهيزات وتفاصيل السفر. أضيفا العدّادات للتواريخ التي تختارانها بأنفسكما.

حافظا على القرب: لمسات حب ورسائل واطمئنان يومي وذكريات وألبومات مشتركة.

لكل شريك حساب مستقل، مع خيارات للمشاركة والخصوصية. يدعم التطبيق العربية والإنجليزية. تختلف إتاحة بعض المزايا بحسب نظام الهاتف خلال فترة الإطلاق.

المزيد والتسجيل للحصول على دعوات المؤسسين: https://onespace.live.

- **Keywords (100 max):** couples,relationship,long distance,shared calendar,checklist,reminders,memories,love letters

- **Promotional text:** مساحة خاصة لشخصين يخططان معًا ويتذكران التفاصيل الصغيرة ويبقيان قريبين.
- **Description:** Adapt the Arabic Google Play narrative after a native-speaker review and iOS feature acceptance.

##### Proposed screenshot narrative — use genuine app captures only
1. **A private space for two** — real Today screen, not synthetic UI.
2. **Plan and decide together** — actual Coordinate options and decision record.
3. **Remember the little things** — actual shared checklist and reminder.
4. **More thoughtful everyday connection** — real check-in and love gesture picker.
5. **Make memories together** — real memories and scrapbook screen.
6. **Two places or one home** — show the living mode relevant to the audience.
7. **Trips, places and counters** — show only fully tested platform features.

Use genuine captures from fictional demo accounts; no fabricated user reviews or store ratings. iOS screenshots must be recaptured on an iPhone/TestFlight build, not cropped Android screenshots.

##### Before publishing
- Confirm Apple bundle identifier, signing, iOS features and app review access.
- Verify every claim against latest release and maintain parity with Privacy, Terms, Account deletion.
- Complete Google Play Data Safety and Apple App Privacy questionnaires from measured production behavior.
- Confirm production subscriptions/entitlements and do not claim unapproved benefits.
- Ask a native Arabic reviewer to edit the final Arabic listing and screenshots.
- Use official store badges only after actual listing URLs exist.

##### Official references
- Google Play limits: https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9859152
- Google Play metadata policy: https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/9898842
- Apple product page: https://developer.apple.com/app-store/product-page/
- Apple App Store Connect app name/subtitle: https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/reference/app-information/app-information/

Date: 2026-11-18. The editable copy is in `docs/ASO_PRELAUNCH.md`. **Not submitted to Apple or Google.**

- App name `OneSpace` meets both stores' 30-character limit.
- English Google Play short description <80 characters; full description <4,000 characters. Arabic short description likewise <80 characters.

- Do not claim iOS native Share Extension or unreleased Apple device features.
- Screenshots must be from working build; Apple screenshots must be captured on actual iOS/TestFlight, not resized Android imagery.

- **Core:** couples app, couple planner, relationship organizer
- **Living apart:** long distance couple, shared calendar, reunion planner
- **Living together:** couple checklist, household planning, shared responsibilities
- **Features:** couples reminders, relationship memories, gift planning, shared watchlist

Use each term only if it naturally describes the supported feature and respects platform metadata policies. No keyword stuffing or speculative ranking claims.

##### Ready or produced locally

- English and Arabic Apple name, subtitle, keyword candidates and promotional copy.
- Existing press/store screenshot assets in `assets/press`, subject to verification against the current app and its language, device and OS.

### A117. onespace-app · `docs/CAMPAIGNS_PROPOSAL_REVIEW_ONLY.md`

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Proposal / draft — not an approved decision.*

#### OneSpace founder launch — four-campaign plan
Draft date: 2026-11-18. **PLANNING ONLY. Nothing approved, scheduled, published or bought.**

##### Single conversion goal
Get qualified couples to join and **confirm** the Founding 500 waitlist (one shared Founder number per couple; the first 250 confirmed couples = 500 people). Registration is open to owners of Android **and iPhone** now; the app invitation is platform-dependent. Do not promise a release date, perpetual free access or lifetime discounts.

##### Campaigns, concept and creative
| Campaign | Audience | Message / proof | Creative | Primary CTA |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the abandoned slogan | Long-distance couples across time zones | Couples can share practical life while apart: plans, trip documents, counters by choice, gestures and updates | 30-second real Android walkthrough + an illustrated fictional reunion journey, explicitly labeled illustrative | Join the Founding 500 |
| Same Home, Shared Load | Couples living together | Shared routines, recurring checklists, decisions and reminders reduce forgotten responsibilities | Before/after household Sunday reset using authentic app UI | Join the Founding 500 |
| From a Shared Link to a Shared Decision | Couples planning purchases, trips or events | A link, photo or location can lead to a shared comparison and decision in one place | Three-panel Android-supported smart sharing into Coordinate; no iPhone share extension claim | See the demo → Join |
| Every Reunion Has a Story | Long-distance couples preparing a visit | Flight comparisons, checklists, travel documents and a memory when the visit ends | A 4-beat carousel with characters Lina & Omar labeled fictional | Read the journey → Join |

##### Four-week organic pilot (subject to approval)
- **Week 1 — explain:** 2 posts about the problem, 1 short demo, 1 founder invitation. A/B test the two audience messages.
- **Week 2 — show:** 2 scenario carousels, 1 real product clip, 1 founder FAQ post. Lead with actual workflows.
- **Week 3 — engage:** 1 voluntary feedback poll, 2 micro tutorials, 1 screenshot carousel. Invite constructive comments, not reviews or ratings.
- **Week 4 — learn:** 2 highest-performing creatives adapted, 1 honest progress update, 1 founder invitation. Compare conversion, not vanity reach.
Maximum **4 posts/week per owned channel**, cross-post thoughtfully with each platform's norms. No automatic mass messaging, no purchased follows or group spam.

##### Channels and permission
- Owned website, a product social account, relevant voluntary test communities and indie-launch directories.
- WhatsApp / Telegram / Facebook communities: only share after group admin permission; disclose founder affiliation. Immigration support groups are especially sensitive: acknowledge hardship sincerely without exploiting visa uncertainty or implying app utility speeds official processing.
- Arabic and English first; other website languages are not evidence that the mobile app supports them.
- Do not impersonate users or frame fictional scenarios as testimonials.

##### Measurement
UTMs e.g. `utm_source=instagram&utm_medium=organic&utm_campaign=one-life-two-places&utm_content=reunion-card-a`; tag by platform. Review page views → founder form start → email submitted → email confirmed → couple invited → partner joined, and Android/iPhone shares. Target baselines **after** measuring first-week actuals; no fabricated conversion targets.

##### Cost policy
Start at **$0 ad spend**. Produce static images and genuine device captures from existing project assets. Any paid social, influencer sponsorship, email send or external placement requires explicit owner approval; do not imply automatic access to social accounts.

##### Required approval before launch
Owner reviews each headline, CTA, two-language copy, images, dates, group permissions and any cost. Legal/privacy notices and opt-in unsubscribe practices must be respected.

### A118. onespace-app · `docs/FEATURE_PAGES_PROPOSAL_FOR_APPROVAL.md`

#### Seven feature pages — proposed copy improvements (NOT implemented)
Date: 2026-11-18. **Approval boundary:** The owner requested a proposed improvement first. Do not edit these seven page bodies until reviewed.

##### Editing principles
Keep existing screenshots, tutorial walkthroughs, theme selector, menu identity and page flow. No long blog walls. Each page receives at most one short decision-oriented section (120–160 words) with a three-step workflow and one honest caveat. No invented app store results, ratings, testimonials, iPhone share extensions or untested features. Ensure EN/AR/ES/FR/DE parity.

| Page | Specific proposed improvement | What changes after approval | What stays |
|---|---|---|---|
| Smart sharing `smart-sharing.html` | Show one real action: an Android user shares a product link to a relevant shared activity, checks the suggested destination, and leaves a comment. Clarify that iOS native Share Extension isn't yet live. | Replace the sweeping "from any app" language with qualified supported-source language and a three-panel example. | Existing visual demonstration |
| Decide together `decide-together.html` | Show a choice moving from two options through comments and pros/cons to a recorded decision, with a separate follow-up task. | One compact decision-lifecycle component, not more feature tiles. | Comparison interface |
| Trips `trips.html` | Make the trip object explicit: flight comparisons, packing, booking confirmations, PDFs/photos of required documents, optional countdown. | One annotated trip view and short safety reminder to keep original required documents. | Screenshots and itinerary flow |
| Occasions `occasions.html` | Explain the difference between private surprise preparation and shared celebration; illustrate saved date → automatic seasonal accent when enabled → shared memory. | One split private/shared example, accurately noting the automatic appearance setting. | Existing budget workflow |
| Watchlists `watchlists.html` | Illustrate saving a film, rating separately and selecting a shared watch night, without implying streaming playback inside OneSpace. | A compact viewing decision strip. | Watchlist presentation |
| Memories `memories.html` | Surface the actual 22 scrapbook layouts, invitation/acceptance before partner edit, and whole-book PDF export. | One mini sequence from photo to book to PDF. | Current authentic app captures |
| Everyday `everyday.html` | Clarify per-cycle recurring checklist reset, shared/private visibility, remembered contacts and reminders, with a two-person weekly reset example. | One concise routine example. | Existing tabs and feature illustrations |

##### Suggested layout
Each enhancement fits inside the existing page: short subheading, three concise numbered steps, one genuine screenshot and a contextual link to an existing journey. Avoid new homepage blocks or promotional claims. Every draft will be shown for copy approval **before** implementation.

### A119. onespace-app · `docs/INTERNATIONALIZATION.md`

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Active — moved into the website README on 26 Nov 2026.*

#### OneSpace website internationalization

##### Full visual and functional parity
- English is the source layout. **Arabic, Spanish, French and German reuse the original website experience.** ES/FR/DE are generated from the complete English HTML DOM in `content/full_localization.py`, not simplified independent landing pages.
- The same app screenshots (currently English/Arabic images), phone frames, interactive walkthroughs, theme controls, comparison tables, guides, legal and support pages, Founder registration, privacy protections, and product sections are retained. **Do not invent translated app screenshots** when only English/Arabic screenshots exist.
- Every page in `PAGES` gets ES/FR/DE counterparts; confirm and unsubscribe pages also exist in all languages and stay `noindex`.
- Original `assets/os.js`, `assets/os.css`, GoatCounter and Founder Supabase functions are shared. Dynamic form and copy feedback is localized via `SITE_MESSAGES` and `FORM_MESSAGES`.

##### Translation approach
- Committed language catalogs: `content/translations/es.json`, `fr.json`, `de.json`. Source inventory: `source_catalog.json`.
- English phrases are mapped to translated text nodes and user-facing attributes while preserving all HTML structure, links, event data attributes, media and forms.
- `content/locale_editorial.py` provides cumulative human-authored editorial corrections to high-visibility marketing, navigation, product benefits, support, privacy headlines and tutorials in each language. Review all additions before publishing. `GLOSSARY` handles core app terminology and privacy labels.
- New missing translations make the build **fail**; they do not silently fall back to English. Rebuilds do not call any translation service.
- For future source-copy changes: regenerate English source; run the one-time `scripts/translate_full_site.py` to populate **new** missing keys, review changed language strings, then build. This script translates *public marketing copy only* and is not used in production.
- Style: Spanish broadly understandable, warm and clear; French natural, conversational and polished; German friendly for product marketing, precise for support/privacy language.
- Review proper names, software terms, quantitative claims, privacy and legal wording before each release. English remains the original reference for interpretation of legal terms. Full long-form native-speaker proofreading (especially privacy and terms) is still an outstanding QA milestone; machine-assisted coverage alone must never be described as native-certified.

##### SEO, analytics and Founder limitations
- Canonical URLs, reciprocal `hreflang` and XML sitemap cover every indexable page across en/ar/es/fr/de. `x-default` remains English. IndexNow and Google Search Console submissions use the same sitemap.
- GoatCounter continues tracking localized page paths; UTM source, medium and campaign are preserved when switching languages, without sending email/token query strings to GoatCounter.
- The Founder backend currently sends English/Arabic transactional emails only. Localized Founder forms disclose this rather than falsely promising translations of those emails. Email preference is set to English unless the user explicitly selects Arabic.
- Never claim store listings or automatic Counters updates that do not exist in the app.

##### QA gate
```sh
python3 -m pip install beautifulsoup4==4.15.0
python3 build.py
python3 tests/check_site.py
node --check assets/os.js
node --check assets/ar.js
```
The site test checks localized links, anchors, metadata, sitemap, privacy and identical counts of screenshots, media, forms, theme selectors, interactive demo components and tracking markers for every translated page.

##### Locale-specific UI and brand guidance
- Preserve the original OneSpace design and use compact, natural CTAs instead of stretching navigation for literal long translations. Mobile navigation begins at the language-safe responsive breakpoint.
- Horizontal screenshot/theme rails use OneSpace-colored accessible scrollbars. Do not replace the real app images with generated translations of screenshots.
- The Google Play and App Store cards are **availability previews**, not live download links. Their recognizable in-page platform marks are not official approved download badges. Replace with the officially supplied localized badges and verified store links only once the relevant store listing is available and brand terms permit use.
- Run Chromium browser responsive checks (script `scripts/check_locale_layout.mjs`) at phone, tablet and desktop sizes when adjusting navigation, language copy or store cards.
- For German marketing, address a couple as **ihr/euch** and an individual as **du/dein**; avoid unexplained switches to Sie. For French use **vous** consistently; for Spanish use broadly understood, natural language and distinguish singular visitor versus couple.
- Do not localize proper brand names such as OneSpace, App Store, Google Play, OpenStreetMap, or app navigation identifiers into unrelated words.

##### Long-form editorial review (November 2026)
- Translated legal, FAQ, guide and account-deletion copy is maintained in separate source-keyed review files: `locale_legal_review.py`, `locale_faq_review.py`, and `locale_guides_review.py`.
- Privacy and terms pass: rewrote consent, tracking, notifications, retention, account ownership, advertising, service providers, and deletion descriptions without altering legal durations or product permissions.
- Corrected a **critical broken negation** caused by nested bold markup in the "We do **not** collect" privacy clause. The generator now constructs complete localized sentences in each language.
- FAQ: questions and answers were edited for natural ES/FR/DE usage while preserving Founder, calendar, privacy, notification and iPhone availability details.
- Guides: reviewed actual bullet-point advice, not just introductory headings. Tone is warm, clear and practical; calendar/counter actions remain user-controlled where applicable.
- The localized instructions show today's real **English mobile menu labels** alongside translated explanations. Website language support must not be misrepresented as mobile app UI support.
- Run `python3 tests/check_locale_editorial.py` after a full build; it validates source phrase coverage, critical legal negatives, retention numbers, FAQ completeness, and account-deletion instructions.
- Still outstanding before calling the site *professionally native-certified*: independent native-speaker line editing for all remaining pages, a jurisdiction-qualified legal review of privacy/terms, and confirmation that English policy claims precisely match production app and backend behavior. Automated coverage checks do not substitute for those reviews.

### A120. onespace-app · `docs/SECURITY_ADVISORY_TRIAGE_2026-11-18.md`

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical security record (was publicly visible on the website repository until 26 Nov 2026).*

#### Production Supabase security advisory triage — 2026-11-18
Project: `letcgxbnblohhfyeuigz` (OneSpace production). **Audit only: no destructive database changes.**

##### Latest observations (from live advisor / schema introspection)
- 2 WARN — `anon_security_definer_function_executable`: `log_client_error` and `submit_public_support`. Both are deliberately callable before sign-in for error reporting and support; removing access now would break those flows. Both are `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = ''`. They need targeted abuse testing and rate-limit review, not dismissal.
- 20 WARN — `authenticated_security_definer_function_executable`, 18 distinct authenticated-only routines plus the 2 public routines also callable while signed in. Every signed-in routine exposed by the advisor was found to have an `auth.uid()` reference **except** `submit_public_support`, which is intentionally public. However, a textual check does not prove correct per-resource authorization, so individual IDOR/RLS tests remain necessary.
- 11 INFO — `rls_enabled_no_policy`: 9 internal `private` tables plus `public.client_errors` and `public.public_support_requests`. Verified for each one: RLS enabled, **anon and authenticated have neither SELECT nor INSERT table grants**. No direct browser table access, by design. These are not missing public-read policies to add; adding one would weaken security.
- Website CI: CSP/referrer protections, no privileged frontend API keys, safe external links, five-language link tests, server-side Cloudflare Turnstile fail-closed, Turnstile expected host binding and no bypass flags. Edge source and deployed functions both show the fail-closed guard. This is not a penetration test.

##### Immediate risk priorities
1. Review public `submit_public_support`: accepts free-text inputs via a definer RPC with global 30/hour and per-email 3/hour guard. The global guard can be an availability bottleneck during abuse; add independent IP/device and product-level protection before advertising high volumes. Ensure maximum subject/message/diagnostics lengths and stored PII minimization. Keep unauthenticated help available.
2. Review public `log_client_error`: globally throttled to 500/hour and per-user to 40/hour; anonymous users share the global limit. Rate-limit by source if safe and remove unnecessary sensitive data from stack traces.
3. Test all 18 signed-in definer RPCs with two distinct accounts, crossing ownership boundaries. Particular priority: `create_scrapbook`, `invite_scrapbook_partner`, `remove_scrapbook_page`, `record_activity_decision`, `create_trip_preparation_checklist`, `register_push_device`, `respond_to_reminder`, and `export_my_data`.
4. Preserve intended APIs. Do **not** revoke all execute privileges, remove RLS, or grant broad table policies merely to clear linter messages.
5. Re-run Supabase security advisors and app regression tests after any database change; document whether remaining warnings are accepted by design.

##### Why the warnings remain
The linter identifies the risk category of an exposed elevated-privilege function, not proof that an unauthorized database row is accessible. Testing authorization for each callable function and its arguments requires a deliberate acceptance suite. Unverified warnings are therefore **open for review**; not reported as closed.

Supabase references:
- https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/database-linter?lint=0028_anon_security_definer_function_executable
- https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/database-linter?lint=0029_authenticated_security_definer_function_executable
- https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/database-linter?lint=0008_rls_enabled_no_policy

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Proposal / draft — not an approved decision.*

#### SEO — implementation boundary and proposed content changes
Date: 2026-11-18.

##### Already live / permitted technical work
- Canonical URLs, alternate language links (EN AR ES FR DE), sitemap, robots, accessible internal links, descriptive titles, and structured data are generated by the website build and checked in CI.
- Static website is on GitHub Pages at https://onespace.live; GoatCounter code is present for cookieless visits.
- These technical measures do **not** establish Google indexing, a rank, or Search Console ownership. `GOOGLE_VERIFY` and `BING_VERIFY` in the generator remain empty; a domain DNS verification may exist outside this repository, and needs separate verification.
- No paid backlinks, links farms, automated search submissions or unapproved domain changes.

##### Proposed on-page **copy changes — NOT implemented**
Approval is required before changing page marketing language for SEO:
1. Home: clarify the first visible benefit for couples living apart **and** together with natural language; do not keyword-stuff the headline.
2. Living apart: one short answer about shared schedules, time zones, trips and reminders, tied to the existing scenarios (not a new article).
3. Living together: one short answer about mental load, shared chores and household decisions, linking to relevant journeys.
4. Feature pages: precise, platform-qualified descriptions rather than broad "any app" claims; see `FEATURE_PAGES_PROPOSAL_FOR_APPROVAL.md`.
5. Guides: editorial metadata should describe what the page genuinely teaches; not repeated search phrases.

##### Can be done without copy approval
- Check for broken internal links, valid sitemap/alternate URLs, sensible indexing controls and no invented rating schemas (CI tests).

Seek permission-based, editorially relevant mentions from couples' communities, relationship educators, indie app directories and Android beta testing channels. Prioritize usefulness and clearly disclose the project owner's affiliation. No fabricated reviews or paid dofollow link schemes. Measure referral visits separately from indexed search traffic.

##### Validation criteria after approval
Indexing coverage, page relevance, click-through rate, engaged sessions, founder sign-up completion, correct target language and truthful platform claims. Never promise first-page rankings.

### A122. onespace-app · `docs/brand-identity.md`

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — Brand identity specification

Status: **Working specification for layout and messaging only. Original logo is unchanged.**, November 18, 2026.

##### Official lockup

- **Do not redesign, redraw, modify or recolor the existing OneSpace logo.** Use the original `assets/mark.svg` on the website and `assets/branding/mark.png` in Flutter.
- Product name: **OneSpace** (never "One Space" in the wordmark).
- Signature tagline: **Two of Us. One Space.** Exact capitalization and punctuation.
- The English wordmark and official tagline remain consistent in every locale.
- Optional explanatory Arabic translation, separate from the official tagline: **نحن الاثنان. مساحة واحدة.**
- Adjust only spacing, scale and surrounding layout. When the surface is extremely small, show the original symbol alone and supply accessible brand text.

##### Type

- Primary Latin/web: **Inter**. Name bold (800); tagline medium/semibold (600); body regular (400–500).
- Arabic: **IBM Plex Sans Arabic** for website UI, with Arabic-appropriate line height; avoid letter spacing in Arabic.
- Mobile: use matching brand weights and hierarchy. Bundle a licensed Inter font before claiming pixel-identical typography on Flutter/Android/iOS; current brand widget uses safe platform fallback.
- Never display a tagline in tiny illegible text just to force it into a narrow space.

##### Official colors

| Use | Color |
|---|---|
| Display name / midnight navy | `#14213D` |
| Surrounding interface accent / ocean teal | `#1F6E78` |
| Tagline / slate | `#51657A` |
| Secondary expressive coral | `#E8745A` |
| Dark surface main text | `#F5F7FB` |
| Dark surface accent | `#8AD9D3` |
| Dark surface tagline | `#CCD5E3` |

The original brand asset and its existing artwork are the source of truth. These palette values are for adjacent UI typography and accents, **not instructions to modify the logo**.

##### Layout

- Website pinned header: symbol 48px desktop / 38–42px compact, wordmark ~26px desktop / 19–23px compact, tagline 9–11px only if legible.
- Large marketing applications: use clear space of at least one quarter of the symbol's width around the lockup.
- Email signatures: symbol 40px, name 18–20px, tagline 11–12px, with text fallback (no dependence on remote fonts). Avoid large banner images.
- App loading and sign-in: prominent mark above wordmark, tagline centered below; honor light and dark theme contrast.
- Social graphics: use the same navy, teal and coral accents; avoid novel versions of the logo. Provide the full lockup on primary announcement imagery.

##### Copy examples

English signature:

**OneSpace**  
Two of Us. One Space.  
onespace.live

Arabic supporting copy for posts:

**OneSpace**  
نحن الاثنان. مساحة واحدة.  
onespace.live

##### Rules

- Keep product name in Latin characters, including Arabic/French/Spanish/German UI.
- Avoid replacing the symbol with generic heart, infinity, or couple emoji in product headers.
- Keep brand colors stable across user-selected themes. Theme colors apply to the surrounding UI, not the identity.
- Maintain accessibility: descriptive alt/semantic label for standalone marks; empty alt when adjacent visible name conveys identity.
- Legal-document dates are not marketing copy: each localized legal page must be versioned and dated only after a real publication or revision.

### A123. onespace-app · `docs/website-sitemap-and-feature-hierarchy.md`

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace website — approved sitemap, navigation and feature hierarchy

Status: **Implemented in the public website source**, 18 November 2026.
This document records product positioning and navigational ownership. It does not change the original logo, screenshots, interactive demo, tutorial, theme selector or showcases.

##### Product positioning

**One app for both of us — to connect, share, discuss, decide, and plan together.**

Emotional connection is essential, but not the differentiator on its own. OneSpace goes beyond familiar relationship features by connecting **smart sharing into unified lists**, **two-way editing**, **conversations anchored to activities**, **comparison of options**, **decisions**, **planning for trips and occasions**, and **budgets**. All of this lives alongside letters, affectionate gestures, daily check-ins and the feelings journal.

Marketing must not claim that competitors categorically lack these features. Describe what OneSpace specifically does and how its workflows connect.

##### Top navigation

| Top-level item | Destination | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| Features | Mega menu, `features.html` | Collect & Share, Plan & Decide, Connect & Care |
| Why OneSpace | `compare.html` | The product difference, concrete workflow comparison, no fabricated competitor claims |
| For couples | Mega menu | Living apart (`long-distance.html`), living together (`living-together.html`) |
| Demo | `how-it-works.html` | Existing interactive product demo |
| Learn | Mega menu | Tutorials, guides, FAQ, updates |
| Get the app | `index.html#get-the-app` | Honest Google Play / App Store availability, waitlist |
| Join free | `join.html` | One canonical Founder signup form |

###### Features mega menu

| Family | User-facing item | Destination |
|---|---|---|
| Collect & Share | Smart sharing & unified lists | `smart-sharing.html` |
| Collect & Share | Watchlists & music | `watchlists.html` |
| Collect & Share | Lists, reminders & contacts | `everyday.html` |
| Collect & Share | Memories & scrapbooks | `memories.html` |
| Plan & Decide | Compare options together | `decide-together.html` |
| Plan & Decide | Trips & flights | `trips.html` |
| Plan & Decide | Occasions & gifts | `occasions.html` |
| Connect & Care | Love letters | `connect-care.html#letters` |
| Connect & Care | A little love | `connect-care.html#gestures` |
| Connect & Care | Daily check-ins | `connect-care.html#checkins` |
| Connect & Care | Feelings journal | `connect-care.html#journal` |

Keep **Why OneSpace** (competitive/workflow comparison) distinct from **Compare options together** (one of the app's core workflows).

##### Home page: protected marketing experiences

Preserve the existing content and working UI:
1. Hero with original logo and official tagline, real screenshots and primary CTA.
2. Product differentiation: smart sharing, two-way editing, decisions and collaborative plans.
3. Existing app tour / recorded clips and screenshots.
4. One list per kind: multi-source sharing, one list for both partners.
5. Connect & Care showcase with real Letters and A Little Love images.
6. **Sound familiar?** examples and problem/solution stories.
7. Existing feature showcases (dates, places, gifts, memories and more).
8. Living together / living apart comparison.
9. Interactive five-tab demo and nine-theme selector; the theme gallery must remain functional.
10. Guides.
11. **AI-generated testing preview only** for testimonials, Google Play, App Store.
12. Realistic store availability and Founder signup.

The exact visual ordering may adapt for UX and performance; none of the protected interactive elements above should be removed.

##### Footer (without a Join button)

| Column | Contents |
|---|---|
| Identity | Original official logo, readable OneSpace name, tagline, contact email |
| Product | Features, demo, tutorials, What's new, availability, Founding 500 |
| For couples | Living apart, living together |
| Help | Guides, FAQ, support, can't sign in, Share kit |
| Legal | Privacy policy, terms of use, delete account |

A page's own CTA and the sticky header CTA are sufficient. No duplicate footer Join button.

##### Real screenshots and screenshot robot

The website already has real screenshot assets for `assets/app/en/letters.webp`, `assets/app/en/love.webp` and their Arabic equivalents. The new Connect & Care section uses those originals. The check-in and feelings journal sections currently use an existing *general Connect screen* or illustration; they are not passed off as dedicated captures of these features.

A real Android robot workflow exists at `.github/workflows/app-screens.yml` and requires manual GitHub Actions dispatch, access to the private mobile app and configured demo credentials. Once fresh check-in and journal screenshots exist, import them into `assets/app/en` and `assets/app/ar`; ensure captions accurately describe the captured screen. Do not publish screenshots of personal user information. This website iteration did not trigger a robot run.

##### Test-only reviews, testimonials, and ratings

The home page has a clearly identified **AI-generated fictional design preview** (`#reviews-preview`). It includes hypothetical testimonial wording and placeholder panels for Google Play and App Store.

- Do not claim these quotes were written by users or infer reviewer names, ages or locations.
- Do not publish fictitious ratings, counts, store review links, verified-buyer badges, `AggregateRating` structured data or misleading star scores.
- Show the text **“TEST ONLY · AI-GENERATED PLACEHOLDERS · NOT REAL CUSTOMER REVIEWS OR RATINGS”** prominently.
- Generated section carries `data-nosnippet` to discourage use in search excerpts.
- Set `SHOW_AI_REVIEW_PREVIEW = False` in `build.py`, rebuild, and the full preview disappears after review.
- Replace content only with real verified user/store feedback and appropriate permissions.

##### Localization and guardrails

Website language variants: **English, Arabic, Spanish, French, German**. All five share one canonical HTML structure and assets; native-quality copy is reviewed via `content/locale_editorial.py` and the existing localization system.

`tests/check_site.py` and `tests/check_megamenu.py` verify navigation, screenshots, preservation of the demo/theme selector, protected content, no footer Join button, three actual Smart Sharing steps, and safe AI-preview labels.

**Mobile app:** no Flutter release, APK or store submission is required for this website-focused iteration. Existing mobile Arabic translation remains under separate review.
