### A124. onespace-app · `docs/website-ux-enhancement-implementation-brief.md`

*First written 18 Nov 2026 · last changed 18 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — Website UX Enhancement Implementation Brief

**Status:** Proposed implementation specification, pending detailed design review  
**Date:** 18 November 2026  
**Scope:** Marketing website only; **no Flutter release** or changes to the app's production data.  
**Source of truth:** `build.py`, `assets/os.css`, `assets/os.js`, existing screenshot assets, and the approved website sitemap.  
**Product identity:** **OneSpace · Two of Us. One Space.** Use the **original** symbol and wordmark artwork unchanged.

##### 1. Objective and positioning

###### Hero/value proposition

**Headline:** “Stay close. Build a life together.”  
**Subheadline:** “One private space for two people to share ideas, talk through options, make decisions, plan what’s next, and keep the little moments that matter.”  
**Supporting line:** “For couples living together or miles apart.”  
**Actions:** “Explore how it works” → `how-it-works.html`; “Join free” → `join.html`.

**Core differentiation:** Emotional-connection features are the foundation, not the unique selling point on their own. OneSpace goes **beyond letters, gestures, and check-ins** by connecting **smart share, unified collective lists, two-way editing, in-context discussion, side-by-side comparisons, decisions, follow-ups, trips, occasions and budgets**. Phrase competitive claims positively; do not assert other products lack capabilities without verified evidence.

**Signature line for the “Why OneSpace” section:**  
“More than staying connected. A shared life, organized together.”  
**Explainer:** “A love note brings you closer. OneSpace also gives you a place to save the link you found, compare your options, discuss the details, decide together, and turn that decision into a plan.”

###### Non-negotiable preservation rules

Do **not** remove, replace, or reduce:
- Official OneSpace logo, brand identity, tagline, fonts already chosen and theme colors.
- Existing real app screenshots or phone-frame demonstrations.
- Recorded product tour, tutorial pages and their navigation.
- Interactive demo workflow and its existing step-specific real screenshots.
- Working nine-theme selector, five-tab interactive homepage, and theme showcases.
- “Sound familiar?” problem/solution examples.
- One-list-per-kind / Smart Share explainer and existing feature showcases.
- Responsive RTL Arabic and English, Spanish, French and German website copies.
- Founder signup flow, single canonical form, legal pages, SEO metadata, original app availability wording.

The new elements are **additions or refinements**, not grounds for redesigning everything else.

##### 2. Navigation and sitemap (retain approved)

| Primary navigation | Content |
|---|---|
| **Features** | Three mega-menu groups: **Collect & Share**, **Plan & Decide**, **Connect & Care** |
| **Why OneSpace** | Competitive positioning and connected workflows (`compare.html`) |
| **For couples** | Living apart / Living together |
| **Demo** | Guided six-step product wizard (`how-it-works.html`) |
| **Learn** | Tutorials, guides, FAQ, updates |
| **Get the app** | Honest Google Play / App Store availability |
| **Join free** | Single Founder signup (`join.html`) |

**Features mega menu:**
- **Collect & Share:** Smart Sharing & Unified Lists; Watchlists & Music; Lists, Reminders & Contacts; Memories & Scrapbooks.
- **Plan & Decide:** Compare Options Together; Trips & Flights; Occasions & Gifts.
- **Connect & Care:** Love Letters; A Little Love; Daily Check-ins; Feelings Journal.

Add **Global Search** and **Notifications** prominently to the Features page as *cross-cutting product capabilities*, not cluttering the mega menu. Keep “Compare options together” distinct from the “Why OneSpace” competitive explanation.

##### 3. Homepage — refined editorial sequence

Maintain **all existing showcase sections** while clarifying the visual hierarchy. Preferred storytelling sequence and exact copy:

| Block | Heading | Short supporting copy | Visual treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hero | **Stay close. Build a life together.** | One private space for two people to share, discuss, decide, plan, and remember. | Existing official mark, actual app devices; no substituted assets |
| Differentiation | **More than staying connected. A shared life, organized together.** | What you find becomes a shared idea. What you discuss becomes a decision. What you decide becomes a plan. | Existing restrained editorial four-part layout |
| Smart Share | **Share from anywhere. Find it together.** | Turn scattered links, places, watchlists, and ideas into one shared destination for each kind of item. | Preserve existing one-list-per-kind demonstration |
| Planning/decisions | **From “what do you think?” to “we’ve decided.”** | Compare options side by side, add both perspectives, discuss with context and keep the decision linked to the next step. | Reuse activity/decision screenshot |
| Connect & Care | **A little love, and a lot of life together.** | Letters, gestures and daily check-ins keep you close. Shared plans, discussions and decisions carry you forward. | Retain genuine Letters / Love Gestures screenshots |
| “Sound familiar?” | **Love is easy. Logistics aren’t.** | Small everyday problems, solved without losing the conversation. | **Protect current cards/interaction** |
| Product showcase | **Familiar ideas, thoughtfully extended.** | The details work together: calendars, memories, trips, occasions, dates, lists. | **Protect current feature showcases** |
| Living arrangement | **Apart or together, OneSpace fits your life.** | The right information for where and how you live. | **Protect existing living-mode toggle** |
| Interactive preview | **Five tabs. Nine moods. One shared space.** | Explore the real product layout and switch its look. | **Protect existing five-tab & nine-theme experience** |
| Smart utilities | **Find it fast. Know what matters.** | Global search recovers the right detail; contextual notifications help you act on it. | Two editorial rows with real screens |
| Testimonials | **A glimpse of what couples might say.** | Illustrative quotes for layout review only. | 2.5-card swipe carousel, **fictional AI test data** |
| Store proof preview | **App ratings & reviews — layout preview.** | Example interface only; official store data isn't published here yet. | Google Play / App Store panels; no deceptive ratings |
| Store availability | **Your Space, soon on your phone.** | Android early access, iPhone planned; link only to real waitlist until stores go live. | **Protect existing logos/status text** |
| Founder signup | **Be among the first 250 couples.** | One Founder number for two people. | Existing single canonical form CTA |

Placement may vary if responsive constraints require, but the protected blocks must remain.

##### 4. Demo page — six-step guided wizard

**Route:** `how-it-works.html`. Reuse all six existing views and existing real app screenshot switching; do not build a fake app simulator.

**Eyebrow:** “TRY ONESPACE TOGETHER”  
**H1:** “One idea. Two perspectives. One shared plan.”  
**Lead:** “Follow a real example, from something that needs attention to a decision and a small moment of affection. Explore at your own pace.”

**Desktop layout:** two columns, approximately **42% controls / 58% device preview**. Left side includes progress label, a **2 × 3 grid** of compact selectable step tabs (at widths ≥ 1080px) or a single-column vertical list if the available width would force text overflow. Right side holds one prominent phone and a short caption. This avoids the current six oversized stacked cards. Do **not** use side-by-side long explanations in every tab.

**At a glance:** “Step 2 of 6” with accessible `<progress>` or `aria-valuenow/min/max` status and a slim coral progress track.

**Exact six tab labels and descriptions:**

| Step | Tab label | Active detail |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Notice what matters** | “See what needs your attention in Today.” |
| 2 | **Save the idea** | “Capture the idea in a shared note so it doesn’t get lost.” |
| 3 | **Compare the options** | “Move into Coordinate and collect links, prices and possibilities.” |
| 4 | **Talk it through** | “Discuss the options right next to them, with both opinions in context.” |
| 5 | **Decide and follow up** | “Record your choice and keep next steps, reminders and dates linked.” |
| 6 | **Stay close** | “Finish with a thoughtful gesture, because planning isn’t all you share.” |

**Wizard behavior:** Select any step; it updates the selected style, title, detailed copy, progress, phone screenshot and URL hash (`#step-1` … `#step-6`) without reloading. Provide **Previous** and **Next** buttons. On step 1, Previous is disabled; on step 6, Next becomes **“Explore again”** and resets explicitly (no unexpected circular loop). Support arrow-key navigation, Enter/Space, clear focus, `aria-selected`, `aria-controls`, and accessible announcements when the displayed view changes.

**Theme selector:** clearly separate from navigation, under heading **“Make it yours”**, subtitle “Try a theme without changing the story.” Arrange all **nine existing themes** into equal-width chips (desktop 3 columns; tablet 3 columns; narrow mobile 2 columns). A swatch and label must not wrap awkwardly or change chip height. Selected theme has a visible ring plus `aria-pressed`; minimum touch target 44px. Keep all current theme keys, screenshot behavior, and theme-switching logic. Changing the theme must **not** reset wizard progress.

**Mobile:** single column; progress + active step first, phone second, navigation third. Step tabs may be a horizontal scrollable row with a clear active state rather than a cramped 2 × 3 grid. Ensure long German/French labels do not overflow.

**CTA:** after the demo, one restrained “Join early access” CTA; avoid stacking multiple Join buttons in the wizard itself.

##### 5. Global Search and Notifications — new cross-feature showcase

**Section eyebrow:** “SMALL DETAILS, BIG DIFFERENCE”  
**H2:** “Find it fast. Know what matters.”  
**Lead:** “The important details shouldn’t disappear into long conversations.”

###### Global Search

**Feature title:** “One search across your shared space.”  
**Copy:** “Remember the link, place, note or decision—but not where it was saved? Use OneSpace’s in-app global search to find what you need without hunting through every feature.”  
**Bullets:** “Search from one place”; “See results with their context”; “Open the original item directly.”

**Disclosure:** Search categories and permissions must be checked against the actual mobile build. Do not claim search covers letters, message bodies, attachments or every private field unless verified by app QA. Search results should respect **Shared / Only me** privacy. When confirmed, list actual indexed object types on the website.

**Screen:** Dedicated real `global_search.webp` screenshot still needs capturing. Use the current general search explanation with a neutral empty screenshot frame or omit the visual until captured; do not relabel a different screenshot as Search.

###### Notifications

**Feature title:** “Notifications with context, not noise.”  
**Copy:** “Know who shared something, what changed and which decisions or reminders need your attention. Open the relevant item instead of searching for it later.”  
**Bullets:** “Who, what and when”; “Grouped updates”; “A direct path to what needs attention.”

**Screen:** Reuse existing real `assets/app/en/notifications.webp` (and Arabic if present) in the phone frame. Validate the explanation against the current notification center and only promise behavior actually implemented.

**Placement:** homepage editorial pair after the existing theme showcase, plus dedicated “Search & Notifications” section on `features.html`. Neither feature replaces the Today/Remember/Coordinate/Connect/Me stories.

##### 6. Love-gesture popup and additional captures

**Existing genuine assets:** `assets/app/en/letters.webp`, `assets/app/en/love.webp`, `assets/app/en/notifications.webp`. Arabic counterparts must be checked before use.

**Capture required:** Love Gesture **popup/modal**, global search, and (if available) focused daily check-in + feelings journal screens.

**Required popup composition:** Keep the current real Love Gesture tab screenshot as the main device; show a **second, smaller real screenshot** of the gesture opened, overlaid/offset by about 16–24px. Label:
- “Choose a gesture” — the tab/gallery.
- “Make it personal” — popup with gesture text, optional message and send action, **only if those controls exist in the captured build**.

Do not recreate a screenshot with CSS or AI and present it as real.

**Robot:** Existing GitHub Actions screenshot workflow `.github/workflows/app-screens.yml` is **manual dispatch**. It builds the private Flutter app and runs a demo-couple emulator against `integration_test/screenshots_test.dart`, publishing outputs to the `app-screens` branch. Verify that the screenshot script actually supports the extra states; extend robot steps if needed, then run only with the fictional Omar/Lina demo environment. Never expose production accounts or private content. Import approved EN and AR captures through the existing importer; no mobile release is needed.

**Fallback:** If a capture cannot be produced, ship accurate text and existing screenshots, leaving a labeled image slot for later; do not delay the rest of the site iteration.

##### 7. Testimonials — compact peek carousel

**Eyebrow:** “DESIGN PREVIEW · AI-GENERATED EXAMPLES”  
**H2:** “A glimpse of what couples might say.”  
**Subheading:** “Illustrative examples only. These are not real user testimonials.”

**Desktop behavior:** **2.5 cards visible** in the viewport (two whole cards, a third peeking in). Tablet: 1.6–2 cards. Mobile: 1.15 cards. Support drag/swipe, visible Previous/Next arrows, pagination dots, keyboard operation, and a progress indicator. No forced auto-rotation; respect reduced motion. Keep carousel height stable across quotes.

**Card:** short quote (≤ 25 words), context label (“Long-distance”, “Living together”, “Planning a trip”), and permanently visible **“AI-generated test example”** caption. Do not add invented human names, portraits, star ratings or “verified” badges.

**Exactly four sample quotes (fictional):**
1. “We kept sending each other links. Now our shortlist lives in one place.” — Long-distance.
2. “We can discuss different options and actually remember what we decided.” — Living together.
3. “Our trip ideas, packing list and decisions finally have a place together.” — Planning a trip.
4. “The sweet messages are still there, alongside everything we need to plan.” — Long-distance.

Use editorial cards and a subtle partial-card peek; avoid oversized testimonial blocks.

##### 8. App reviews and ratings — responsible testing-only layout

**Eyebrow:** “TEST ONLY — AI-GENERATED DESIGN PREVIEW”  
**H2:** “App ratings & reviews.”  
**Subheading:** “This is a design prototype, not verified Google Play or App Store data.”

**Two side-by-side store panels** (stack on mobile), each containing:
- Official platform icon and correct store name.
- Overall score **out of 5**.
- Number of ratings/reviews.
- Downloads/installs if the platform provides trustworthy public data.
- **Five-row histogram** (5★ to 1★) with percentage or count labels.
- A short review excerpt region that can show an authentic review later.
- Optional “View on Google Play / App Store” link **only when a verified live store listing exists**.

**Before launch:** show `—` and **“Not available yet”** instead of fictional live metrics. Show gray empty histogram tracks with captions “Awaiting verified data.” Keep visual completeness through layout, not fabricated numeric scores.

**Optional internal prototype mode:** Simulated example metrics (such as “SAMPLE 4.8 / 5”, “SAMPLE 128 reviews”, “SAMPLE 10K downloads”, with 82/11/4/2/1 percentage bars) may be used only inside a **clearly watermarked test-only preview**, never as production store results. Label **every simulated number**, not just the section header. Do not publish fake `AggregateRating` or review schema data. For Apple, do not assume Apple publishes download counts; if unavailable, show `—` even after launch.

**Required permanent preview disclosure (all languages):**  
“**TEST ONLY — AI-GENERATED PLACEHOLDER CONTENT. THESE ARE NOT REAL CUSTOMER TESTIMONIALS, REVIEWS OR APP-STORE RATINGS.**”

**Gating:** Keep the existing `SHOW_AI_REVIEW_PREVIEW` toggle in `build.py`. This controls the whole review/testimonial section without deleting the component code; configure it off when design approval is finished. Do not use examples in SEO metadata, social preview snippets, search review snippets or paid marketing.

##### 9. Footer — social media placeholders

Keep existing readable original OneSpace identity. Do **not** add another Join button.

**Footer columns:**
1. **Product:** Features, Demo, Tutorials, What’s new, Availability, Founding 500.
2. **For couples:** Living apart, Living together.
3. **Help & Resources:** Guides, FAQ, Contact & Support, Can’t sign in?, Share Kit.
4. **Legal:** Privacy, Terms, Delete Account.

**Social placeholder row:** Place compact official-style icons under the footer brand/email, in one line wrapping as needed: **Instagram, Facebook, YouTube, TikTok, LinkedIn**. Labels/hover or focus text: **“Coming soon”**. While URLs are unknown, render **non-interactive placeholders** with a disabled appearance and accessible text—not dead `href="#"` links, fabricated profile URLs or clickable inert controls. Once authentic channels exist, turn those placeholders into real links with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`.

##### 10. Type scale and UI system

Use a single responsive typography hierarchy in `assets/os.css`, scoped consistently across marketing pages. Keep existing **Inter** for Latin and **IBM Plex Sans Arabic** for Arabic. Do not change the official logo or redesign the brand typeface.

| Token | Desktop | Tablet | Mobile | Recommended line-height |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Display / Hero H1 | 56px | 46px | 34px | 1.10–1.18 |
| H2 / Section | 40px | 34px | 28px | 1.16–1.25 |
| H3 / Feature | 25px | 23px | 21px | 1.25–1.35 |
| H4 / Card | 19px | 18px | 18px | 1.3–1.4 |
| Lead copy | 19px | 18px | 17px | 1.55–1.7 |
| Body | 16px | 16px | 16px | 1.6–1.75 |
| Supporting text | 14px | 14px | 14px | 1.5–1.6 |
| Eyebrow/metadata | 12–13px | 12–13px | 12px | 1.45–1.6 |
| Button label | 15px | 15px | 15px | 1.3–1.45 |

Implement via CSS custom properties and `clamp()`, minimizing page-specific inline font declarations. For Arabic, use natural line heights (often 1.65+) and **no letter-spacing on Arabic text**. Nav dropdown labels must not exceed the size of top-level nav. Text buttons should grow modestly in width or wrap to two balanced lines—not become enormous blocks. Maintain accessible contrast, 44px touch targets, stable heights, real focus styles and consistent container radius.

##### 11. Responsive, accessibility and UX tests

**Viewport targets:** 360, 390, 768, 1024 and 1440px; EN + Arabic RTL plus ES/FR/DE long labels.

**Functional checks:**
- Demo: exactly six distinct steps; Next/Previous; progress and phone change in sync; final state explicit; hash/deep link restores selection; no unexpected theme reset.
- Theme selector: exactly nine existing selectable themes, consistent grid/chip dimensions, all current theme assets preserved.
- Testimonials: 2.5 desktop cards (visible peek), swipe, arrows, dots and accessible keyboard navigation; no forced autoplay.
- Ratings: two separate platform panels, metric count fields and 5-row breakdown; no fabricated production values; visible AI/test disclaimer; no review schema.
- Global Search: screenshot verified as actual app screen; exact object coverage checked and no exposure of private entries.
- Notifications: original real screenshot reused; action labels and actual behavior verified.
- Love Gesture: existing gallery screenshot plus genuine popup screenshot, when captured, with truthful captions.
- Footer: real wordmark contrast on dark background, no footer Join button, social placeholders clearly unavailable, link categories correct.
- Home: original screenshot section, recorded clips, tutorials, interactive five-tab demo, all nine themes, Sound familiar?, and showcases remain accessible and functional.
- Translation: no missing EN/AR/ES/FR/DE strings; natural localization and RTL alignment. Localize all new labels and data disclaimers, not just headings.
- Quality: no horizontal overflow, clipped CTAs, giant translated buttons, inaccessible icons, broken links, broken image assets or console exceptions.

**Technical integration:** `build.py` (page generation), `assets/os.js` (wizard & carousel behavior), `assets/os.css` (type scale/layout), `content/locale_editorial.py` / existing translation catalogs, `tests/check_site.py` / `tests/check_megamenu.py`; existing screenshot workflow and importer.

##### 12. Delivery sequence

**A. Protect and baseline:** Document all preserved screenshot URLs, sections and theme keys; run current site CI, no redesign of the mark.

**B. Demo + typography:** New guided wizard and aligned 9-theme grid; unified type scale; validate all five locales.

**C. Product storytelling:** Add Global Search and Notifications copy + real Notifications screenshot; keep existing Connect & Care; prepare love popup composition.

**D. Social proof preview:** Replace oversized testimonial block with 2.5-card carousel; add structured but explicitly fictional testing reviews with 5-row rating breakdown and honest empty metrics; include one central test toggle.

**E. Footer:** Add disabled/coming-soon social placeholders; keep correct link grouping.

**F. Capture and QA:** Extend/rerun screenshot robot as required for popup, search, check-in and journal; validate genuine captures; build/regenerate five locales, run automated and keyboard/responsive checks, deploy website only after tests pass.

**Definition of done:** All changes above pass checks, every protected section survives, screenshots used are genuine, store proof is clearly testing-only, no fake social accounts or ratings appear as live data, and no mobile app release is triggered.

---

**Important scope note:** This document is an implementation specification; writing or approving it does **not** mean the visual changes, screenshot robot runs or runtime QA described above have been performed yet.

### A125. OneSpace · `docs/INVITATION_EMAIL_GMAIL_FIX_2026_11_19.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 19 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Solo-user QA — Gmail invitation rendering and branded email

Reported: 19 Nov 2026. The original Gmail draft showed literal plus signs
in the subject and body and did not render the link as a button.

##### Root cause
Flutter `Uri(queryParameters:)` uses form-query encoding and replaces
spaces with '+'; RFC 6068 mailto expects '%20'. Email clients vary in
whether a URL looks hyperlinked while a draft is being composed.

##### New delivery paths
- **Email → Send branded email**: enter partner's email. Supabase Edge
  Function `onespace-send-invitation` reads the existing active token
  under the signed-in creator's RLS. Sends HTML + plaintext using the
  provider's server-side RESEND_API_KEY.
- **Open email draft**: corrected RFC 6068 percent-encoded plain text.
  Does not claim to support logo images or official store artwork.
- Both use the **same active code**; no replacement and no expiry extension.
- Branded HTML uses the existing OneSpace website logo and official tagline
  "Two of Us. One Space."; clickable call-to-action and visible fallback
  link. When a store's real product link is not configured, show a
  nonclickable "Coming soon" object, never a fake store destination.
  Once real store links are configured via ONESPACE_PLAY_STORE_URL and
  ONESPACE_APP_STORE_URL, render the official provider badges with links.

##### Security
- Authenticated only (verify_jwt=true and /auth/v1/user validated).
- The server looks up the invitation using the user's access token;
  client-supplied tokens/forged sender names are ignored.
- Rate-limit guard is service-role-only: 5 sends per user per hour;
  at most one per recipient per 15 minutes. Only hashed recipient IDs
  appear in the private sending log; never passwords or raw email.
- Invalid/revoked/expired/other people's invitations are refused.
- Domain's Resend API key never appears in app or web code.
- Do not invite anyone until user explicitly presses Send branded email.

##### Verification
1. QA Android: Email → Open email draft in Gmail. No literal '+'
   characters for spaces; subject/body are readable and text is spaced.
2. Email → send branded email to an **authorized test address** and read the
   actual delivered HTML email in Gmail; images load, invitation CTA is
   clickable, same 64-char code and expiry as the Today card.
3. Unavailable stores show non-clickable tiles. Never advertise an
   unapproved App Store or Play app listing as available.
4. Send twice to the same address within 15 min: second request fails.
   A different authorized recipient may receive another within hourly cap.
5. Recipient with app installed follows invitation; recipient without
   app gets manual-code fallback on /app-invite/.
6. Repeat on QA iOS; ensure sender identity and brand signature are correct.
7. Recheck endpoint accepts **no anonymous calls** and does not send
   expired/revoked/other-user invitations.
8. Production backend and app separately; do not treat a passing QA
   test as evidence that production SMTP credentials or links work.

---

### A126. OneSpace · `docs/ONESPACE_PAIRING_SEPARATION_ARCHIVE_RESTORE_DECISIONS_2026-11-19.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 19 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by ADR-2026-11-20 (no archiving, 20 Nov 2026). Kept for history.*

#### OneSpace — Pairing, Separation, Archive, and Restore Decisions

**Recorded:** 19 November 2026  
**Status:** Product decision and handover record; **not an authorization to implement**.  
**See also:** The full downloadable handover `OneSpace_Pairing_Separation_Archive_Restore_Decisions_2026-11-19.md` provided in the ChatGPT conversation (196 lines, including detailed acceptance tests and open decisions).

> **Newest correction overrides earlier proposals:** Automatically downloaded encrypted archives must live in **OneSpace's installed-app private file storage**, not in the public Files/Downloads area. They **disappear when the app is uninstalled**. Independently, the user receives an email with an authenticated archive download link and can deliberately save a durable external copy. Temporary server archives are deleted upon **verified successful download or after 30 days**, whichever occurs first. **Decision 19 Nov: only an explicit USER-INITIATED external archive download/save, completed and verified, triggers early deletion. An automatic copy to app-private storage NEVER triggers server deletion.**

##### Confirmed product decisions

1. **No new Remember me checkbox.** Continue secure persisted sessions, OS biometric/device unlock, and Google/Apple password managers; no storing recoverable passwords inside OneSpace.
2. **No input popups.** All form entry and multistep workflows occur on full subpages. Use confirmation popups only, with no text fields.
3. **Change password:** Subpage, exactly current password, new password, confirm new password; do not show account email. Plain-text **Send a reset email** CTA appears after the three inputs.
4. **Invite my partner:** Replace invitation CTA label; show a dedicated full page asking for B's email. Invite is disabled until valid email. Backend saves recipient email, invitation status and code; sends branded email from `invitations@onespace.live`. Show QR and formatted copyable code on A's page (no raw invitation URL). Disable Invite after submission; allow status/resend same code/replacement with confirmation/revoke. Today continues to show the compact invitation card.
5. **Invited recipient B:** Email includes clickable invitation, OneSpace logo, proper tagline and official app store icons/badges. Badge links only if valid/applicable; otherwise nonclickable. App link opens installed OneSpace, or install/manual-code fallback if absent. After signup/signin and verified email, automatically join the original owner's existing space; QR and manual code also work. The backend must verify B's authenticated verified email equals the intended recipient, including for QR/manual entry.
6. **Space permissions:** Original owner A **cannot leave**, cannot transfer ownership, and may **Remove partner** or **Delete space**. Invited B may **Leave space** only. Add **My Space** under Profile immediately before Change password. Separation and deletion actions need a simple confirmation popup.
7. **Owner continuity after B leaves or is removed:** A keeps the same OneSpace experience and can invite C **without creating a space manually**. Backend internally establishes a fresh relationship-space boundary (potentially new internal ID) carrying only A's personal/private data. C can never see B's old shared content, notifications, attachments or pending actions. This is a UI continuity rule, **not permission to reuse old relationship history**.
8. **Invited B after leaving/removal:** Becomes unpaired, keeps own private account/data, can create a space or join another person.
9. **Owner Delete space:** Both users become unpaired and keep private accounts/data. Distinguish from Remove partner: do **not** automatically re-create a shared space after deliberate Delete space unless separately approved.
10. **Private versus shared:** Personal/private notes, contacts, reminders, memories, attachments etc. belong to their original account and survive separation. Old relationship shared objects/media are archived for each entitled partner, then removed from live backend when safe. Any old shared reference within a private item becomes harmless, inert, non-navigable historical text.
11. **Archive composition:** Each entitled user receives own private-data archive and an authorized old shared-history archive, with actual files/media and integrity information. Do **not** include the other partner's private records. **Only private archives can ever be restored**; old shared-history archives are reference-only.
12. **Archive locations:** (a) automatic encrypted local copy inside **installed OneSpace app private files** (lost on uninstall); (b) temporary, private encrypted **backend archive object**; (c) optional durable external copy **deliberately saved by user** through emailed authenticated download link. No email ZIP attachments/public permanent links. Email notifies each partner of archive readiness.
13. **Archive cleanup:** Delete temporary server archive objects after verified successful download or at 30 days, whichever is earlier. No indefinite cloud archives. Ensure recipient-specific and race-safe cleanup. Explicit user download/save with verification—not automatic app-private save—triggers early purge; otherwise purge after 30 days. App-local archive and deliberately saved external archive are distinct.
14. **Live storage cleanup:** Once entitled archives are actually verified and the safety gate is met, delete old shared database records **and actual Storage files via Storage API**, not SQL-only Storage metadata deletion. Retain private records and attachments. Maintain only legally/operationally necessary audit status.
15. **Restore private data:** Preferred automatic continuity via user-owned Personal Vault when same account joins another space; no import if data already exists. Where recovery is needed after joining, offer a **full restore subpage** from (a) this installed app's private archive, (b) server archive if still retained, or (c) manually selected user-saved external archive. Validate cryptographic authenticity, identity/account ownership, integrity, archive type and per-record privacy; dedupe and show real progress. Private remains private. Shared content must never enter a new relationship.
16. **Delete account cascades:** Owner A's Delete account must automatically orchestrate space retirement, exports, notifications and cleanup before account deletion. No separate manual Delete space step. B's Delete account similarly performs a B-leave path so A continues in a clean private-only context. Existing account-deletion grace period requires reconciliation.
17. **Notifications:** Both receive account-level in-app notifications and email for leave/remove/delete/export. Do not store lifecycle notifications solely under a space that will be deleted.

##### Suggested end-to-end actions

###### Invite
A opens **Invite my partner** → enters partner email → Invite → backend persists recipient and invitation securely → email sent/status recorded → A sees QR + copyable manual code, Close → B opens link, installs if necessary, authenticates and verifies email → backend checks recipient identity, code validity and membership → B joins current relationship exactly once → both get confirmation.

###### B leaves / A removes B
Confirmation-only dialog → persist separation request / block old shared writes and invitations → retain both users' private data and media → make per-user private/shared archive snapshots and verify → prepare app-private automatic downloads and email download links → delete active shared records/media safely → A silently transitions to clean owner space and can invite C; B is unpaired → per-user server archives expire on successful download (trigger pending) or at 30 days → app-local copies remain until app uninstall, externally saved copies persist under user's control.

###### A deletes space
Same export/cleanup/notification protections → both accounts become unpaired, private data stays → do not create replacement space automatically.

###### A deletes account / B deletes account
Higher-level account deletion invokes respective space-close / B-leave sequence automatically. Grace-period cancellation semantics and how a deleted account authenticates to retrieve an archive remain open.

###### Restore into later space
User joins/creates new relationship → if user's private data already exists, continue with no duplication → if recovery needed, offer installed-app private backup, still-available server backup, or Choose archive → validate user/signature/integrity and private-only entries → import privately, detach old shared references, display progress/errors → no old shared content can be restored into the new relationship.

##### Archive rules — critical distinction

| Location | How created | Removal | Survives uninstall? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| App-private installed OneSpace files | Automatic encrypted download | App uninstall / app-data removal (plus any separately approved cache policy) | **No** |
| Private temporary server archives | Backend export job | **Successful verified download OR 30-day deadline**, first event; **explicit user-initiated, verified external download** | Only until server purge |
| External user-saved archive | User follows authenticated email link and saves deliberately outside app | User controls own copy | **Yes, if actually saved outside app** |

**Loss warning:** The automatic app-private copy MUST NOT cause backend archive deletion. The user-initiated external download must finish saving and pass integrity verification before cleanup can begin. The user must be told that the archive will be removed from the server after that action.

##### Verified existing-system findings (read-only QA/repository investigation)

- Current `spaces.created_by` captures creator; `space_members` maps account to relationship.
- **33 direct tables** have a `space_id` foreign key with `ON DELETE CASCADE`; many contain mixed private/shared items. Direct space deletion would risk deleting private data. Separate user-owned Personal Vault before destructive changes.
- Current `Download my data` (`lib/features/settings/account_data_screen.dart`) exports JSON via `export_my_data` and saves to phone Downloads; **not** the proposed encrypted archival system, media-inclusive export, or app-private temporary archive.
- `space_notifications` are space linked and would be lost under cascade; add account-scoped lifecycle notifications.
- Supabase Storage contains activity media/voice, memory photos, note images and trip documents. SQL-only deletion from Storage metadata is insufficient; Storage API must remove actual objects.
- Current account deletion has a **seven-day grace period** requiring explicit integration into high-level cascading Delete account.
- Secure session / device unlock already exist; no new Remember me feature.

##### Pending decisions (NOT settled)

- **P01 RESOLVED 19 Nov:** Only a deliberate user-initiated archive download to a user-chosen external location, after confirmed save and checksum verification, triggers early server deletion. Automatic app-private downloads never count. The 30-day deadline remains the fallback. Each recipient and archive type is independent.
- **P02:** Whether to expose an additional **Save external copy** CTA inside the app and warn before server copy is purged.
- **P03:** Exact start of the 30-day countdown (separation, archive creation, or user notification) and reminder cadence.
- **P04:** Local app-private archive growth, quotas and user-initiated local cleanup; is it kept until uninstall by default?
- **P05:** Portable encryption/recovery credential policy for user-saved external files after reinstall/device loss; account-bound signature validation.
- **P06:** Canonical data classification and migration of mixed private/shared rows and linked attachments.
- **P07:** Owner-continuity implementation: personal workspace + fresh relationship ID versus backend copy of private content; preserve invisible UX.
- **P08:** Seven-day account deletion grace period, timing of separation, cancellation effects, and archive download access after account deletion.
- **P09:** Handling partner offline/undeliverable email through 30-day expiry, irreversible loss warning.
- **P10:** Durable export queue, chunking, retry and integrity validation, encryption, Storage limits/cost, and provider failures.
- **P11:** Exact UI after explicit owner Delete space versus owner Remove partner.
- **P12:** Verified Android/iOS App/Universal Links, QR/manual recipient checks, official store URLs and invitation delivery statuses.
- **P13:** Audit/legal/backups retention exceptions versus removal of active data and temporary archives.
- **P14:** Restore prompting only for missing data; progress/duplicates/partial import handling.

##### QA acceptance checklist (when authorized)

- A invites B with validated email; invite cannot be redeemed by another email, duplicate/expired/revoked codes fail.
- B signs up/verifies and joins once, including link/QR/manual code and app restart.
- A removes B or B leaves → owner stays in continuous private-only OneSpace and can invite C immediately; no old shared data actions/notifications remain.
- A deletes space → both unpaired with private data; owner is not silently auto-repaired.
- Old-space shared data/media included in authorized exports and deleted using Storage API once safe; personal data retained.
- App-private archives are present while installed, **lost after uninstall**; external copies users deliberately saved remain accessible; server archive success/30-day purge is verified according to resolved P01.
- Only account-authentic private data restores from app-local/server/manual archive; shared content and other user's data fail verification. Old shared links inert; duplicated imports safe.
- Owner account deletion cascades without requiring separate manual space deletion, and invited partner is correctly unpaired.
- Confirm UI has no data-entry popup, owner Leave/Transfer actions do not exist, no new Remember me checkbox.

##### Implementation order — proposal only

1. Finalize P01, 30-day retention clock, portable archive key policy and data classification.
2. Refactor schema and ownership so private data/media survive space retirement.
3. Build encrypted per-user archives, integrity checks, background jobs, account-level notifications, app-private auto-copy, external authenticated email download, and lifecycle purge.
4. Build secure role-based leave/remove/delete and owner's continuous clean relationship context.
5. Build private-only archive restoration and integration into higher-level Delete account.
6. Build subpage-only UX and recipient-specific invitation/verified-email auto-pairing.
7. Exercise complete QA lifecycle on disposable data + real media, including failure conditions, and review Production security before release.

---

**Handover:** This file records current decisions and explicit open questions. **No database, app, user-account, or storage changes are authorized by this document.** When resuming work, **read this first; P01 is resolved: only verified user-initiated external save counts toward early purge**.

### A127. OneSpace · `docs/PROPOSED_SIMPLIFIED_SEPARATION_NO_ARCHIVE_20261119.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN — the proposal became ADR-2026-11-20 (20 Nov 2026).*

> **SUPERSEDED by the founder's confirmed 20 November 2026 requirement:**
> Archive **and restore remain required** for structured private/shared data.
> Uploaded media, photos, voice recordings and documents are excluded.
> This old *no-archive* proposal is NOT approved. See `QA_STRUCTURED_DATA_MILESTONE_20261120.md`.

#### OneSpace product scope review: simplify separation without archive generation

Date: 2026-11-19 — founder feedback; **proposal, not implemented deletion authorization**.

##### Clarifications
- Risk of deleting a space is **structural for new records too**, not merely old
  QA rows. Many currently writable records have space_id CASCADE and can
  contain private personal content. Deleting QA sample records won't repair it.
- An **external copy saved by the user** and a **downloaded archive file** are
  the same artifact. The UI should say simply **Download archive**; no separate
  redundant "external copy" workflow.
- Founder asked whether OneSpace could **offer no media/content archival or
  restoration**, with an explicit warning. This is technically possible, but it
  is a product-policy change versus the former user-owned archive specification.

##### Minimal alternative (if adopted)
1. Protect private data in a user-owned store independent of relationship-space
   cascade deletion, including private attachments, and verify across all
   dependent tables. Private content remains available to its owner.
2. Warn clearly before irreversible separation: old shared records, chats,
   photos, documents and linked files **will be permanently deleted**; they
   cannot be archived or restored by OneSpace. Never silently promise a backup.
3. Offer a deliberate, truthful data export before irreversible deletion.
   Current Download my data is JSON only without actual media; do NOT represent
   it as full-data export. Any download must be user-initiated.
4. After explicit irreversible consent, delete only shared relationship
   metadata and media through Storage API; verify privacy and cleanup.
5. Owner who removes partner receives a clean relationship boundary; invited
   partner becomes unpaired. Owner Delete space leaves both unpaired; personal
   records are retained. Account deletion still needs explicit grace-period
   policy and independent authentication/session cleanup.

**Status:** No account/space/partner deletion enabled, no Production changes,
no QA data hard-deleted, and archive generation remains unimplemented pending
formal decision. Prior archive specs are not silently overridden.

### A128. OneSpace · `docs/QA_AUTH_SENDER_BRANDING_2026-11-19.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 19 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### QA Auth password-reset sender — configuration gate

**Finding:** Password reset emails originate from Supabase Auth's built-in SMTP. The app's separate `invitations@onespace.live` Resend Edge Function does **not** set the Auth mail sender. The QA screen is correct; the sender still displays `Supabase Auth` and `noreply@mail.app.supabase.io` until Auth SMTP is configured.

**Desired sender**: `OneSpace <support@onespace.live>`. First verify that `onespace.live` is authorized to send from `support@` in the current email provider and SPF/DKIM are properly configured.

**QA only — required operator action in Supabase Dashboard (not exposed by current connector):**
1. QA project > Authentication > SMTP Settings: enable custom SMTP. Use a verified provider (e.g. Resend SMTP `smtp.resend.com`, port 465/587 based on documented settings), configured credentials from the provider's secrets store. **Do not commit API keys to GitHub.**
2. Set sender name **OneSpace**, sender address **support@onespace.live**.
3. Authentication > Email Templates > Reset Password: use `supabase/email-templates/password_recovery.html`, preserving Supabase's `{{ .ConfirmationURL }}` replacement variable.
4. Check verified sender identity (SPF/DKIM/DMARC), SMTP quotas/rate limits, and return URL `onespace-preview://auth-callback/`.
5. Test **Send a reset email** while signed in and **Forgot password** when signed out with a fresh QA test account. Verify the message's real From headers, not only its displayed name, and ensure secure app callback and password recovery.
6. Repeat separately for Production **only after the QA test is accepted**.

**Current status**: template committed; Auth SMTP is **not configured or independently verified** by this change. No custom password emails are sent using an administrator bypass. Keep the standard secure Supabase recovery flow until proper SMTP is available.

Source: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/auth-smtp

#### OneSpace confirmation branding — QA hosted Supabase

##### Hosted project setting (NOT automatically deployed by a schema migration)
1. Open the **QA Supabase** project `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`.
2. Authentication → Email Templates → **Confirm signup**.
3. Subject: `Confirm your OneSpace email address`.
4. Paste `supabase/email-templates/confirmation.html` in the message body and save.
5. Verify the QA Supabase Auth URL Configuration includes
   `onespace-preview://auth-callback/` as an allowed redirect URL.
6. Send a fresh QA signup confirmation to a disposable address and verify
   that the branded HTML, logo, correct redirect and OneSpace QA app open.
7. Repeat the template setup for **Production** separately only when approved;
   Production's allowed callback is `onespace://auth-callback/`.

Never change the authentication sender/from address back to Supabase branding.
Custom SMTP determines the sender; this file determines email body/subject.
**A GitHub commit is NOT evidence that a hosted Supabase template is live.**

The image URL must be confirmed accessible in a normal email client.
Do not claim the app's email is branded until a delivered email proves it.

### A130. OneSpace · `docs/QA_FEEDBACK_2026_11_19_INVITES_PASSWORDS.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 19 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace QA — Invitation + password feedback (Nov 19, 2026)

##### Source observations
QA screenshots from installed Android:
1. Profile > Change password: the **Update password** button was enabled with empty/incomplete inputs; there was no Cancel beside it.
2. Reset password message arrived as `Supabase Auth <noreply@mail.app.supabase.io>`, instead of OneSpace `support@onespace.live`.
3. Invite my partner: actions had uneven spacing/alignment. Resend sent email with no confirmation dialog.
4. Today invitation card repeated **Generate replacement code** and **Revoke invitation** controls already in the invitation full page. Refresh status was left aligned.
5. Tapping invitation email link opened a website instead of opening the installed app.

##### Changes completed in QA mobile
- Valid-change-only Update button; Cancel beside Update. Password validation tests.
- Same-code email resend requires confirmation with displayed recipient and preserved expiry.
- Responsive invitation buttons arranged in evenly spaced row; replacement/revoke controls separated inside subpage.
- Removed replacement/revoke from Today; centered Refresh status.
- Verified Android HTTPS App Link intent for **QA-only** path `/qa-invite/`. Production path `/app-invite/` must NOT be claimed by QA app.
- Deep-link parser/tests updated to isolate QA vs Production.
- QA invitation backend email updated to use `https://onespace.live/qa-invite/?code=...`.

##### Website changes
- Published `.well-known/assetlinks.json` with observed stable QA signing fingerprint for `onespace.qa`; deployed `.nojekyll` to serve association route from GitHub Pages.
- Published `qa-invite/index.html` as a lightweight *mobile-app handoff only*, no web sign-up.
- Corrected the existing invitation landing's CSP image rule that previously blocked the OneSpace logo.
- Production mobile app/backends not modified. The public website invitation fallback/association files **were** changed.

##### Pending external configuration / real-world checks
- Supabase Auth must be configured with custom SMTP sender `OneSpace <support@onespace.live>` in **QA** (and HTML recovery email template installed). Existing invitation email provider does not control Supabase Auth. Details in `docs/QA_AUTH_SENDER_BRANDING_2026-11-19.md`. Do not claim this sender is fixed until tested.
- An uninstalled QA app cannot be installed from the public store because QA distribution is separate. Store badge must not link to Production or an unverified listing; the QA fallback instead explains to use the QA test installer.
- Real Android Chrome/Gmail and device Settings > Opening links verification required for app links, because repository and package checks do not establish OS-level real-world behavior.
- iOS Universal Link association/signing is not part of this Android QA iteration.

##### QA acceptance
1. Update disabled initially; stays disabled for missing current, too-short new, mismatch, or same password.
2. Valid change enables button; Cancel returns to Profile without change.
3. Reset email still works securely, while sender branding remains pending SMTP configuration.
4. Tap Resend: confirmation dialog; Cancel sends nothing, Confirm sends same code and expiry.
5. Today no longer duplicates Replace/Revoke, Refresh centered.
6. Buttons spaced and aligned; QR and manual code still work.
7. QA recipient opens `/qa-invite/` and launches installed `onespace.qa` directly; absent app sees only mobile install instructions, no web signup.
8. With both QA and Production apps installed, Production `/app-invite/` must never open QA; security still validates recipient verified email.
9. No Production Android build or Production backend deployment.

### A131. OneSpace · `docs/QA_LIFECYCLE_IMPLEMENTATION_SLICE_1_2026-11-19.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 19 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by ADR-2026-11-20 (no archiving, 20 Nov 2026). Kept for history.*

#### OneSpace — QA lifecycle implementation, first safe slice

Date: 2026-11-19
Branch: qa/lifecycle-first-slice-20261119
Target: signed Android QA APK only. No Production build or deployment.

##### Implemented in this QA slice
- Change password moved from large form dialog to full page with Current/New/Confirm and Send a reset email link below.
- Today Invitation CTA renamed Invite my partner and opens full-page email entry.
- Recipient email, expiry and delivery state stored under invitation record.
- Invite disabled until valid email; recipient's matching verified account is required to accept.
- QR, formatted code with copy icon, status, SMS/WhatsApp sharing, same-code resend and confirmation before replacing/revoking on full page. No raw long link displayed.
- QA backend RPC create_addressed_invitation enforces creator-only, 2-member capacity, one code per recipient and replacement confirmation.
- QA backend RPC accept_invitation compares signed-in user's VERIFIED email with stored invitation recipient. Also applies to manual/QR joins.
- QA email Edge Function enforces stored recipient before sending, updates provider-accepted status; production function unchanged.
- Pending deep link can automatically attempt pairing after signup/email verification; backend remains authority.
- Profile My Space view shows role and member count.

##### Deliberately NOT enabled because unsafe before archival
- Partner leave/removal; owner deletion/continuation; higher-order account deletion changes.
- Shared-media destruction; archive TTL cleanup; backup import/export/restore.
- Reason: 33 tables cascade delete by space_id, and some mix private and shared items.

##### Decisions to resolve before enabling deletion
- P01: whether an app-private-only download qualifies as successful server-delete trigger. Conservative interim rule: NO, and no automatic server purge is implemented.
- Portable archive encryption and recovery key strategy after reinstall.
- Grace-period/cancellation behavior for owner Delete account.
- Verified personal-vault migration, shared/private classification, media integrity.

##### Required QA tests
1. Change-password fields and plain reset link on full subpage, no popup.
2. Invite button disabled until valid recipient.
3. Recipient email stored; status, QR, code and copy shown.
4. Re-send same code and original expiry; replacement/revoke requires input-free confirmation.
5. Verified B email from link/QR/manual joins A; different verified email rejected.
6. New signup + verification can use preserved incoming invite; old unaddressed code rejected in QA.
7. My Space identifies owner vs partner but exposes NO dangerous destructive action.
8. Production remains unmodified.

Next implementation gate: user-owned Private Vault, media-inclusive verified per-user encrypted exports, safe asynchronous cleanup and restore, then leave/remove/delete.

### A132. OneSpace · `docs/QA_LIFECYCLE_IMPLEMENTATION_SLICE_2_2026-11-19.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 19 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by ADR-2026-11-20 (no archiving, 20 Nov 2026). Kept for history.*

#### OneSpace QA lifecycle — second safe implementation slice (2026-11-19)

This is a **QA-only** implementation milestone. Production untouched.

##### Decision locked
Automatic backup into **OneSpace installed-app private files** does **not** trigger archive deletion. After an explicit user-initiated **Save archive to Downloads**, Android reads the written file back and verifies SHA-256 before the authenticated backend can mark that recipient's individual archive for deletion. If not explicitly downloaded, an archive expires 30 days after **ready_at**. The hourly archive janitor removes due encrypted files through Supabase Storage API; database keeps minimal audit metadata (without archive content). Other recipient's archive independently retained.

##### Implemented
- Private Supabase archive bucket, per-recipient per-type archive metadata with strict RLS (read own metadata only).
- Public acknowledgement requires signed-in matching recipient, ready/not-expired archive and identical SHA-256; anonymous cannot acknowledge, no ack from automatic app-private download.
- QA authenticated archive-download Edge Function checks recipient's JWT and owner-bound Storage path before streaming encrypted bytes.
- Android My Archives: automatic encrypted app-private copy (removed on uninstall, Android app backup disabled), and explicit manual save to Downloads with native readback checksum before acknowledgement. No confirmation popup containing form fields.
- Hourly service-secret archive janitor deletes only temporary archive Storage objects after explicit verified external download or 30-day TTL, not shared/private live objects.
- Read-only separation preflight counts space-linked records and marks destructive workflow not yet safe.
- QA legacy account deletion request/purge disabled to prevent old behavior deleting records before shared-space export/cleanup.
- Added unit tests for app-private exclusion, explicit external success, and 30-day expiry.

##### Not implemented yet — must NOT be misrepresented as complete
- Real encrypted media-inclusive archive **generation and delivery** (registry currently has no ready archives).
- Email to each partner when archive ready.
- Private Vault migration from existing tables and safe detach of historic shared references.
- Active live space retirement, remove partner, leave and Delete space.
- Account deletion cascades/recovery; restore-from-archive.
- iOS external save proof and full iOS archive handling.
- Readiness worker for large exports and retries; notifications.
- End-to-end real-account and media-integrity QA.

##### Safety verification
- The archive bucket is private; no direct client Storage policy.
- No user archive rows or existing shared/private data mutated.
- 30-day expiry starts at ready_at; hourly cleanup scheduled.
- No production schema, code release or worker touched.
- Real account deletion remains unavailable in QA pending the new lifecycle.

##### QA testing this slice
My Profile > My Space > My Archives shows empty state with policy; My Space > Check separation readiness runs a read-only safety check. Existing invitation and password QA tests remain available. Archive download buttons appear only for genuine server-ready encrypted archive rows; none are fabricated.

---

### A133. OneSpace · `docs/QA_PERSONAL_DATA_MIGRATION_AND_LIFECYCLE_2026-11-19.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 19 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by ADR-2026-11-20 (no archiving, 20 Nov 2026). Kept for history.*

#### OneSpace QA — Private Data Migration, Archive and Separation Implementation (2026-11-19)

**Environment:** QA Supabase project `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`; Android application branch `qa/lifecycle-first-slice-20261119`.  
**Scope:** Additive QA-only migration; no destructive operation performed against real QA records, users, or Storage.

##### Live QA inventory verified
| Inventory item | Result |
| --- | ---: |
| Existing relationship spaces | 3 |
| Existing space memberships | 5 |
| Source tables with direct `space_id` cascading foreign key | 33 |
| Explicitly private primary source records | 10 |
| Account-owned personal days off | 1 |
| Preserved Personal Vault primary records | **11** |
| Preserved private-parent owned dependent records | **18** |
| Migrated source table families with visibility+owner fields | 14 |
| Trigger mirrors including personal day and private descendants | 19 |
| Private source-record content/owner/visibility mismatches | **0** |
| Storage objects (five media buckets) | **59** |
| Database-to-storage path matches | **59 / 59** |
| Missing Storage paths | **0** |
| Unclassified media | **0** |
| Current media classified shared | **59** |
| Current media classified private | **0** |
| Archive objects ready | **0** |

##### Implemented in QA
1. `0146_qa_personal_vault_shadow_migration.sql`: Add account-owned `personal_vault_records`, original `visibility`, source identifiers and full source snapshots. Existing tables remain canonical for now. `SECURITY DEFINER` mirror triggers for 14 typed source tables track new private records/updates and mark formerly-private records non-restorable when visibility turns shared. Authenticated clients cannot query or overwrite raw shadow snapshots.
2. `0147_qa_personal_vault_dependents.sql`: Personal-day protection and child snapshots for owner-authored private occasion steps, budget items and proposals, plus owner-specific reminder recipient records. Do not sweep in other partner's private children.
3. `0148_qa_personal_vault_status.sql`: User-scoped, count-only private-vault readiness report for My Space.
4. `0149_qa_space_media_inventory.sql`: Index all six known media reference types (memory photos, note images, trip documents, activity comment voice, activity-component images and voice). All 59 Storage objects are matched, with original parent visibility preserved. **Actual bytes and encrypted archive integrity are not yet verified.**
5. My Space > Check separation readiness now shows each signed-in member's protected primary/private-dependent record counts. No private payload exposed.
6. `SpaceLifecyclePolicy` and tests encode B Leaves / A Removes B / A Deletes Space / A Deletes Account / B Deletes Account, with continuous owner experience and shared space retirement.
7. Previously implemented secure temporary archive bucket, 30-day retention, user-initiated verified external download acknowledgement, app-private local copy and archive-only janitor remain intact.

##### Explicit safeguards still active
- No Leave, Remove partner, Delete space or Delete account operations are enabled in QA. The old QA account deletion flow is disabled.
- The shared data still lives in original space tables. Do not delete any `spaces` row: 33 cascading foreign keys can erase private data and related objects.
- No media file was uploaded, copied, exported, purged, or deleted by the inventory migration.
- Personal Vault snapshots are NOT yet the fully normalized final app data model; original screens continue to use legacy tables. The next app cutover must preserve ownership and actual attachment relationships.
- No false claims about archive readiness. Current archive table has zero ready exports.
- No production code, production database, or production mobile app deployments were made in this migration.

##### What still must be implemented before enabling lifecycle
1. Durable asynchronous export job that serializes **all authorized shared objects and dependent rows**, each partner's private records/children, and all actual media bytes, without including the other partner's private information.
2. Portable AES-GCM encryption, authenticated per-recipient archives, secure recovery-key strategy, integrity checks and manifest signing. Work across device loss/reinstall/account deletion.
3. Verified app-private auto copy (not a server-deletion trigger), user-deliberate external download and 30-day expiry; reliable email notifications and progress/retries.
4. Private-record cutover from legacy `space_id` foreign keys; link detachment/inert historical references, storage ownership and cross-space RLS enforcement.
5. Transactional, idempotent owner-continuity retirement/clean-space creation, B leave, A remove, explicit A Delete Space, and higher-level account-deletion cascading workflow. Never delete original shared media until both partners' authorized archives are verified.
6. Real Android and iOS restoration validation: private-only import, owner account match, duplicate-safe merge, progress bar, no old shared content reappears.
7. QA destructive scenario tests on **synthetic/disposable** accounts, real representative media, app reinstall, offline partner, expired archive and failure retries.
8. Re-enable QA deletion actions only when safety preflight and recovered archive tests are green, not when records are merely copied into vault metadata.

##### SMTP
The real password-reset sender is controlled by Supabase Auth custom SMTP settings, not the branded invitation Edge Function. User must configure QA project's Auth SMTP with a verified Resend domain, from `OneSpace <support@onespace.live>`, host `smtp.resend.com`, port 465 (SSL/TLS), username `resend`, password a private Resend API key; then apply the branded Supabase email template in `supabase/email-templates/password_recovery.html` and test. Do not paste key into chat or GitHub. Production SMTP remains untouched.

##### QA release
The newest source also includes previous password validation/Cancel, resend confirmation, Today CTA cleanup, QA-only `/qa-invite/` Android App Link, and branded invitation landing fallback. Build only `onespace.qa` after Flutter analyze/test; do not deploy to Production.

##### Resumption gate
Read this document and `docs/ONESPACE_PAIRING_SEPARATION_ARCHIVE_RESTORE_DECISIONS_2026-11-19.md` before modifying lifecycle RLS or enabling deletion. The **explicit user-initiated external download** (verified after physical write) is the only early archive cleanup trigger; app-private automatic copies never trigger deletion.

##### Private-record deletion semantics
Added `0150_qa_personal_vault_delete_tombstones.sql`: deleting a personal item in normal operation marks its private Vault copy non-restorable and clears stored content, preventing an old deleted item from resurfacing later. The eventual vetted space-retirement transaction must explicitly set the guarded `onespace.retiring_space` session context to keep private snapshots while cascading legacy old-space rows. These protections are staged; no destructive QA member action has been enabled.

### A134. OneSpace · `docs/QA_REMAINING_LIFECYCLE_GATES_2026-11-19.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by ADR-2026-11-20 (no archiving, 20 Nov 2026). Kept for history.*

> **Policy correction (20 November 2026):** Earlier media-inclusive archive
> requirements below are superseded. OneSpace must archive and restore
> **structured private/shared records, excluding media and uploaded documents**.
> Source: `docs/QA_STRUCTURED_DATA_MILESTONE_20261120.md`.

#### QA backlog — next hard gates (19 November 2026)

**Scope:** QA Supabase `ugwrpeduzyzrujkizqfo`; no Production migration or destructive action.

##### Verified repairs
- QA source restored to `main` at merge `81a03000de949e99f258fdbd81402255569d1ea5`; avoid divergent release tags, and ensure APK's release target equals the tested commit.
- Release guard uses **recovered-main ancestry plus critical file checks**, not immutable hashes of invitation/archive source: normal future fixes must remain possible.
- My Space only invokes QA lifecycle APIs in QA builds. Production must still show Profile/My Space without calling missing QA-only RPCs.
- My Archives can be opened even by an **unpaired** user; old-space archives are user-owned rather than current-space-owned.
- JSON `Download my data` is explicitly labeled **unencrypted and media-excluding**, not a media-inclusive secure archive.
- Server-side archive dependency inventory dynamically traces public-schema FK children up to five links, counts records scoped by the signed-in user's space and never returns record contents.
- The inventory finds **35 dependent tables** including comments, revisions, budget logs, scrapbook content and attachment metadata. It identifies **five multi-parent tables** requiring explicit relation ownership review:
  - `occasion_budget_items` — canonical `plan_id` → `occasion_plans`; optional `source_activity_id` is only a reference.
  - `occasion_budget_log` — canonical `plan_id`.
  - `occasion_proposal_items` — canonical `proposal_id` → `occasion_proposals` → `occasion_plans`.
  - `task_checklist_items` — canonical non-null `task_id`; optional legacy task pointer is not canonical.
  - `task_checklist_checks` — canonical non-null `task_id`; also has `item_id`.
- The audit is deliberately **not an authorization to export**, since references across two parents need integrity constraints, and the per-user privacy/media lineage must be proven. `safe_to_export` always returns false.
- **Privacy hardening migration 0155:** signed-in clients receive only schema-level dependency coverage, archive progress and generic blocker summaries—not the partner's private-record counts, root-table cardinalities or nested row detail. The internal scoped audit remains available for server-side verification.
- **Cross-parent integrity migration 0157:** internal-only (never replaces the sanitized public RPC). Checks occasion budgets against optional activity/component references, budget logs vs items, proposal items vs budget plans, checklist checks vs their items, and legacy tasks vs current task relationships. Synthetic cross-space relationship regression passes; all rows rolled back.
- **P0 access hardening migration 0156:** blocked QA account deletion runs as `SECURITY INVOKER`; anonymous users cannot execute it. Security advisor authenticated privileged-function warning count fell from 20 to 19. All real account-deletion operations remain blocked.
- QA SQL regression: inserted a temporary private activity + nested comment, verified count and cross-space isolation; inserted an occasion budget item **without a source activity**, verified it still resolves to its plan; all tests explicitly rolled back. Anonymous RPC execution denied.

##### Still unfinished / must not be represented as complete
1. **Canonical classification of all roots and dependent tables.** Inventory counts are not archive serialization; handle mixed shared/private, linked audit rows, cross-parent conflict checks, inheritance and entity/attachment ownership.
2. **Actual media-inclusive encrypted archive generator.** Identity-bound encryption keys, portable recovery, authenticated server Storage, idempotent queue, byte integrity proofs, per-user private + old shared archive. No archive objects exist yet.
3. **Restoration.** Only a user's private archive may be restored into a new space, with dedupe, attachment recovery and inert old shared references. Old shared-history archives remain reference only.
4. **Real lifecycle transition.** After independently verified per-user archives, safely retire old shared data and Storage objects through Storage API, give owner a clean new relationship boundary on leave/removal, unpair B, and leave both unpaired on owner Delete Space. Do not expose old shared history to a new partner.
5. **Delete-account cascade.** Seven-day grace policy and cancellation/notification/retrieval semantics, then archival before account purge. Until then the request RPC must stay blocked.
6. **Lifecycle email, offline/undeliverable partner notifications, iOS explicit external save, cross-device archive recovery, platform link testing** and real phone acceptance.
7. **Security advisor**: remaining privileged public RPCs need permission + abuse-case review; public support API intentionally supports anonymous callers and cannot be blindly revoked without breaking the site.
8. **Release completion**: confirm final QA signed APK, package, certificate, Firebase push, two Android phones, both account roles and not just CI analysis.

##### Hard release principles
- Code only merges from a branch based on the current `main`; exact source tag/commit verified.
- Never trigger a signed QA APK for an untested or incomplete final integration.
- SQL changes go QA first, tracked with unique migration numbers, with privilege/RLS tests.
- QA/Production configurations must remain isolated and never delete personal data to pass tests.
- No space/account deletion or partner separation before an independent, all-media archive plus restore proof.

### A135. OneSpace · `docs/SOLO_INVITATION_QA_2026_11_19.md`

*First written 19 Nov 2026 · last changed 19 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### Solo-user QA — invitation and sharing (19 November 2026)

##### Scope
No Supabase migrations. QA/Production `space_invitations` already allow the
creator to SELECT their own invitation under RLS. Never expose another
person's invitation. Do not modify paired accounts or release markers.

##### Acceptance checklist

1. Sign in on QA Android with exactly one space member; Today shows a compact,
   **collapsed** invitation card with a single primary action.
2. When no invitation exists: primary CTA is **Create invitation code**.
   Tap it. An invitation is created; dialog offers WhatsApp, SMS, email,
   copy, QR and the manual code. No additional code is generated by sharing.
3. With an outstanding invitation: primary CTA is **Resend invitation**.
   Tap it three times and compare codes and original expiry: both remain
   identical. Confirm server has no new `space_invitations` row.
4. Expand the card. **Generate a replacement code** opens a confirmation.
   Cancel leaves the first invitation active. Confirm makes the old code
   unusable immediately, and a distinct new code is shown.
5. Expand and revoke. The invitation stops working and Today again offers
   **Create invitation code**.
6. Email/WhatsApp/SMS: readable invitation, branded first-party landing URL,
   original code for manual fallback; email includes Google Play and App Store
   placeholders. If the device has no configured messaging application,
   display a useful failure message and offer copy.
7. Open the link in a recipient's **matching environment** (QA vs production).
   Tap **Open in OneSpace**. With the app installed, the app opens and
   prepopulates Join your partner; do not auto-accept. Sign in if required,
   then confirm Join space. A code cannot be accepted twice.
8. Open the same link without the app installed. The page explains how to
   install later or manually copy the code; it must not claim the store
   releases are already available.
9. Test wrong-environment links, tampered codes, expired invitations,
   self-invitation, unpaired recipient and an account already paired.
10. Repeat on iPhone and verify URL-scheme registration before shipping.
    Browser auto-opening via verified HTTPS links additionally requires
    Android assetlinks.json signed certificate association and iOS AASA
    Team ID / Associated Domains; do **not** claim this works until verified.

##### Release policy
One reviewed release per iteration. This branch intentionally does not
change a release marker or publish an APK.

##### Password-manager and recovery regression

1. On a device where Google Password Manager stores a OneSpace password,
   open Me → Profile → Change password. The current-password field advertises
   the platform autofill hint; Android and iOS may offer the saved credential
   after the user taps it. App code must never extract a Google password.
2. Check current password remains obscured. The app never logs, persists, or
   pre-populates it from session tokens.
3. Incorrect current password must fail without changing account credentials.
   Matching new password and confirmation of length >=8 is required. Server
   leaked-password protection and policy remain authoritative.
4. Tap "Forgot current password? Send a reset email"; it calls Supabase Auth
   with the signed-in user's email and environment-specific redirect. Verify
   the email arrives on QA, and its link reaches the OneSpace password-recovery
   screen, then requires sign-in with the new password.
5. Repeat the reset on Production with a separate test account. Never email
   secrets or use a production account to test QA.
6. Verify reset-email rate limiting, no account enumeration, no password
   stored in SharedPreferences, and temporary input controllers are disposed.
7. Test both system light/dark, Arabic/English, Android/iPhone.
