### A136. OneSpace · `docs/ACCOUNT_PRIVATE_SHARED_SCHEMA_20261120.md`

*First written 20 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### OneSpace — unified UI, two independent data scopes (approved 2026-11-20)

##### Product contract

Each person always owns **one private space**, by Auth account ID. Each *current, specific couple* owns **one separate shared space**, by relationship space ID and its exactly authorized members. An unpaired person still has their private space. OneSpace shows ONE experience for Today, Remember, Coordinate, Connect and other modules. Every object editor uses the same screen and a visibility selector:

- Private -> user-owned private-space ID. Independent of partner join/leave/remove/delete space.
- Shared -> current relationship's shared-space ID. Accessible by current pair only, and permanently erased when that relationship ends.
- No pairing -> private creation works; shared creation is not available.
- A new relationship starts with a brand-new shared scope. No previous shared content is inherited.
- Changing visibility must be an authorized atomic move: private -> shared explicitly exposes content to partner; shared -> private cannot silently duplicate formerly shared data or grant the prior partner access.
- Replies, checklists, comments, mentions, audit revisions, files and Storage objects must inherit the correct parent scope and remain private or shared. No orphaned children or cross-scope links.

##### Physical model and why not duplicate every content table

`public.user_private_spaces` is the **personal namespace** (one immutable owner UUID, unique per active Auth account), while existing `public.spaces` + `public.space_members` are the current relationship namespace (with legacy singleton spaces temporarily supported). Eligible root tables keep one schema with mutually exclusive references: existing `space_id` for relationship-scoped content, new `private_space_id` for account-scoped content. There are 22 eligible root families, not 44 separately duplicated content tables.

Supabase RLS and every read/write/RPC must check the owner for private references; shared scope uses actual currently paired membership. The UI service eventually combines both queries into one list and routes one create/edit screen based on requested visibility. Personal and shared content need correct separate Storage and notification authorization.

##### Completed QA foundation

Migration 0227: `user_private_spaces`, one automatically on Auth signup and one for each existing active QA user. Creator-only read RLS, no arbitrary client INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE. Read-only `public.my_storage_scopes()` returns private ID, shared ID only when paired, and legacy space, with `content_routing_enabled=false` until the full cutover.

Migration 0228: all 22 eligible root tables have nullable `private_space_id` referencing `user_private_spaces(owner_id)`, nullable legacy `space_id`, and mutually exclusive space-scope constraints. Where available, source creator and `visibility='private'` checks apply to the new personal path. Existing rows are untouched, and legacy creates remain on their former path; no private data is yet moved.

Flutter: retire old 'prepare secure archive' actions and remove My Archives from Me. My Space transparently shows the permanent shared-loss rule, disabled destructive actions until safe, and prior unexecuted preparation requests with their cancel option. Account deletion screen now explains the correct seven-day private/shared policy but keeps the destructive request disabled.

Account-purge QA Edge Function v7: **legacy soft-delete and keep-shared path removed**. Active runner fails CLOSED if any due entries appear until true survivor-preserving v2 finalizer is deployed. Existing `request_account_deletion`, `purge_due_accounts_list`, and `purge_account_data_service` remain disabled; no account has been purged or scheduled.

##### Remaining mandatory cutover before any destructive action is enabled

1. **Data migration**: audit 69 family policies, 22 roots, dependent edge references, all authored private records (including private media); re-home private rows to `private_space_id`, clear legacy `space_id`, retain correct author/visibility, and prove per-user record counts and checksums unchanged. Non-author-owned tables need explicit ownership mapping.
2. **RLS, triggers and APIs**: write authenticated owner-only private policies and paired-only shared policies in every affected table, RPC, Storage rule, notification, active stream and query. Existing legacy private RLS requires membership, so it must be migrated before data movement. No broad grants or bypassing RLS.
3. **Unified app reads/writes**: use `my_storage_scopes()` and a common scope resolver for every create/edit/list/view; private works when solo; refuse shared write without a current pair; update realtime, search, mentions, navigation, deep links and caches. Flip server + app feature flags only after all compatible clients accepted.
4. **Irreversible relationship transaction**: authenticated explicit warning, lock pair, record exact member IDs, delete all shared roots and children/storage pointers, revoke invitations and former access, detach B or retire pair, and leave both personal namespaces unchanged. If anything would delete personal data or orphan dependent rows, roll back. Storage byte deletion postcommit needs idempotent retries; access revocation must be immediate.
5. **Account deletion**: implement true seven-day request/cancel with exact UTC expiry, no early changes, scheduled finalizer (user + their personal content + current pair shared content erased, surviving partner private untouched/unpaired), Storage cleanup and auth/account record deletion with accurate status + retry. The old runner now fails closed and may NOT silently retain shared content.
6. **QA tests**: user A/B remove, leave, close, join C, B join new partner, unpaired content CRUD, bidirectional visibility changes, heavy 69-family dataset, private file preservation and shared storage deletion, stale JWT attacks, and cancel vs deadline races. Test both phones. Production isolated.

No archival, export/download link, data restore or retention workflow is a prerequisite to relationship/account actions. Optional standalone manual 'Download my data' is a separate user-initiated export, not part of this lifecycle.

### A137. OneSpace · `docs/ADR_2026-11-20_PERSONAL_SHARED_OWNERSHIP_AND_DELETION.md`

*First written 20 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: **Active** decision record.*

#### ADR-2026-11-20 — Independent private content, relationship-owned shared content, and irreversible lifecycle

**Status:** ACCEPTED — authoritative product decision; implementation in progress (QA first).  
**Decided:** 20 November 2026.  
**Supersedes:** The 19–20 November partner separation **archive/export/restore**, archive-before-separation, `awaiting_verified_export` readiness and asynchronous My Archives proposals; see supersession notices in older documents.  
**Companions:** `DECISION_LOG.md`, `ACCOUNT_PRIVATE_SHARED_SCHEMA_20261120.md`, `NO_ARCHIVING_RELATIONSHIP_LIFECYCLE_20261120.md`.  
**Scope:** Android, iOS, backend, Auth, Storage, audit/history, invitations, search, notifications; QA prior to Production.

##### How we arrived here — discussion and final choices

1. **Problem found:** On QA, a person asked to *remove a partner* and another asked to *leave*. Both remained at `awaiting_verified_export`; neither request had progressed. The old separation process demanded verified private/shared archives before membership could change. Even empty user content stalled, because the archiving/export/restore engine was incomplete. That was unacceptable user experience.
2. **Intermediate idea (rejected):** Make separation immediate, then asynchronously enumerate, encrypt, verify, deliver and delete archive files, showing My Archives progress and 30-day temporary links; delay account deletion by seven days, with cancellation of archive jobs. Some prototype inventory infrastructure and UI were built while discussing this, but this is **NOT** the approved product behavior.
3. **Final decision:** **NO automatic archiving, export, restore, email links, 30-day archive windows, or My Archives** as part of leaving, removal, space deletion, or account deletion. Instead, show explicit irreversible warnings and carry out the destructive relationship transition immediately after confirmed consent. Each person keeps every private record they own; all former-couple shared data is permanently removed. OneSpace will not restore erased shared data. Ordinary provider disaster-recovery backups are subject to their own retention policies and are not a user archive.
4. **Root-cause fix:** Previously many private items had `space_id` and cascading foreign keys, so deleting a couple space risked deleting a user's personal records. The founder chose **two separately owned backend scopes per account**: (a) their permanent account-owned personal/private space; and (b) a relationship-owned shared space for the **specific current pair**. Content marked Private belongs in (a); Shared belongs in (b). The app must still provide **one Notes/Tasks/Activities/etc. creation, editing, list and search experience** — not duplicate modules. The implementation may reuse a content table with mutually exclusive `private_space_id` and `space_id`; it does not need physically duplicated tables.
5. **Delete-account rule replaced:** The earlier soft-delete/retain-the-couple's-shared-content worker is rejected. **Seven full days of grace**, cancellable any time before the deadline, with *no deletion of account, private or shared content during the grace period*. At deadline: permanently delete the requesting account and their private data, and delete the current pair's shared data; keep the other account's private content and unpair that user.
6. **Execution agreement:** Implement without asking the founder to re-decide settled rules. Document decisions in repo; complete and test via QA; do not touch Production or activate irreversible actions before end-to-end private-preservation and shared-erasure tests pass.

##### Invariants — never violate

- Each authenticated account has **exactly one persistent owner-only private namespace**. An unpaired person can read and create private content. Private records, children, audit history and files stay with the account across leave/remove/close/re-pair. Never expose them to a prior or future partner by default.
- Each specific relationship has a **distinct shared namespace** and two authorized members at most. Any newly formed relationship gets a clean shared scope — never recycle old shared records or grants.
- An object has **one storage ownership scope**; `Private` and `Shared` selections route writes accordingly. UI merges authorized content in one interface and retains the chosen visibility while editing.
- Only the current owner may write private records, and the specific current pair may read authorized shared records; enforce in PostgreSQL RLS, security-sensitive RPCs, Storage, push, search, realtime, child links and audit. A previously paired user, a new partner and a guessed object ID must not bypass this.
- Edges/children inherit their parent's scope; cross-scope links never reveal private content to the shared partner. File access must be revoked immediately when shared content is deleted; idempotent background *physical file cleanup* is allowed, but not a user archive.
- Do not silently change a saved item's visibility; moving Private→Shared is deliberate disclosure and needs explicit authorization. Shared→Private cannot be used to steal the couple's shared data before separation; implement authorized ownership and lifecycle rules.
- Database state changes and all required integrity checks occur in a transaction, or roll back completely. Storage byte removal is not transactional; use access revocation and an auditable, retryable deletion queue.
- Disabled lifecycle controls stay disabled until *all* relevant private/media preservation, shared data/children deletion, RLS, storage and concurrency tests pass. **No automatic conversion of old archive-preparation requests** into deletion consent.

##### Final user journeys

| Scenario | Timing | Private records | Old shared records | Post-state |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A removes B from Space 1** | Immediately after deliberate warning/confirmation and safe DB transaction | A and B each keep own content | Permanently delete all former A/B shared content, history and shared files | A unpaired in own space; B unpaired in own private space |
| **B leaves Space 1** | Same | A and B each keep own content | Same permanent deletion | Both unpaired and private content readable |
| **A deletes Space 1** | Same | A and B each keep own content | Same permanent deletion | Shared Space 1 gone; both retain account-owned private space |
| **C joins A later** | Standard join/accept flow | A's and C's personal scopes isolated | No previous A/B shared history available | New distinct shared scope for A+C |
| **B joins Space 2, or stays unpaired** | Standard join/accept flow | B's same personal scope, unchanged | Never import old A/B shared content | New relationship has only newly shared items |
| **A or B requests account deletion** | No deletion during 7 full days; may cancel | All current accounts' data untouched during grace | Untouched during grace | Pending, with deadline/status and cancellation |
| **Account deletion cancelled before deadline** | Immediate cancellation | All users' private records unchanged | All shared records unchanged | Relationship and account stay as they were |
| **Account deletion finalizes after 7 days** | At expiry via authenticated, idempotent worker | Requester account + requester private records permanently deleted; survivor's private data retained | Delete that pair's shared content | Survivor unpaired, retains private namespace; revoked deleted user sessions |

**Delete Space** is not **Delete Account**. Losing relationship data is immediate on space action; deleting an account always has the seven-day grace period.

##### Required warnings, confirmations and progress

**Leave / remove / delete shared space**: Explain prominently *“The shared content with this partner—including shared activities, notes, tasks, conversations, memories, history, and shared files—will be permanently deleted and cannot be restored in OneSpace. Both of you will retain only your own private content.”* Show exact target space/partner and action-specific destructive button. A deliberate confirmation is required; Cancel is the default. Do not claim the user is “preparing securely” or “waiting for archives.”

**Delete account**: Explain *“In seven days, your account and its private data will be permanently deleted. Your current relationship's shared content will also be permanently deleted. Your partner keeps their own private data and becomes unpaired. Cancel any time before the deadline to keep everything.”* Display a reliable server-side UTC deadline and cancellable pending state. No deletion may happen during grace. Confirmation requires a deliberate action.

**UI:** One unified Today, Remember, Coordinate, Connect and Me; one create/edit/list per object kind; explicit visibility selector; owner-only content remains available while unpaired. There is **no My Archives progress tab**, no archive emails or delivery/restore workflows.

##### Technical model and phased work

- Personal namespace: `public.user_private_spaces(owner_id → auth.users.id)` (one per person), independent of couple.
- Relationship namespace: distinct `public.spaces` + `space_members` for pair (legacy singleton rows must be handled explicitly during migration).
- 22 eligible root families already received nullable `private_space_id`, mutually exclusive with `space_id`; 69 classified source families (roots and dependents) must be audited before destructive actions.
- A unified per-item owner-/pair-authorized read/write layer, used by app and every privileged API; do not assume a legacy `space_id` query can see a personal record.
- Migrate legacy Private records and descendants only when ownership, checksums, file references and RLS are verified; no blind `space_id = NULL` updates on already populated modules.
- Start with Notes then extend to Tasks, Calendar, Activities, Reminders, Memories, Contacts, Trips, linked records, attachments, cross-module search, change history and notifications.
- Rework seven-day account request/cancel/finalizer; old keep-shared worker must stay retired.
- Implement atomic leave/remove/close shared-erasure, revoke storage/notification access, use idempotent storage cleanup, and verify on both phones with heavy datasets.

##### Status (as checked 20 Nov 2026)

- QA: personal namespace and 22-root exclusive scope schema; source inventory archive cron retired; legacy archive request and deletion RPCs fail closed. PR #117 merged.
- QA: Notes private/shared RLS and scoped audit database tests pass; PR #118 app cutover in validation at decision-writing time (do not assume merged until verified).
- **Not yet operational:** all-module migration, private media/child ownership, permanent shared-data deletion, seven-day account finalizer and release acceptance. Irreversible controls remain disabled. Two old `awaiting_verified_export` QA requests are **not deletion authorizations**; original requesters must reconfirm under the new warning.
- Production unchanged.

##### Explicitly superseded documentation

The following files are historical exploration or outdated design and **MUST NOT** be used as current implementation instructions:
- `ONESPACE_PAIRING_SEPARATION_ARCHIVE_RESTORE_DECISIONS_2026-11-19.md`
- `QA_ARCHIVE_COVERAGE_CATALOG_20261120.md`
- `ASYNC_ARCHIVE_LIFECYCLE_20261120.md`
- `QA_LIFECYCLE_IMPLEMENTATION_SLICE_1_2026-11-19.md`, `QA_LIFECYCLE_IMPLEMENTATION_SLICE_2_2026-11-19.md`
- `QA_REMAINING_LIFECYCLE_GATES_2026-11-19.md`
- `PROPOSED_SIMPLIFIED_SEPARATION_NO_ARCHIVE_20261119.md` (intermediate ideas)
- Any “must archive before separation,” “download 30-day link” or “restore old shared couple history” language anywhere in the repo.

**This ADR controls when older records conflict. Change only by a newly recorded founder decision.**

---

### A138. OneSpace · `docs/ASYNC_ARCHIVE_LIFECYCLE_20261120.md`

*First written 20 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by ADR-2026-11-20 (no archiving, 20 Nov 2026). Kept for history.*

#### OneSpace — immediate relationship transitions + asynchronous archives

Approved product direction: **Two of Us. One Space**. QA first. Production untouched until independent acceptance.

##### Contract (the requested experience)

1. **Leave / remove / close:** the active relationship is ended or detached in the user's response transaction, not after an archive download or a restore operation. Existing old shared records are frozen, invisible to future partners and available ONLY to the original eligible recipients through authorized export infrastructure. No new pair may inherit old shared data.
2. **Atomic source preservation before the response:** take a durable, immutable, membership-scoped snapshot (not just a count, hash or pending row), or fail the action without detaching anyone. Preserve all eligible structured/written records including dependent history, with media, file pointers, photos, audio, videos and documents excluded. This is a technical dependency, not a user-facing archive wait.
3. **Separate recipient jobs:** one private and one original-pair shared archive per original member. Progress stage includes: queued -> source inventory (records identified) -> immutable snapshot verified -> records packaged (accurate numerator) -> encrypted -> ciphertext readback and digest validated -> available for 30 days -> user external save verified / TTL expiry -> purged. All values are server recorded and owner-scoped. Failed and retried jobs must be idempotent, bounded and resume from checkpoints. No invented percentages.
4. **After detachment:** original space tombstone must be preserved and never silently converted into a new couple's home. The previous shared space is **not** reused with a replacement partner. Existing requests and their archive progress must remain accessible to the original account even after membership is revoked.
5. **Delivery:** email to the verified email address of each recipient only after BOTH encryption and storage integrity checks pass. Use branded OneSpace from-domain. Email contains no data, raw storage path, ciphertext key or service token. Use an authenticated, revocable per-recipient link to **My Archives**. A normal Storage signed URL cannot be individually revoked, so use the authenticated download proxy with database status checks.
6. **Local copy:** on Android, the installed app may perform a user-authorized MediaStore Downloads save and hash readback, as supported by the existing bridge. The server cannot write to arbitrary phone Files storage while the app is not running. iOS requires a user-authorized document export. Browser downloads do not prove durable save; only a verified native save or a supported explicit proof/acknowledgement may trigger early purge.
7. **Retention:** make files unavailable as soon as a recipient's external save is proved, else at 30 days after issuance. Remove temporary encrypted Storage objects via janitor and retain audit metadata only as legally required. The email must state the expiry time accurately. Do not delete original source prematurely if any required snapshot or archive validation failed.
8. **Delete account:** user initiates a seven-day grace period, and export begins during it. On cancel, atomically cancel the deletion and every related outstanding archive job, revoke all download capabilities, purge unfinished temporary objects, and leave original account/space membership untouched. If a job already reached a verified external save, its **device copy is under user's control and cannot be remotely erased**; server artifacts can still be invalidated.
9. **Privacy:** private source rows appear only in that person's archive. The original pair's shared history appears as *read-only reference* in each original recipient's archive; it is never restored into a new couple's active shared tables. An archive can never give one partner the other's private content.
10. **Failure and retries:** a client may see "relationship ended — archive preparation requires attention." It must not silently present a pending action as completed or archive records as issued when the worker has only performed census. Preserve support and retry path until recovery.

##### Technical engineering gates

- **A. Source-of-truth boundary**: enumerate and securely snapshot **all 69 classified families** using recipient-specific authorization, exclusions, validated dependent ownership, provenance and schema drift guards. The 23-family private mirror and census hashes alone do not meet this gate.
- **B. Portable encryption**: user-recoverable encrypted file envelope, integrity checks, tested key management and valid archives for **zero** records. Server-only encryption with an unrecoverable deleted key does not count. Build/verify chunked or ranged export with resumable continuation; remove 5,000-record/8-MiB prototype limitations.
- **C. Worker**: durable queued leases, retries, dead-letter handling, idempotent file issuance, per-recipient progress and cancellation; do not rely solely on an Edge Function instance remaining alive across deployment or timeouts.
- **D. Transition**: one atomic DB operation that snapshots origin references and detaches participants/marks original space closed while preventing old data exposure to newly paired users; never delete original source rows before durable snapshot.
- **E. Delivery**: verified address, branded provider acceptance and bounce handling, revocable authenticated link, **30 days** TTL or earlier verified external download, OS-level verified file save and independent ciphertext readback.
- **F. Account grace**: 7-day cancellation and purge scheduler gate tied to exporter state, including safe undo and concurrent worker cancellation.
- **G. QA acceptance**: original two empty QA spaces, synthetic heavy dataset, overlapping private/shared dependent history, large payloads, failed email, failed download, app offline, retries, cancellation at each boundary, RLS attempts from previous/new partners, QA/Production isolation, actual Android/iOS file saves.

##### Actual implemented status on Nov 20, 2026

- **Working QA backend stage:** migrations 0224–0225; source census of all 69 family classifications using the pre-existing authorized functions; per-recipient RLS progress table; scheduled QA cron/Edge worker; transaction-tested SQL cancellation of inventory and row access. Both actual requests inventoried automatically: 8 recipient/kind items; 0 private data rows, 1–2 shared metadata rows, **0 packaged/encrypted**.
- **Working QA client in PR #115:** My Archives displays accurate identified/archived counters, refresh/poll, and an entry from Me. QA only.
- **Not yet built:** atomic source snapshot of full 69-family content; long-running exporter; user-decryptable final files; archive issuance/email; immediate membership/space change; proper account deletion cancellation cascade; iOS Files external save. Current request statuses remain `awaiting_verified_export`. Do not unlock destructive behavior or label the engine operational.
- **Superseded:** PR #114 was closed because it assumed permanent waiting before separation, contrary to the revised requirement.

### A139. OneSpace · `docs/NO_ARCHIVING_RELATIONSHIP_LIFECYCLE_20261120.md`

*First written 20 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: **Active** decision record.*

#### OneSpace definitive relationship lifecycle — NO ARCHIVING (2026-11-20)

> **Correction (20 Nov 2026, later):** the user journeys, warning texts and acceptance tests below still apply.
> The *mechanics* in rules 1–5 ("move private content into a solo space", "move before dropping S1") are **out of date**.
> Private content now lives in the account's own private space (`private_space_id`), never in the couple space, so nothing
> is moved when a couple ends: shared rows are deleted, private rows are untouched. Follow
> `ADR_2026-11-20_PERSONAL_SHARED_OWNERSHIP_AND_DELETION.md` where the two differ.

This specification **replaces** the November 2026 verified archive/restore workflow. **No archive, export, download link, email, restore or archive progress step belongs to any relationship or account deletion action.**

##### Rules and durable state

The original space `S1` has original owner `A` and invited member `B`. Personal content means `visibility='private'` and is owned only by its original `created_by` or correct owner field. Shared content means `visibility='shared'` or `shared_only`, plus associated shared history, reactions, attachments, media, notifications and mixed-scope edges.

1. **A removes B:** after an explicit irreversible-loss warning and authenticated confirmation, immediately end the relationship, revoke B membership/invitations/tokens to S1, and permanently delete **all** old S1 shared content and shared storage. Keep A's private content in S1 (now an unpaired space); move B's private content, with private dependent records and private media, into B's new solo space so B can view/edit it immediately. Existing private IDs are preserved where safe. Prevent any new couple from inheriting earlier shared history.
2. **B leaves:** identical outcomes for A and B. B never deletes A's private content; the authenticated transaction enforces member identity.
3. **A deletes S1:** confirm same loss, destroy S1 shared content and revoke current pair access. Create/retain independent solo spaces for A and B and **move both users' private root/dependent content before dropping S1**, because all existing private root tables have mandatory S1 foreign keys with ON DELETE CASCADE. Both members remain signed in as unpaired.
4. **C joins A's S1:** only A's authorized private data may already be present; C cannot access it. No old shared data exists. C brings their own personal private records from their prior solo space into S1, or use future independent private namespace if migrated, without duplicating relationships or leaking private content.
5. **B joins S2:** move B's owned private records from B's solo space into S2 (while preserving ownership/visibility) before retiring B's old solo space. B can remain unpaired and read/edit them instead.
6. **Account deletion:** seven calendar days after explicit request, *not at request time*, delete target account, its personal/private data, personally stored files, sessions and devices, all shared relationship data, and leave the surviving member unpaired with their private data intact. Cancellation during the seven days leaves all account and relationship records untouched. If the deleted user was S1 owner, transfer space ownership to survivor or allocate a clean solo space before deleting the original owner auth identity. Ensure the worker is idempotent and retries incomplete storage deletion safely; never report full success on partial cleanup.

**No new relationship may inherit old shared content.** A solo user needs their own space/namespace even if they were previously invited.

##### Irreversible warning copy

Title (remove / leave / delete space): **Permanently delete our shared content?**

Body: **This action ends the current shared space relationship and permanently deletes all content shared between you and this partner, including shared activities, notes, tasks, conversations, memories, and attachments. OneSpace cannot restore the deleted shared content. You and your partner will each keep only the private content you personally created. The other person's private content will never be shared.**

Action buttons: **Cancel** / **Delete shared content and [remove partner | leave space | delete space]**. Require a deliberate destructive acknowledgment/confirmation, not a harmless `Prepare securely` button. Display exact partner/action context and avoid cross-action language.

Title (account): **Schedule permanent account deletion?**

Body: **Your account and all your personal information will be permanently deleted after 7 days. The shared content with your current partner will also be permanently removed. Your partner keeps their own private content and becomes unpaired. You can cancel before the seven-day deadline; no data is deleted during the grace period. OneSpace cannot restore data after deletion completes.**

Buttons: **Cancel** / **Delete my account in 7 days** (with explicit deliberate confirmation). The authenticated owner alone can request/cancel their own account deletion.

##### Security/engineering requirements

- **Keep database actions atomic:** server transaction holds a lock on original space, verifies the actor's current role and exact members, checks no concurrent join/leave/delete, rehomes private roots and every private child, sanitizes mixed references and shared pointer fields, permanently deletes shared root/dependent rows, updates membership and ownership, and commits only when private preservation is proved. On any mismatch raise an error and roll back **everything**; never leave an orphaned private record.
- **Private attachments preserved; shared attachments erased:** storage objects are not transactional with PostgreSQL. Immediately revoke Storage read access on shared objects with DB tombstones/row removal, then delete the actual bytes through idempotent post-commit cleanup jobs, auditing all failures. Private media must be moved/rekeyed without breaking private access. Deleting a database row is not sufficient if its Storage object remains public or signed links remain live.
- **Audit cross-module edges:** all 69 historically classified source families, including dependents, history, contacts, notes, checklist items, reminders, gestures, calendar, space events, comments, reviews, notifications, trips, uploaded private/shared media, and any pointers inside JSON. These are deletion/preservation categories now, not archive types.
- **User/space attribution:** all private roots currently carry nonnullable `space_id`, normally a cascade FK into `spaces`. An unpaired user must therefore still have a valid solo `spaces` row. No user may have two active `space_members` rows. Any join moves owned private records before old solo space deletion. Ownership checks always use database-authenticated `auth.uid()` and verified roles.
- **RLS and triggers:** ensure all existing policies and SECURITY DEFINER RPCs deny former members or future partners access to prior private records. Update private-vault mirror triggers and any task/trip cross-reference guards for legitimate private rehoming. Never skip triggers broadly or turn off RLS to make migration pass.
- **Account purge worker:** QA R59 worker currently deliberately retains shared content and soft-deletes auth identity. Replace that workflow. Test cancelled, due and already-deleted branches; prevent deletion during grace. Real deletion and its storage purge should be auditably completed, not inferred from task submission.
- **Legacy QA preparation requests:** two actual `awaiting_verified_export` jobs exist. They were signed under an old **non-destructive** "Prepare securely" warning. Never automatically convert these into permanent deletion commands. Ask their respective authenticated initiators to reconfirm using the new irreversible warning after the new transaction is tested.
- **Production:** no Production migration, change or account mutation before QA acceptance. Keep old audit logs only with limited privileged access if required; remove My Archives UI, archive generation routes and cron once no longer used. QA inventory schedule was unscheduled by 0226. Keep janitor on QA only to destroy any pre-existing temporary staging objects safely.
- **Backups:** "cannot restore in OneSpace" is a truthful product promise; normal database disaster-recovery backups may still temporarily retain encrypted copies under the platform's retention policy. Do not falsely claim all backup media is instantly physically erased.

##### Acceptance matrix

- Both members private text + private file persist across all three relationship exits, including unpaired state and joining a *new* space.
- All shared root/dependent records, cross-references and media become inaccessible immediately. Storage physical cleanup finishes with auditable retries.
- A new partner C sees no B private records and no previous shared A/B history, even via old IDs, REST, RPC, push, Storage URLs or cached app state.
- Explicit negative security tests: unauthorized stranger cannot remove another member; invited B cannot delete A's space; repeated concurrent exits are idempotent; revoked users cannot read old space by stale JWT.
- Seven-day request/cancel/purge on both owner and invited partner; surviving member remains unpaired, retains own private items and media. All exact-date calculations are server-side.
- Heavy dataset, chain of private dependent records, mixed-visibility content, media references, failed storage cleanup, cross-space join, QA-vs-Production isolation; zero private data loss.

##### Implementation status at specification date

The policy and QA archive schedule retirement are recorded. **Actual relationship removals and account deletion remain disabled** while rehoming, permanent shared purge, private RLS regression and seven-day purge behavior are implemented/tested. Both original preparation requests remain recorded but have NOT executed. No Production changes.

### A140. OneSpace · `docs/QA_ACTIVITIES_MEMORIES_SCOPE_SLICE_20261120.md`

*First written 20 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: Historical record (iteration notes / test scripts / setup at that date).*

#### QA slice — Activities and Memories account-owned scope (20 Nov 2026)

**Status: source changes prepared for review; NOT a completed, released or two-phone-tested module.**

Policy source: [ADR-2026-11-20](ADR_2026-11-20_PERSONAL_SHARED_OWNERSHIP_AND_DELETION.md).
Tagline remains **Two of Us. One Space**. This slice does not change the tagline.

##### What this branch changes
- `AccountContentScope` calls QA `my_storage_scopes()` and verifies that the personal namespace belongs to the authenticated account. Production retains its pre-cutover routing.
- Activities: new personal records use `private_space_id` with no `space_id`; paired shared records use current `space_id`. Solo personal creation is supported in Coordinate. The list combines both scopes through existing RLS. Quick Add research uses the same scope resolver.
- Activity image and voice services choose personal versus shared Storage buckets from the authenticated RLS-visible parent. QA media payload/voice-message metadata carry the correct bucket identifier. Playback and preview sign within the same parent scope.
- Memories: journals, daily selfies and list/deep-link retrieval include solo account-owned records, while paired shared records remain supported. Personal photos upload into `onespace-private-memory-photos`; signing checks RLS-visible photo metadata. Signed URL cache is partitioned by user.
- The UI hides the shared-creation selector when unpaired for these new editors. There is no automatic archive, export, restore, or separation-triggered move.

##### Database verification on QA (transaction ROLLBACK; no lasting rows)
1. Simulated authenticated account A: create/read a personal Activity, a step child, and a personal Memory. **Passed**.
2. As authenticated account B: cannot read A's Activity or Memory; switching to A restores access. **Passed**.
3. Post-test QA counts: Activities 0; Memories 0; Activity components 0; Memory photos 0. **Verified**.

SQL regression: `supabase/tests/qa_personal_activities_memories_owner_isolation.sql`.
Pure Dart routing tests: `test/account_content_scope_test.dart` (five cases, **not executed**).

##### Not complete / required before merge or release
- Flutter compile/analyze and test suite: **not run**. No local Dart/Flutter SDK in this execution environment, and GitHub Actions build/upload remains subject to account billing/runner availability. PR is deliberately **draft**.
- Full Activity descendants: comment/component paths need deeper integration and negative tests. Unsupported private component types, mentions, notifications, links, search and audit remain open.
- Memory descendants: personal scrapbooks/pages/invites, private photo roundtrip, signed-URL revocation and two-account Storage tests remain open.
- Legacy private rows and descendants/media were not migrated. Do checksummed migration and backward-compatibility review first.
- Lifecycle races, shared media byte deletion/revocation, new-partner onboarding, remove/leave/delete space and seven-day account deletion remain disabled.
- Other roots (Contacts, Trips, Occasions, Check-ins/Goals, Places, Letters, Gestures, Items to sort, Links, Days off) still need combined scope/release audit.
- Two-device QA acceptance not done. Production untouched.

##### Required gates
1. Flutter analyze/tests green; fix any compilation and integration errors.
2. QA two phones / two accounts: owner-private and pair-shared CRUD with photos and voice.
3. Negative RLS and Storage tests: former partner, unrelated user, stale session, URLs and media access.
4. Close cross-module write routes and legacy-data migration before enabling irreversible lifecycle.

**All relationship/account destructive controls remain disabled.**

### A141. OneSpace · `docs/QA_ARCHIVE_COVERAGE_CATALOG_20261120.md`

*First written 20 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by ADR-2026-11-20 (no archiving, 20 Nov 2026). Kept for history.*

#### QA structured archive table catalog — 20 November 2026

##### Contract
Archive and restore **structured private/shared content**. Exclude media bytes and uploaded files/documents, plus their Storage pointers. Never expose a person's private records to the other partner. Shared-history archives are reference-only when a new relationship begins.

##### Deployed policy catalog
- SQL migration: 0164_qa_structured_archive_table_catalog.sql.
- **69 registered table types; 69 explicitly classified; ZERO awaiting policy classification.**
- Root policies: 18 visibility-scoped private/shared content, 4 owner-private, 5 system-excluded and 7 member-shared types; **0 pending**.
- Dependent policies: 24 parent-scoped private/shared types, 5 member-shared types, 4 media exclusions and 2 operational exclusions; **0 pending**.
- Unregistered roots: 0; unregistered FK-linked dependent types: 0.
- Public QA status always says **safe_to_archive=false and safe_to_separate=false**, even if everything gets classified. The preview remains unverified and un-restorable.
- Synthetic future root + dependent tables were discovered as missing and blocked by the QA regression. Both synthetic tables were rolled back.
- Policy table resides in private schema with RLS enabled and no authenticated/anonymous direct table grants.

##### Five additional root types reviewed and approved as member-shared scope
shared_experiences, partner_gestures, space_special_dates,
space_holiday_sources, spaces.

These classifications are grounded in existing RLS SELECT policies for both
active members. This approves **visibility scope**, not archive serialization,
encryption, restoration or destruction.

##### Root classification completed
`item_links` and `record_action_history` are now approved after original-space endpoint/actor checks and exhaustive 15-kind event scope mapping.

**Love Letters protection implemented in QA 0184:** author-private drafts and future-open letters cannot be previewed by a partner before their normal open date. This has not yet been integrated into a complete archive.

##### Dependent classification completed
`note_references`, `occasion_budget_items`, `occasion_budget_log`, `occasion_plan_revisions`, `occasion_proposal_items`, `reminder_actions` and `reminder_recipients` are all approved for their reviewed owner/space scopes.

**Four excluded media-dependent types**: memory_photos, note_images,
trip_attachments, memory_photo_revisions. This exclusion does not yet prove there are no media URLs
embedded elsewhere; the structured sanitizer and coverage tests must remain.

##### Next implementation sequence
1. Review actual columns, FK direction, ownership, RLS and visibility for every pending type. Explicitly model recipient-private actions and comments authored on shared content.
2. Resolve the five known multi-parent routes before serializing a child record; reject conflicting cross-space references.
3. Build the durable encrypted structured-data package including correct nested children and integrity manifest; download is its own external copy.
4. Restore private content into a fresh space with source-ID-to-new-ID remapping, idempotence and no old shared references; shared archive remains reference-only.
5. Prove full two-user lifecycle and finally enable leave/remove/delete behind the verified gate.

No real accounts, spaces, invitations, archives or media were deleted to create this catalog. Production was untouched. QA 468 is still the latest signed APK until a full integration milestone is ready for phone acceptance.

##### QA migration 0166 (deployed, regression passed)
- Additional owner-private roots: gesture_message_overrides, gesture_schedules, gesture_templates; authorship MUST be checked in the eventual serializer.
- Additional shared roots: partner_checkin_requests and space_tags; only the original pair may receive their old shared reference archive.
- Additional operational exclusions: calendar_partner_reminders, object_shares, space_notifications (not portable content or sharing permissions).
- Parent-scoped structured descendants: occasion_plan_steps, occasion_proposals, shared_experience_interests, shared_experience_reviews, trip_flight_options, trip_items, trip_messages.
- Photo revision records are media-excluded; narrative text in separately supported structured records remains in scope.
- **No portable archive, private restore or destructive action is authorized by the extra classifications.** 41 classified, 28 pending, four media classes excluded; fail-closed regression passes. Production unchanged.

##### QA migrations 0167–0168: private trip child preservation (deployed, test passed)
- Adds owner-private vault mirroring for flight options, itinerary items, and trip messages on shared_trips with private visibility. Child authors must equal the private trip's owner; all source tables are explicitly whitelisted.
- Private→shared invalidates child snapshots. Shared→private resynchronizes eligible children; deletion tombstones deleted children. Existing private QA trips are backfilled after the vault type-constraint extension.
- Nested file/Storage pointers are sanitized while retaining written narrative. No physical media is backed up.
- Rollback-only QA regression passed for all transitions, owner mismatch rejection, sanitization and deletion tombstone. No real accounts, spaces or content removed.
- These snapshots are not encrypted downloadable archives; full nested serialization, restoration, relationship lifecycle and Production remain gated.

##### QA migration 0170: indirect media/document pointers excluded (deployed, verified)
- The recursive structured sanitizer now removes scrapbook `photo_N_id` and `image_N_id` fields, upload/file metadata (`file_name`, `file_mime`, `file_size`, `voice_seconds`) and direct attachment/document/media ID keys.
- Captions, notes, original written text and normal non-storage hyperlinks remain intact.
- QA regressions for written text, nested private-trip package, owner-only root snapshots and the new indirect-ID test all pass.
- QA had zero restorable vault root/dependent rows at migration time; no existing snapshots needed re-sanitizing. Future migrations need a backfill check if data exists.
- No changes to archive availability, encryption, restoration or lifecycle safety gates. Production unchanged.

##### QA migrations 0171–0174: private checklists preserved and previewed (deployed and tested)
- The `tasks` private root is already mirrored. Added owner-scoped dependent mirroring for `task_checklist_items` and `task_checklist_checks`, including edits, deletes, visibility transitions, and existing private data backfill.
- For private tasks, `created_by` for a checklist item and `user_id` for a completed check must equal task owner; check `task_id` must resolve through the same checklist item. Partner-authored historical rows cannot be silently transferred into a new private snapshot.
- Explicit catalog classification updated to **43 approved / 26 pending** (4 root types and 22 dependent types still pending).
- Read-only owner-scoped nested checklist preview contains root, items and checks with a SHA-256 payload checksum and rejects corrupt vault snapshots and partner-ownership contamination. Anonymous access blocked.
- Both rollback-only QA regressions passed. Synthetic rows were rolled back. This preview is **not an encrypted download, a verified archive, or a functioning restore**. Lifecycle safety gates remain false.

##### QA 0175 — revoked operational states excluded
- `activity_attention_snoozes`: per-user attention delay is transient state; do not restore obsolete snoozes. The underlying activity remains eligible.
- `scrapbook_invites`: historical invitation/access permissions are not portable; never restore an old partner's collaboration grant. Original memory content remains separately classified.
- Both tables now `system_excluded`; after the QA regression, **45 approved of 69; 24 pending (4 roots, 20 dependents)**. This classification alone does not permit archive generation, deletion or restoration.

##### QA 0181 — recipient-scoped shared-experience written history (deployed, tested)
- Classified `shared_experience_comments` and `shared_experience_revisions` as `shared_only`, following their existing member-shared parent `shared_experiences`. **47/69 approved; 22 pending (4 root, 18 dependent)**.
- A member may see all live shared comments, but a comment the other partner archived is excluded from that member's archive preview. The comment's author can still include their own archived text. Reply pointers to unavailable/hidden comments are removed to avoid exposing private comment identifiers.
- Historical before/after snapshots retain ordinary written descriptions but omit media paths and scrapbook photo IDs through the recursive sanitizer.
- Current-pair authorship, recipient-only scope, deterministic digest, outsider-denial, incomplete-lineage rejection and non-restorable shared-history flags were verified in a rollback-only two-account regression. **No completed encrypted shared archive or shared-history restoration is claimed.**

##### QA 0182–0186 — written nested history reviewed and tested
- **13 new table types** classified with per-root and per-recipient privacy; **60/69 approved, 9 pending**.
- Private/shared parent invariants apply to scrapbook pages and editable revision history; no other partner's old contributions may silently be claimed as new private records.
- Activity components, comments, reactions, decisions and two audit streams are rendered into deterministic, capped structured previews; recursive media/file path redaction preserves textual captions.
- Love Letters drafts and future-open sent letters remain author-private; read receipts and hidden replies are restricted by real open status.
- Place visit captions remain while old memory/trip navigation IDs are inert and stripped, cross-space associations rejected.
- Check-in acknowledgements are accessible only to the original sender/recipient of a responded request, as reference-only history.
- Five synthetic two-account rollback-only QA regressions passed, including outsider requests, media leaks, digest integrity, and share→private collisions. **No portable full archive, trusted restore, or destructive lifecycle permission is implied.**

##### QA 0187–0194 — 69/69 review complete, lifecycle still blocked
- Reminders: recipients/actions preserve user-written completion and follow-up history; transient scheduling, snooze and notification state excluded. Private recipient must be original owner; shared recipients/actors belong to pair.
- Occasion budget items, logs, revisions and proposal-item joins: both ends constrained to the same original plan and space. Keep written budget/audit details, recursively strip media references and avoid replaying orders or purchases.
- Note references/item links: both polymorphic endpoints must exist in same original space and be recipient-visible. A shared link never exposes a private target; private target creator must be original owner. Former-partner shared creators rejected.
- Action history: all 15 record kinds mapped, including archived comment author privacy and check-in request pair lineage. An absent or foreign-space source is rejected rather than silently dropped.
- **Classification complete does not imply archive/restoration complete.** QA public catalog now explicitly reports `encrypted_portable_archive_complete=false`, `trusted_private_restore_complete=false`, `source_of_truth_cutover_complete=false`, `safe_to_archive=false` and `safe_to_separate=false`. Tests for future unregistered tables preserve fail-closed behavior.
- The present preview interfaces are individually scoped and not a 69-type complete encrypted archive. Full manifest completeness, durable verified archive, independent private restore and two-device acceptance remain next P0 priorities.

##### QA 0195–0198 — honest source completeness versus classification
- **69/69 types privacy-classified does not equal 69/69 actual records captured**. The implemented root mirror currently covers 14 specified private source types, and dependent mirroring covers nine specific families. `qa_private_archive_completeness_preflight()` now tests missing/stale/orphan source-vault equality for those supported types.
- **Four remaining private/shared roots outside current root mirroring:** `couple_letters`, `item_links`, `place_visits`, `record_action_history`.
- **Fifteen private/shared dependents outside current child mirroring:** `activity_comments`, `activity_component_reactions`, `activity_component_revisions`, `activity_components`, `activity_decisions`, `activity_revisions`, `couple_letter_reads`, `note_references`, `occasion_budget_log`, `occasion_plan_revisions`, `occasion_proposal_items`, `reminder_actions`, `saved_contact_revisions`, `scrapbook_pages`, `trip_revisions`.
- **Not part of 14+9 source-vault mirroring:** four owner-private root families, seven shared-only root families, five shared-only dependents and operational/media-excluded types. Actual archive policy must separately capture eligible written content and exclude operational/media data.
- New owner-only QA preflight reports per-family counts and missing/stale/orphan/foreign lineage faults, never raw partner-private snapshots. Its canonical subset SHA-256 binds the original owner, source-space and all audited private snapshot hashes. Fingerprint issuance fails if any supported snapshot mismatches, or a future schema type is unreviewed. This fingerprint is **not authenticated archive provenance**, not a real portable encrypted archive and does not authorize restore/leave/remove/delete.
- Four rollback-only SQL tests independently exercised owner/partner isolation, corrupted/missing roots, missing/altered children, old shared-vault tombstones, source-text hash changes and new table schema drift. All fixtures were rolled back.

##### QA 0199–0206 — source enumeration extended; not yet full archive
- Existing owner-private vault subset: **14 roots + 9 descendants** verified against source-of-truth rows and fingerprinted.
- Previously unmirrored private/shared source rows: **4 root + 15 dependent families** now counted with owner-specific SHA-256 over recursively sanitized content. These new source-only censuses **do not persist archived copies** and are not evidence of complete backups.
- Four owner-only roots are enumerated using creator/sender/owner/user-specific attribution, independently of partner privacy: gesture drafts, schedules, overrides and personal days off.
- Twelve shared-only types (7 roots, 5 dependents) have original-pair-scoped source counts/hashes. In shared experiences, archived comments remain visible to the original author only; check-in acknowledgements cannot be included before response or by unauthorized participants.
- **Category accounting: 46 private-eligible + 12 shared-only + 11 media/operational-excluded = 69 privacy-classified families.** It does NOT imply all source rows are counted: **shared rows in mixed-private/shared tables** remain an explicit independent serialization gap; complete multi-family durable encrypted packaging and restoration still do not exist.
- Audit history safety: actions taken by A on B's old shared comment cannot become either user's new private restorable records when B later archives it; missing audit parents still fail closed.
- `public.qa_archive_source_coverage_report()` returns per-recipient hashes and counts only, with `mixed_visibility_shared_rows_censused=false`, `full_per_row_source_manifest_complete=false`, `independently_verified_encrypted_archive=false`, `can_restore=false`, `safe_to_archive=false` and `safe_to_separate=false`.

##### QA 0207–0208 — mixed shared source records independently enumerated
- **42 additional visibility-specific source scans:** 18 root + 24 dependent `private_shared` families are now counted when their parent is shared, including eligible sent/opened Love Letters, shared activities and discussions, tasks/checklists, trips, occasions/budgets, places, links and original-space action history. Author, recipient, original pair and parent-child scope are checked.
- This completes **per-table privacy-aware current-source census coverage**: private-scoped records across 46 private-eligible families, the 12 shared-only families, and the shared side of all 42 mixed-visibility families. Eleven excluded operational/media families remain excluded by policy. It is **not a source snapshot archive**.
- Two-account tests include 18 nonempty shared families, same hashed written discussion for both members, changed shared messages changing hash for both, private note changes not affecting partner's hash, cross-author contributions rejected and orphan comment references kept non-restorable.
- Source census still returns only counters and hashes; **most eligible source records are not durably saved, encrypted, signed, downloadable or restorable**. Never treat successful per-table inventory as authorization to leave, unpair, delete or migrate relationship ownership.

### A142. OneSpace · `docs/QA_PRIVATE_SHARED_SCOPE_MILESTONES_20261120.md`

#### QA four-module two-scope lifecycle cutover — progress and next gates (20 November 2026)

**Authoritative product policy:** [ADR-2026-11-20](ADR_2026-11-20_PERSONAL_SHARED_OWNERSHIP_AND_DELETION.md). This is a status report, not a new decision.

##### Completed and merged

| PR | Outcome | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| [#119](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/pull/119) | Final no-archive, account-owned private space / current-pair shared space, irreversible warnings and seven-day account-deletion rules captured in ADR + Decision Log | Merged |
| [#118](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/pull/118) | QA private and shared **Notes** through one interface. Account-owned private RLS + personal audit; paired shared notes; search, voice and share-in routes | Flutter analyze/tests green; authenticated DB rollback tests owner/partner/outsider green |
| [#120](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/pull/120) | QA private and shared **To-do and Checklists** through one interface, including solo private lists. Private task, checklist items/checks and audit scoped to account; paired shared lists. No legacy archive mirror required for new personal tasks | Flutter analyze/tests green; authenticated DB rollback tests owner, partner, outsider, child/check and audit green |
| [#122](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/pull/122) | QA private and shared **Reminders**. Personal reminder recipients, history, due scheduling, notifications and push queue/claim now work without pair membership; ownership/scope immutable for Notes/Tasks/Reminders. Unified app, voice and search. | Flutter analysis + suite passed; transaction-rolled-back owner/outsider, unpaired delivery, snooze, due notification, FCM claim and notification write-security tests passed |
| [#123](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/pull/123) | QA private and shared **Calendar events** in one UI, with solo private event creation, owner-only RLS and audit, paired shared visibility and account-owned reminder creation. UI clears old couple records on refresh; private event linking withheld pending safe cross-scope rules. | Flutter analysis + suite passed; rollback-only owner/partner/outsider, edit, ownership tamper and simulated unpaired event tests passed |
| [#117](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/pull/117) | Personal namespace per account, all 22 eligible content root tables prepared with mutually exclusive scope columns; obsolete account-deletion path retired | QA migrations & RLS contract tests green |
| [#116](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/pull/116) | Archive inventory schedule retired, no-archive policy adopted | QA cron absent |

###### Latest QA integrity and state

- Notes, Tasks, checklist-item, checklist-check, Reminders and Calendar event test records: **0**, verified after rollback-only tests.
- Space memberships: **6**; pending account deletions: **0**.
- The two original preparation requests are **cancelled** (`partner_leaves` transition 2026-11-20 03:03:48 UTC; `owner_removes_partner` transition 2026-11-20 03:04:44 UTC). **Neither was executed as an irreversible membership/deletion action.** This check does not establish who cancelled them.
- Obsolete archive inventory cron: **0 scheduled**. 
- QA security advisor still has pre-existing warning categories: 7 `rls_enabled_no_policy`, one anonymous-executable security-definer category, and 18 authenticated-executable security-definer category findings. The latest scan did not mark Notes/Tasks/Reminders/Calendar changes, but those warnings are **not** closed by these PRs.
- **Production untouched. No signed QA APK has been generated from the new Notes/To-do/Reminders/Calendar code.** It is merged in source and backed by applied QA migrations, not yet two-phone acceptance-tested.

##### Mandatory next engineering gates, in priority order

1. **Finish remaining module scopes**, not new duplicate UIs: Activities, Memories, Contacts, Trips, Occasions, shared experiences, and remaining eligible roots and descendants. Calendar and Reminders have initial QA cutovers but also require cross-module integration and two-phone acceptance. Normalize direct-create routes, unified search/realtime, mention handling, shared-to-private/vice-versa editing, and unpaired access.
2. **Cross-object integration and private file/child ownership:** Reminders and Calendar personal objects currently avoid unsafe source references; update their authorized private source-link behavior, Today/agenda and notification context before broad release.  Notes photos/linked references remain deliberately unavailable for newly account-owned private records until independent Storage, child policies, and access revocation are verified; same for other module attachments. Ensure private files remain accessible after leave/space deletion.
3. **Legacy records and old-app compatibility:** migrate any existing legacy `visibility='private'` roots and dependents into account-owned scopes without data loss. Verify snapshots/checksums and retain rollback plan. Do not change their `space_id` blindly; stale app versions must not reintroduce private rows to couple storage.
4. **Immediate relationship lifecycle:** transactional remove/leave/delete shared scope after deliberate irreversible warning, with all former-couple shared roots, children, media pointers and session access revoked. Private account scopes unchanged. Retryable physical file cleanup after DB commit. Test races, new partner C, and leave/unpaired state on two phones.
5. **Seven-day account deletion:** request shows exact expiry, no deletion during grace; cancellation restores normal account status. At deadline, idempotent deletion of requester + requester private + current couple shared; survivor private untouched and survivor unpaired. Verify auth sessions, Storage, notification and login safety. Legacy keep-shared soft purge is forbidden.
6. **Launch gates:** QA data integrity, permissions/RLS, heavy and empty datasets, false/failed network, Android and iOS QA builds, Production isolation, then deliberate staged Production release.

**Safety rule:** Until steps 1–5 pass, destructive relationship and account deletion controls remain disabled. No archive, restore, My Archives or 30-day delivery scheme is part of this lifecycle.

### A143. OneSpace · `docs/QA_PRIVATE_VAULT_SUBSET_OSA_FORMAT_20261120.md`

*First written 20 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by ADR-2026-11-20 (no archiving, 20 Nov 2026). Kept for history.*

#### OneSpace QA private vault subset: first real encrypted `.osa` file

> QA only, source prototype. **NOT** a complete OneSpace relationship archive, signed backup, trusted restore, account deletion approval or Production feature. The canonical product tagline remains **Two of Us. One Space**.

##### What runs now

- QA migration `0209`: `public.qa_private_vault_subset_manifest()`, signed-in user only. First checks source-to-vault equality, owner membership in exactly one original space, full root/child lineage and the QA schema policy. Returns bounded *real sanitized snapshot JSON strings* for **14 root + 9 child** currently mirrored families, their original PKs and parents, exact PostgreSQL JSON UTF-8 byte lengths, SHA-256 for each snapshot and the independent source-vault aggregate SHA.
- `supabase/functions/_shared/qa_vault_subset_osa.mjs`: verifies source hashes again **before encryption**; produces real binary UTF-8 `.osa` file content with AES-256-GCM ciphertext (random 16B salt + 12B nonce, PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 600,000 iterations), authenticated additional data binding original owner, space and full source subset SHA.
- After opening a `.osa`, the verifier checks file/ciphertext size and SHA, AES-GCM authenticity, original owner and space, all source rows, exact SHA and byte lengths, root-parent mapping and original private ownership. Local disk-file roundtrip is exercised by Node 22 GitHub Actions.
- `supabase/scripts/qa_private_vault_subset_export.mjs` provides `exportQaPrivateVaultSubset({url,anonKey,accessToken,passphrase,destination})`. Only the actual **QA** Supabase hostname is accepted, only with a caller-provided authenticated user's token. Password remains local and never appears in RPC requests; output is created with exclusive 0600 permissions and will not overwrite existing files.

##### Source format

The authenticated SQL response carries `schema=onespace.qa.private-vault-subset-manifest.v1`, an `owner_id`, original `source_space_id`, `records` sorted by kind/table/source ID, and `subset_sha256`.

Each record contains `kind`, `source_table`, `source_id`, `parent_table`, `parent_id`, `snapshot_json` (the actual PostgreSQL JSONB `::text`), `snapshot_bytes`, and `snapshot_sha256`.

The snapshot SHA is over **exact UTF-8 `snapshot_json` bytes**. The subset digest follows:

```text
SHA256(UTF8("onespace.qa.private.vault-subset.v1|" +
  owner_id + "|" + source_space_id + "|" +
  sorted_records.map(r =>
    r.kind + "|" + r.source_table + "|" + r.source_id + "|" +
    r.snapshot_sha256
  ).join("\n")))
```

The `.osa` envelope includes `format=onespace.qa.vault-subset.osa.v1`, `owner_id`, `source_space_id`, `ciphertext_sha256`, `ciphertext_bytes`, `subset_sha256`, `kdf`, `iterations`, `salt_b64`, `nonce_b64`, and `ciphertext_b64`. The entire manifest is encrypted; user-written notes are not present in the outer file metadata.

##### Safety properties

- Original owner and original space are in AES-GCM authenticated data. Wrong password, other owner's ID, other space ID, altered file bytes, record hashes, missing/duplicate children, foreign authorship, orphan checklist items or unreviewed tables cause failure.
- Private content is never silently reclassified as shared. Private-mirror root verification includes every source row, not just records already in the mirror.
- Storage object paths, uploaded documents, images, voice/media identifiers, embedded binary data and direct/indirect document URLs are refused by the client verifier. Native source sanitizer strips known media references while preserving adjacent prose. Media itself is **not recoverable** through this subset.
- The encryption password is known only to the client running export; it is not transmitted to Supabase, embedded in `.osa`, or printed in CI logs.
- This QA endpoint returns **plaintext to the authorized account** over HTTPS before local encryption. Do not log, cache or forward the RPC JSON. This source implementation is **not integrated into Android/iOS** and has **not yet been exercised using an actual QA user token**; test export network calls are mocked.
- Bounds: max 5,000 records, up to 8 MiB source snapshots and 512 KiB per source record; aggregate encrypted file bounded at 20 MiB.

##### What this does *not* prove

1. **Completeness**: only 23 mirrored owner-private family types are packaged; the other private-eligible and shared written records are still source-censused, not physically archived.
2. **Server origin**: anyone possessing a passphrase can create a valid encrypted file. AES-GCM authenticity proves integrity under that passphrase, **not** a trusted source-of-truth service signature or authenticated issuance.
3. **Supabase Storage download**: QA-only authenticated staged ciphertext upload, readback verification, owner-only download and 24h cleanup are now deployed via `onespace-qa-subset-file`. This remains a **staged partial file**, not a real `space_archives` lifecycle object; no genuine signed-in user file has yet completed a live QA Storage roundtrip.
4. **Restore**: no transactionally verified private restoration, ID remapping for the other 46 types, replay protection or original-pair shared reference preservation.
5. **Mobile acceptance**: Flutter/iOS/Android UI and two-account device tests are not wired to this prototype. Do not enable partner removal, leave, delete or account deletion.

##### Next engineering phases

1. Expand durable per-row manifests to **all eligible written content** within the 69 classified families; keep media/operational exclusions and original-pair author privacy.
2. Provide verified authenticated *server issuance* with immutable source snapshot ID/revision and a cryptographic signature verifiable independently after download. Never store the recovery passphrase on the server.
3. **Staging infrastructure complete (QA 0210–0212)**: now run a real authenticated user's `.osa` upload/download through QA private Storage, and independently verify that downloaded bytes decrypt locally. Then implement the separate trusted `space_archives` issuance path, complete source signatures and real downloader acceptance; staging is not lifecycle readiness.
4. Implement target-space member authorization, transactionality, idempotent ID remapping, private restoration and old shared reference-only history. Run both Android phones and then separately review deletion gates.

CI and QA transactional tests run with temporary synthetic records; all test fixtures must roll back. Do not deploy these QA facilities to Production until the full milestone is accepted.

##### QA 0210–0212 staging addendum
- New private internal 24h registry, service-only reservation/verification/lookup/purge, three-file concurrent-safe owner quota, exact owner/UUID Storage paths, dedicated signed-in subset upload/download function and a 24h janitor extension are deployed on QA only.
- Existing client adapter now also supports locally validated `uploadQaPrivateVaultSubset` / `downloadQaPrivateVaultSubset`; recovery passphrase remains client-only.
- All status flags remain nonrestorable and unverified as full lifecycle archives. This is verified **ciphertext transport/storage integrity**, not authoritative **source provenance** or full record coverage.
- See [QA Subset Encrypted Storage Staging](QA_SUBSET_ENCRYPTED_STORAGE_STAGING_20261120.md).

### A144. OneSpace · `docs/QA_STRUCTURED_DATA_MILESTONE_20261120.md`

*First written 20 Nov 2026 · last changed 20 Nov 2026 · Status: ⚠️ OVERRIDDEN by ADR-2026-11-20 (no archiving, 20 Nov 2026). Kept for history.*

#### OneSpace QA Lifecycle Milestone Board — 20 November 2026

##### Approved product rules (do not reinterpret)
- Official tagline: **Two of Us. One Space**.
- Founder authorizes deleting historical QA records if needed for a **database ownership redesign**. No Production data changes.
- Archive **AND restore** must preserve structured private/shared content. **Exclude uploaded documents, photos, audio/video and other media**, including file pointers. Tell users clearly those excluded files are not recoverable from the archive.
- Private structured records belong to each person across relationships. Old shared history must never be loaded into a new partner's space.
- Leaving/removing a partner: the original owner continues in a clean new relationship boundary; partner unpaired. Deleting the space: both unpaired. Account deletion: separate seven-day grace-period workflow.
- Keep all destructive actions disabled until real end-to-end private/shared recovery and privacy checks succeed.

##### Founder’s four consecutive closure gates — current honest status

| Milestone | Current status | Explicit exit criteria (not achieved until all pass) |
|---|---|---|
| **1. Stabilize QA app** | **OPEN** — QA signed Android 0.1.468 published; latest signed-in recipient QA invitation RPC returns `ready`, backend invitation regressions exist. **Not tested end-to-end on both phones.** | Both new-account and existing-account invite flows; confirmation standalone page; deep-link persistence; no false expired warning/three unrelated setup cards; reset email and hosted branded confirmation verified; cancellation, role tests, app session/biometric tests on both Android phones; canonical branding in **new signed APK** |
| **2. Encrypted structured archive** | **OPEN** — 69/69 table privacy classification and source censuses; 23 already-mirrored private table families have per-record manifest and real locally encrypted `.osa` format; QA encrypted Storage staging with mocked end-to-end transfer and expiry. **No real live-user archive or full recovery.** | Include all eligible private and shared **written/structured** records; **exclude all uploaded photos, audio/video and documents with clear unrecoverable warning**; trusted original source issuance; verified external download, decrypt, integrity and full private restoration into a safe relationship boundary |
| **3. Real lifecycle** | **BLOCKED / DISABLED** | Prove B Leave, owner Remove B, owner Delete Space, clean-space continuity, 7-day account-deletion grace, re-login/cancel and safe cleanup. No destructive switch before Milestone 2 restore acceptance |
| **4. Full QA and security sign-off** | **OPEN** | Two-device signed QA acceptance, offline-partner failure paths, RLS/20 privileged API abuse review, Firebase push, media cleanup, recovery and encryption, QA/Production isolation, release go/no-go |

###### Immediate accountability notes (20 November 2026)
- OneSpace's **only** approved tagline is **Two of Us. One Space**. Earlier tagline strings remained in active Theme Concept and Appearance UI previews and many docs. Branch/PR #102 removes them and expands a recursive regression guard. **Source patch is not yet included in the signed 0.1.468 APK.**
- Production's 3 activities, 2 notes and 2 tasks were created in the **same database timestamp batch** (19 Nov 2026 10:40:10.435625 UTC), with corresponding audit rows seconds later and no recorded auth-user sign-ins. This supports an administrative SQL/seed-origin hypothesis, **not proof of which person/tool ran it**. Preserve Production records and investigate provenance; **no Production deletes or changes**.
- The latest QA invitation (19 Nov 18:24 UTC) is active and addressed to a confirmed, not-yet-paired QA account. A rollback-only read as that recipient confirmed its backend resolver reports **`ready`**, not expired. The **device UI/deep-link handoff is still not accepted**. Do not blame an old invitation email without evidence.
- Product owner permission to hard-delete QA history applies only if needed to redesign ownership and archive rules; it never means discard structured archive-and-restore or touch Production.
- Application build guard checks Android package `onespace.qa` against QA Supabase; Production requires `live.onespace.app` and the distinct Production project. This verifies source configuration boundaries, **not** user/device acceptance.
- Email HTML templates are in source with the approved brand; **Supabase-hosted Auth templates and the actual delivered confirmation/reset emails cannot be considered verified from GitHub source alone**.

##### P0 Android QA black screen: source-level cause introduced by R468
- **Observed by tester:** QA Android screen entirely black from R468 onward; test releases 0.1.468 and 0.1.471 compiled/published, yet CI did not test actual first app frame.
- **High-confidence code-level root cause:** GitHub R464→R468 comparison proves `lib/main.dart` changed the bootstrap tagline from context-free `Text(tt('Two of us, One Space'))` to `Text(tr(context, 'tagline'))`. The `context` belongs to `_OneSpaceBootstrapState.build`, **outside** the newly constructed `MaterialApp`; `tr(context,...)` calls `Localizations.localeOf(context)` and cannot find a `Localizations` ancestor at first build. This throws while drawing the root bootstrap and can leave a black/blank launch screen. Actual affected phone logcat is still needed before excluding other native/graphics problems.
- **P0 fix merged PR #113**, source commit `4625aa84f435facd8f6bd828d40b6bec1dece8eb`: bootstrap uses the approved exact literal **Two of Us. One Space** without context lookup, initializes a readable light surface and fallback icon; native Android package gate added in R468 now has a 6-second deadline, retains strict QA/Production mismatch denial; session startup has a timeout and visible errors; native dark launch background is no longer opaque black.
- **Tests:** dedicated first-frame widget regression and native package timeout/mismatch tests passed, as did full QA Flutter verification for the earlier commit of this PR. New Android debug and release-mode installed-app emulator smoke workflows are underway, *not yet accepted*. This code does not delete/corrupt QA or Production user data, clear device storage, alter invitation state or enable lifecycle deletion.
- **Signed Android hotfix workflow:** [#44652189859](https://github.com/BoyofKay/OneSpace/actions/runs/44652189859) triggered by `release/READY_TO_BUILD.md`, not yet accepted/published at the last check. Upon completion, test installation over the current QA app (never uninstall), launch in normal and dark device mode, authenticated restore, latest invitation and two-device Firebase push.
- **Status:** source bug repaired, pending signed APK build and real phone startup acceptance. Do **not** label black screen resolved on-device until the tester confirms the first frame.
